## XIV A Broken Tile / 第十四章 一块碎裂的瓷砖

===Original===
Almost exactly four months after he had completed his second inventory of the contents of Beech Lodge, Mr. Jarrad, again accompanied by Mr. Dawkins, stood once more in the paneled study. He had come to the house with his admirable manner, in which was blended this time a rather full knowledge of what had recently happened. Mr. Dawkins, who also read the papers, and was, as well, impressed by the air of the older man, seemed rather taciturn. There had been opportunity to say a good deal on the way down from London, and he was distinctly thrilled when they turned in at the white gate. Now the inventory book was opened and laid on Millicent’s desk. Mr. Jarrad then took out a large handkerchief and blew his nose with a trumpet-like sound as though he enjoyed it. He had ascertained that the Derricks were in the garden, and both servants back in the kitchen. The morning was fine and clear.

“I don’t know,” he said with a touch of unction, “when I’ve heard of a case just exactly like this. Here we are, paid to do precisely the same thing over again simply because a foolish woman killed herself. We’ve both seen houses that were enough to make any really sensitive person commit suicide, but”﻿—he glanced round with open approval﻿—“they were not houses like this. It all brings back to me the great truth that the foundation of our business is the undeniable suspicion that well-bred people have of each other. There’s practically no inventory connection with the lower and lower middle classes. Do you happen to remember a remark I made about ‘things’ when we were here last?”

“I do,” replied Dawkins; “and, what’s more, I’ve been thinking about it ever since.”

“Well, these are not the kind of things to make one tired of life. There’s another point. I expressed my conclusions about the manner in which ‘things’ occupy the greater part of the time of so many women.”

“You did,” said Dawkins soberly, “and I said it wasn’t that way with us because we hadn’t any. But my young woman has started since then.”

===Chinese===
在他完成对山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge）全部藏品的第二次清点之后，差不多整整四个月，贾拉德先生（Mr. Jarrad）再次由道金斯先生（Mr. Dawkins）陪同，站到了那间镶着护墙板的书房里。他到宅子来时依旧端着那副令人叹服的气派，只是这一回，气派之中又掺进了一份对近期种种变故相当详尽的了解。道金斯先生也读报纸，又被年长者的气度所慑，显得颇为沉默。从伦敦下来的路上有的是畅谈的机会，而当车子拐进那扇白门时，他心头分明掠过一阵兴奋。此刻，清点册已经摊开，放在米莉森特（Millicent）的书桌上。贾拉德先生随即掏出一块大手帕，像吹号似的擤了一声鼻涕，仿佛乐在其中。他已经探明：德里克（Derrick）一家在花园里，两个仆人都待在后头的厨房。早晨天气晴朗，澄澈明净。

“我不记得，”他带着几分洋洋自得的郑重说，“几时听说过一桩跟这案子一模一样的事。瞧瞧咱们，就因为一个蠢女人自寻了短见，便收了钱来把同一件事原封不动再做一遍。咱们俩都见过那种房子，足够叫任何一个真正敏感的人寻短见，可是”——他环视四周，满脸毫不掩饰的赞许——“那样的房子可跟这一座不一样。这一切又让我想起一条伟大的真理：咱们这一行的根基，就是有教养的人彼此之间那种不容抵赖的猜疑。跟下层阶级和下层中产阶级，几乎谈不上有什么清点业务。你可记得咱们上次在这儿的时候，我就‘东西’发表过的一句高论？”

“记得，”道金斯答道，“而且打那以后，我就一直在琢磨这句话。”

“嗯，这些‘东西’可不是叫人活腻了的那一类。还有一点。我发表过一通结论，讲‘东西’如何占去了那么多女人的大半光阴。”

“你确实发表过，”道金斯正色道，“我还说咱们不是那样，因为咱们压根儿一件也没有。可打那以后，我那位年轻太太也置办起来了。”

===Original===
Mr. Jarrad smiled. “Quite so; that was inevitable; and now that Mrs. Millicent has disposed of hers to Mr. Thursby, Miss Millicent, who will marry Mr. Derrick next week, is already starting another collection. I hope she may do as well as this. She can’t do better. I don’t know when I’ve seen a room I like more. Her mother’s work, of course, all of it.”

“Why do you suppose that woman killed Mr. Millicent?” asked Dawkins thoughtfully. “I read it all several times over in several papers, but it always struck me there was a good deal that didn’t meet the eye.”

Mr. Jarrad smiled again. “Why, do you suppose, does a woman do anything?”

“I don’t know yet. I’ve only been married a year.”

“Then you know more now than you will in ten. The appearance of Perkins suggested that she might do anything at any moment, if you remember. If the cause was what it usually is with a woman﻿—jealousy, or, in other words, love that has grown the wrong way﻿—I can only wonder why she waited so many years. There are a good many queer things about the case; for instance, that foreigner who shammed dead when he was under arrest, then slid out of the station.”

===Chinese===
贾拉德先生微微一笑。“正是；这也在所难免；如今米莉森特太太已把她的那一批出让给了瑟斯比先生（Mr. Thursby），而下星期就要嫁给德里克先生的米莉森特小姐，已经着手另起一摊收藏了。但愿她也能集到这么好的一批。再好也不可能了。我不记得几时见过比这更合我意的房间。当然，这全是她母亲的手笔。”

“你揣摩，那个女人为什么要杀米莉森特先生？”道金斯若有所思地问。“好几家报纸的报道我都反复读过几遍，可我总觉得，这案子里大有内情，远不是表面看得透的。”

贾拉德先生又笑了。“那你揣摩，一个女人无论做什么，又是为了什么呢？”

“我还说不上来。我结婚才一年。”

“那你如今懂得的，比十年之后懂得的还要多。珀金斯（Perkins）那副相貌就透着，她随时都干得出任何事情来，不知你记得不记得。倘若起因是女人惯常的那一种——嫉妒，换句话说，是长歪了的爱情——那我只能纳闷，她为什么等了这么多年。这桩案子里古怪的事情着实不少；比如那个外国人，落网时装死，随后又从警察局里脱身溜走。”

===Original===
“I wonder what he was doing here?”

“Might as well ask why Mr. Millicent’s old gardener came back as though he wanted to stick his head into the noose,” said Mr. Jarrad sententiously. “Might as well ask why my client is willing to pay through the nose to get this house back just after letting it for a term of years﻿—though I suspect there’s a woman in that, too. Might as well ask why your client began by trying to hunt out Mr. Millicent’s murderer and finished by finding his daughter. Might as well ask a heap of things that will never be answered, and perhaps in the long run it’s just as well they’re not. We know as much as is good for us as it is, and what we don’t know can’t hurt us much as long as we keep on not knowing it. Now what about the contents of this room?”

“The stuff seems the same with a few additions, but a little differently arranged; that’s all.”

Mr. Jarrad strolled about, his sharp eyes very active, returned to the desk, leaned over, then adjusted his glasses. He peered for a moment and frowned.

“That’s really very odd.”

===Chinese===
“我倒纳闷，他到这里来干什么？”

“这你不妨问问看，米莉森特先生那位老园丁，为什么偏要回来，一副自己巴不得把脑袋伸进绞索里的架势，”贾拉德先生用格言般的口吻说。“也不妨问问，我的委托人为什么刚把这宅子按年约租了出去，转眼又不惜血本地要收回来——虽说这一层，我疑心背后也有个女人。也不妨问问，你的委托人怎么起初一心要揪出杀害米莉森特先生的凶手，到头来却找到了他的女儿。也不妨问一大堆永远得不到回答的事——而长远看来，得不到回答也许倒也好。照眼下这样，我们知道得已经够多了，再多就无益；那些不知道的事，只要一直不知道下去，便伤不着我们分毫。好了，这间屋子里的东西怎么说法？”

“东西看上去还是原样，添了几件，只是摆法稍有不同；就这些。”

贾拉德先生踱来踱去，一双锐利的眼睛四下扫个不停，随后回到书桌旁，俯下身去，扶了扶眼镜。他端详片刻，皱起了眉头。

“这真是非常奇怪。”

===Original===
“What is?”

“You remember we didn’t agree about a stain here, and returned so that I could satisfy you on the point? It was a little difficult to detect.”

Dawkins wetted his thumb and turned a few leaves in the big book.

“Yes, here it is, a post entry, and initialed by both of us. ‘Large, irregular stain on near left-hand corner of leather desk top, nearly effaced.’ Right ho! let’s have a look!”

He came over, stared hard, and straightened up with an exclamation. “You must have mesmerized me into seeing that before. It’s certainly not there now, and the light is excellent. What do you make of it?”

===Chinese===
“什么奇怪？”

“你可记得，关于这儿的一处污痕，咱们俩意见不合，后来还专门回来过一趟，好让我在这一处说服你？那污痕有点难以辨认。”

道金斯舔湿拇指，在那本大册子里翻了几页。

“有了，在这儿，一条补记，咱们俩都签了姓名缩写。‘皮革桌面上靠身这一侧的左手一角，一大片不规则污痕，已近乎磨灭。’好嘞！来瞧瞧！”

他走过去，死死盯住桌面，随即一声惊呼，直起身来。“上回准是你对我施了催眠术，我才看见那污痕的。现在肯定是没有了，何况光线这么好。这事你怎么看？”

===Original===
“What we don’t know won’t hurt us,” said Jarrad with a slow shake of the head. “Initial this erasure, will you. What’s next?”

Dawkins looked troubled, and a little anxious. “But I say﻿—”

“I began just the way you’re going on now, but I got over it. I suggest that so far as this room is concerned we just count the books and articles of furniture, pass on their general condition, and call the thing a go. Your clients are not the kind who give me any worry.”

Dawkins nodded and began the recital, reading from the book in a rapid and level singsong as though he were chanting the creed of his profession.

“General condition excellent,” he concluded, and shut the book.

===Chinese===
“咱们不知道的事，伤不着咱们，”贾拉德缓缓摇着头说。“在这条涂销处签个缩写，劳驾。下一条是什么？”

道金斯面有难色，还有几分不安。“可是我说——”

“我起初也正像你现在这副样子，后来就想通了。我提议，这间屋子嘛，咱们就点一点书和家具的件数，对整体状况给个评语，就此了结拉倒。你的委托人不是那种会叫我操心的人。”

道金斯点点头，开始照册逐条诵读，语调急促而平板，像是在诵念他那一行的信条。

“整体状况：极佳。”他念完这一句，合上了册子。

===Original===
Mr. Jarrad shook his head. “I can’t agree to that now. The maintenance is not what it was. Quite obvious that the housemaid is untrained or lazy; possibly both. Look at this mantel.”

He drew a finger across the top of the mantel behind the clock, and left a faint trail where the dust had been displaced.

“Couldn’t do that the last time we were here. No, the upkeep is not as good. Condition fair, I should say, at the most. See for yourself.”

Dawkins sniffed and investigated. “Perhaps you’re right. I suppose my client is a little short of help. All right, ‘condition fair.’ Anything else?”

Mr. Jarrad glanced at the hearth. “Yes, one thing. One fireplace tile split. You have no note of that, I think, and it’s the only real damage we’ve seen.”

===Chinese===
贾拉德先生摇了摇头。“这一条我现在可不能同意了。维护已大不如前。明摆着，那女仆要么没受过调教，要么偷懒；兴许两样都占。瞧瞧这壁炉台。”

他伸出一根手指，从座钟后头的壁炉台面上抹过，在拂开积尘的地方留下一道淡淡的痕迹。

“咱们上回来的时候，可抹不出这个来。不，维护确实不如从前了。要我说，状况至多算‘尚可’。你自己瞧吧。”

道金斯哼了哼鼻子，俯身查验。“也许你说得对。我想我的委托人是手头缺了点人手。好吧，‘状况尚可’。还有别的吗？”

贾拉德先生朝壁炉瞥了一眼。“有，还有一件。一块壁炉瓷砖裂了。你的册子里没有这一条吧，我想，而这是咱们见到的唯一真正的损坏。”

===Original===
“No, I’ve nothing here. Let me see it.”

He was bending over the hearth when Derrick came in. Jarrad made his well-known bow.

“We have just completed this room, sir, and the only real dilapidation we find is in this hearth. It’s a small matter, but nothing is too small for us to note. Perhaps you may remember when it happened, as it’s evidently quite recent.”

Derrick stared at the cracked tile.

“Yes,” he said slowly, “I remember that very distinctly.”

===Chinese===
“没有，我这儿没记。让我看看。”

他正俯身朝壁炉望去，德里克走了进来。贾拉德行了个他那出名的一躬。

“先生，这间屋子我们刚刚查完，发现的唯一真正的破损就在这座壁炉上。区区小事，不过事再小，我们也照例记上一笔。这事明摆着就发生在最近，您或许还记得是什么时候裂的。”

德里克盯着那块裂开的瓷砖。

“是的，”他缓缓说道，“我记得清清楚楚。”

## VII A Mysterious Peddler / 第七章 一个神秘的货郎

===Original===
He went to the Millicents’ that afternoon, the bangle in his pocket, and found Jean unaffectedly glad to see him. Mrs. Millicent had said nothing to her daughter, but her manner had been that of one who approves. She liked Derrick and had conceived a genuine fondness for Edith. The contemplated summer in France was becoming a little indefinite. In a few moments she murmured something and disappeared. Derrick thought rapidly and looked straight into the girl’s clear eyes. Then he held out the bangle.

“Will you take this from me? It has a curious something about it.”

Jean hesitated, the look on his face being unmistakable. “It’s charming. Where did you get it?”

“From Burma,” he said slowly. “It arrived this morning by a peddler who is staying the night with Martin. He seemed grateful for my allowing it and insisted that I take this from his pack.”

===Chinese===
当天下午，他揣着那只手镯去了米莉森特（Millicent）家，发现琴（Jean）见了他，是发自心底的高兴。米莉森特太太（Mrs. Millicent）对女儿只字未提，可她的态度分明是一位首肯者的态度。她喜欢德里克（Derrick），对伊迪丝（Edith）也生出了真心实意的喜爱。原本盘算好的法国之夏，渐渐变得有点没准儿了。不多一会儿，她含含糊糊说了句什么，便走开了。德里克思绪飞转，直直地望进姑娘那双清澈的眼睛。然后他掏出了那只手镯。

"愿意把它从我这儿收下吗？它身上有点说不出的古怪。

琴犹豫了一下，他脸上的神情已明明白白，无可误会。"真好看。你是从哪儿弄来的？

"从缅甸来的，"他缓缓说道。"今儿早上，一个货郎把它送了来，眼下正在马丁（Martin）家过夜。我准他留宿，他像是颇为感激，非要我从他的货担里取下这个不可。

===Original===
She stared at the yellow circlet. “Does he know Martin?”

“He pretended that he did not, but Martin knew him without question and was horrified to see him.”

Jean did not speak, but her eyes were full of swift wonder. “And then?”

“Then it was my turn to pretend that I had noticed nothing. They are together now and will be till tomorrow morning, at any rate. That’s one reason I came here.”

===Chinese===
她盯着那黄澄澄的镯圈。"他认识马丁吗？

"他装作不认识，可马丁毫无疑问是认得他的，而且一看见他，吓得不轻。

琴没有作声，眼里却掠过一阵急速的惊疑。"后来呢？

"后来就轮到我装作什么也没瞧见了。这会儿他们俩正待在一处，至少要待到明天早上。这就是我今天来的缘由之一。

===Original===
She did not ask the other but slid the bangle on her wrist with a slow and lingering touch. Derrick’s gaze did not leave her. He saw the color flood and desert her cheek, and the pulse throbbing in her slim throat. How utterly desirable she was! This was the indescribable quality about which Edith had talked with a cheerfulness that he now saw must have cost her dearly; the thing that secured what all women at some time long to possess.

He waited breathlessly, but she was still silent. Her heart whispered one thing, but over her there yet hung a cloud of memories that well nigh blotted out all else. For so long she had thought of herself as the child of a foully murdered man, for so long had the menace seemed to be transferred to herself, that the promise of a future such as she believed she saw in Derrick’s eyes seemed almost as unreal as it was divine. She was already more than fond of him and admitted it in secret hours. It was something new and strange and alluring for the mind to feed on. But what escape would it mean till the secret of Beech Lodge had been read, and the weight lifted from her soul? She took the bangle because she did not want to hurt him, but her eyes avoided his.

“What do you think is going to happen now?” she asked shakily.

===Chinese===
她没有问那另一个缘由，只把那只手镯缓缓地、恋恋地套上手腕。德里克的目光没有离开她。他看见红晕涌上她的双颊，又退了下去；看见她纤细的喉间脉搏突突地跳动。她是何等令人心醉神驰的人啊！这就是伊迪丝说起过的那种难以描摹的气质——她说起来那样轻快，如今他才明白，那份轻快必定让她付出了不小的代价；也正是这样一种东西，稳稳握住了所有女人在一生某个时候都渴望得到的那一切。

他屏息等待着，她却仍不作声。她的心低语着一件事，可她头顶仍悬着一团记忆的阴云，几乎把其余一切都遮没了。这么久以来，她一直把自己当作一个惨遭凶害之人的女儿；这么久以来，那威胁仿佛已经转到了她自己身上，以至于她相信自己从德里克眼中望见的那种对未来的许诺，神圣固然神圣，却也几乎同样地不像真的。她早已不止是喜欢他，在无人知晓的时刻里也这样向自己承认。这是一种崭新的、奇异的、撩人的东西，正好供心思去咀嚼。可是，山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge）的秘密一天不曾读破，压在她灵魂上的重负一天不曾卸下，这又能算得了什么解脱呢？她收下了手镯，因为她不愿伤他的心，可她的眼睛避开了他的眼睛。

"你说，接下来会发生什么？"她声音发颤地问。

===Original===
“I don’t know. I wanted to see you first of all. Do you remember such a man ever coming to Beech Lodge before?”

“What is he like?”

He told her, and she shook her head. “I can’t think of anyone. Martin had no friends even in the village, and father had no visitors from the East. Can it be the image that brought him?”

“Nothing else, as I see it.”

“But how could he know it was there?”

===Chinese===
"我不知道。我头一件要紧的，是先见你一面。你可记得，从前有过这样的人到山毛榉山庄来吗？

"他生得什么模样？

他描绘了一番，她摇了摇头。"我想不起有这样的人。马丁连村里都没有朋友，父亲也从没有过东方来的客人。难道会是那尊神像把他引来的？

"依我看，不是这个，再没有别的了。

"可他怎么会知道神像在那儿呢？

===Original===
Derrick smiled. “How did I know? It’s all part of the main puzzle, and perhaps the missing part. I hoped you might be able to tell me something that would throw some light on this man’s arrival. I have a queer idea that it closes the circle, and am going to get him into the study on some pretext.”

“Alone?”

“Yes, to begin with.”

“Have you told the police about him?”

“I’m not ready for the police yet. The first thing to find out is whether the study means anything to him. That little god, or devil, is there, safely out of sight and touch, but if the peddler is what I take him to be, he will know it, and if he has come here for it, some attempt will be made before long.”

===Chinese===
德里克微微一笑。"那我又怎么会知道？这一切都是那个大谜团的一部分，兴许还是缺着的那一部分。我原指望你能告诉我些什么，好照亮一下这个人此来的底细。我有个古怪的念头：这一来，那个环就闭合了。我打算寻个由头，把他带进书房去。

"就他一个人？

"对，先就他一个。

"他的事你报告警察了吗？

"我还没打算惊动警察。头一件要弄清的是，那间书房对他有没有意义。那尊小神像——或者说小魔鬼——就在那儿，藏得稳稳当当，看不见也摸不着。可要是这货郎真是我料想的那种人，他会知道它在；要是他就是冲着它来的，用不了多久就会有所动作。

===Original===
“But what about you?” she asked nervously.

“He’s not interested in me, but I expect he has something to say to Martin. He’s probably saying it now. Oh, my dear!” he went on unconsciously, “don’t you see that we’re getting nearer to the end of it every hour?”

Nothing he might have said could have touched her more, or given her a swifter assurance of what lay next his heart. It moved her to see that he did not know he had said it. So tender was the thought that she hid it away to delight in after he had gone. She was ready to love in secret, but he must not know that yet. Then, in this new light, she was suddenly afraid for him.

===Chinese===
"那你自己怎么办？"她不安地问。

"他对我没兴趣，不过我看，他有话要对马丁说。多半这会儿正在说呢。噢，亲爱的！"他浑然不觉地往下说，"你难道看不出，咱们离这一切的收场，一个钟头比一个钟头近了吗？

他即便说出别的任何话来，都不可能比这一句更打动她，也不可能让她更快地确知，他心底紧挨着的是什么。看到他自己并不知道说漏了这句话，她心里一热。这个念头如此温柔，她把它悄悄收藏起来，等他走后再独自品味。她愿意暗暗地去爱，可这一点他还不能知道。随后，在这片新的光亮里，她忽然为他害怕起来。

===Original===
“Are you quite sure there’s no danger?”

“The danger,” he said slowly, “is to the man who committed the crime.”

There was a little silence till instinctively they turned to other things. It was a strange talk, of the lips and mind only, veering sometimes to ground where as yet it was trespass to enter, and just as often diverted with a deftness that only added to the growing reality of what they both felt but must not declare. He studied the girl, wanting her the more as moments passed, finding in her the charm that is beyond explanation, delighting in her perception, caressing her with the arms of his spirit, and wondering a little at the strangeness of his own voice. Often in days to come they would remember this meeting and smile at each other.

And Jean, timid lest she show what must not yet be shown, discovered in him a companion of her fancy, a swift interpreter, creative, sensitive, and ambitious, whose nature was fresh and unexhausted. She did not realize how secluded a life had been hers. She only knew that never before had she met a man just like this. And, above all, he made her feel safe.

===Chinese===
"你敢肯定没有危险吗？

"危险嘛，"他缓缓说道，"是落在那个犯下罪行的人头上的。

两人小小地沉默了一会儿，随后便出于本能地转而谈起别的来。那是一场奇特的谈话，只动唇舌与心智，时而偏到一处眼下还不得踏入的禁地，时而又被灵巧地岔开，而这种灵巧反倒只让两人心中那份渐长的情意愈发真实——那份他们都感觉到、却还不许宣之于口的东西。他端详着姑娘，时光每过一刻便更想要她一分，在她身上发现那种无法解释的魅力，为她的善解人意而欣喜，用心灵的臂膀拥着她，又对自己嗓音里的那点陌生微微纳罕。往后的岁月里，他们常常会记起这次相会，相视一笑。

而琴呢，生怕露出那尚不可露的心事，怯怯地在他身上发现了一个与她心念相投的伴侣——一个敏悟的解语人，富于创造，心思敏锐，胸怀志向，天性清新而未竭。她没有意识到，自己过的日子有多与世隔绝。她只知道，从前从不曾遇见过这样一个人。而最要紧的是，他让她觉得安稳。

---
===Original===
He walked thoughtfully back to Beech Lodge and, approaching the gates, unconsciously slackened his pace. He pictured the jade god in its hidden cabinet, ominous behind the mellow oak, its creamy fingers resting on its rigid miniature knees. Who had lifted this thing from the place where it should be, and where was that place? It had brought death to Millicent. What would it bring to others? He pictured Perkins, haunting the room of tragic memory that would not let her go. How much more did Perkins see than that to which she had sworn? He pictured Martin, his thick fingers among the rose-trees. What was written on the screen of Martin’s mind, what had jerked him out of the jungle, and why should fear be written on his swarthy face at sight of the stranger of that morning? How could he fear a man he did not know? But he did know him!

Pondering this last, and with the cottage but a few yards ahead, Derrick thought he could hear voices, and stepped close against the high hedge that fronted the grounds of Beech Lodge. Peering through this, he could make out the window of the cottage kitchen, and it was from here that the voices came. There was a little stirring of wind that made it difficult to distinguish anything clearly, but even at this distance it was evident that some kind of heated argument was in progress. Martin was speaking with a stubborn sort of rasp in his tones that carried with it a queer suggestion of nervousness, while the other man talked with a contemptuous lift in his voice as though he reminded the gardener of things he had culpably forgotten. Coming as close as he dared, and, leaning tensely forward, Derrick listened. He could not understand one word.

The men were using some unknown language, sometimes sharp, sometimes liquid, shooting it out with a speed that showed complete familiarity. Into Derrick’s brain flashed his sister’s description of how Perkins had talked in her sleep, and he knew that this was the same tongue. Breathless at the discovery, he listened the more intently. Martin was rapidly getting on the defensive, jabbering a jargon of defiance, in which, however, fear seemed always present. Derrick started at the sound of his own name, then Millicent’s, then Thursby’s. The word “Buddha” was repeated, but always linked to some unintelligible prefix, and never with the usual respect accorded to the god by the Oriental.

What the peddler now said appeared to take the form of some kind of pronouncement as though he were delivering a verdict, framed almost in a mysterious chant that sounded as though it came from an infinite distance. In the middle of this Martin burst forth in a great English oath, to which the stranger replied with one word that came like the hiss of a snake, whereat Martin choked audibly and fell silent. Then Derrick, his brain working like an engine, stepped back on the road, strolled on to the gate at his usual pace, and, turning in, went casually on to the house. No sooner had his foot touched the gravel than instantaneous silence spread in the cottage. And at that he smiled grimly.

===Chinese===
他一路沉思着走回山毛榉山庄，将近大门时，不觉放慢了脚步。他想象着暗柜里那尊玉神，在温润的橡木后面透着不祥，乳白的手指搭在僵直的小膝上。是谁把这件东西从它本该在的地方取了出来？那地方又在哪里？它给米莉森特带来了死亡。它又会给别人带来什么？他想象着珀金斯（Perkins），被那间装满惨痛记忆、不肯放她走的屋子缠住。珀金斯亲眼看见的，比她起誓所说的多出多少？他想象着马丁，那双粗大的手指正在玫瑰树间。马丁的心屏上写着什么？是什么把他从丛林里猛地拽了出来？为什么一见到那天早晨的陌生人，他黝黑的脸上就写满了恐惧？他怎么会怕一个不认识的人？可他分明认识！

他心里掂量着这最后一点，小屋就在前面不过几码远，德里克觉得听得见人声，便紧贴着山毛榉山庄园地前面那道高高的树篱站住。透过树篱望过去，他能辨出小屋厨房的窗户，说话声正是从那里传出来的。风有些簌簌地响，叫人难以听真切什么，可即便隔着这段距离，也听得出一场激烈的争辩正在进行。马丁说话的嗓音里带着一种执拗的粗厉，其中又隐隐夹着一丝异样的紧张；另一个人说话时声调里带着轻蔑的扬起，仿佛在提醒园丁那些他罪不可赦地忘掉了的事情。德里克挨到再往前一步就要败露的地方，紧张地俯身向前，侧耳细听。他一个字也听不懂。

两个人用的是一种听不懂的语言，时而尖利，时而缠绵如水，吐词飞快，一听便知运用自如、烂熟于心。妹妹当年如何描述珀金斯在睡梦里说胡话的情形，倏地闪进德里克的脑海，他立刻明白：正是同一种语言。这一发现让他屏住了呼吸，越发凝神细听。马丁眼看着步步退到守势，急促地嚷着一串抗辩的行话，只是那话语里恐惧似乎始终都在。听到自己的名字，德里克一惊，接着是米莉森特的名字，再接着是瑟斯比（Thursby）的名字。"佛陀"这个词反复出现，可前面总缀着某个听不懂的前缀，而且从不像东方人对这位神明惯常的那样带着敬意。

这时货郎说的话，像是成了某种宣告，仿佛他在颁下一纸裁断，腔调近乎一种神秘的吟诵，听着像是从无穷远处飘来。话到中途，马丁猛地迸出一句十足的英国式咒骂，那陌生人只回了一个字，那字来得像蛇的嘶嘶声，马丁顿时噎得出了声，哑了下去。德里克于是退回大路上，脑子像引擎似的飞转，照平常的步子踱到大门前，一拐弯进了门，若无其事地朝宅子走去。他的脚刚踏上砂砾路，小屋里霎时静了下来。见了这光景，他冷冷地笑了。

===Original===
Passing directly to the study, he closed the door and, making sure he was not observed from the lawn, opened the oak panel. Inside was the jade god and its waxen copy. Weighing these in either hand, he deliberated a moment; then, putting the original back, he closed the cabinet and dropped the model into his pocket. From the top drawer of the big desk he took a small automatic. Finally, with god and gun balancing each other in their concealment, he lit his pipe and strolled back toward the cottage.

This time he knew he was observed, for, as he neared the gates, Martin emerged from the front of the cottage and touched his cap. His face was of a curiously mottled appearance, and betrayed signs of great tension, but as his eyes met those of his master he pulled himself together and assumed his ordinary gruff though respectful manner. Derrick nodded affably.

“Well, Martin, what do you think of those Lady Hillingdons for next year? I see you’ve been at them.”

===Chinese===
他径直进了书房，关上门，先看清草坪上没有人望见他，才打开那块橡木暗板。里面是那尊玉神和它的蜡制仿品。他把两件东西分搁在两只手上掂了掂，思量了片刻；随后把原件放回原处，关好柜子，把蜡模揣进口袋。他又从大书桌的顶层抽屉里取出一支小型自动手枪。最后，神与枪各藏一处，两下相抵，他点上烟斗，朝小屋踱了回去。

这回他知道有人在看他，因为临近大门时，马丁从小屋前面走了出来，碰了碰帽檐。他的脸显出一副古怪的斑驳颜色，透着高度紧张的痕迹，可一触到主人的目光，他便振作起来，摆出平日那副粗声粗气却恭恭敬敬的样子。德里克和气地点了点头。

"喂，马丁，明年那些'希灵登夫人'（Lady Hillingdon）你觉得怎么样？我看你一直在侍弄它们。

===Original===
“They promise well, sir, but I don’t think so much of the Richmonds.”

“Sorry to hear that. Why not?”

“One thing, they weren’t properly pruned last winter, and for another the mildew’s been at them.”

“You don’t seem to think much of the man who was here last.”

“I don’t, sir, and that’s a fact!”

===Chinese===
"长势有望，先生，不过那些'里士满'（Richmond）我不怎么看得上。

"听你这么说，可惜了。为什么呢？

"一来，去年冬天没给它们好好剪枝；二来，白粉病也缠上它们了。

"上一个在这儿的那个人，你好像也不怎么看得上。

"是看不上，先生，这是实话！

===Original===
“And what do you make of your visitor of this morning? Does he know anything about flowers?”

“No, sir, flowers aren’t exactly in his line from what I make of him. Queer sort of chap, I should say, but I don’t take it there’s any harm in him.”

“He told me he came from the East. Does he know any of the parts you know?”

“Yes, sir, some.”

“Never happened to come across him before, did you?”

===Chinese===
"那你今儿早上那位客人，你看他是个什么路数？他懂花卉吗？

"不懂，先生，照我看，花卉算不得他的本行。要我说，是个古怪家伙，不过我看他没什么害处。

"他跟我说他是从东方来的。你知道的那些地方，他也认得一些吗？

"认得，先生，认得一些。

"以前可曾碰巧遇上过他？

===Original===
Martin stiffened ever so slightly. “No, sir, never set eyes on him. The East is a big bit of country, and there’s room for all kinds there.”

“You know some foreign lingos?”

“Yes, sir, a trader needs them if he’s going to do any business.”

“Have you tried your friend in that respect?”

“I tackled him just now with Hindustani, but that beat him.”

===Chinese===
马丁极其轻微地僵了一下。"没有，先生，从来没照过面。东方是老大一片地界，什么样的角色都容得下。

"你懂几门外国话？

"懂一些，先生。跑买卖的要想做成生意，就少不了这个。

"你那位朋友，你在这方面试过他没有？

"方才我拿印度斯坦语（Hindustani）跟他搭了搭，他招架不住。

===Original===
“It would beat me, too. Does he know any Malay?” Derrick smiled a little. “Not that I know any myself.”

“Only a word or two, sir.”

“Curious that two traders like you, both of whom have lived in the Orient, should have to fall back on English to converse.”

Martin’s eyes were unfathomable, and Derrick searched his mind for the next move. The man had twice been proved a liar, but the object of his lies was as remote as ever. Then suddenly came the thought of Perkins, babbling what was probably Malay in her dream-haunted sleep.

===Chinese===
"换了我也招架不住。他懂马来语（Malay）吗？"德里克微微一笑。"虽然我自己也一句不懂。

"只懂一两个词，先生。

"真是奇怪，你们两个都是跑买卖的，都在东方住过，到头来要交谈，竟得靠英语。

马丁的眼睛深不可测，德里克在心里搜罗下一步棋。这人已经两次被证明在撒谎，可他撒谎的目的一如既往地杳不可寻。忽然，他想起了珀金斯，在梦魇缠身的睡梦里叽叽咕咕，说的多半是马来语。

===Original===
“I wonder if Perkins happens to know any of those Eastern lingos?”

The man’s face underwent a swift change. There was fear in it now. He ground his heel nervously into the soil, while the big fingers clenched tight. There was in his manner that which suggested a new anxiety, and for the moment he seemed oddly helpless.

“I couldn’t say, Mr. Derrick, but if I may make so bold, I wouldn’t try. She’s a queer woman, and”﻿—here he touched his forehead meaningly﻿—“she’s best left alone. Mr. Millicent never bothered her, and he knew her well.”

Derrick nodded. “You may be right. Where are you putting your visitor tonight?”

===Chinese===
"不知珀金斯可巧也懂那些东方话里的哪一门？

那人的脸上霎时起了变化。此刻那上面有了惧意。他紧张地把鞋跟往土里碾，粗大的手指攥得紧紧的。他的神气里透出一种新的焦灼，一时间竟显得出奇地束手无策。

"这我说不上，德里克先生，不过恕我斗胆说一句：还是别去试的好。她是个古怪女人，还有"——他说到这儿，意味深长地碰了碰自己的额头——"这样的人，还是随她去最好。米莉森特先生从来不招惹她，而他是最了解她的。

德里克点了点头。"也许你说得对。今儿晚上你安顿客人睡哪儿？

===Original===
“On the floor in the kitchen, sir; he says that’s good enough for him. He’s about used up and asked if he might rest for another day or two. Showed me his feet. They’re in bad shape. I told him it was for you to say.”

Derrick felt a quickening of his pulse. Once again everything fitted in. The peddler would stay, but not on account of sore feet. He pressed his fingers against the image in his pocket, but his mind sped to the dark recess where the real god stared malevolently into the darkness and waited till his servants should gather at his baffling summons. Then he glanced at Martin, experiencing a throb of pity for one who was so secretly tortured. He began to see how the man must already have suffered, anticipating the inevitable, paying in advance, with the pangs of two years, part of the price of a blow that took place in a second. But there was no room now for compassion.

“Did you happen to see the inside of the peddler’s pack?” he asked carelessly.

===Chinese===
"就睡厨房地上，先生；他说那样就尽够了。他差不多累到头了，问能不能再歇上一两天。还给我看了他的脚。那脚可不成样子了。我跟他说，这得由您来定。

德里克觉得脉搏猛地加快了。一切又一次严丝合缝地对上了。货郎是要留下的，可绝不是因为脚疼。他用手指抵住口袋里的神像，心念却飞向那处幽暗的壁龛——真正的神正在那里恶狠狠地瞪视黑暗，等着他的仆从们应着他那令人难解的召唤聚拢过来。随后他看了马丁一眼，为这个暗地里受着煎熬的人心头一颤。他开始明白，这人此前必定已经吃了多少苦：预感着躲不掉的那一幕，用两年的痛楚，预付了那发生在须臾之间的一击的一部分代价。可眼下容不得怜悯了。

"你可曾碰巧瞧见过那货郎担子的里面？"他漫不经心地问。

===Original===
Martin shook his head. “No, sir, he won’t trouble to show that to the likes of me.”

“I don’t know! I’d ask him if I were you, and have a look at them. They’ll probably remind you of a good many places you ought to know. Also I think I’d keep an eye on him tonight.”

“He’s all right so far as that’s concerned,” put in the gardener hastily.

“He may be, but one can never tell. I fancy he wouldn’t mind picking up anything portable, especially if it happened to be in his own line. One can never be sure about men like that. I’ve known them to wander about the country picking up odds and ends that were of no value to most people, but of particular interest to others. I’ve half a mind to send him along to the village as it is.”

===Chinese===
马丁摇了摇头。"没有，先生，他可懒得费那个事，给我这样的人看。

"这倒难说！我要是你，就去问问他，把东西瞧上一瞧。它们多半会让你想起好些你本该认得的地方。还有，我看今儿夜里得盯他一盯。

"那一层他是靠得住的，"园丁急忙插话。

"也许靠得住，可谁说得准。依我看，凡是拿得动的东西，他都不嫌顺手捡上一件，尤其要是恰好对他的路子。那种人，谁也拿不准。我就知道他们那一路人，在全国各地游荡，收些零七八碎，在多数人眼里一文不值，对另外一些人却有特别的意思。就照现在这样，我都有一半心思，要把他打发到村里去了。

===Original===
“That will be all right, sir,” put in Martin hurriedly; “he’s a harmless old soul with not as much strength as a cat. I’ll stand good for him.”

He spoke with great earnestness and unconsciously raised his voice. Derrick at this moment felt his gaze drawn toward the cottage and, glancing over Martin’s shoulder, noted that at one of the tiny windows of the kitchen the blind had been drawn slightly aside. The window was open. Pitching his own tones a little higher, he looked straight into Martin’s troubled eyes.

“You remember that talk we had about Mr. Millicent’s death the first night you came to see me?”

===Chinese===
"不会有事的，先生，"马丁急忙插话，"他是个没害人的老好人，力气还不如一只猫。我替他打包票。

他说得极其恳切，不觉提高了嗓门。就在这当口，德里克觉出自己的目光被引向小屋，越过马丁的肩头一瞥，只见厨房一扇小窗上的窗帘，已被微微撩开。窗子开着。他把自己的声调也略略抬高，直直望进马丁那双惶惶不安的眼睛。

"你还记得吧，你头一晚上来见我，咱们谈过关于米莉森特先生之死的那番话？

===Original===
“Yes, sir,” replied the gardener with reluctance.

“Well, I’ve said nothing about it since then, but I’ve thought a good deal. What about you?”

“I don’t forget it, either, Mr. Derrick, but what else is there to be said? I told you what I know.”

“Then I take it that nothing has occurred to you since?”

===Chinese===
"记得，先生。"园丁勉强答道。

"那以后我一个字也没再提，可想得却不少。你呢？

"我也没忘，德里克先生，可还有什么别的可说呢？我知道的都告诉您了。

"那么我当是，打那以后你再没想起过什么新东西？

===Original===
“What could occur, sir? It’s more than two years ago now. The poor gentleman’s cold in his grave, and the world has moved on. I’m trying to forget it as hard as I can.”

“Yes, I know, but sometimes, Martin, when a man comes back to a well-known place which is associated with an event like that, the mind takes a curious turn and pitches on something it did not see before. It’s almost as though the place had kept something up its sleeve to reveal later on. Perhaps it’s your friend’s arrival that has started me thinking.”

Martin sent him an indescribable glance. “I don’t quite follow, sir.”

===Chinese===
"还能想起什么，先生？到如今两年都不止了。可怜的先生在坟里都凉透了，世道也往前走了。我正拼命想法子把它忘掉呢。

"是，我知道。可是马丁，有时候，一个人回到一个熟地方，那地方又跟那样一桩事连在一起，心思就会拐出一个古怪的弯，一下子撞上些先前没看见的东西。几乎就像那地方袖子里藏了点什么，留着往后再揭出来似的。兴许正是你那位朋友的到来，勾得我琢磨起这些来。

马丁递给他一个说不清道不明的眼色。"我不大明白您的意思，先生。
===Original===
“I was wondering,” went on Derrick in the same clear tones, “whether it was possible that anyone answering to the description of this stranger had been hanging about the night Mr. Millicent was killed. Things like that have been known to happen.”

“For God’s sake don’t talk that way, sir.” Martin’s face was now desperate, and he glanced apprehensively over his shoulder.

Derrick smiled reassuringly. “I can’t see that there’s any harm done by mentioning it, and it might be as well to let your friend know that we’re not asleep.”

===Chinese===
"我一直在想，"德里克还是用那种清亮的声调说下去，"米莉森特先生被害的那天夜里，会不会有个跟这位陌生人对得上号的人，在这一带转悠。这种事，从前也是发生过的。

"看在上帝份上，别这么说话，先生。"马丁的脸这时已是绝望的颜色，还提心吊胆地回头望了一眼。

德里克宽慰地笑了笑。"我看提一提这事没什么害处，兴许倒可以让你那位朋友知道，咱们并没有睡着。

===Original===
The man winced as though struck. “Mr. Derrick, sir, if there’s anything you want to say about Mr. Millicent now, couldn’t we go a few steps up the drive? It isn’t wise, is it, that this fellow should know anything about it?”

“What’s the matter with you, Martin?”

“Nothing, sir, but I can’t help being upset when I talk about the thing.”

===Chinese===
那人像挨了一击似的瑟缩了一下。"德里克先生，您要是这会儿还有什么关于米莉森特先生的话要说，咱们能不能顺着车道往上走几步？让这家伙听到半点风声，总不是聪明的事，对不对？

"你是怎么了，马丁？

"没什么，先生，只是一说起那件事，我就忍不住心神不定。

===Original===
Derrick hesitated, then thrust the probe still deeper. “I can’t see what difference would be made if he did learn of it. However, let that go, and perhaps you’re right. You remember my asking you if anything was missed at that time?”

“Yes, sir, and I told you all I knew.”

“And the motive for the crime is as much a mystery to you as ever?”

Martin’s lips were trembling now, and he could only nod.

===Chinese===
德里克迟疑了一下，随即把探针又往深处送了送。"我看就算让他知道了，又能有什么两样。罢了，这事不提，也许你是对的。你可记得，我问过你当时有没有丢失什么东西？

"记得，先生，我知道的都告诉您了。

"那么，这桩罪案的动机，对你说来也还是跟从前一样是个谜？

马丁的嘴唇这时直打颤，他只能点点头。

===Original===
“Well, I had a chat the other day with a man who was on the case, and he told me that another thing, not that kris, was missed and has never been seen since. It was a sort of image, carved in jade.”

“I never heard of that, sir,” stammered Martin thickly.

“Yes, and apparently it had been picked up by Mr. Millicent in the East years before.”

Martin made a convulsive gesture. “Please, sir,” he begged, “don’t talk like that here.”

===Chinese===
"是这样，前几天我跟一个当年办过这案子的人聊了聊，他告诉我，当时还丢了一样东西——不是那把克里斯短剑（kris）——打那以后就再没人见过。是一尊小小的神像，玉雕的。

"这个我从没听说过，先生。"马丁喉头堵着，结结巴巴地说。

"是的，而且明摆着，那是米莉森特先生好些年前在东方收来的。

马丁痉挛似的做了个手势。"求您了，先生，"他哀告道，"别在这儿说这样的话。

===Original===
Simultaneously his gaze was drawn to the cottage window as though by mesmeric power. It seemed that now he had ceased to feel anything except a mounting fear that struck to his very heart. Little tremors ran through his massive frame, and he began to sway with a slow, rhythmic motion as if endeavoring to maintain his balance. His face was a changing mask in which there was not so much of guilt as of a deadly recognition that he was being overtaken by some remorseless destiny from which there was no escape. No longer a gardener, a pruner of rose-trees, or a traveler from far countries. He became in that moment a man under a curse.

Again Derrick felt a fleeting pang of pity for such torture, but remembered the triangle of death, with Martin standing at one corner. At the same time he sensed the strangeness of the situation, in which he, a dweller in a quiet countryside, should be inextricably involved in a problem so grim and unexpected. Might it be some period of fantasy or subconscious phase from which he would presently awaken? To this there were two apparent answers. One, the faint tingle that seemed to spread from the thing hidden in his clenched hand. The other, the picture of a girl waiting, waiting. At that, all thought of compassion vanished from his mind. It was real, all real, and destiny was at work in Beech Lodge. Then in a flash the next move became clear.

“I wonder,” he said slowly, “if this was the sort of thing that was missed from the desk?” He took the image from his pocket and balanced it openly in the palm of his hand. “Of course,” he added, fixing Martin with a steady eye, “you can’t tell me, because you say you never saw it.”

===Chinese===
与此同时，他的目光像被什么催眠的力量拽向小屋的窗户。此刻他仿佛什么都不觉得了，只剩一股步步高涨的恐惧，直扎到心口。细小的战栗一阵阵穿过他那魁伟的身躯，他开始缓缓地、有节奏地摇晃，像是在竭力保持住平衡。他的脸成了一副不断变幻的面具，上面与其说是负罪之色，不如说是一种致命的了悟——他正被某种无情的命运追上，而这命运无从逃脱。不再是园丁，不再是修剪玫瑰树的人，也不再是来自远邦的旅人。那一刻，他成了一个身负咒诅的人。

德里克又一次为这样的煎熬感到一阵转瞬即逝的怜悯，可他记起了那个死亡三角，马丁正站在一角。与此同时，他也觉察到这处境的离奇：他，一个安居僻静乡间的人，竟会挣不脱身地卷进这样一桩森然而意外的问题里。这会不会是一段幻境、一层潜意识的迷雾，转眼他就会从中醒来？对此有两个现成的答案。一个，是紧攥的手里那件藏着的东西仿佛透出的一丝微微的麻意。另一个，是一个姑娘等待着的图景，等着，等着。想到这里，怜悯的念头全从他心里散尽了。这是真的，全都是真的，命运正在山毛榉山庄里做工。跟着，下一步棋倏然清楚了。

"不知，"他缓缓说道，"从书桌上丢失的，是不是就是这一类东西？"他从口袋里取出神像，光明正大地平托在掌心里。"当然，"他目光定定地盯住马丁，补充道，"你是告诉不了我的，因为你说你从来没见过它。

===Original===
The gardener’s figure seemed to shrink visibly, and his eyes protruded. He made a choking sound, the blood rushed in a mottled flood to his cheeks, and the big hands clasped and unclasped mechanically. Derrick, staring at him, felt a throb of triumph and slid the image out of sight.

“God!” said Martin chokingly. “Oh, God! Where did you get that?”

Then he swung round and glared at the cottage.

===Chinese===
园丁的身量眼见着仿佛缩小了，眼睛也凸了出来。他发出一声哽咽，血斑斑驳驳地涌上双颊，两只大手机械地握拢又松开。德里克盯着他，感到一阵得胜的悸动，便把神像收得看不见了。

"上帝啊！"马丁哽着嗓子说。"噢，上帝啊！您这是从哪儿弄来的？

说罢他猛地转过身，狠狠瞪着小屋。

===Original===
Out of the door came the figure of the peddler, and Martin, watching him, made a gesture of despair foreign to so powerful a man. The stranger’s eyes were preternaturally bright, and there was now no trace of the weary limp with which he had moved only a few hours ago. His head was erect, the bent shoulders were straight, his body was lithe and had taken on something of the springy contours of youth. Instinctively Derrick’s fingers tightened round the image, but it was at him rather than at his pocket that Blunt looked first.

“Excuse me, sir,” he began, “but when I was smoking inside just now I couldn’t help hearing you say that someone had been killed in your house. Might I ask who it was?”

The audacity of the thing made Derrick blink. He could not trust himself to glance at Martin but knew that the gardener’s eyes were fixed intently on the peddler’s face. There followed an instant of silence. Derrick realized that he was hunting big game, the biggest game of all, and it behooved him to keep his head.

“Will you tell me first why the matter is of any interest to you?”

===Chinese===
门里走出了货郎的身影。马丁望着他，做了个绝望的手势——对一个如此强健的人，这手势本是不属于他的。那陌生人的眼睛亮得异乎寻常，几个小时前他走动时那副疲惫的跛态，此刻已无影无踪。他头颅挺直，佝偻的双肩展开了，身子轻捷柔韧，竟恢复了些青年人富有弹性的轮廓。德里克的手指本能地攥紧了神像，可布伦特（Blunt）最先望的，是他这个人，而不是他的口袋。

"打扰了，先生，"他开口道，"方才我在里头抽烟，不由得听见您说，府上有人被害了。能不能请问，那是谁？

这份胆气让德里克眨了眨眼。他不敢保险自己瞟马丁一眼会是什么脸色，但他知道，园丁的眼睛正死死盯住货郎的脸。接着是片刻的沉默。德里克意识到，自己追猎的是大猎物，是天下最大的猎物，他必须沉得住气。

"能不能先请您告诉我，这事于您有什么相干？

===Original===
Blunt’s lips formed an inscrutable smile, but his gaze was as blank as seawater.

“It’s of no more interest than anything else of the same kind, but I’ve seen a bit of that sort of thing in the East, and it may be I can be of use in getting at the bottom of it, if that’s not been done yet.”

Derrick pondered. “This was not the usual kind of sudden death, and there were no clues left.”

The man nodded understandingly. “There ain’t so many deaths of what you would call the usual kind where I come from, either, but there is most always a clue of some sort if one knows where to look. That’s a matter of instinct. Can’t explain it, but I reckon I’ve got it.”

===Chinese===
布伦特的唇边浮起一个莫测高深的微笑，目光却像海水一样空茫。

"比起别的同类事情来，这事说不上更有意思。不过在东方，这类事儿我多少见过一点，要是还没人把它查个水落石出，兴许我能帮上点忙。

德里克沉吟了一下。"那不是寻常样子的暴死，也没留下什么线索。

那人心领神会地点点头。"我来的那地方，照您说的那种寻常死法，也不见得有多少。可只要知道往哪儿瞧，十有八九总有一星半点的线索。这凭的是本能。说不清道不明，不过我估摸着，我这本能是有的。

===Original===
Over Martin’s features crept a shade of admiration. Derrick saw this, and it stiffened his resolution. The hunt was afoot now, one against two. Soon, he was convinced, it would be one against three, when Perkins joined in. She would prove perhaps the most elusive of all. Then his mind jumped back to the man in front of him.

“I don’t see how a complete stranger could spot at first sight anything that skilled detectives failed to discover after very close examination,” he said coolly. “You’ll have to convince me that it’s something more than mere curiosity on your part before I go any further.”

“And against that there’s such a thing as looking at some object for so long that after a while one doesn’t see it at all. It’s the fresh eye that picks things up. Would it surprise you if I said that you’ve got something close to you at this minute that might be a clue, and you never guess it.”

Martin drew in his breath sharply, but Derrick’s eyes never left the stranger’s face.

===Chinese===
一丝钦佩之色爬上马丁的脸。德里克看在眼里，决心更硬了。围猎此刻已经开跑，一个对两个。他深信，等珀金斯也入了伙，很快就得一个对三个。而她，兴许会证明是里头最难捉的一个。随后他的心思又跳回面前这个人身上。

"我不明白，一个十足的外乡人，怎么能在初见之下，就一眼看出那些老练的侦探细细查验之后都没能发现的东西，"他冷冷地说。"您得先让我信了，您这不只是单纯的好奇心，我才肯再往下谈。

"可反过来讲，有这么一种事：一件东西盯得太久，过上一阵反倒什么都看不见了。能拾起东西来的，是新鲜的眼睛。要是我说，您这会儿身边近处就有一件东西，兴许就是条线索，而您压根儿猜不着——您会不会吃惊？

马丁猛地倒吸了一口气，德里克的眼睛却始终没有离开那陌生人的脸。

===Original===
“Isn’t that a rather wild shot of yours?”

“It may be, but I’ll risk it. I reckon I’ve sucked in something from the places I’ve been in that helps to get under the skin at times. Getting back to clues, this world is full of clues that go unnoticed just because people don’t know how to look at them. Same thing when you get so used to a thing that you can’t tell whether it’s in the room or not, without making sure. That’s because you don’t hear what it says.”

“Ah,” put in Derrick swiftly, “then you believe that things talk?”

“It’s the only talk worth listening to now and then.”

===Chinese===
"您这一箭，未免放得太悬了吧？

"兴许悬，可我情愿冒这个险。我估摸着，我到过的那些地方，多少在我身上渗进了点什么，有时候能帮人钻到皮底下去。回到线索上来——这世上到处都是没人理会的线索，只因为人们不知道怎么看它们。就像一件东西你用惯了，不特意去瞧一眼，你都说不上它到底在不在屋里。那是因为你没听见它说的话。

"啊，"德里克敏捷地插进来，"那么您相信，东西会说话？

"那可是唯一值得时不时听一听的言语。

===Original===
Derrick’s pulse quickened. “Is that what you depend on in this case?”

The peddler nodded. “Perhaps it would surprise you if I said that something was talking at this very minute, a queer kind of stuff that I only half get.”

Saying this, he lifted his eyes, and sent Derrick an extraordinary look. There was power in it, and a certain mesmeric weight, and in a strange but unmistakable fashion it invited the young man to acknowledge what he himself believed. This look stated very plainly that the stranger saw through Derrick’s camouflage, and also quite understood the present necessity for it; but it suggested, too, that behind the newcomer was an authority that as yet he had no intention to disclose. There were no words in which to phrase what Derrick felt. Presently, and as though to make the thing as easy as possible for the master of Beech Lodge, the little man gave a short laugh.

===Chinese===
德里克的脉搏加快了。"这桩案子里，您凭的就是这个？

货郎点了点头。"要是我说，这会儿就有个东西正在说话——一种怪东西，我只听得懂一半——您会不会吃惊。

说着，他抬起眼睛，向德里克投来异乎寻常的一瞥。那一瞥里有力量，有一种催眠般的分量，并且以一种奇特却明白无误的方式，邀请年轻人承认他自己所相信的东西。这一眼说得再明白不过：陌生人看穿了德里克的伪装，也全然明白此刻何以必须如此；但它同时也暗示，这位新来者背后有一种权威，眼下他还无意披露。德里克心里的感受，找不出言语来表达。片刻之后，那小个子像是存心让山毛榉山庄的主人尽量好下台，短促地笑了一声。

===Original===
“You might as well let me try it, sir. If I fail there will be no harm done.”

Derrick, without realizing it, took his cue. “Well,” he said good-humouredly, “at any rate, you can’t do much harm by having a look at the room. What do you say, Martin? I’ll let you decide, since you’re responsible for Blunt while he’s here.”

Martin twisted his lips in a vain effort to speak, but it seemed that any reminder of responsibility was almost too much for him. He shot the peddler a swift glance, in which fear and respect were mingled, and when he looked at his master his eyes implored that he be not further involved. In that moment Martin acted like an honest man. Then the expression passed, and his face was once more a mask.

===Chinese===
"您不妨让我试一试，先生。我要是不成，也没什么害处。

德里克不知不觉地顺势接了话。"好吧，"他和颜悦色地说，"不管怎样，看看那间屋子总坏不了什么事。你说呢，马丁？这事让你来定，布伦特在这儿期间，你可是替他担着干系的。

马丁扭了扭嘴唇，想说却白费力气，似乎一提起责任二字，就几乎要把他压垮。他朝货郎飞快地溜了一眼，那眼色里惧意与敬意兼有；再望向主人时，他的眼睛在哀求，别再把他往里头拖。那一刻，马丁的举止像个正直的人。随后这副神情过去了，他的脸重又成了一副面具。

===Original===
“That’s just as you feel about it, sir.”

Derrick turned to Blunt. “Well, then, you can come up, say, at six o’clock, and you’d better bring Martin with you. And, by the way,” he added, “if you want any details about this murder before you come, Martin knows a good deal more than I do, so you’d better pump him.”

Blunt shook his head. “It’s just as well I shouldn’t know anything at all, sir. Sometimes the more one thinks one knows the less one finds out.” Again he sent the young man that extraordinary look.

“All right; but if you change your mind, and Martin gets stuck, I’ll put you in touch with Perkins at the house.”

===Chinese===
"全凭您怎么想，先生。

德里克转向布伦特。"那好，你就六点钟上来吧，比方说，最好把马丁也一并带来。对了，还有一句，"他补充道，"你要是想在来之前打听些这桩谋杀的细节，马丁知道的可比我多得多，你最好从他嘴里掏一掏。

布伦特摇了摇头。"我干脆一无所知，倒也正好，先生。有时候，一个人自以为知道得越多，查出来的反倒越少。"他又向年轻人投来那异乎寻常的一瞥。

"行；不过你要是改了主意，而马丁又卡了壳，我可以让你跟宅子里的珀金斯接上头。
===Original===
Martin started at this, but Blunt seemed unmoved. “Who might Perkins be?”

“The maid who was here when Mr. Millicent died. She found him.”

The man’s expression did not change in the slightest.

“I won’t want to bother her, sir; and look here, if you doubt my faith you can take my pack till you’re satisfied I’m straight. Anything else?”

===Chinese===
马丁听了这话一惊，布伦特却似乎不为所动。"珀金斯又是哪一位？

"就是米莉森特先生去世时在府上的那个女仆。人就是她发现的。

那人的表情没有丝毫变化。

"我不会想去找她的麻烦，先生；再有，您瞧，您要是信不过我的诚意，可以把我的货担押在您手里，等您满意了、确信我行得正为止。还有什么吩咐吗？

===Original===
His voice lifted as he spoke, and Derrick knew what he meant. The sharp eyes peering from the cottage window had missed nothing. The stranger was aware that something lay hidden in that pocket, nor could all his art conceal the hunger that was growing in his soul. Derrick, his mind tense, and realizing that every step taken now must inevitably affect the last scene of the drama, gripped the image with fingers that felt suddenly cold, then drew it out and dropped it carelessly into the peddler’s hand. The man quivered at the touch.

“While we’re on the subject, there’s something that may interest you. Ever see anything like it before?”

A tremor ran through the lean form, and the bright eyes became clouded with emotion. The brown fingers closed caressingly, till, all in a breath, a look of concentrated shrewdness spread over the swarthy face. The man stared at the molded wax, then at Derrick. “You clever devil!” was what the eyes said. He grasped the meaning of this model, there could be no doubt of that, and telegraphed an unconscious admiration to the one who had fashioned it. He scanned the small square base, the cloaked shoulders, the tiny folded hands, and the hellish sneer on the pygmy features, and nodded. Yes, it was all there, and nothing was there. A great gulf yawned between wax and jade. But the peddler remained master of himself, while Martin, at his elbow, seemed rooted to the ground.

===Chinese===
他说话时嗓门抬高了，德里克明白他的用意。从小屋窗户里窥探的那双锐利的眼睛，什么也没漏掉。那陌生人知道那只口袋里藏着东西，而他纵有浑身解数，也遮掩不住灵魂里日益滋长的饥渴。德里克心弦紧绷，明白眼下走的每一步都必定牵动这出戏的最后一幕，便用骤然发凉的手指攥住神像，随即抽出来，漫不经心地掉进货郎的手心。东西一沾手，那人便浑身一颤。

"正好说到这个题目，这儿有件东西，您兴许会感兴趣。以前可曾见过像这样的物件？

一阵战栗掠过那精瘦的身躯，发亮的眼睛也因心绪激荡而蒙上了云翳。棕色的手指爱抚般地合拢了，可转瞬之间，一副凝成一团的精明神色又布满那张黝黑的脸。那人盯着这块模出来的蜡，又望望德里克。"你这个机灵鬼！"他的眼睛这样说。他领会了这尊蜡模的用意，这一点毫无疑问，还向造出它的这一位无意识地传去钦佩之意。他把那方正的小底座、披着斗篷的双肩、交叠的小手，还有侏儒面相上那副地狱般的狞笑，一一扫过，点了点头。是的，样样都在，又什么也不在。蜡与玉之间，横着一道深渊。可货郎始终管得住自己，倒是站在他肘边的马丁，像是把根扎进了地里。

===Original===
“What do you think of it?” asked Derrick smoothly.

The peddler shook his head. “Of this, sir, nothing at all; but if I could see the original it might be another matter. Do you happen to have it?”

“I do, but not here. And it doesn’t belong to me. Ever see anything like it?”

Blunt nodded. “Yes, but not often. The original of this may have come from Indo-China, up northeast of the Bay of Bengal. I reckon it would be about five hundred years old. They don’t make them often nowadays. These things sometimes drift down into the Malay country, but they’re not supposed to. Look here, sir, I’ve a leaning for carved jade, which brings a good price from the Chinese, and I’ll trade you anything in my pack for the original of this.”

===Chinese===
"您看它怎么样？"德里克不动声色地问。

货郎摇了摇头。"这个嘛，先生，我一点看法也没有；可要是能让我见见原件，那就是另一回事了。您手头可有？

"有，可不在这儿。再说，它也不是我的。您可曾见过这样的东西？

布伦特点了点头。"见过，可不常见。这东西的原件，兴许出自印度支那（Indo-China），在孟加拉湾往东北去的地方。我估摸它总有五百来岁了。如今这种东西不常做了。它们有时候会流落到马来地界去，可按理是不该外流的。您瞧，先生，我对玉雕有些偏好，玉雕在中国人手里能卖大价钱。您要是肯拿这件的原件来换，我货担里随您挑什么都行。

===Original===
“But I’ve told you it’s not mine.”

“Maybe, sir, but if you’ll put me in touch with the owner I’ll make it worth his while to sell.”

“We’ll see about that later. Why did you say that these things are not supposed to get out of Indo-China?”

“Let me ask first, sir, if this ever brought any bad luck to the man who owned it?” He paused and smiled cynically. “I mean the original.”

===Chinese===
"可我告诉过您了，它不是我的。

"也许是，先生，可您要是能让我跟物主搭上线，我会让他卖了觉得值。

"这个回头再说。您为什么说，这些东西照理不该流出印度支那？

"先容我问一句，先生，它可曾给拥有它的人带来过什么厄运？"他顿了顿，冷冷地一笑。"我指的是那原件。

===Original===
Derrick nodded. The daring of it was prodigious.

“Does it happen to be the man you spoke of just now?”

“Yes.”

Again the odd smile, and the peddler handed back the image. “It’s a queer thing,” he said slowly, “but I’ve heard tell that the spirit of Buddha doesn’t like these things drifting about. It’s talk of the East, of course, and perhaps it isn’t worth much in England. But there’s something at work in those parts that gets hold of people without their knowing it. It isn’t so long ago that I was in a temple up country where there was something like this, and it just looked at me and dared me to steal it. I reckon I would have tried to if it hadn’t been guarded by about a hundred priests. It was the same size as this, and just as ugly, and carved out of jade, too.

“All round it there were the usual images, but arranged like rows of policemen. Next it was an empty stand, and I guessed that that was where another one just like it had been, but when I asked where it had got to there was a hell of an excitement, because the beggars thought perhaps I had it and had come after its mate. It took me all my time to get them quieted down. Queer sort of game, wasn’t it, sir?”

===Chinese===
德里克点了点头。这份胆量真是大得可以。

"那位物主，可巧就是您方才说起的那个人？

"正是。

又是那种古怪的微笑，货郎把神像递了回来。"这事真有点邪门，"他缓缓说道，"可我听人讲过，佛陀的精魂不喜欢这些东西四处漂流。这自然是东方的说法，在英国兴许值不得什么。可那些地方确乎有个什么在暗中使劲，人不知不觉就被它攥住了。就在不久前，我到过内地一座庙，里头有一尊跟这个差不多的东西，它就那么望着我，逗弄着我去偷。要不是有百来个和尚守着，我估摸我真会去试一试。它跟这个一般大小，一样地丑，也是玉雕的。

"它四周照例供着别的神像，只是排列得像一排排警察。它旁边是一座空台子，我猜那原是另一尊跟它一模一样的待的地方。可我一问那一尊哪儿去了，好家伙，顿时闹了个底朝天，因为那帮家伙疑心兴许就是我拿的，这回是冲着它的同伴来的。我费了好大的劲才把他们安抚下去。这种事真是古怪，您说是不是，先生？

===Original===
“Yes,” said Derrick, in a strained voice. “Anything else?”

“We had a lot of talk back and forth but didn’t get anywhere. They seemed to claim that the thing was a sort of link between what one saw and didn’t see, and in a way joined them up to make a kind of general picture. I didn’t take much stock in all that, for Indo-China is stuffed with temples where they palaver about such subjects year after year. So that, sir, is why I happen to be interested in the original of this, and if you could put me in the way of getting it I’d make it worth your while.”

Derrick glanced involuntarily at Martin. On the man’s face had settled a look of utter hopelessness. There was no sullenness now, nothing grim or repellent. His eyes, at times so furtive, held only despair. His figure was slack, the broad shoulders dropped, and the big hands hung inert by his side. As though conscious of his master’s scrutiny, he looked up and pulled himself spasmodically together.

“Well,” said Derrick, “I don’t know if the present owner puts any value on the thing, but I’ll find out.” He took back the wax impression and slipped it into his pocket. “I don’t suppose this model really interests you from what you tell me.”

===Chinese===
"是的，"德里克声音发紧地说。"还有别的吗？

"我们你来我往谈了半天，也没谈出个名堂。他们好像认定，那东西是眼见的与眼不见的之间的一种链环，多少能把两边接起来，拼成一幅整图。这一套我没大当真，因为印度支那遍地是庙，这样的题目，他们在里头一年又一年地空谈。所以呢，先生，这就是我为什么偏偏对这件的原件上了心；您要是能给我指点一条到手的路子，我必不叫您白费神。

德里克不由自主地朝马丁瞥了一眼。那人脸上已经罩定了一副彻底绝望的神色。此刻再没有愠怒，没有半点阴沉或叫人厌恶的东西。他那双时而那样躲躲闪闪的眼睛里，只剩了颓唐。他浑身松垮，宽阔的肩膀耷拉着，两只大手毫无生气地垂在身旁。像是察觉了主人的端详，他抬起头来，抽搐似的强自振作了一下。

"好吧，"德里克说，"如今这位物主把不把这东西当回事，我不知道，不过我会问明白的。"他取回那尊蜡模，塞进口袋。"照您这话，这蜡模您大概不会真感兴趣。

===Original===
The peddler shook his head. “The copy is dead,” he replied slowly, “but, from what I gathered in the East, the real thing may have a sort of life in it.”

“All right, I’ll see you both at six o’clock.”

The man touched his cap. Derrick strolled on through the white gates, and, turning to the right, took the road that led away from Bamberley. Following this a quarter of a mile, he left it abruptly, traversed a neighboring copse, and doubled back along a parallel lane. He walked fast and came to the village in a little more than half an hour. In the tiny police office sat Sergeant Burke. Derrick waved his hand, went in, and took the proffered chair. Burke’s face was full of sudden interest, but he asked no questions. Presently Derrick leaned forward.

===Chinese===
货郎摇了摇头。"仿品是死的，"他缓缓答道，"可照我在东方耳闻的种种，那真家伙里头，兴许有一种活气。

"好吧，六点钟我见你们两位。

那人碰了碰帽檐。德里克信步穿过白门，向右一拐，上了那条离开班伯利（Bamberley）的大路。沿路走了四分之一英里，他突然下了大路，穿过旁边一座小树林，又顺着一条平行的巷道绕了回来。他脚程很快，半个多钟头就到了村里。小小的警察办事室里坐着伯克巡佐（Sergeant Burke）。德里克挥了挥手，走进去，在让过来的椅子上坐了。伯克满脸突然来了兴致，却一句也不问。过了一会儿，德里克俯身向前。

===Original===
“I think, sergeant, that an attempt at robbery will take place at Beech Lodge within the next hour or so.”

Burke sat up straighter than ever. “What’s that, sir?”

“I’ll explain in a minute, but first I want to make sure that, so far as the evidence went, no stranger was seen in the vicinity of the Lodge about the time of the murder.”

“No, sir. That seems to be without question.”

===Chinese===
"巡佐，依我看，个把钟头之内，山毛榉山庄会发生一桩盗窃的图谋。

伯克把腰杆挺得比先前更直了。"您说什么，先生？

"我马上就解释。不过先得弄确实了：照当时的证据，凶案发生前后，山庄左近没被人见过什么生面孔吧？

"没有，先生。这一点似乎毋庸置疑。

===Original===
“No peddler or traveling tinker had been in Bamberley that week?”

“No, Mr. Derrick, these people are all licensed and registered, and we examine the license of everyone who comes along. They are under the head of itinerant vendors.”

“Well, there’s an itinerant vendor at the Lodge now, and he’s more keen on buying than selling. He doesn’t make any bones of the fact that he’d like to get hold of the original of this.”

Derrick put the model on the table, and Burke fingered it curiously.

===Chinese===
"那一礼拜里，班伯利没来过货郎，或是走街串巷的补锅匠？

"没有，德里克先生。这路人个个要领执照、登记在册，谁来我们都要查他的执照。他们归在流动商贩的名目底下。

"好啊，眼下山庄里就有一个流动商贩，而且他买的心思比卖的还热。他毫不讳言，想弄到手的就是这件的原件。

德里克把蜡模放到桌上，伯克好奇地用手指拨弄着它。

===Original===
“Neat sort of job you’ve made of it, sir. Weighs about the same, too, doesn’t it?”

“Yes, I put some shot inside the base and balanced it with the other. It’s the other that my peddler friend is coming to see at six o’clock. Martin will be there with him.”

“When did this fellow turn up?”

Derrick told him all that had happened, Burke’s face growing ever more tense, while he thrilled to the belief that the Millicent case was alive again.

===Chinese===
"您这活儿做得真巧，先生。分量也差不多一样，是不是？

"是的，我在底座里搁了些铅砂，把它跟那一件配得两平。六点钟，我那位货郎朋友要来看的，就是那一件。马丁会跟他同来。

"这家伙是什么时候冒出来的？

德里克把事情的经过原原本本讲了，伯克的脸越听越绷得紧，心里却一阵阵发热，笃信米莉森特的案子又活了过来。

===Original===
“You haven’t missed much, sir,” he rambled presently. “Now what can I do?”

“At six o’clock those two men will be in the study. Blunt will be apparently in charge of Martin, whom I have made responsible for him, but actually I suspect it is the other way round. From what I can see, Martin is under Blunt’s thumb. Blunt will be asked if the room suggests anything to him in connection with the murder. He will probably pretend it does, and begin some kind of queer story, which may after all have something in it. I expect that he will in some way involve Martin, and that’s what Martin is in such fear of. At the same time, so far as Blunt is concerned, I can’t feel that Martin is so very important. It’s the image he’s after. Whether he can resist the impulse when he sees the real thing I can’t tell, but if he does not, that’s where you come in. The Millicent case will then start all over again with an attempted burglary, and I shall be in a position to testify that Martin lied to me about the burglar. And that’s as far as I can go at the moment.”

Burke nodded approvingly. “Then you want the grounds guarded?”

“Yes, in any way you think best. I would not bother about the front door; it would take too long to get out that way. The French window is the place.”

“The trap will be set at a quarter to six,” said Burke, glancing at the clock.

Derrick grinned contentedly. “It would be a bit of a feather in your cap, sergeant, if you could pull this thing off after two years.”

===Chinese===
"您可真没漏掉什么，先生，"他这会儿东拉西扯地说。"眼下我能做点什么？

"六点钟，那两个人会到书房里。明面上是布伦特归马丁管——我让马丁替他担着责任——可我疑心实际上恰恰倒过来。照我看，马丁是在布伦特的手心里攥着。到时候会问布伦特，这间屋子可曾让他想起什么跟凶案相干的东西。他多半会装作想起，接着编出一段什么古怪故事，那故事倒也未必全无内容。我料他会想方设法把马丁牵扯进去，而那正是马丁怕成那样的缘故。可与此同时，就布伦特而论，我又不觉得马丁有多要紧。他要的是那尊神像。等见了真家伙，他抵不抵得住那份冲动，我说不上；可他若是抵不住，就该您出场了。到那时，米莉森特案将以一桩盗窃未遂重新开场，我也能出面作证：马丁在窃贼的事上对我撒了谎。眼下我能走到的，就到这里。

伯克赞许地点点头。"那您是要园子有人看守？

"是的，怎么稳妥您怎么来。前门不必费心；从那儿出去太费工夫。落地窗才是要紧的地方。

"差一刻六点，圈套就布设停当。"伯克瞧了瞧钟说。

德里克心满意足地咧嘴一笑。"事隔两年还能把这案子办下来，巡佐，您帽子上可要添一根漂亮的翎毛了。

## X A Night of Tragedy / 第十章 悲剧之夜

===Original===
It took all Jean’s courage to go with Edith when the time came. She had had a not altogether comforting talk with her mother, in which, knowing that it was unwise to tell Mrs. Millicent too much, she only said that Edith wanted her to dine at Beech Lodge and that she might be able to help Derrick in his self-imposed task. Her mother assented, with a curious glance that suggested that it was not altogether the task that took her daughter to her old home. Jean, realizing the futility of fuller explanation, said little more.
It was something of a help that Edith understood so much and yet, in a way, understood so little. Her sanity, her cheerful hope that the tableau would frighten Martin into saying something that would settle the matter, and the growing affection in her manner, all combined to act like a much needed tonic. Jean found herself talking more freely than she ever imagined she could talk. She realized that this was because Edith was aware what was in her heart, and could perceive love, though the occult was beyond her. And the difference between the two girls did much to cement their friendship.
The affair of that night was tacitly avoided, Edith talking for the most part about that which lay nearest her heart. This was Derrick. She did not grudge him, wanting only his happiness, and the generosity of her nature touched Jean enormously. Edith took it for granted that whether the *tableau vivant*, as she put it, was successful or not, the next important event would be of a brighter character, and her contented assumption of this had an intriguing effect. It was strange to be regarded as a sister-in-law before the word was spoken. She was still talking about her brother, his art, his ambition, and the unexpectedness of him that she loved so much, when they came in sight of the gates of the Lodge.

===Chinese===
到了动身的时刻，琴（Jean）是拿出了全部勇气，才跟伊迪丝（Edith）一同去的。她跟母亲有过一场并不怎么让人宽心的谈话；她明白，对米莉森特太太（Mrs. Millicent）说得太多是不明智的，便只说伊迪丝请她去山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge）吃饭，又说她兴许能在那桩德里克（Derrick）自揽的差事上帮上一把忙。母亲应允了，可那好奇的一瞥分明在说：把女儿带回旧宅去的，未必全是那桩差事。琴知道再多解释也是枉然，便没有多说什么。

伊迪丝懂得那么多，却又在某种意义上懂得那么少，这倒算得上一种助力。她神志清明，满心欢喜地指望那场活人画（tableau vivant）会把马丁（Martin）吓得吐出什么话来，把事情做个了断；再加上她举止间日益亲热，这几样合在一起，恰似一副急需的强心剂。琴发觉，自己谈得竟比从前想象中能够谈的畅快得多。她明白，这是因为伊迪丝懂得她心里装着什么，认得出爱情，只是玄虚超凡之事，非她所能领会。而两个姑娘之间的这种差异，倒大大加深了她们的情谊。

那一夜的事，两人心照不宣地避开了，伊迪丝谈的，大半是贴着她自己心口最近的东西。那就是德里克。她对他并无芥蒂，只盼他幸福，她天性中的这份宽厚，深深触动了琴。伊迪丝想当然地认定：她所谓的'活人画'成功也罢、不成功也罢，接下来的一桩大事总要明朗些；她这份怡然自得的笃定，自有一种撩人的效力。名分上的字眼还不曾出口，就被人当作了嫂子看待，这滋味真是奇妙。她还在絮絮说着哥哥，说他的艺术，他的抱负，说他身上那份让她钟爱不已的出人意料，说着说着，山庄的大门已经遥遥在望。

===Original===
Jean fell silent as they passed the cottage, again untenanted, and the rose-trees that bore the marks of Martin’s skill. She recalled her last visit here, and marveled at its outcome. These familiar windows, this well-known door, and most of all that she would soon meet the blank eyes of Perkins, all moved her profoundly. She came to the house again not as a visitor, or to revive memories of the past, but actually to rebuild that past in such a way as to drag into the open the secret of so many years. It was a crusade on behalf of the dead, a high mission that involved putting aside all else till it be performed. Though the wound in her own heart ached, it must ache till the mission be discharged. And behind that was the whisper of love. It was this thought that enabled her to meet Derrick with a glance of high resolution that he found infinitely inspiring.
Looking back at it afterward, she always wondered whether dinner was not the greatest test of that memorable night. In spite of their combined efforts, it was very voiceless. Perkins, who glanced less at Jean than at her mistress, moved silent-footed as ever, blank to everything except her duties, and even these were carried out with a sort of subconscious detachment. She both cooked and served the meal, and with the same unaltered perfection. Nothing in her had changed, and as of old she made no lost motions. She knew that Martin was in jail, charged with complicity in the murder of her former master, yet no sign of it appeared on her ageless face.
But from her emanated something that made the usual conversation well nigh impossible. Had she shown her knowledge, the tension had been less. As it was, Jean pictured her father and mother in the chairs occupied by Derrick and Edith, heard the tones of a remembered voice, saw the same trim, straight figure moving with the same soundless precision﻿—and could hardly forbear to cry out. When, a little later, she entered that other room of grim significance, it was with a feeling that almost amounted to relief. There was no Perkins here.

===Chinese===
走过那间重又空无一人的小屋，走过留着马丁手艺印记的玫瑰树，琴沉默了下来。她回想起上一次来此的情景，对那次的结果惊叹不已。这些熟悉的窗户，这扇熟识的门，尤其是想到不久便要迎面碰上珀金斯（Perkins）那双空茫茫的眼睛，无一不令她心潮翻涌。她这次回到宅子，不是来作客，也不是来追怀旧事，而是当真要把往昔重新搭起来，好把积年的秘密拖到光天化日之下。这是替死者进行的一场征讨，一桩崇高的使命，须把其余一切暂且搁下，直到功成。她自己心头的创口虽然在作痛，也只得痛到使命完结为止。而在这一切背后，是爱的低语。正是这个念头，让她迎着德里克的目光时能带着高度的决绝，而他在那目光里找到了无穷的鼓舞。

事后回想起来，她始终疑心，那顿晚餐恐怕才是那个难忘之夜最严峻的考验。尽管三人一齐使劲，席间却几乎寂然无声。珀金斯照旧脚步无声地来去，看琴看得少，看女主人看得多，除了分内的差事，对一切恍若不见，就连那些差事，做起来也带着一种下意识的恍恍惚惚。饭是她做的，也是她端的，仍旧是那么分毫不差的完美。她身上什么都没有变，还像从前一样，没有一个多余的动作。她明知马丁已下了狱，被控同谋杀害旧主，可那张不见年岁的脸上，丝毫不露端倪。

可从她身上发散出一种东西，教寻常的攀谈几乎无从谈起。倘若她显出有所知晓的样子，这份紧张倒还会轻些。如今呢，琴把父亲和母亲的面影，安在德里克和伊迪丝坐着的椅子上，听见记忆里那个嗓音的腔调，看见同一个利落挺直的身影，带着同样无声的精准来去移动——几乎忍不住要叫出声来。过了一会儿，她走进另外那间意义森然的屋子时，心里竟近乎一种解脱。这里没有珀金斯。

===Original===
Derrick, whose eyes were unusually bright, waited till the maid had disappeared with the coffee-cups, then turned swiftly to Jean.
“Now we must act. Edith has given Perkins enough to keep her busy till half-past nine. That’s an hour. It was not safe to do anything here before this, so we must move things ourselves, and if possible without making a sound. One thing I want to ask: was your father dressed as in that picture?” He made a gesture toward the portrait.
“Yes, he always put on that coat after dinner.”
Derrick nodded, opened a drawer in the bottom of the desk, and produced a velveteen smoking-jacket.
“I thought that might be, so rooted out this old one of mine. Now we must shift the desk; then you can arrange the things on it. In a general way, are the contents of the room just the same?”
“Exactly, I think,” said the girl, after a swift scrutiny.

===Chinese===
德里克的眼睛亮得异乎寻常，他等着女仆端着咖啡杯退出不见了，才霍地转向琴。

"现在咱们得动手了。伊迪丝已经给珀金斯派足了活儿，够她忙到九点半。那是一个钟头。在这之前，在这儿做什么都不稳妥，所以东西得咱们自己搬，还要尽可能不出声响。有一件事我想问你：你父亲穿的衣服，跟那幅画像里的一样吗？"说着，他朝那幅肖像摆了摆手。

"是的，晚饭过后他总要穿上那件外衣。

德里克点了点头，拉开书桌底层的抽屉，取出一件平绒吸烟服来。

"我料到多半是这样，所以把我这件旧的翻了出来。现在咱们得把书桌挪一挪；挪完你来摆桌上的东西。大体上说，屋里的陈设都还是原样吗？

"我想一点不差。"姑娘飞快地环视了一遍，说道。

===Original===
“And that French window, was it locked?”
“Yes, always before dinner at this time of year.”
He gave a curious smile. “Tonight I think we’ll leave it open.”
“How stupid, Jack!” interjected Edith, “and let the man escape.”
“He can’t, because he’ll be chained to the sergeant. It’s with another object. Now are you ready?”

===Chinese===
"还有那扇落地窗，当时锁上了吗？

"锁了，每年这个时节，晚饭前总是锁上的。

他脸上浮起一个古怪的微笑。"今儿晚上，我想咱们就让它开着。

"多蠢哪，杰克（Jack）！"伊迪丝插嘴道，"那不是放那人逃了吗。

"他逃不了，他会被拴在巡佐身上。这是另有用意的。好了，你们预备好了吗？

===Original===
Jean sent him a quick glance. She guessed the object, and it made her heart beat faster.
Gradually the room assumed its former appearance. Edith assisted with a businesslike, good-humored alacrity, in the manner of a housekeeper who helps to arrange a stage for young people’s tableaux. To her these were chairs, tables, and rugs, nothing more. She wondered a good deal why a practical man like Sergeant Burke should be willing to take part, a man responsible for the custody of his prisoner, then reflected that it was all rather queer, and there was no point in worrying about what one didn’t understand. The consoling phase of it was her conviction that this was the last act of the somewhat disconcerting drama of the past few months, that it would soon be followed by the wedding of two of the principals, and then her brother would settle down and get on with his work. The thing that really most bothered her was the lease of Beech Lodge. She knew that Jean would never live here again.
It was as well she took her present occupation so placidly, for to Jean and Derrick, especially the former, the rearrangement of the study brought with it an austere and growing significance. They moved in the presence of what had been Millicent, recreating a poignantly familiar scene, directed by the gesture of an unseen hand. They were automatons, obeying they knew not what elusive instinct. And it seemed that as the room took shape it throbbed once more with a medley of tiny voices, each thrilling its own message in a fine, thin, vibrating tone. The chair where the dead man used to sit, the desk over which he leaned, the blurred stain that bore its cloudy witness to his passing; all these became vocal, joining in a mysterious communication which announced that nothing is ever utterly dissipated or lost, but in some form or quality remains, an imperishable record for all time.

===Chinese===
琴敏捷地瞟了他一眼。她猜到了那个用意，心也随之跳得更快了。

屋子渐渐恢复了旧日的模样。伊迪丝带着一副干练和气、说干就干的勤快从旁相助，那样子活像一位帮着年轻人布置活人画舞台的管家婆。在她眼里，这些不过是椅子、桌子和地毯，再无其他。她好一阵纳闷，伯克巡佐（Sergeant Burke）这么一个务实的人，一个身负看押人犯之责的人，怎么肯来凑这份热闹；转念又想，这一切本就相当离奇，自己不懂的事，犯不上操心。其中聊可宽慰的是，她笃信这是几个月来那出颇教人无所适从的戏的最后一幕，落幕之后，紧跟着便是两位主角的婚礼，往后哥哥就能安下心来，接着做他的事了。真正最让她烦心的一件事，倒是山毛榉山庄的租约。她知道，琴是万万不会再住到这儿来了。

幸而她能这样心平气和地对待眼下的活计，因为对琴和德里克来说——尤其是前者——书房的重新布置，带来的是一种严峻而日益沉重的意义。他们在米莉森特的遗范之中动作，仿佛有一只看不见的手打着手势，指挥他们重演一幕锥心刺骨的熟悉旧景。他们成了两具自动机偶，服从着一种自己也说不清的飘忽本能。而屋子渐渐成形之际，它仿佛又随着一片细碎人语的杂沓悸动起来，每一种都以纤细、尖锐、震颤的调子，传递着各自的消息。死者惯坐的椅子，他伏案的书桌，那摊以自己混沌的见证述说他死灭的模糊污渍——这一切都开了口，加入一场神秘的通传，宣告天下从无一物会彻底消散或失落，总要以某种形式、某种质地留存下来，成为一份永不磨灭的万世记录。

===Original===
Nine o’clock struck, and Derrick glanced from the French window into the darkness. The night was profound, and over the countryside rested a great blanket of fog. Putting out his hand, he could hardly see it. Beyond was the world, populous with life, lost and infinitely removed. From the trees bordering the lawn came a slow, soft drip, sounding like a vast, subdued weeping in this black obscurity. Anything might move here and be undetected. All in a breath he became convinced that there was something close by. But it did not move.
He pictured what must be going on now in Bamberley jail. Burke in his shiny cape, tramping down the barred passage to Martin’s cell, handcuffs dangling, grim, resolute, conscious of the desperate risk he ran, his jaw like iron. How had Burke disposed of his constables, and what kind of story had he told? Again Burke, with his dark-lantern at Martin’s barrier, the glint of yellow light on the gardener’s sullen face, the brief word of command, the click of metal that chained them together. Did Martin ask questions? Was he surprised, or unwilling, or did he take it all with his customary dogged silence? Then two burly figures engulfed in the fog, the wet glimmer from Bamberley windows﻿—if Bamberley were not already asleep﻿—the scrape of heavy feet on the graveled road, this strangely assorted pair moving up the long hill beneath trees that stretched ghostly arms overhead. What must Burke be thinking now?
He turned abruptly, leaving the window ajar, and drawing the curtains close. Crossing to the mantel, he beckoned to the two girls.
“Now I’m going to show you a part of the mystery of Beech Lodge.”

===Chinese===
九点钟敲响了，德里克从落地窗望进黑暗里去。夜深极了，乡野上覆着一大床雾的毯子。伸出手去，几乎看不见自己的手。手之外便是世界，众生熙攘，却已迷失，隔得无穷遥远。草坪边缘的树上传来缓慢而轻软的滴漏声，在这漆黑的晦暝里，听去像一片被压抑住的浩大哭泣。这里无论什么东西动一动，都不会被人察觉。倏忽之间，他忽然确信近处有一个什么。可它不动。

他想象着此刻班伯利（Bamberley）监狱里必定正在上演的情景。伯克穿着他那油亮的斗篷，咚咚地踏过装着铁栏的甬道，走向马丁的囚室，手铐在手上晃荡，面色冷峻，意志决绝，心里明白自己冒的是何等孤注一掷的险，下巴像铁打的一样。伯克是怎样把手下的警员打发开的，又编了一套什么样的说辞？又是伯克，提着遮光提灯来到马丁的栅栏前，黄光一闪，照上园丁那张阴沉的脸，简短的一声号令，金属咔哒一响，把两人锁在了一处。马丁问过什么吗？他是吃惊，是不情愿，还是照他惯常那样，闷声不响地全盘受了？随后是两个魁伟的身影没入雾中，班伯利窗户上湿漉漉的微光——倘若班伯利还没有睡的话——沉重的脚步擦着砂砾路面，这一对搭配奇特的搭档走上长长的山坡，头顶的树木伸出鬼影般的臂膀。此刻伯克心里在想些什么呢？

他猛地转过身，让窗虚掩着，把窗帘拉严。走到壁炉台前，他朝两位姑娘招了招手。

"现在，我要给你们看山毛榉山庄的一处秘密。

===Original===
He touched the woodwork, a small panel fell forward, and inside gleamed the jade god.
“Isn’t that clever?” said Edith cheerfully.
Jean did not stir. Her eyes, very wide open, were fixed on the image. It was all very extraordinary﻿—and very simple. Had her father found this hiding-place, or had he made it during the long evenings he spent alone after it became imperative to have some hidden shrine for his deadly trophy? Here was the spot, so near and yet so safe, whence came the mysterious authority that gave tongues to inanimate things. Yes, the jade god was safe there. Again she looked at Derrick.
“I begin to understand now,” she said under her breath.

===Chinese===
他碰了碰木饰，一块小小的暗板翻落下来，里面亮着那尊玉神。

"这多巧妙呀！"伊迪丝欢欢喜喜地说。

琴一动不动。她的眼睛睁得大大的，定定地落在神像上。这一切委实离奇——却又简单之至。这个藏匿之处，是父亲找到的，还是他亲手造出来的——在那些他独自捱过的漫长夜晚里，自从必须给那件致命的战利品寻一处隐秘的神龛之后？就是这个地方，这么近，却又这么稳妥，那赋予死物以人语的神秘权威，正是从这里来的。是的，玉神藏在那里是安全的。她又望了望德里克。

"我现在开始明白了。"她压低声音说。

===Original===
Edith moved close and peered in. “I’ll have that well scrubbed out tomorrow. It’s dreadful!”
Derrick laughed. “Please wait till I ask it.” He took out the thing and set it beside the lamp.
“It used to stand on the other side of him.” Jean’s voice was quiet and steady.
“I know, but that won’t matter this time, and,” he added thoughtfully, “I want it to be visible from the window.”
He paused, then sent her a glance that gave her renewed fortitude. “Now I’m going to get into position. Please don’t try and help me unless you feel you must, and it can only last a few minutes. You and Edith stand behind the screen, if you feel that staying there won’t be too much for you, and above all don’t stir till I do. It will all turn on Martin’s first words. If anything happens at the window, leave it to me. When Perkins knocks do not answer on any account. Is the lamp right?”

===Chinese===
伊迪丝凑上前去，朝里张望。"明儿我得叫人把这地方好好刷洗干净。太不像话了！

德里克笑了。"请等一等，总得让我先请教过它再说。"他把那东西取出来，放在灯旁。

"它从前是摆在他另一侧的。"琴的声音平静而安稳。

"我知道，不过这一回不要紧。还有，"他若有所思地补充道，"我要让它从窗外看得见。

他顿了顿，又递给她一瞥，那一瞥给了她新的勇气。"现在我就要各就各位了。请不要想着从旁帮我，除非你们觉得非帮不可；这顶多持续几分钟。你和伊迪丝站到屏风后面去，要是你们觉得待在那儿还撑得住的话；最要紧的是，我不动，你们千万也别动。成败全系在马丁开口的头几句话上。窗口要是有什么动静，交给我。珀金斯来敲门，无论如何都不要应声。灯这样行吗？

===Original===
Jean nodded.
He pressed her hand comfortingly, and again their eyes met in a gaze of perfect understanding.
“Get behind the screen now,” he whispered, “and don’t look at me.”
He put on the velveteen jacket and took the dead man’s chair. Leaning his head forward on the desk, the blurred stain was but a few inches from his throat. The deadly kris was beside him. He could see the jade god, its sardonic eyes bent on him, the cruel lips curved as though they comprehended the grim irony of the moment. Under that scrutiny he felt once more the mesmeric power evidenced here only the day before.

===Chinese===
琴点了点头。

他安慰地握了握她的手，两人的目光再次相遇，在那对视里是完全的相知。

"现在到屏风后面去，"他低声说，"不要看我。

他穿上那件平绒吸烟服，在死者的椅子上坐定。头朝前伏在书桌上，那摊模糊的污渍离他的喉咙不过几英寸。那把致命的克里斯短剑（kris）就在他手边。他看得见那尊玉神，它讥诮的眼睛俯视着他，残忍的唇角弯着，仿佛它参透了此刻这森然的反讽。在这道注视之下，他又一次感到那种催眠般的力量——就在前一天，它才在这里显露过身手。

===Original===
“Edith,” he murmured.
“Yes?”
“Twitch the curtains so that they are about an inch apart. Then get back quickly.”
She did this without a sound. Derrick lay still, his eyes closed. He knew that a narrow rib of light was streaming out over the sodden lawn and that the one who hid there could view the strange scene inside. Then silence fell. The tick of the clock sounded heavy and fateful. Shadows danced on the oaken walls, as they had danced two years before, and the flicker of fire cast an intermittent glow on Millicent’s face as it looked down from its gilded frame. From a nearby covert came the soft hooting of a barn-owl.

===Chinese===
"伊迪丝。"他低唤道。

"嗯？

"把窗帘拨动一下，让中间留出约莫一寸宽的缝。然后赶快回去。

她悄没声儿地照办了。德里克静静躺着，阖着双眼。他知道一道窄窄的光正流泻到外头浸透夜露的草坪上，藏在那儿的那一位，可以把屋内这奇异的一幕尽收眼底。随后一片沉寂落下。时钟的滴答声听来沉重而带着命定的意味。影子在橡木墙壁上舞动，正如两年前舞动过的那样，炉火的摇曳，把断断续续的光投在从镀金画框里俯视下来的米莉森特脸上。近处一处隐蔽的树丛里，传来仓鸮（barn-owl）柔缓的啼声。

===Original===
A faint whisper from the outer world reached Derrick, lying motionless with the blood pounding in his temples. It was that of movement, not sound; the merest fraction of movement, and transmitted by the most delicate waves of air. His senses, tuned to the utmost pitch, caught this, though it was no more than the suggestion that the atmosphere had been displaced not far off. Close to him someone had changed position. That was all he knew, and by the quality of this sensation he also guessed that the change had been made stealthily.
In the midst of this, and while the air seemed to transmit a steady singing monotone, came a sharp knock at the front door. He held his breath for the click of the latch, presently catching Burke’s voice, deep and husky. Followed a sound of heavy feet, and Perkins’s tap at the study door. She waited a moment; Derrick felt a slight draft and knew she was in the room with the two men behind her.
From Martin came a strange, throaty cry, and from the woman a choking gasp. Derrick’s hair prickled, and all power seemed to leave him. Again the gasp. Then flying feet crossed the floor with inconceivable swiftness, and Perkins flung herself beside his chair. He felt the grip of frenzied arms on his shoulders and heard tones of unutterable anguish.

===Chinese===
外界一阵轻微的窸窣传到德里克这里——他躺着一动不动，血在太阳穴里突突地撞。那不是声音，是动静；极微的一丝动静，借着最细密的空气微波传了过来。他的感官已调到极致，捕捉到了它，尽管那不过是一种暗示：不远处的空气被排开了。就在近旁，有人变换了位置。他知道的就是这些，而从这感觉的质地上，他又猜出，那变动是蹑手蹑脚做下的。

正在这当口，当空气仿佛还在传送一种绵延不断的蜂鸣似的单调声响时，前门传来一记敲响。他屏住呼吸，等着门闩咔哒一声，随即听见伯克的嗓音，低沉而沙哑。接着是沉重的脚步声，然后是珀金斯在书房门上的轻叩。她等了片刻；德里克觉出一丝微风，知道她已进了屋，两个男人跟在她身后。

马丁发出一声古怪的、堵在喉咙里的喊叫，那女人则发出一声哽咽的喘息。德里克只觉毛发倒竖，全身的力气仿佛都散尽了。喘息又是一声。随即一双飞奔的脚以不可思议的迅疾掠过地板，珀金斯扑到他椅子旁边。他觉出狂乱的双臂攫住自己的肩膀，听见那倾诉着说不出的悲苦的声调。

===Original===
“Master, master, what is it? Speak to me, speak to me! You’re not dead! I didn’t mean it. I didn’t know I did it. I was asleep; don’t you understand? And when I woke your blood was on my hands. Speak to me, master; for God’s sake, speak!”
For an instant Derrick was unable to move. Perkins crouched on the floor beside him, her body shaking, her face buried in her arms. Another cry from Martin, and he plunged, dragging Burke with him. He put his one free hand on the woman’s head.
“Don’t you go on like that, lass. It’s only a plant. You didn’t do it. I’ll swear you didn’t.”

===Chinese===
"主人，主人，您怎么了？跟我说话呀，跟我说话呀！您没有死！我不是故意的。我做下那事的时候，自己都不知道。我当时是睡着的；您不明白吗？等我醒来，您的血就在我手上了。跟我说话呀，主人；看在上帝份上，说句话呀！

有一瞬间，德里克动弹不得。珀金斯蜷伏在他身旁的地板上，身子发着抖，脸埋在臂弯里。马丁又是一声喊，整个人抢上前去，把伯克也拽了个趔趄。他把那只空着的手，放在那女人的头上。

"别这个样子了，姑娘。这不过是设下的一个局。不是你干的。我敢起誓，不是你干的。

===Original===
Perkins staggered to her feet. Her eyes were glazed. She stared wildly up at Martin, then at the sergeant as though she did not see him, then at the French window. The curtains had parted, and in the gap crouched the tense figure of Blunt, poised for a spring. At this last, her features became distorted. All the suffering of the damned crowded into them. With a motion of incredible swiftness, she grasped the kris and plunged it into her heart. Simultaneously Blunt darted forward.
What happened in that instant happened in a flash. Martin fell on his knees beside the woman. Burke, half dazed as he was, flung out his great fist and caught Blunt on the temple. There came a cry from the two who had been hidden behind the screen. Derrick leaped up. He saw Perkins, her breast stained scarlet, with Martin beside her, rocking in an abandonment of grief. Against the wall, as though it had been thrown there like a rag, huddled the insensible figure of the peddler. Burke was breathing hard and already fumbling at the lock of the handcuff that bound him to the gardener. On one side stood Jean and Edith, their eyes starting with horror.
In a moment the sergeant got himself free with a clink of metal. He glowered at the inert body of Blunt with a sort of animal satisfaction, then, kneeling beside Perkins, stared at her hard, and finally put his big head against that crimson heart. Martin did not move but gave one long shuddering sigh. A moment thus, till Burke heaved up, his face very grave, and made an unmistakable gesture. At that Edith put her arms round Jean and held the girl close.

===Chinese===
珀金斯摇摇晃晃地站了起来。她的眼神发了僵。她狂乱地仰望马丁，又望向巡佐，仿佛根本没看见他，再望向落地窗。窗帘已经分开，缝隙里蹲着布伦特（Blunt）那绷紧的身影，作势欲扑。看到这最后一眼，她的五官扭歪了。沉沦者所受的一切苦难，都涌进了那一张脸上。她以快得难以置信的动作抓起克里斯短剑，直扎进自己的心口。同一瞬间，布伦特向前窜出。

那一瞬间的事，都是在一闪之中完的。马丁双膝跪倒在那女人身边。伯克虽然半是发懵，仍挥出那只大拳头，一拳打在布伦特的太阳穴上。屏风后面藏着的两个人发出一声惊叫。德里克一跃而起。他看见珀金斯胸前染成一片猩红，马丁跪在她身旁，伤心得前后摇晃，全然不能自持。墙根底下，货郎那不省人事的身影瘫作一团，像是被人当破布似的扔在了那里。伯克喘着粗气，已经在摸弄那副把他和园丁锁在一起的手铐的锁。一旁站着琴和伊迪丝，眼睛惊得都要瞪了出来。

片刻之后，巡佐解开手铐，金属叮的一响，脱开了身。他带着一种兽性的满足，狠狠瞪着布伦特那具瘫软的躯体，随后跪到珀金斯身旁，死死盯住她看了半晌，最后把自己那颗大脑袋，贴上那颗猩红的心口。马丁一动不动，只发出一声悠长的、战栗的叹息。就这样过了一会儿，伯克直起身来，脸色极其凝重，做了一个不会让人误认的手势。见此，伊迪丝伸出双臂搂住琴，把姑娘紧紧抱在怀里。

===Original===
“I’ll take charge now, sir,” said Burke grimly. “These two men must come to the station with me. As for this poor woman, we can’t do better than take her to the cottage, if you don’t mind her being there till morning, and I’ll send a man up there as soon as possible. And,” he added, “perhaps I’d better take this knife for safekeeping till the inquest.”
“No, no!” Martin turned his grief-stricken face, clutching at the officer’s arm. “For God’s sake don’t do that. Let me stay with her,” he implored hoarsely.
Burke stared at him. “What are you talking about?”
“Don’t leave her in the cottage with anyone but me. I’ll be there in the morning. I won’t run away. I’ll do anything else you like, but for God’s sake let me stay with her tonight!”

===Chinese===
"现在由我接管了，先生。"伯克冷冷地说。"这两个男人必须随我回警局。至于这位可怜的女人，最妥不过把她抬到小屋去，要是您不介意她在那儿停放到明早，我尽快派一个人上来。还有，"他补充道，"这把刀兴许还是由我收着妥当，等验尸审讯（inquest）过了再说。

"不，不！"马丁转过那张悲痛欲绝的脸，一把抓住警官的胳膊。"看在上帝份上，别那样。让我陪着她。"他嗓音沙哑地哀告道。

伯克瞪着他。"你在说些什么？

"别把她交给除我以外的任何人，搁在小屋里。明早我一定到。我不会跑的。您要我做什么都行，可看在上帝份上，今儿晚上让我陪着她吧！

===Original===
Burke shook his head. “You’ll do what you’re told, and do it now. What is this woman to you?”
“My wife,” groaned Martin, and burst into throttling sobs.
Utter silence fell upon this room of death. Against the wall, Blunt gave a slow shiver and raised his head, regarding the scene with a strange calm, as though such tragedies were only passing incidents in a still greater drama. He made no attempt to move but lay there, resting on one elbow, part of it all, but infinitely removed. Derrick stared at the two girls. Edith’s arms were still round Jean, but their eyes were fixed on what lay on the floor. Jean looked at the man she loved. The terror was leaving her face, being replaced by a vast incomprehensible wonder mingled with a profound pity. In that moment she was his, and yet unspeakably distant. It was like traversing a forest of dreadful shadows and emerging, suddenly blinded, into the light, where one had to find oneself before seeing anything else. A great pity enveloped her altogether. She came quickly forward and knelt beside the still form.
“Jack, you must ask the sergeant to permit that. Don’t you understand? One poor woman among all these men,” she whispered. “Oh, the poor, poor soul!”

===Chinese===
伯克摇了摇头。"叫你怎么做你就怎么做，这就去办。这女人是你什么人？

"我的妻子。"马丁呻吟道，随即迸出一阵噎在喉头的恸哭。

这间死亡的屋子里，落下了绝对的寂静。墙根下，布伦特缓缓打了个寒噤，抬起头来，以一种奇特的平静注视着这一幕，仿佛这样的惨剧，不过是一出更大戏剧里转瞬即过的插曲。他不打算动弹，只侧着身子用一肘支在那里，是这一切的一部分，却又被隔在无穷远处。德里克望着那两个姑娘。伊迪丝的手臂仍搂着琴，可她们的眼睛都定在地板上躺着的东西上。琴望着她爱的那个男人。惊怖正从她脸上退去，代之以一种浩大的、无从索解的惊奇，又掺着深深的怜悯。那一刻，她整个人是他的，却又隔着说不出的遥远。就像穿行过一座可怖阴影的森林，骤然间两眼发花地踏进光里，先得找回自己，才看得见旁的任何东西。一片浩大的悲悯整个儿裹住了她。她快步上前，在那具一动不动的躯体旁跪了下来。

"杰克，你一定得求巡佐答应这一件事。你难道不明白吗？这么多男人里头，只有她一个可怜的女人，"她低声说，"噢，这可怜、可怜的人啊！

===Original===
Burke nodded. “Perhaps that will be all right, miss,” he broke in with a queer, deep gruffness. “We’ll let it go at that, but I’ll have to send a man up to stay outside till morning. He won’t come into the cottage. Is there anything you want to say, Mr. Derrick, before﻿—”
Derrick shook his head. “I think it has all been said.”
The sergeant touched Martin’s shoulder. “Will you﻿—” He glanced at the body. “Blunt goes with me.”
Martin nodded speechlessly. With infinite tenderness he picked up his wife as though she had been a child and, staring straight ahead with unseeing eyes, strode through the door which her lifeless hand had so recently opened for him. Then into the hall alone with his burden. The others heard the front door open and close, and after that the sound of slow feet on the gravel. This dwindled. Burke stepped across to where the peddler lay on his side and snapped on a handcuff. At the ring of metal, Derrick felt his eyes suddenly drawn to the jade god.

===Chinese===
伯克点了点头。"这么办兴许行，小姐，"他用一种古怪的、低沉粗厉的嗓音插进来，"就这么定了，不过我得派一个人上来，在外头守到天亮。他不会进小屋。您还有什么要说的吗，德里克先生，趁现在——"

德里克摇了摇头。"我想要说的，都已经说了。

巡佐碰了碰马丁的肩头。"你可愿意——"他朝那具尸体瞥了一眼。"布伦特跟我走。

马丁无言地点了点头。他以无限的温柔抱起自己的妻子，仿佛她是个孩子，双眼直视前方，什么也看不见，大步走出了那扇她那只再无气息的手方才还为他推开的门。随后独自抱着他的负担进了门厅。其余的人听见前门开了又关上，之后是缓慢的脚步踩上砂砾路的声响。那声响渐渐消逝。伯克跨到侧身躺着的货郎跟前，咔哒铐上一只手铐。金属一响，德里克觉出自己的眼睛，突然被拽向那尊玉神。

===Original===
The thing still rested, the light soaking into its emerald depths, and it seemed that on the tiny features rested a smile of sardonic satisfaction, as though it had known it all, and all the time.
