She hardly speaks to him as it is. Didn’t you say just now that she was not the marrying kind?”

===Chinese===
"我要是你，就把这个念头从脑子里撵出去。"他平静地说。

伊迪丝有点失望。"为什么？比这更奇的事，从前又不是没发生过。

话到嘴边，他本想说：更奇的事多半还会发生呢。可他只是笑了笑。

"他们的过去，我们一无所知——我是说，来山毛榉山庄之前——他们的将来，是他们自己的。这事情太微妙。珀金斯不喜欢马丁，虽说她不得不把他举荐为一个顶好的园丁；要说她于他有什么意思，那真是把话扯得太远了。眼下她就几乎不同他讲话。你刚才不还说，她不是结婚的那类人吗？

===Original===
“Yes, I did; but since there’s no probability of my arranging my own wedding, I rather like to potter about with other people’s. That may be useful to you, Jack, later on. As to Perkins, I dare say you’re right, and after all, if they did ultimately come together, it couldn’t be utterly festive, could it?”

“No,” he laughed, “it couldn’t. What else is there in the mind of the thoughtful Martha?”

“Nothing except that I’d like to make those two lives a bit more cheery, if I could; and naturally one’s mind pitches ahead.”

“It does,” he admitted. “Do you feel prophetic at the moment?”

She sent him a keen glance, at which he colored in spite of himself.

“I don’t believe, old boy, you’re quite ready for me to go on yet.”

===Chinese===
"是说过。可既然我自己这门亲事张罗无望，我倒挺乐意替别人的婚事瞎操操闲心。这本事往后再许对你有用，杰克。至于珀金斯，我敢说你说得对；再说了，他们倘若真到头来凑成一对，那也决计喜庆不起来，是不是？

"对，"他笑道，"喜庆不起来。心事重重的马大（Martha），心里还有什么？

"没有了。只是要是我办得到，我想让那两条性命过得稍微欢快一点；人自然而然要想在前头。

"是要想在前头。"他承认道，"这会儿你可有预感？

她投来锐利的一瞥，看得他不由自主红了脸。

"老伙计，我看你呀，还没准备好让我往下说呢。

---
===Original===
Now, if one takes the case of a highly sensitive and imaginative young man, whose mind is continually exploring for new sensations, and plunges him into a situation that is clothed with grimness and mystery, there will inevitably be set up a series of reactions such as Derrick had been experiencing for weeks past. And if, further, he then comes into touch with the girl whom he desires for his own, discovers her to be involved in the mystery, and realizes that she will remain out of reach till the problem is solved and her spirit set free, there will be added to his efforts the greatest incentive of all.

So it was with Derrick. Both from Jean herself and from Jean’s mother he now knew exactly where he stood. Though not told in so many words, he was under no misapprehension. All thought of his own work disappeared. This was his work, and the call of it was irresistible. As for Edith, and he smiled when he thought of her, she was in no danger. She stood too far outside the sweep of the drama, and it would be an error in tactics to tell her too much. He believed he would need her help at the end, but the end was not yet.

He was returning from a long and solitary walk when, nearing Beech Lodge, he noted on the road ahead a curious figure. It was that of an elderly-looking man who tramped some hundred yards in advance. His clothing was loose and weather-beaten. He stooped a little forward as he walked, and supported himself on a staff which he had evidently cut by the way. As Derrick drew abreast he took a sidelong glance and at once remarked the brightness of the stranger’s eyes. Physically he did not seem more than fifty years old. A first impression of age was given by the whiteness of his beard, but in spite of both stoop and stick he moved with an agility that belied his apparent years. His skin was a dark olive shade, his nose hooked like a raven’s beak, and his cotton shirt was open at the neck, showing where a thin gold chain lay yellow against the swarthy flesh.

===Chinese===
如今，倘若把一个高度敏感、想象力旺盛的年轻人——他的头脑无时无刻不在探求新的感受——投入到一片裹着阴森与神秘的境地里，那就必然激起一连串反应；德里克这几个星期所经受的，正是这样一串。倘若进而让他接触到他想要据为己有的那位姑娘，发现她缠在这团谜里，又明白在谜底揭开、她的性灵得到解脱之前，她始终可望而不可即，那么他的努力之上，就又添了天下最强的一股动力。

德里克正是这样。从琴本人和琴的母亲那里，他如今确切知道了自己站的位置。虽然没有谁挑明说破，他心里却没有一丝误解。自己那份工作的念头，烟消云散了。这就是他的工作，它的召唤无可抗拒。至于伊迪丝——想到她，他微笑了——她没有危险。她站得离这出戏的旋涡太远，对她讲得太多，是失策。他相信末了他会需要她出力，可末了还没有到。

一天，他长途独自散步归来，将到山毛榉山庄时，望见前头路上一个古怪的身影。那是个看去上了年纪的男人，走在前面约莫一百码的地方。他衣衫宽大，饱经风霜。走路时身子微微前伛，拄着一根手杖，看那样子，分明是路上现砍的。德里克赶得同他并排时，斜眼一瞥，立刻注意到那生客一双眼的亮。论身板，他看上去不过五十岁。头一眼觉他老，全因胡子的白；可尽管伛着背、拄着杖，他行动的轻捷，却同那表面的年纪不相称。他的皮肤是深橄榄色，鼻子弯得像渡鸦的喙，布衬衫领口敞着，露出一条细金链，黄澄澄地贴在黧黑的皮肉上。

===Original===
Derrick, meeting a swift look, experienced a sudden thrill. What manner of man was this to find in a Sussex lane? It seemed that something invisible but enormously potent moved down the road beside him. Then, instinctively, he halted at the gate of Beech Lodge and waited till the stranger came up. The latter made a sweeping gesture of salutation, and swung forward the pack that had been balanced on his shoulders.

“Good morning, sir. Will you buy a trinket and help an old man on his way? Cheap, sir, cheap, so cheap that they’re nothing short of presents, trade is that bad. Worse than I ever saw it in this country before.”

He spoke in a thin singsong voice that carried with it a sort of outlandish lilt. No British peddler this, but one from foreign parts. Derrick felt a now familiar thrill, and the spirit of him scented the Orient.

===Chinese===
两下一照面，德里克陡然一凛。在萨塞克斯（Sussex）的一条小路上，怎么会碰上这样一个人物？仿佛有什么看不见却大有力量的东西，顺着大路，同他并肩而行。于是他出于本能，在山毛榉山庄的院门口站住，等那生客赶上来。后者潇洒地一抬手，行了个礼，把担在肩上的一副货担往前一晃。

"您好，先生。买件小玩物儿，帮衬帮衬老人上路吧？便宜，先生，便宜，便宜得简直就等于白送，生意坏到了这个地步。比我在这个国家从前见过的哪一回都坏。

他说话嗓音细细的，抑扬如唱，带着一种异乡的调子。这不是英国的货郎，是海外来的。德里克感到一阵如今已熟悉的战栗，他的性灵嗅出了东方的气息。

===Original===
“What part of the world do you hail from?”

“Any and every part, sir. So long as it’s south of the line it makes no difference to me. Central America, Bengal, Borneo, the Cape, Cochîn, and Singapore, they’re all the same.” He shivered a little. “Time was when I thought the old country was the only place in the world, but I’ve got over that now, specially in winter.”

“Have you been here long this time?”

“A matter of a few months, but I’m going back East. This wind is too much for my bones.”

===Chinese===
"您是天下哪一方的人？

"哪儿都是，哪儿都算，先生。只要在赤道线以南，对我全是一样。中美洲、孟加拉、婆罗洲、好望角、科钦（Cochîn）、新加坡，还不都是一回事。"他微微打了个寒噤。"从前有一阵，我以为故国是世上唯一的地方，如今这想法丢了，尤其是在冬天。

"这一回来了很久了吗？

"拢共几个月，可我要回东方去了。这风，对我的老骨头太狠了。

===Original===
“What have you got?”

The pack was unrolled deftly on the wet grass, and inside lay a long strip of raw silk. Opening this after a swift glance down the road, the stranger revealed a medley of things, some beautiful, many valuable, and none of them ordinary. No Manchester stock was this. He had chains of native workmanship, hammered bangles of gold and silver, semiprecious stones carved with amazing cleverness, bits of oddly shaped ivory, all the paraphernalia of the peddler of the Far East. These he showed with obvious and lingering interest as though he loved them, pattering meantime of the Sunda Islands, the Moluccas, Bali, Lombok, and a host of Eastern ports and places whose accustomed names fell from his lips with glib fluency. There was no doubt about his knowing the East.

“This, sir, is a bit of hammered tin from Kuantan in Pahang, and you don’t get much of that kind of work nowadays. They wash the tin out of the gravel on the hillsides, and there are only three men in Malaysia who turn out this grade of art. This gold bangle is from Berak﻿—all Chinese labor there﻿—and you can have it for ten shillings. Better take it, sir, for it weighs twenty pennyweight and is worth a sovereign for the gold alone.”

===Chinese===
"你担子里是什么货？

货担在湿草地上麻利地摊开，里头是一幅长长的生绸。那生客先朝大路两头飞快扫了一眼，才把绸子打开，露出五花八门的东西：有的美，许多值钱，没有一件是寻常货色。这可不是曼彻斯特的机制货。有本地手工打就的链子，金银锤成的镯子，雕工精妙得出奇的半宝石，还有奇形怪状的象牙件——尽是远东货郎的全副行头。他亮出这些东西，兴味明显而恋恋不舍，像是打心眼里爱它们；嘴里滔滔不绝，讲着巽他群岛（Sunda Islands）、马鲁古群岛（the Moluccas）、巴厘（Bali）、龙目（Lombok），还有一串东方的港埠地名，那些烂熟的名字从他嘴上溜出来，顺溜得像流水。他对东方的熟稔，无可怀疑。

"这一件，先生，是彭亨（Pahang）关丹（Kuantan）来的锡，一锤一锤打出来的，如今那种手艺见得不多啦。他们把锡从山坡的砂砾里淘出来；整个马来亚（Malaysia），做得出这一路活计的，不过三个人。这只金镯子是贝拉克（Berak）来的——那儿全用中国工匠——十先令您拿去。收下吧，先生，它足足二十本尼威特（pennyweight）重——恰好合一金衡盎司——光金子就值一镑。

===Original===
“Then why not sell it as gold?”

“I wouldn’t offer it unless I were footsore and had to have somewhere to sleep. Can’t sell this sort of thing in an English village. I’d get arrested for having it; that’s why I’m heading for London.”

His piercing eyes rested on Derrick while he spoke, and in them moved something more than a mere interested scrutiny. Then they roamed curiously about the neighborhood. A brain was working behind those eyes, and it occurred to Derrick that this man knew well where he was.

===Chinese===
"那为什么不照金子卖？

"要不是我脚底走烂了、非寻个睡觉的地方不可，才不拿出来呢。这种东西，在英国的村子里脱不了手。带着它就得叫人抓起来；所以我正往伦敦（London）去。

他说话时，那双逼人的眼睛一直停在德里克身上，眼里流动的东西，不止是一种好奇的打量。随后那双眼又好奇地四下逡巡这一带。那对眼睛后面有一个脑子在转，德里克忽然想到：这个人很清楚自己到了什么地方。

===Original===
“Ever been in this part of England before?”

The lean brown fingers hung motionless over the trinkets. “No, sir, there’s nothing to bring my kind here unless it’s the June race meet. Won’t you take this bangle? There’s a good twenty pennyweight of fine gold in it. There isn’t a lady who would turn up her nose at it. I’ve seen a woman bought and sold for one not half as good.”

Derrick hesitated. Strange thoughts were coursing through his head and with them the growing conviction that this, like all the rest of it, was meant to be. Perhaps it was grotesque, but had not Perkins said weeks ago that others were coming to Beech Lodge, drawn by mysterious signals they could not withstand? Then Martin had come, and Jean Millicent, and who should say that here was not the last of the gathered company. It was not a bundle of trinkets that had brought this wanderer to these tragic gates.

===Chinese===
"英格兰这一带，从前来过吗？

棕色的瘦手指悬在那些玩物上头，一动不动。"没有，先生。除非赶六月的赛马会，没有一样能把我们这路人引到这儿来。这只镯子您就收了吧？里头足有二十本尼威特成色十足的金子。天底下没有哪位太太小姐会朝它撇嘴。我见过一个女人，为一只不及它一半好的镯子，被人买来卖去。

德里克迟疑了。种种古怪的念头在他脑子里驰过，随之俱来的，是一种越来越强的确信：这一桩，同其余的一切一样，都是命定的。这念头也许荒唐，可几个星期前珀金斯不是说过，还有别人要来山毛榉山庄，被他们抵挡不住的神秘信号牵引着来吗？后来马丁来了，琴·米莉森特来了；谁能说眼前这个，不是聚齐的最后一位呢。把这样一个浪游人引到这几扇惨祸之门的，决不是一担小玩物。

===Original===
“What’s your name? You speak good English, but you’re not English, are you?”

The peddler shook his head. “No, sir, my name is Blunt. My father was English and my mother a Malay woman. I was born out there and spent most of my time between the islands. Now I’m for getting back as soon as I can, so I’m heading for the East India Docks, where I’ll sign on. It’s too cold for me in this country. Couldn’t I spend the night in one of the outhouses, sir?”

“Well,” said Derrick thoughtfully, “I think perhaps my gardener might find a corner for you in his cottage. I’ve no objections. You can see him about it, if you like.”

The man’s dark eyes took on a sudden gleam. “That’s good of you, sir, and I won’t be a bit of trouble to anyone. If there’s any work to be done, I’ll do it. Here, you’d better take this bangle now.”

He held out the yellow circlet. Derrick was about to refuse when something whispered to him to take it. Slipping it into his pocket, he was surprised at its weight.

===Chinese===
"你叫什么名字？你英国话说得好，可你不是英国人，对吧？

货郎摇了摇头。"不是，先生。我叫布伦特（Blunt）。我父亲是英国人，母亲是马来（Malay）女人。我生在那边，大半辈子在各处岛子之间过。如今只求尽早回去，所以正要上东印度码头（the East India Docks）去，到那儿签约上船。这个国家对我太冷了。今晚我能不能在您一处外屋里过一夜，先生？

"嗯，"德里克想了想，说，"我想我的园丁兴许能在他的小屋里给你腾出一个角落。我不反对。你愿意的话，可以自己去同他说。

那人的黑眼睛陡然一亮。"您真厚道，先生。我不会给任何人添一点麻烦。有什么活计要做，我都做。来，这只镯子您这就收下吧。

他递过那只黄澄澄的镯圈。德里克正要推辞，心里却有什么低声劝他收下。他把它塞进口袋，那分量叫他吃了一惊。

===Original===
“Why do you offer something worth a sovereign for a night’s lodging?” he queried.

The peddler sent him a curious glance. “That’s all right, sir. A few pennyweight of gold is neither here nor there in a lifetime.”

Derrick nodded. “Perhaps not﻿—to either of us. If you turn in here I think you’ll find the gardener just on the other side of the cottage.”

===Chinese===
"值一镑金子的东西，你为什么拿它换一夜宿头？"他问。

货郎朝他投来古怪的一瞥。"不打紧，先生。一辈子几十年，几本尼威特金子，无足轻重。

德里克点了点头。"也许无足轻重——对你我都一样。你要是从这儿进去，园丁就在小屋另一边，我想你一找就着。

===Original===
The man rolled up his pack and moved along the drive toward the house. Derrick stood irresolute for a moment; then something impelled him to follow. Presently he stopped and, making no noise, slipped behind a sheltering tree. The peddler was now thirty yards ahead. At this moment Martin, who had been working among his rosebushes, looked up and saw the stranger.

What happened next was all over in an instant. He made a swift involuntary gesture in which fear and astonishment were tensely blended. The spade slipped from his fingers, and his eyes protruded. He seemed to sway a little as he stood with an uncouth elephantine motion, and his lips trembled, but no sound came from them. Then, as Derrick emerged from behind the tree and came carelessly toward him, he made an extraordinary noise in his throat and turned again to his work. And, so far as the master of Beech Lodge could determine, the peddler had given no sign whatever.

Derrick lounged forward with a manner of complete indifference.

===Chinese===
那人卷起货担，顺着车道朝宅子走去。德里克踌躇地站了片刻，随即有什么驱使他跟了上去。走了一程，他停下脚步，蹑手蹑脚闪到一棵蔽身的树后。货郎这时已在前头三十码开外。正在这时，一直在玫瑰丛里做活的马丁抬起头来，看见了那生客。

接下来的一切，一眨眼就完了。他猛地做了个不由自主的手势，惊愕与恐惧紧绷绷地绞在里头。铁锹从他手指间滑落，眼睛凸了出来。他站在那里，粗笨得像头象似的，微微晃了一晃；嘴唇哆嗦，却发不出一点声音。随后，等德里克从树后转出、满脸无事地朝他走来时，他喉咙里发出一声异样的响动，又低头做他的活去了。而就山毛榉山庄主人所能察见的，货郎那边连一点表示也没有。

德里克慢吞吞地踱上前去，一副全不在意的做派。

===Original===
“Martin, this man has asked that he might sleep somewhere on the place tonight, and I told him I had no objection to his spending it in the cottage if you’re willing. His name is Blunt, and it’s for you to say. You will be responsible for him if he does stay, so you can settle it between you.”

The gardener’s face had become rigidly impassive, but there was no concealing the blood that surged into it. He glanced first at his master, then at the mysterious stranger, and moistened his dry lips.

“Name of Blunt, sir,” he said thickly. “That will be all right as far as I’m concerned. I’ll look after him.”

===Chinese===
"马丁，这个人问，今晚能不能在园子里找个地方睡。我告诉他，只要你乐意，让他宿在小屋里，我也不反对。他叫布伦特，由你说了算。他要是住下，你得对他负责，你们俩自己商量着定吧。

园丁的脸僵成了一副铁板，可涌上来的血色却掩藏不住。他先看看主人，又看看那神秘的生客，舔了舔发干的嘴唇。

"叫布伦特，先生。"他口齿含混地说。"那就没有问题。我会照看他。

===Original===
Derrick, fearing that his curiosity might become too apparent, nodded and strolled on toward the house. He was very deep in thought. Another factor was now added to the problem and had to be dealt with. In a way it was not unexpected. There had been built up a triangle with a dead man in the center and an undeciphered personality at each corner. Was this all coincidence, or was not destiny rather arranging the puppets of a great drama without any extraneous assistance?

His first instinct was to report the new arrival to Sergeant Burke, but on second thought he decided to say nothing at the moment. The sergeant’s methods were too heavy-handed, too likely to disturb whatever process was now at work. However vague to human eyes it might be, he was convinced that subtle causes were in motion, wheels of fate that revolved within other wheels, a mechanism that operated silently, mysteriously, and with some inflexible purpose. As to himself, he could only wait. Instructions would come, as they always had come, and in the appointed time, from the same imperceptible and unchanging source.

As though in search of these, he went into the study and gave himself up to thought, leaving the windows of his mind open to the lightest breath of influence. His vision embraced four divergent figures, all of them inextricably linked. Perkins, with the half-told tale of her life shrouded behind her sphinx-like face, a domestic automaton as imperturbable as the jade god itself, the rigid guardian of her own secret, who talked a strange language in her sleep, and in that sleep mourned the disappearance of her murdered master. Martin, new come from round the world, the recipient of viewless signals that reached and followed him through the rotting jungles, signals that worked and whispered till they penetrated his slow brain and he came back perforce ten thousand miles of land and sea, a suspect to the source of suspicion, to work within sight of the window of the dead man of whose violent passing he no doubt knew the secret.

===Chinese===
德里克怕自己的好奇心显得太露，点点头，朝宅子信步走去。他陷入了深深的思索。难题里如今又添了一个因素，须得应付。这也算不得全出意外。一座三角已经立了起来：当中是个死人，三个角上各是一个不曾解读明白的人物。这一切全是巧合呢，还是冥冥中自有天意，不借一点外力，调度着一出大戏里的人偶？

他的第一个本能，是把新来的人报告伯克巡佐；转念一想，决定此刻什么都不说。巡佐的手段太重，太容易惊动眼下正起着作用的无论什么过程。那过程在人眼里无论多么渺茫，他坚信有一些精微的因由正在运转：命运的轮子套着轮子转，一部机括无声地、神秘地运转着，抱着某种绝不通融的目的。至于他自己，只有等。指示自会来，一如它们往常的来法，在注定的时刻，从那个同样无从察觉、从不更改的源头来。

仿佛正是要寻这些指示，他走进书房，听凭自己沉入思索，把心灵的窗子敞开着，任凭影响之气最轻的一息吹进来。他眼前浮起四个方向各异的人影，彼此缠结，解也解不开。珀金斯，她那斯芬克司般的脸后头，裹着她讲了一半的身世；一架操持家务的自动机器，同玉神一样处变不惊；她自己秘密的森严看守，睡梦里说一种古怪的语言，在睡梦里哀恸她那被害的主人的失踪。马丁，刚绕了地球回来，是无形信号的收受者，那些信号穿过朽烂的丛林追上他、跟着他，作用着，低语着，直到钻透他那迟缓的脑子，逼着他跨越万里海陆回到这里，回到嫌疑的源头来当嫌疑的人；他做工的地方，正望得见那死人的窗口，而那桩横死的底细，他无疑一清二楚。

===Original===
Then the peddler, with restless intelligence in his ageless eyes, himself a traveler from the same land of strange peoples, tongues, and gods, tramping indomitably along the deep Sussex lanes till he arrived as though by chance at the door of one who apparently knew him not, yet regarded his advent with fear and astonishment. And, last of all, Jean Millicent, the shadow of tragedy clouding her bright youth, a creature made for love and tenderness and care but weighted with brooding apprehensions, toward whom his own spirit had begun to move, striving, seeking, and hoping.

Compassed with thoughts like these, he saw himself in relation to those profound forces which, whether acknowledged or not, dominate our lives. The winds of circumstance seemed to him no longer the winds of chance. There was purpose behind all, some high and remote goal to which we are led along roads that might seem strange and byways that wander apparently from the general direction. He knew now that it would be futile to attempt anything save the task that lay directly ahead, and till that task was discharged Jean Millicent could never be his.

===Chinese===
再就是那货郎，那双看不出年纪的眼睛里闪着不安分的机智；他自己就是从那同一片奇人、奇语、奇神的土地来的旅人，不屈不挠地一步步踏过萨塞克斯的深巷，直到仿佛出于偶然，来到一家的门前——那家的人分明不认得他，见到他来，却又惊又怕。而最后一位，是琴·米莉森特：惨祸的暗影罩着她明亮的青春；一个生来该被爱着、疼着、护着的造物，却压着沉沉的疑惧；而他自己的性灵，已经开始朝她移近——努力着，寻索着，盼望着。

环绕着这样的念头，他把自己放进那些深不可测的力量里去看——那些力量，无论我们承认与否，都主宰着我们的生命。际遇的风，在他看来不再是偶然的风。一切背后都有一个目的，有一个高远渺茫的目标：我们被引向它，走的路也许古怪，绕的岔道仿佛偏离了总的方向。他如今明白，除了摆在眼前的这一桩任务，尝试什么都是徒劳；而这桩任务不了结，琴·米莉森特永远不能是他的。

===Original===
He was still plunged in reflection when Edith’s entrance brought him sharply back to earth. She came into the study, noted that he was not working, seemed about to speak, then smiled at him inquiringly. He smiled back. She took a penny from her pocket and laid it silently on the desk. Derrick was feeling for another when his fingers closed round the gold bangle.

“Can you wear this?” he asked casually.

She examined it with delighted and intense interest. “It’s perfectly lovely, Jack; but where on earth did you get it? Not in Bamberley?”

“Not much,” he laughed. “I got it as a present a few minutes ago from my paying guest, or rather Martin’s.”

===Chinese===
他还沉在沉思里，伊迪丝走了进来，把他猛地拉回地上。她走进书房，看出他没在做活，像是正要说话，随即又询问地朝他一笑。他也回以一笑。她从口袋里取出一枚便士，一声不响放在书桌上。德里克正要再摸一枚，手指却合拢在了那只金镯子上。

"这个你能戴吗？"他随口问。

她又惊又喜，兴致浓浓地察看。"可爱极了，杰克。可你到底是打哪儿弄来的？总不会是在班伯利吧？

"可不是嘛。"他笑道。"这是几分钟前人家送我的礼，我那位付房钱的客人送的——或者说得更准些，是马丁的客人。

===Original===
“What do you mean?”

“There’s a peddler down at the cottage now. He has a pack full of things like that.”

She shook her head. “Jack, you know you can’t afford it.”

“It’s all right and didn’t cost me anything. It’s the price of a night’s lodging with Martin.”

“Then why didn’t the man give it to Martin?”

“That never occurred to me. He was tired and footsore, wanted shelter for the night, and I suggested to Martin that he take him in and be responsible for him. The man insisted that I take this, so there you are. Cheap at the price, I call it. There’s a sovereign worth of gold in it.”

===Chinese===
"这话怎么讲？

"这会儿小屋里来了个货郎。他一担子全是那样的东西。

她摇了摇头。"杰克，你明知道你付不起。

"不妨事，一个子儿也没花我。这是在马丁那儿宿一夜的房钱。

"那他为什么不把镯子给马丁？

"这一层我可没想到。他又累，脚又走烂了，想寻个过夜的地方，我便向马丁提议，收留他、担保他。那人非要我收下这个不可，事情就这样了。依我说，这价钱便宜得很。里头的金子足值一镑呢。

===Original===
Edith pushed the bangle on her wrist and twisted it thoughtfully. “Why don’t you tell me the real truth, old boy?”

“I have. Want to see him? Interesting sort of person, white beard, bright eyes, and been everywhere. You’ll never guess where he’s come from now.”

“Where?”

“Burma,” said Derrick meaningly.

“Isn’t that where you told me Martin had come from when he turned up here?” she asked slowly.

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
伊迪丝把镯子推上手腕，若有所思地转着它。"老伙计，你为什么不跟我说实话呢？

"说的就是实话。想见见他吗？挺有意思的一个人：白胡子，亮眼睛，什么地方都到过。他这回从哪儿来，你永远猜不着。

"从哪儿？

"缅甸。"德里克意味深长地说。

"马丁在这儿冒出来的时候，你告诉过我，他不就是打那儿来的吗？"她缓缓地问。

"是。

===Original===
She was silent for a moment. “Well, Jack?”

He glanced at her thoughtfully. “It’s all part of the rest of it. I’m caught up in something stronger than myself. I can’t help it.”

She took off the bangle and laid it on the desk. “Do you know where it’s leading you?”

He nodded, smiling. “Yes, I think so.”

“To Jean Millicent?”

===Chinese===
她沉默了片刻。"嗳，杰克？

他若有所思地看了她一眼。"这都是那件事的一部分。我让一样比我强的大事卷了进去。我自己做不了主。

她褪下镯子，放在书桌上。"你知道它要把你引到哪儿去吗？

他点点头，微笑着。"知道，我想我知道。

"引到琴·米莉森特身边？

===Original===
“I believe that,” he said gravely. “It seems now that it was meant I should find her like this. It was all meant.”

Edith nodded. “When I saw you two together the other day I felt the same thing, so you’d better give her this. It’s more appropriate. You see, Jack,” she went on with a smile that was rather sober, “I’m not the marrying kind.”

“Nonsense,” he expostulated.

“It’s quite true, and girls know it by instinct rather early in life. Then they try to forget it, and settle down in a sort of way to making other people comfortable. But they can’t help seeing what’s going on all round them﻿—I mean other girls with their men﻿—and feeling a bit out of it. It’s a bit solemn for a woman to realize that she’ll never waken the biggest thing in the world in the heart of a man, because she lacks the indescribable something that is necessary, and it makes a good many of us queer and cantankerous. You see we don’t possess what every woman longs for.

===Chinese===
"我相信正是。"他郑重地说。"如今看来，我注定该这样找到她。这一切都是注定的。

伊迪丝点了点头。"那天看你们俩在一处，我也觉出是这个意思，所以这镯子，你还是给她好。给她更相宜。你要知道，杰克，"她接着说，脸上带一丝颇为郑重的微笑，"我不是结婚的那类人。

"胡说。"他劝止道。

"千真万确。姑娘家凭本能，人生很早便知道这一点。随后她们想法子忘掉它，安下心来，马马虎虎地专门让别人舒舒服服。可她们免不了看在眼里：四下里一切照旧热闹——我是说，别的姑娘同她们的男人——自己却有点插不进脚。一个女人明白，她永远唤不醒一个男人心里世上最大的那样东西，因为她缺那样没法形容的、必不可少的什么；这份了悟有点叫人肃然，也叫我们不少人变得古怪、爱找茬。你要知道，我们不具备每个女人渴望的东西。

===Original===
“Sometimes, too, she has a sort of perception about others. I had it when I saw you with Jean; and, Jack, it made me happier than in a long time. That’s why I want you to give her this bangle, which is really lovely, and also tell me just what I can do to help. You needn’t make any bones about that. It’s my job, and I’m thankful for it. And for goodness’ sake, old boy, don’t think of me as being down in the mouth. I’m not. I understand about you and Jean, and nothing would make me happier, but as for all the rest of this queer affair I don’t understand it at all. So tell me what I can do, and I’ll do it. And don’t you ever think of me as a good-hearted and deserving spinster, or I’ll never forgive you.”

It was a long speech for Edith, who but seldom let herself go. Derrick was oddly touched and patted her arm affectionately. He knew she wanted no thanks and felt that in the next few days he might need her more than ever before.

“I’d like to tell you something. You probably won’t accept it as I do, but you ought to know, and somehow I’m glad you don’t believe in the occult.”

“I don’t,” she said frankly.

“Well,” he laughed, “please carry on. Nothing could help me more. I’ve no desire to spend the rest of my life in criminal investigation. I know you think I’m being carried too far by this one and am collecting a lot of unimportant data that I anticipate will produce something remarkable later on. Perhaps I am, but I’m going to see it through, and you know what I’m working for.”

===Chinese===
"有时候，这样的姑娘对别人还有一种知觉。那天见你同琴在一处，我就有了那种知觉；杰克，它让我高兴，好久没那么高兴过了。所以我才要你把这只镯子给她——它实在可爱——还要你直截了当告诉我，我能帮上什么忙。这个不必吞吞吐吐。这就是我的本分，有这个本分，我也感激。千万，老伙计，别把我当作垂头丧气。我没有。你和琴的事我明白，没有比这更叫我高兴的了；只是这桩古怪事里其余的一切，我一点儿也不懂。所以告诉我能做什么，我就做什么。还有，你永远别把我当成好心肠、守本分的老姑娘，不然我永远不饶你。

对伊迪丝来说，这是一篇长话，她难得这样敞开自己。德里克被莫名地打动，怜爱地拍了拍她的手臂。他知道她不要人道谢，又觉得往后几天，他也许比以往任何时候都更需要她。

"我想告诉你一件事。你多半不会像我这样接受它，可你应当知道。不知怎么，你不信那些玄秘的东西，我倒很高兴。

"我是不信。"她坦率地说。

"那就好，"他笑了，"请照旧走下去。再没有比这更能帮我的了。我可不打算把下半辈子花在侦查罪案上。我知道你的想法：这一件把我带得太远了，我在收罗一大堆不要紧的碎料，指望它们日后变出什么了不得的东西。兴许是这样，可我要把它查到底；而且你晓得，我图的是什么。

===Original===
“She’s a darling,” murmured Edith, thoughtfully.

“It means everything to have you say that.” He looked at her keenly and, deliberating how much to tell her of what was in his mind, decided to leave the matter where it stood and disclose only what was necessary.

“You’ll think it wild of me to associate the coming of this peddler with Millicent’s murder, but I do. I want to keep him here a few days if I can. There’s something, I don’t know what, in the air; but the thing that brought him is probably what brought Martin. I’ll talk with him later on. Would you like to go up to town for a few days?”

===Chinese===
"她是个可人儿。"伊迪丝若有所思地低声道。

"你肯说这句话，比什么都要紧。"他锐利地看了她一眼，心里掂量着，所想的事该告诉她几分，末了决定就停在原处，只把必需说的说给她。

"我把这货郎的到来，同米莉森特的凶案连在一处想，你会觉得我异想天开，可我就是这么想。我要是办得到，想留他住上几天。空气里有什么东西，我说不清是什么；而把他引来的那样东西，多半同引来马丁的是一样的。回头我同他谈谈。你愿不愿意进城去住几天？

===Original===
“Why?” she asked, puzzled.

“I’ve been thinking it’s rather unfeeling of me to expect you to be here at present under the circumstances. A good many women would not like it.”

Edith smiled and shook her head. “My dear, I can’t take it as seriously as you do, and I’m not nervous. Do anything you like that you believe will bring you nearer Jean. Be as mysterious and occult as you please. It won’t worry me a bit. But the house must be looked after, and that’s for me. I hope this won’t upset Perkins, as things are going very smoothly in her domain, and don’t forget that it will imperil next year’s roses if you demoralize Martin. Now it’s time for lunch.”

===Chinese===
"为什么？"她不解地问。

"我一直在想，眼下这种情形，还指望你待在这儿，未免太没心肝。换了好多女人，是不乐意待的。

伊迪丝笑了笑，摇了摇头。"亲爱的，我没法像你那样把这事看得严重，我也不胆小。只要你信它能使你离琴更近，你爱做什么就做什么。你尽管神秘、尽管玄乎去，一点儿也搅扰不着我。只是这宅子总得有人照管，那是我的事。但愿这别搅了珀金斯，她那一亩三分地如今井井有条；也别忘了，你要是搅乱了马丁的心神，明年一年的玫瑰可就危险了。好了，该吃午饭了。

## XII “I Love You!” / 第十二章 「我爱你！」

===Original===
Derrick got back to Beech Lodge in time for lunch and plunged at once into a vastly different atmosphere. The house was servantless, and this very fact had kept Edith too busy to indulge in any morbid reflections, even had her resilient nature felt so inclined. She was moved by the knowledge that her brother had been under a strain which, however incomprehensible to herself, was nevertheless to him very real. It was reflected in his eyes, his restless manner, and the notes that had lain untouched for weeks. She wanted him to get back to his work, to be normal, and above all things happy. She recognized and admired the creative side of him, made allowances for what she considered the essential vagaries of his temperament, and had long since decided to sacrifice herself if necessary on so unusual an altar. She could feel for him, if not with him.

So, returning from the grim scene of Bamberley jail, he found an energetic, practical young person, obviously full of work, and over whom hung but little of the tragedy of the immediate past. She supplied the touch that the moment demanded. He welcomed this, leaned on it far more than he realized, and sat down at the table with a feeling of prodigious relief. The hand of the domestic artist was visible here, and if at times the diaphanous shape of the stiff figure of Perkins seemed to stand close to his shoulder, the sensation did not oppress him. Edith talked generalities till, nearly at the end of the meal, she sent him a frank questioning look.

“Of course I’m just dying to know if anything new came out this morning. Martin turned up an hour ago. He seemed to me like another man, got out his tools and went to work without a word, and it made me more curious than ever. That queer puzzling expression has gone out of his eyes, and I couldn’t help thinking he was something like a dog that had been stolen and found his way back to his old home.”

===Chinese===
德里克（Derrick）赶回山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge），正好赶上午饭，随即一头扎进了一种截然不同的气氛里。宅子里没了仆人，单是这一桩，就够伊迪丝（Edith）忙的了，忙得无暇沉溺于任何病态的遐想——纵使她那富有韧性的天性当真想那么想。她知道弟弟心头一直压着一股紧张，那紧张她自己再怎么也领会不了，于他却千真万确地真实。它照在他的眼睛里，照在他那坐立不安的举止里，也照在那搁了几个星期没动的手稿上。她盼他回到工作上去，盼他恢复正常，更要紧的是盼他快活。他身上那创作者的一面，她认得出也佩服，把他性子里那些她认作与生俱来的古怪处，一概宽容过去，而且早就打定了主意：事到必要，把自己牺牲在这座异乎寻常的祭坛上也罢。她能体谅他，只是不能同他一起感受。

就这样，从班伯利（Bamberley）监狱那阴惨的场景里回来，他见到的是一个精力充沛、脚踏实地的年轻人，手边显然满是活计，近在眼前的惨事，在她身上几乎没有挂多少影子。眼下的时刻需要怎样的一笔点染，她便给了怎样的一笔。他欢迎这个，倚靠着它，倚靠得远比他自己觉出的更深，带着一种说不尽的宽慰在桌边坐了下来。这里处处看得见持家巧手的手笔；即便珀金斯（Perkins）那僵直身形的轻薄魂影，偶尔仿佛又立到他肩旁，那感觉也不再来压他了。伊迪丝只谈些不着边际的闲话，直到这顿饭快吃完了，才朝他递来一记坦率的、探询的目光。

"我当然急着要知道，今儿早上可问出了什么新鲜事儿。马丁（Martin）是一个钟头前回来的。我看他像换了一个人，一声不吭地取出工具就干起活来，这倒教我比先前更好奇了。他眼睛里那种古怪费解的神气没有了，我不由得想，他有点像一条被人偷走的狗，自己寻回了老家。

===Original===
Derrick nodded cheerfully. “I rather fancy he feels like that, just for the present, anyway, but we’ll probably have to find another gardener. He won’t want to stay here.”

“No, I suppose he couldn’t.” She hesitated a moment, then gave him the straightforward glance he knew so well. “Do you know, Jack, I think we’ve all been rather stupid about that poor woman; yes, I mean you, too.”

“It’s quite possible,” he admitted, “but why?”

“Well, I suppose it’s easy to put things together, afterward; but, looking back at everything, what happened seems in a way as natural as it was dreadful. The poor soul had her terrible secret and took the only way out of it, but couldn’t we have anticipated that somehow?”

===Chinese===
德里克快活地点了点头。"我看他自己的滋味也正是那样——至少眼下是这样——不过我们多半得另寻一个园丁了。他不会再愿意待在这儿。

"是啊，我看他待不下去的。"她迟疑了片刻，随后朝他投来他再熟悉不过的那率直的一瞥。"你可知道，杰克（Jack），我想我们大家对那可怜的女人都够蠢的；对，连你也算在内。

"这很有可能，"他承认道，"可为什么？

"唉，事后把事情串到一起看，总是容易的；可回头把这一切再看一遍，发生的事在某种意义上，可怕归可怕，却又自然得很。那可怜的人揣着她可怕的秘密，走了仅有的一条出路——可我们就一点儿也没法预先料到吗？

===Original===
“It was the last thing one could imagine.” He went on, and told her some of what had transpired that morning in Bamberley jail, but not all. She listened silently, with little gestures of wonder, and a softened light in her honest, brown eyes. At the story of Martin’s devotion they filled with tears.

“One has heard of men like that with one great passion in their lives that no one else can understand because there seems nothing to bring it to life. Perhaps women are apt to be hard on women, but it’s hard to see how Perkins could have roused such a thing. After all, it may be the men who are queer, and not us. I suppose this story will be all over England in a few days?”

He made a grimace. “I’m afraid so. The reporters will gather like a flock of crows.”

“But after that’s over will you be able to settle down to work, and—and the other thing?”

“What other thing?”

===Chinese===
"那是任谁也顶料想不到的事。"他接着讲下去，把那天早晨班伯利监狱里问出的一些情形告诉了她，但不是全部。她静静地听着，做出些小小的惊叹姿态，那双诚实的棕色眼睛里泛起柔和的光。听到马丁那份忠贞，她眼里充满了泪水。

"这样的男人，人家也不是没听说过：一辈子怀着一场旁人无从理解的热爱，因为瞧不出有什么东西能叫它显出真身来。兴许女人对女人总是容易苛刻，可实在难明白，珀金斯怎么会唤得起这样一场热爱。说到底，古怪的兴许是男人，倒不是我们。我想，这故事过不了几天就要传遍全英国了吧？

他做了个苦脸。"怕是这样。记者们要像一群乌鸦似的聚拢来了。

"可等那一阵过去，你就能安下心来做事了么，还有——还有另一桩事？

"哪一桩事？

===Original===
“When do you go to see the Millicents?” she asked cheerfully. “It’s all a frightful mixture, I know, and it seems rather appalling that you two should have been brought together like this, but perhaps stranger things have happened.”

“Not much stranger,” he said thoughtfully. “I’m going there in an hour or so. They’re expecting me.”

“Well,” she went on with growing earnestness, “I know it’s your affair, but I wouldn’t say a word more than necessary. The thing is done with, Jack, all except this horrid inquest, at which you say Jean and I won’t have to appear, and you don’t know how glad I am of that. I’ve a feeling that you’ll have a good many years in which to tell her the rest of it—I mean anything more you think she should know—but don’t burden her with what is so grim, if you can help it. She’s too young. Girls like her often seem to offer themselves unconsciously to wounds, but they don’t find out till afterward how deep the thing has gone. As for Mrs. Millicent, I wouldn’t attempt to say much to her. Let Jean do that in her own way. Nothing can be as close as mother and daughter in a time like this, and they can’t hurt each other. You’ll probably think me dreadfully cheeky, but I rather feel that you and Jean have been dwelling mentally far too long on things you both think I can’t understand because I’m not occult, but I do understand them just enough to feel that they’re neither cheerful nor in a queer way healthy for people of your age. So please forgive all this, and give me a cigarette, and help clear this table, and for goodness’ sake tell me where I can get a cook and housemaid who won’t imagine Beech Lodge is full of horrors.”

===Chinese===
"你什么时候去看米莉森特家（the Millicents）的人？"她轻快地问，"我知道事情全给搅成了一团，你们两个竟是这样凑到一处的，看着也着实吓人，不过比这更离奇的事，兴许也有过。

"离奇不了多少。"他若有所思地说。"过一个钟头左右我就去。他们在等着我。

"唉，"她的语气越来越恳切，接着说了下去，"我知道这是你的事，可我还是要多一句嘴：能不说的一个字也别说。事情了啦，杰克，只剩下那场讨厌的验尸审讯（inquest），你说我和琴（Jean）都用不着到场，你不知道我听了多高兴。我有这么个感觉：往后有的是年头，够你把剩下的讲给她听——我是说，凡是你觉得她该多知道的，都可以慢慢讲——可那些阴惨的东西，能不叫她背就别叫她背。她太年轻了。像她这样的姑娘，常常仿佛不知不觉地就把身子迎向刀口，事后才发觉那东西扎得有多深。至于米莉森特太太（Mrs. Millicent），我看你也不必打算多说什么。让琴用自己的法子去说。眼下这种时候，什么情分也比不上母女之间那么贴身，她们伤了彼此。你多半要嫌我冒失得不像话了，可我总觉着，你同琴在心思里泡在那些事儿上头，泡得也太久了——你们俩都当我领会不了，因为我不通玄妙；可我也领会得刚好够，知道那些事对你们这样年纪的人，既不叫人快活，在那层古怪的意思上，也算不得有益身心。所以这一切请你多多原谅，递我一支烟，帮着收拾桌子，千万千万告诉我，上哪儿能寻来一个厨娘、一个女仆，不会把山毛榉山庄想成满是妖魔鬼怪的地方。

===Original===
He laughed outright, the first real laugh for weeks. “You’ve got my future pretty well mapped out, but I think you’re right about the Millicents. Been in the study this morning?”

“Yes, and the room is just as it was when we came here. But that desk was a fearful weight.”

“You moved it yourself?”

“Of course, seeing there was no one else, and all the time I had an odd feeling that the things were glad to be moved back. Is that sort of feeling accounted for in your philosophy?”

===Chinese===
他放声笑了出来，几个星期以来的头一回真笑。"你把我往后的事都铺排得差不多了，不过关于米莉森特家，我想你说得对。今儿早上进书房去了？

"去了，屋里同我们刚来时一模一样。只是那张书桌沉得吓人。

"你自个儿挪的？

"可不是，又没有旁人，而且自始至终，我都有一种古怪的感觉，觉得那些东西巴不得搬回原处去。这一类感觉，在你的哲学里讲得通么？

===Original===
“It is now, thanks to you.”

“I’ve been wondering what you’re going to do with that jade image. I couldn’t find the panel this morning.”

Derrick told her.

“But have you the right? It isn’t yours.”

“I’ll chance that.”

===Chinese===
"如今讲得通了，多亏了你。

"我一直在琢磨，你打算把那尊玉像怎么处置。今儿早上，我找不着那块暗板了。

德里克告诉了她。

"可你有这个权利么？那不是你的东西。

"这个险我来冒。

===Original===
“But, Jack, if it was included in the inventory you can’t destroy it without all kinds of dilapidations to the Thursbys. Isn’t it supposed to be valuable?”

“It may be, but most decidedly it was not in the inventory, therefore it was not sold to the Thursbys, and consequently I needn’t answer to them, but only to the Millicents. And I fancy I know what they’ll say.”

“Well, you ought to by this time, and, speaking of the Thursbys, I’ve an idea that if everything that has taken place since we came had happened eighteen months ago they wouldn’t have let this house when you came along and fell in love with it.”

===Chinese===
"可是杰克，那东西要是列进了清点册，你把它毁了，就免不了给瑟斯比家（the Thursbys）招来种种亏累。人家不是都说它很值钱么？

"值钱兴许是值钱的，可它千真万确没有列进清点册，所以它没有随宅子卖给瑟斯比家，这样一来，我无须向他们交代，只须向米莉森特家交代。他们会怎么说，我料得到。

"到了这步田地，你也该料得到了。说起瑟斯比家，我有个想法：要是我们来了以后出的这些事，早十八个月就出了，等你来租这宅子、一眼看上它的时候，他们就不肯放租了。

===Original===
“But they weren’t in it then.”

“No, but they would have been; at least, something suggests they would.”

“Why do you say that?” he asked curiously.

“Because she’s not the kind of woman to be afraid of anything obvious, anything she can see and even partly understand. If poor Perkins had done away with herself then, I rather think Mrs. Thursby would have been as much fascinated as horrified. Don’t you know that sort? It would have given her something to talk about for the rest of her life with no one to interrupt; something infinitely more intriguing than her husband’s grenades, or whatever they are. How do you feel yourself about that?”

===Chinese===
"可那时候他们并不在这宅子里。

"对，可他们本来是会住进来的；至少，有点什么迹象显示他们会。

"你为什么这么说？"他好奇地问。

"因为瑟斯比太太（Mrs. Thursby）不是那种女人，见了明摆着的东西会害怕——凡是看得见、多少还懂得一点的东西，她都不怕。要是可怜的珀金斯那时候自尽了，我倒想，瑟斯比太太会是又惊又迷，惊也惊得真，迷也迷得真。这一路人你难道不认得？那可就给了她一样东西，够她后半辈子讲的，还没有人插得进嘴；比她丈夫的手榴弹——还是别的什么玩意儿来着——可有意思得无穷多了。你自己觉得怎么样？

===Original===
“I’m not quite sure,” he said candidly. “What I did feel about the house until yesterday seems to have gone this morning, as though a wind had blown through it with all the windows open. But I wouldn’t mind subletting now, if there were any chance of it, which there isn’t at this time of year. So we have it for another nine months anyway.”

“You couldn’t very well bring Jean back here,” she murmured thoughtfully.

He shook his head. “No, I couldn’t.”

Edith got up with the sudden remembrance that her hands were very full.

===Chinese===
"我说不大准，"他坦白地说，"我先前对这宅子的那点感觉，到今儿早上仿佛都散了，好像一阵风穿堂而过，满宅的窗子都敞着。不过如今这宅子要是转得出去，我倒不介意转租了——只是这个时节断然转不出去。反正再怎么说，这宅子我们还能住上九个月。

"你可不大好把琴带回这儿来。"她若有所思地低声道。

他摇了摇头。"是啊，不好。

伊迪丝蓦地想起自己手边还有一大摊事，便站起身来。

===Original===
“Well, I suppose there’s time enough for that, and anyway you have to marry her first. Wouldn’t it be queer if—” She broke off with a little laugh.

“If what?”

“Nothing, I’m only wandering, and of course just when there’s no time for it. Please put these things on that tray and open the pantry door. I won’t expect you for tea.”

He went off a little later, passing Martin, who only touched his cap. He did look like another man, but neither of them spoke. The shadow of despair seemed to have left his face and to be replaced by a gravity that was new and dignified. Derrick strode on with the consciousness that the wind had blown through himself as well as Beech Lodge. He admitted his debt to Edith and now saw her cheerful sanity in a fresh light. It was strange to have leaned on a person, however dear, because they were incapable of being torn by one’s own reactions. How bright she was! How helpful and practical! What a standby!

===Chinese===
"唉，那件事来日方长，再说你总得先娶了她。你说怪不怪，要是——"她轻轻一笑，把话收住了。

"要是什么？

"没什么，我只是在胡思乱想，偏偏又赶着最没工夫的时候。请把这些东西搁到那只托盘上，把配膳室的门打开。茶点我就不指望你了。

过了一会儿他动身走了，路过马丁跟前，对方只碰了碰帽檐。他的确像换了一个人，可两人都没有说话。绝望的阴影似乎已从他的脸上退去，换上了一种前所未有的庄重气度。德里克大步向前走，心里明白：那阵风不但穿过了山毛榉山庄，也穿过了他自己。他承认欠了伊迪丝的情，此刻又以一种新的眼光，看见了她那令人宽心的清明。倚靠一个人——不管那个人多么亲——就因为她不会被自己的心绪撕扯，这真是桩奇怪的事。她多么明朗啊！多么得力、多么务实！多么靠得住的一个人！

===Original===
But he never knew what the past hour or two had cost her—she was too good an actor for that; nor did he guess that she had watched him to the gate, her eyes dim, feeling more lonely than ever before in her life. She admitted there was much she did not understand, or even want to understand, but he did not perceive how often she had come nearly to the breaking-point. With Edith it was as with many another woman, the cost of whose sacrifice is hidden too deep for discovery, and only the beauty of it revealed.

Jean and her mother were together, and Mrs. Millicent greeted him with a quiet affection that touched him deeply. It meant that not only had Jean told her of the tragedy of the night before but also that she saw in him more than the man who had solved the mystery of her husband’s death. Jean’s eyes met his own as she gave him her hand, and they carried a message that needed no speech. Mrs. Millicent regarded them both with a gentle pleasure in which there was no surprise, then waited a little nervously. The picture of the study of Beech Lodge and what had happened there still haunted her brain.

“Jean told me you were to have a talk with Martin and the peddler this morning,” she said. “Did you see them?”

===Chinese===
可是他永远不会知道，方才那一两个钟头于她耗去了什么——她是太好的一名演员，瞒得过他；他也猜不到，她一直目送他到大门口，眼里一片迷蒙，比她这辈子任何时候都更觉孤单。她自认有许多事她不懂，甚至也不想懂，可他没有察觉，她曾多少回几乎到了断裂的边缘。伊迪丝同许多别的女人一样：那牺牲的代价藏得太深，深得无从发现，露在外面的，只有它的美。

琴同母亲在一处。米莉森特太太迎着他，带着一种静静的慈爱，深深打动了他。这意味着，琴不但已把前夜的惨事告诉了她，还意味着，在她眼里，他不只是解开她丈夫死亡之谜的那个人。琴伸手给他的时候，目光同他的相遇了，那目光捎来的口信，无须言语。米莉森特太太温存地望着他们两个，那欣然里没有半分诧异，随后又略带紧张地等待着。山毛榉山庄书房的图景，和那里发生过的一切，仍然萦回在她脑际。

"琴告诉我，说今儿早上你要同马丁和货郎谈一谈，"她说。"见着他们了吗？

===Original===
“Yes,” he said quietly.

“Did they tell you anything new about my—my husband?” She had summoned all her courage for this question and wanted it over.

Derrick shook his head. “There was very little about that and nothing of real importance. It was mostly about the image he found in Burma which Blunt says has a good deal of past history that makes it of special interest to certain people there. Both men agreed that it carried bad luck, and sometimes danger, wherever it went. It’s quite obvious that in some way it fascinated Mr. Millicent; and”—here he hesitated an instant—“it seems to have exercised later on the same influence over Perkins; and,” he concluded slowly, “the thing worked in her brain till finally she did what she did.”

Mrs. Millicent shivered. “I know it impressed him tremendously. That was clear from the day he got back from Burma. He once told me he thought it was valuable, but it always frightened me because of its effect on him. It seemed to carry some dreadful secret with it. I asked him to destroy it several times, but that rather shocked him. He never let it out of his own hands and always hid it where you found it.”

===Chinese===
"见了。"他平静地说。

"关于我——我丈夫，他们可说了什么新的没有？"为了这一问，她鼓足了全部的勇气，只求快些问完。

德里克摇了摇头。"关于那一节谈得很少，也没有什么真正要紧的。多半谈的是他在缅甸（Burma）寻见的那尊神像——照布伦特（Blunt）的说法，它有一大段来历，那地方的某些人因此对它特别上心。两个人都认定，它走到哪儿，就把厄运带到哪儿，有时还是凶险。很明显，它不知怎么迷住了米莉森特先生；还有"——说到这里他迟疑了一瞬——"它后来似乎也对珀金斯施了同样的影响；还有，"他缓缓地收束道，"那东西在她脑子里作动，直到末了，她做下了她做下的那件事。

米莉森特太太打了个寒噤。"我知道它给他的印象深极了。打他从缅甸回来那天起，这一点就看得清楚。他有一回跟我说，他觉得它很值钱，可它一向叫我害怕，怕的就是它对他起的那作用。它仿佛随身带着什么可怕的秘密。我不止一回求他把它毁了，可那样说倒叫他不自在。他从不让它离自己的手，总是把它藏在你们发现它的地方。

===Original===
“Do you feel that way about it now?”

“Yes, more than ever.”

“Then may I destroy it?” he asked quickly.

“I should be very glad and feel happier than in a long time if you did.”

“I will, and I think others may be happier, too, in the long run.”

===Chinese===
"如今你还是这样觉得么？

"是的，比先前更厉害。

"那我可以把它毁了么？"他紧接着问。

"你若毁了它，我会非常高兴，会觉着许久以来没有这么快活过。

"我一定毁。而且我想，往长远了说，旁人也会因此快活些。

===Original===
She nodded. “Isn’t it strange?”

“What?” he asked curiously.

Her eyes rested a moment on Jean’s lovely face, then turned back to him.

“My dear boy,” she said with a sort of soft impulsiveness, “do you think I can’t see how it is between you two? The strange part is that the last three months should have resulted in this, that out of shadows and uncertainty should come something so different. I’m afraid I have not understood much of all you’ve done at Beech Lodge, but I remember so distinctly the day when Jean said she must go in and tell you what had happened there. I can’t say anything more about it now, for I’m too conscious of the effect of it all on this child of mine, but soon you and I must have a long talk. How is your sister?” she added unsteadily.

“All right, I think. Her hands are rather full now till she gets some help.” He knew that Jean’s eyes were fixed on him and found it hard to speak.

===Chinese===
她点了点头。"你说怪不怪？

"什么怪？"他好奇地问。

她的目光在琴那张可爱的脸上停留了片刻，随后又转回到他身上。

"我亲爱的孩子，"她带着一种温柔的冲动说道，"难道你以为，我看不出你们两个是怎么回事？奇怪的是，这三个月竟会有这样的结果，从重重阴影和惶惑不定里，竟走出了这样一样全然不同的东西。你们在山毛榉山庄做的这一切，我怕是懂得不多，可我记得那样清楚，琴说她必须进去、把在那儿出的事告诉你的那一天。眼下我不能再多说这一节了，我心头太放不下这一切对我这孩子的影响，不过不久，你我得好好长谈一回。你妹妹好吗？"她补了一句，声音有些不稳。

"我想还好。在寻着帮手之前，她手边的事够她忙的。"他知道琴的眼睛正定在他身上，一时觉得难以开口。

===Original===
“I’m sure of that. She’s splendid, and something tells me we’re going to be great friends. You’ll stay for tea, won’t you?”

After that she got up, put her hand on his shoulder for an understanding instant, and went out. She felt as though a new grasp, young and strong, had laid hold of the wheel of life, and was comforted. They heard her step on the stair. Derrick, his breath coming faster, crossed the room, stood for a moment beside Jean’s chair, and put out his arms.

“I love you,” he whispered; “I love you!”

She gazed at him, her cheeks pale, then flooding with an exquisite color, and came to him with a quick little sigh of happiness. It was not thus they had clung together the evening before. Now there was joy in the clinging, and the sweet promise of more joy that awaited them.

===Chinese===
"这我拿得准。她真了不起，而且有个声音告诉我，我们要成了不起的朋友。你留下来用茶点，好么？

说完这话，她站起身，把一只手在他肩上搁了一个心照不宣的瞬息，便出去了。她觉得仿佛有一只新的手，年轻而有力，握住了生命之轮，心里得到了安慰。他们听见她的脚步踏上了楼梯。德里克呼吸急促起来，穿过房间，在琴的椅边站了片刻，张开了双臂。

"我爱你，"他低声说；"我爱你！

她凝望着他，双颊先是苍白，随即涌上一片绝佳的红晕，带着一声急促而幸福的小小叹息，投进他的怀抱。前一夜他们相拥，可不是这样。此刻这相拥里有欢喜，还有等着他们的、更多欢喜的甜蜜许诺。

===Original===
“Do you remember that first morning we met?” he whispered again.

“I don’t know why I went to Beech Lodge. I think I had to.”

“Yes, that was it. I thought you were so wonderful and brave. The house was never quite the same after that.”

“Do you think I was wise to come?” she smiled.

He answered with a kiss, and she stirred in his arms, only to be drawn closer.

===Chinese===
"你还记得我们初见的那天早晨么？"他又低声说。

"我不知道那天为什么去了山毛榉山庄。我想我是不得不去。

"是啊，正是那样。我当时就觉得，你又出色，又勇敢。打那以后，这宅子就再不完全是先前的样子了。

"你说我去得明智么？"她微笑道。

他用一个吻回答了她；她在他的臂弯里微微一动，却被搂得更紧了。

===Original===
“I was tremendously interested in you, even then,” she confided, “and rather frightened. I hope I didn’t show it. Did Edith think I was very bold?”

“Edith thinks no end of you. She’s a trump.”

Jean nodded happily. “You and I need someone like that near us, Jack.”

“I don’t want anyone near us for a while,” he protested. “How did your mother know?”

===Chinese===
"那时候我就对你着迷得很了，"她吐露道，"还有些害怕。但愿我没露出来。伊迪丝是不是觉得我太大胆了？

"伊迪丝把你夸到天上去了。她是个顶呱呱的人。

琴幸福地点了点头。"你和我身边，就需要这样一个人，杰克。

"这一阵子我可不想任何人挨近我们。"他抗议道。"你母亲是怎么知道的？

===Original===
“I’m afraid she must have gathered something from me. Does Edith know?”

“I began to think she knew as soon as I did, if not before. She’s awfully pleased about it.”

The girl was silent for a moment. “Jack, dearest.”

“Yes?”

===Chinese===
"我怕她准是从我身上看出点什么来了。伊迪丝知道么？

"我已经觉得，她知道得不比我晚，兴许还更早些。她为此高兴得很。

姑娘沉默了片刻。"杰克，最亲爱的。

"嗯？

===Original===
“Is there much you didn’t tell mother; I mean about this morning?”

“I tried just to say what would help her. The rest can keep.”

“And there was nothing that could make any difference to—to us?”

“I don’t quite understand.”

===Chinese===
"你没告诉母亲的，还有很多么——我是说今儿早上的事？

"我只拣对她有益处的说。其余的可以留着慢慢讲。

"也没有什么，会改变——改变我们俩的？

"我不大明白你的意思。

===Original===
“There was something I always felt, but I couldn’t make myself tell you. It was the sensation that whatever had descended on father would also involve me in the same way. I can’t really explain beyond that, but it meant that I couldn’t surrender and let myself love you till all this had been lifted away. Last night, when I saw what happened, and in spite of the dreadfulness of it, the strangest feeling came that it had been lifted in that moment. When you were trying to help Perkins, I couldn’t avoid staring at the jade god, because I knew he had something to do with it. He stared back, and for the very first time I was not afraid of him. It was just as though Perkins had paid for everything and set me free. Tell me that nothing was said this morning by either of those men to upset that; but you must tell me on your honor.” She shivered involuntarily, but gradually her tremor ceased under his nearness and strength.

“All that was said, and I’ll tell you all of it some day, points to the same thing. There is absolutely nothing to fear. We’ll prove that very soon, you and I, and there will be no longer a jade god to work mischief. Don’t you realize, darling, those days are all past?”

===Chinese===
"有一样东西，我一直都觉着，可总没法叫自己对你说。那是一种感觉：无论降临到父亲头上的什么，也总要同样地缠上我。再深的我也实在讲不出了，可它的意思是：这一切没有卸下去之前，我不能缴械，不能任凭自己去爱你。昨天夜里，我亲眼看见了发生的事——它再可怕也罢——最奇怪的感觉来了：就在那一刻，它卸下去了。你想法子救助珀金斯的时候，我没法不直盯着那尊玉神，因为我知道它与这事有干系。它也回瞪着我，而生平头一回，我不怕它了。那情形，正像珀金斯把一切都偿清了，把我放了出来。告诉我，今儿早上那两个人，谁也没有说过一句什么，来搅翻这一点；可你必须凭你的名誉告诉我。"她不由自主地打了个颤，可渐渐地，靠着他的人、靠着他的力量，她的战栗平息了。

"说过的那些话——来日我会一字不漏讲给你听——都指向同一件事：绝对没有什么可怕的。这一点，你我很快就会证实，而且，再不会有一尊玉神来作祟了。亲爱的，你还不明白么，那些日子都过去了？

===Original===
Her arms tightened round his neck. “Why do you love me, Jack?”

“I’ve been waiting for you all my life.”

Then, slowly, she raised her lips to his.

===Chinese===
她的手臂在他颈后收紧了。"你为什么爱我，杰克？

"我等你，等了一辈子。

于是，缓缓地，她把自己的嘴唇迎上了他的。

## IV Jean / 第四章 琴

===Original===
Some two weeks after the staff of Beech Lodge had been completed by the engagement of the gardener, Mrs. Millicent and her daughter were walking along a quiet lane at a little distance from their old home. The house itself they had not seen since the time of the tragedy, and over them still hung the weight of a great grief. It had touched Mrs. Millicent’s hair with gray and given her a strangely wistful expression. Her sorrow was increased by the belief that her husband had had an enemy, the husband who had worshiped her with love and devotion for twenty years of married companionship. What enemy could such a man make in all the world?
For Jean, her daughter, the blow had been no less severe. And it had a deeper significance. Dazed and stupefied, she was nevertheless aware of the power behind the blow, the power that dealt it. Where her mother was inclined to give way with a hopeless wonder at the cruelty of fate, Jean perceived that the hand that thus struck the helpless might not have been stayed by her father’s blood. If her father were in the way of something﻿—she knew not what﻿—might there not be others similarly threatened? The resiliency of her youth refused merely to accept the situation.

===Chinese===
园丁受雇之后，山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge）的人手便算配齐了。此后约莫两个星期，米莉森特太太（Mrs. Millicent）带着女儿，正在离旧宅不远的一条僻静小路上散步。出事以来，那座宅子本身她们再没有见过，一片沉重的悲恸至今压在她们心头。它给米莉森特太太的鬓发染上了灰白，又给她添出一种奇异的怅惘神色。她相信丈夫生前结过仇家，悲哀因此又深了一层——这位丈夫，二十年结缡相伴，一直以爱与忠诚敬奉着她。这样一个人，普天之下能结下什么仇家呢？
对女儿琴（Jean）来说，这一击同样惨重，而且含义更深。她虽昏乱麻木，却仍觉出这一击背后的力量，那出手的力量。母亲偏向于在命运的无情面前屈服，只剩无可奈何的惊愕；琴却看出，那只如此打击无告之人的手，未必会因她父亲的血就此止住。倘若她父亲挡了什么东西的路——她不知道是什么——难道不会有旁人同样受着威胁？她青春的回弹之力，不肯只是认下这个局面。

===Original===
They came to a fork in the lane, one turn of which led past Beech Lodge and then on to their own small house. Mrs. Millicent took the other turn instinctively, but Jean, for some reason she could never explain, felt a sudden impulse to pass this time by the road they had both hitherto avoided. She stopped, and her mother glanced back with surprise.
“What is it, dear?”
“I don’t know, mother, but”﻿—she hesitated﻿—“I rather want to go this way.”
“But why?”
“I can’t tell you, really. It’s rather an odd feeling. Would you much sooner not?”

===Chinese===
小路走到一处岔口，一条岔道经过山毛榉山庄，再通往她们自己的小屋。米莉森特太太凭本能拐上了另一条，琴却出于一种她永远说不清的缘故，忽然起了一股冲动，想这一次走那条她们俩至今都在回避的路。她停下脚步，母亲诧异地回头望她。
"怎么了，亲爱的？
"我不知道，妈妈，可是——"她迟疑了一下——"我倒想走这边。
"可是为什么？
"我真说不上来。这感觉挺古怪的。你是不是宁可不走这边？

===Original===
It flashed into Mrs. Millicent’s mind that perhaps she had been unwise in allowing her own shrinking timidity to influence the girl. The only reason she had to put forward sounded a little too personal to carry much weight, and if time was healing the wound in Jean’s heart, should she not be thankful﻿—and show it?
“Very well, dear,” she said slowly. “Perhaps it is better to begin this way. I think I’d like your arm.”

===Chinese===
米莉森特太太心头忽地闪过一念：自己任凭畏缩怯懦影响了女儿，也许并不明智。她拿得出来的唯一理由，听上去又太嫌私人，压不了多少分量；倘若时间正在愈合琴心上的创口，她难道不该庆幸——并且显露出来吗？
"好吧，亲爱的，"她缓缓地说，"也许这样开始更好。我想挽着你的胳膊。

===Original===
They went on thus, with unvoiced recognition of remembered things. Came the bend in the lane beyond which lay Beech Lodge, and the older woman seemed to feel the knife in her own throat. So many times had she walked here, and so happily. The dip in the hedge, the glimpse of rolling fields patched with woodland, the belt of timber that marked the grounds of Beech Lodge, the cluster of old trees with their pale gray trunks close by the roadside; then the white gates and tiny red-roofed cottage. Her fingers tightened on the girl’s strong arm.
“My dear, my dear,” she whispered. “Just two years ago!”
Jean nodded sympathetically but did not speak. She was staring up the drive at the house with its shining windows, its clustering ivy, and the wide door, in every timber of which seemed to be a welcome.

===Chinese===
两人便这样走下去，对一件件记起的东西心照不宣。走到小路的转弯处，转过弯便是山毛榉山庄，年长的妇人仿佛觉得那把刀就插在自己喉咙里。她从前在这里走过多少回啊，又走得多么快活。树篱的低缺处，绵延起伏、缀着片片林地的田野一望可及，标出山庄地界的那一带树林，路边近处一丛苍老而树干淡灰的老树；然后是两扇白门和红瓦顶的小小屋舍。她的手指在姑娘结实的胳膊上收紧了。
"亲爱的，亲爱的，"她低声道，"就在两年前！
琴同情地点了点头，没有说话。她正顺着车道望向那宅子，望向它明亮的窗户、簇拥的常春藤和那扇宽宽的大门——门的每一根木料里，仿佛都含着一声欢迎。

===Original===
“Isn’t it strange?” she whispered. “So different, and yet so unchanged.” She paused, then went on uncertainly. “I sometimes wonder, mother, whether houses have some kind of consciousness and are aware of us who live in them. Isn’t it queer, but I feel now as though Beech Lodge was somehow glad to see us, and was wondering why we had never come before.”
Mrs. Millicent shook her head. “It’s a pretty fancy, child, but﻿—”
Jean stopped, nearly opposite the white gates. “Who’s that at the window﻿—your old room? Mother, it looks like Perkins!”
“It is Perkins. You knew she stayed on when the Thursbys left.”
“Yes, but I did not know she was still here. And yet I’m not surprised. She’s part of the house. I wonder if the Derricks like her.”
“She always had a very peculiar manner, but she was an excellent servant.”

===Chinese===
"怪不怪？"她低声说，"变得这么不同，却又一点没变。"她停了停，又迟疑不定地说了下去。"妈妈，我有时想，房子兴许也有某种知觉，也认得我们这些住过它的人。说来古怪，可我现在就觉得，山毛榉山庄仿佛见到我们很高兴，还在纳闷我们怎么一直没来。
米莉森特太太摇了摇头。"这是个美丽的遐想，孩子，可是——"
琴停下脚步，几乎正对着那两扇白门。"窗口那是谁——你从前的屋子？妈妈，看着像珀金斯（Perkins）！
"正是珀金斯。瑟斯比家（the Thursbys）搬走时她留下来当差，这你是知道的。
"知道，可我不知道她如今还在。不过我也不惊讶。她算得上这宅子的一部分了。不知德里克一家（the Derricks）喜不喜欢她。
"她那做派总是十分古怪，可她是个顶好的仆人。

===Original===
Mrs. Millicent’s voice faltered. This inspection was becoming too poignant, and she moved on. It seemed that any moment there might emerge that well-remembered figure, with the straight, familiar form and those clear, thoughtful eyes. She had turned away, her lips trembling, when Jean spoke quickly and sharply.
“Mother, who is that?”
From the climbing rosebushes that bordered the wide drive, a figure had emerged, shears in hand, a figure that halted and stared. The broad shoulders, the uncouth head, the powerful and deliberate movements of the man were unmistakable.
“Martin!” she said under her breath. “It’s Martin!”
Mrs. Millicent stopped, turned, and came unsteadily back. Then she too looked, and became weak and agitated.

===Chinese===
米莉森特太太的声音颤了。这样一路看下去太锥心了，她往前走去。那个记忆深刻的身影——那挺直熟悉的身形，那双清澈深思的眼睛——仿佛随时都会走出来。她已转过身去，嘴唇发颤，这时琴急促而尖利地开了口。
"妈妈，那是谁？"
宽阔车道两旁的攀缘蔷薇丛里，走出来一个人影，手里拿着剪枝的剪刀；那身影站住了，直望过来。宽阔的肩膀，粗笨的脑袋，那男人有力而沉稳的一举一动，错不了。
"马丁！"她压低嗓子说，"是马丁（Martin）！"
米莉森特太太站住，转过身，脚步不稳地走了回来。她也望过去，登时浑身发软，心绪激动起来。

===Original===
“It *is* Martin﻿—”
“But where can he have come from, and why come back here?”
For a moment her mother could not answer, being too shaken by this quivering recognition of one who she felt held the key to her husband’s tragic death. It was Martin who had moved with threatening domination through the nightmare of her dreams for the last two years. Now the threat was alive again. It had returned with him. Then she heard Jean. The color had fled from the girl’s cheeks, but her eyes were alight with some thrilling instinct.

===Chinese===
"真是马丁——"
"可他能是从哪儿来的？又为什么回到这儿来？"
母亲一时答不上来，认出这个人，她震颤得太厉害——她总觉得，丈夫惨死之谜的钥匙就握在此人手里。两年来，在她的梦魇里挟着骇人威势来来去去的，正是马丁。如今那威胁又活了。它是随他一起回来的。这时她听见琴说话。血色已从姑娘的双颊褪尽，她的眼睛里却燃着某种令人战栗的本能。

===Original===
“What does it mean, mother?”
“I do not know, child. Come away now, please; I must get home.”
Jean held back. Something more was stirring in her soul than Martin’s return. He had come back to strangers who probably knew nothing of him. If they did, he could not be at Beech Lodge. And Perkins was there, too, and Perkins knew all. It followed, then, that the woman had not spoken. Was it all in preparation for another tragedy? At this thought she felt frightened and choked. Someone must speak﻿—before speech was too late. She glanced again at the motionless figure. Martin was staring, too, and he also had recognized. He touched his cap, and at the curve of that arm she nearly cried out.

===Chinese===
"这是什么兆头，妈妈？"
"我不知道，孩子。走吧，求你了；我必须回家去。"
琴却止步不前。她灵魂里翻腾的，不止是马丁归来这一桩。他回到的是一些生人当中，那些人多半对他一无所知。倘若知道，他就待不在山毛榉山庄了。珀金斯也在那儿，而珀金斯什么都知道。由此可见，那女人没有开过口。这一切，莫非是在为另一场悲剧做准备？想到这里她觉得又惊又闷。总得有人开口——趁开口还不算晚。她又瞥了一眼那纹丝不动的人影。马丁也在直勾勾望着，他也认出了她们。他抬手碰了碰帽檐，见那条手臂弯起的弧度，她险些叫出声来。

===Original===
“Mother,” she whispered again, “we must tell them.”
“Tell them what, Jean? Come along. I can’t stand this.”
The girl held her ground. “We must tell the Derricks about Martin. Don’t you see it would be utterly unfair, and perhaps cowardly, if we didn’t? They’ve taken the place and, being strangers, can have known very little about it. They have probably heard about father’s death through Perkins, but perhaps not. The agent would naturally say nothing about it, and I don’t suppose the Thursbys would advertise the truth. Perkins has evidently said nothing about Martin, or the Derricks would not have engaged him. We know all, and the suspicions as to Martin, and we simply cannot be silent. Oh, we must tell them, and now!”
“If you feel so strongly I’ll write tonight,” protested her mother faintly, “but, Jean, I cannot go in now. I could not walk past that man.”

===Chinese===
"妈妈，"她又低声说，"我们必须告诉他们。
"告诉什么，琴？走吧。我受不住了。"
姑娘寸步不让。"我们必须把马丁的事告诉德里克一家。你难道不明白，不告诉就太不公平了，兴许还是怯懦？他们盘下了这地方，又是生人，对这里的事能知道的极少。父亲的死，他们多半是从珀金斯那里听来的，可也未必。经纪人自然一字不提，瑟斯比家我也不料会把真相四处张扬。珀金斯显然没提过马丁，不然德里克一家不会雇他。我们什么都知道，连对马丁的种种疑心也知道，我们万万沉默不得。唉，必须告诉他们，现在就去！"
"你既然这么执意，我今晚就写信，"母亲有气无力地分辩道，"可是琴，我现在进不去。我没法从那人身边走过去。

===Original===
The girl was unmoved. “That won’t do, mother. There are too many things one can’t put on paper. One of us must speak.”
“I cannot make myself speak now, and you can’t go in there alone.”
“Why not?”
“There’s Martin looking at you. He knows what we are talking about.”
“Perhaps he does, and if so he’s more afraid of me than I am of him. At any rate I must go. You keep on toward the village, and I’ll catch you up. If I have to wait I’ll have someone walk home with me. And please, please understand that I’m not afraid, because there’s nothing to fear. I know now why we came this way today for the first time.”

===Chinese===
姑娘不为所动。"那不行，妈妈。有太多的事是纸上写不出的。我们两个总得有一个开口。
"我如今开不了口，你也不能一个人进去。
"为什么不能？
"马丁正看着你呢。他知道我们在说什么。
"也许知道。真知道的话，他怕我还胜过我怕他。无论如何我得去。你只管朝村子那边走，我随后赶上来。要是我得等上一阵，我就找个人陪我走回去。还有，求你了，求你明白我并不害怕，因为没有什么可怕的。如今我知道了，为什么我们今天头一回走了这边。

===Original===
Mrs. Millicent sighed despairingly and turned away. There was a look on the girl’s face she could not meet, and Martin had not moved.
Jean rallied her courage, passed between the white gates, and walked firmly up the drive. Martin saw her coming and stepped back till he was half screened among his roses. His face was working. When she drew level he touched his cap the second time, and for an instant their eyes met. In hers there was a cold recognition; in his a sort of mute and restless petition. Yes, he knew why she had come and what she was about to impart to his new employer. A surge of impotent anger shot through him, and he turned silently lest he should betray it. He had not reckoned on this when in the Burmese jungle there reached him the first of those discomforting promptings that finally brought him halfway round the world, he knew not why. Jean did not look back. Her eyes were fixed on the too familiar door. It opened almost at once, and she met the changeless look of Perkins. Now she could speak, but the sight of the hall, its rugs and pictures, all as though she had never left them, was nearly too much. They were as unchanged as Perkins herself. Suddenly she felt like an intruder or a thief and wanted to leave. At that she remembered Martin.

===Chinese===
米莉森特太太绝望地叹了口气，转身走了。姑娘脸上有一种她不敢迎对的神色，而马丁一直没挪动。
琴鼓起勇气，从两扇白门中间穿过，坚定地走上车道。马丁望见她来，退后一步，让蔷薇半掩住身子。他的脸抽动着。她走到与他并排时，他第二次碰了碰帽檐，两人的目光刹那一碰。她眼中是冷淡的相认；他眼中是一种无声而不安的恳求。是的，他知道她为什么来，知道她要向他新东家透露的是什么。一阵无可发泄的怒火从他心里窜起，他默默转过身去，唯恐流露出来。当年在缅甸的丛林里，头一阵搅得他不安的催促传到他那里——那些催促终于引着他绕了半个世界，他自己也不知为何——那时他没有料到会有今日这一出。琴没有回头。她的眼睛盯在那扇熟得不能再熟的门上。门几乎立刻开了，她迎上珀金斯那一成不变的目光。这时她能开口了，可门厅里的景象——那些地毯和画，一切仿佛她从未离开过——几乎让她承受不住。它们同珀金斯本人一样纹丝未改。忽然她觉得自己像个不速之客，像个窃贼，只想离开。想到这里，她记起了马丁。

===Original===
“Good afternoon, Perkins. Is Mrs. Derrick in?”
“There is no Mrs. Derrick, miss. It’s Mr. Derrick’s sister who is here.”
“Oh, is she in, then?”
“No, miss, but Mr. Derrick is here.”

===Chinese===
"午安，珀金斯。德里克太太（Mrs. Derrick）在家吗？
"没有德里克太太，小姐。在这里的是德里克先生的妹妹。
"哦，那她在家吗？
"不在，小姐，不过德里克先生在家。

===Original===
“Then I’d like to see him for a moment.”
“Will you wait in the living-room, miss? Mr. Derrick is working in the study.”
“Thanks, I’ll wait here.”

===Chinese===
"那我想见见他，就一小会儿。
"您在客厅等好吗，小姐？德里克先生正在书房里用功。
"谢谢，我就在这儿等。

===Original===
Perkins tapped at the study door.
“Miss Millicent, sir.”
Derrick put down his pen. “Miss Millicent,” he repeated puzzled.
“She is waiting in the hall and would like to see you. She asked for Miss Derrick first, but Miss Derrick is out.”

===Chinese===
珀金斯轻叩书房的门。
"米莉森特小姐（Miss Millicent），先生。"
德里克（Derrick）放下笔。"米莉森特小姐，"他困惑地重复了一遍。
"她在门厅里等着，想见您。她先找的德里克小姐（Miss Derrick），可德里克小姐出去了。

===Original===
He got up, his pulse beating hard, and came quickly into the hall. They glanced at each other, these two, drawn thus together by the shadow of a crime. Instinctively she held out her hand, feeling for a strange moment almost as though no introduction was necessary.
“How do you do, Miss Millicent? My sister will be very sorry to miss you. Will you come into the living-room or”﻿—he hesitated an instant﻿—“the study?”
“I won’t keep you a moment,” she said a little nervously. “Are you working in the study?”
He nodded, smiling. “I think it’s a wonderful room. Please come in.”

===Chinese===
他站起身，脉搏重重地跳着，快步走进门厅。这两个人彼此对望了一眼，被一桩罪行的阴影这样牵到了一处。她本能地伸出手，有那么奇异的一刻，几乎觉得根本无须引见。
"您好，米莉森特小姐。我妹妹没见着您会非常遗憾的。您去客厅坐，还是——"他迟疑了一瞬——"去书房？
"我只耽搁您一小会儿，"她略显紧张地说，"您正在书房里用功吗？
他微笑着点了点头。"我觉得那是间了不起的屋子。请进。

===Original===
He followed her in, while Perkins, after a lingering glance, closed the door. Jean took a big chair by the fireplace, and for a moment neither spoke. Then she saw the manuscript littering the desk.
“I’m so afraid I’ve interrupted you.”
He shook his head ruefully. “What I was writing, or trying to write, is all the better for being interrupted. And,” he added, “we have been hoping to meet you and your mother.”

===Chinese===
他随她进去，珀金斯留恋地望了一眼，掩上了门。琴在壁炉边一张大椅子里坐下，有一会儿两人都没说话。随后她看见了摊得满桌的手稿。
"真怕我打搅了您。
他懊恼地摇了摇头。"我方才在写的，或者想写的那些东西，正巴不得被打搅呢。再说，"他补了一句，"我们一直盼着见到您和您母亲。

===Original===
Again their eyes met. Derrick noted the smooth oval of her face and the sensitive curve of her lips. Her expression suggested imagination, a mind at once alert and subjective. She was looking now at her father’s portrait, and he saw the resemblance between these two. And, try as he might, he could not guess her thoughts or what brought her there. But something whispered that a Millicent was again in Beech Lodge.
“I did not know I was coming here today,” she said gravely, “not till mother and I came past the gates. Then I knew.”
It was all so strange, and yet so utterly real, that Derrick did not answer at once. Here was Millicent’s daughter in Millicent’s study. That to begin with. And there was about the girl a nameless aura she had brought with her that made the ordinary preliminaries of acquaintance seem pointless and out of place. He did not feel that he had always known her, but that somewhere and somehow they possessed something in common.

===Chinese===
两人的目光又碰在了一处。德里克注意到她那光洁的鹅蛋脸和双唇敏感的曲线。她的神情透着想象力，透出一副既敏锐又主观的头脑。她此刻正望着她父亲的画像，他看出了这两人之间的相像。而他无论如何猜测，也猜不透她的心思，猜不透是什么引她前来。可有什么在耳边低语：山毛榉山庄里又有了米莉森特家的人。
"我今天原本不知道要来这儿，"她郑重地说，"直到妈妈和我走过那两扇门才知道。那时我就知道了。
这一切如此离奇，却又如此真切，德里克没有立刻作答。米莉森特的女儿，坐在米莉森特的书房里。这是头一件。而这姑娘周身带着一种无以名状的氛围，使得寻常那套结识的客套显得多余而格格不入。他并不觉得自己仿佛早就认识她，只觉得他们在某个地方、以某种方式，共有着一样什么。

===Original===
“Please tell me,” he said quietly.
“Yes, if I may begin by asking questions.”
“It will be very kind of you.”
“Then, did you know about Beech Lodge when you took it?”
“No; that is, if I understand what you mean. I was looking for a quiet place to work in, found this, and fell in love with it. I went straight to the agent in London and made an offer. He telephoned to Mr. Thursby, and the offer was accepted so quickly that it surprised me﻿—and here we are.”

===Chinese===
"请告诉我。"他平静地说。
"好，若能容我先提几个问题。
"那就太感谢您了。
"那么，您盘下山毛榉山庄的时候，知道它的底细吗？
"不知道；或者说，得看您指什么。我当时正寻一处安静的写作之所，找到了这里，一下子就爱上了它。我径直去伦敦找经纪人开了价。他打电话给瑟斯比先生（Mr. Thursby），价码那么快就被应承下来，倒叫我吃了一惊——我们就这样住进来了。

===Original===
“It was Perkins who showed you over the house?”
“Yes, she was alone here, and in charge.”
“And the rest?” She glanced at him as though counting on his intuition.
“I discovered that after we moved in.”
“I’m so sorry,” she said involuntarily.

===Chinese===
"领您看房子的，是珀金斯？
"是的，她一个人在这儿，照管着一切。
"其余的呢？"她瞥了他一眼，仿佛指望他的直觉。
"那是我们搬进来之后才发现的。
"我很难过。"她不由自主地说。

===Original===
“But why, Miss Millicent?”
“Because I’m sure you would not﻿—”
She stopped abruptly. A whisper came to her that she was saying things of which she was not quite sure. What if Beech Lodge had imparted the edge of its secret, the secret of which she had long been conscious, to its new tenant? His face was that of one who might be able to receive such things.
“You were going to tell me that if I had known what happened here two years ago I would not have taken the house.”
She nodded thankfully. Yes, he did understand.

===Chinese===
"可是为什么呢，米莉森特小姐？
"因为我敢肯定您本来不会——"
她突兀地住了口。一声低语到她耳边来：她正在说的这些，她自己也没有十分把握。倘若山毛榉山庄已把它秘密的一线锋芒——那个她早就有知觉的秘密——传给了它的新住户呢？他的脸，正是那种接得住这类事情的人的脸。
"您方才要说的是：倘若我知道两年前这里出过的事，我就不会要这宅子了。
她感激地点了点头。是的，他果然明白。

===Original===
“Then may I say that I think I realize what it must have meant to you to come in here for that purpose? And, Miss Millicent, while I did not know at the time, I do know now, and regret nothing.”
“Nothing?” she murmured.
He shook his head. “Nothing. Shall I go on?”
She nodded again and, lifting her eyes, took a long straight look at her father’s portrait. Perhaps he was here now, and knew, and was in a way glad she had come. She noted, too, with a sort of thankfulness that Derrick did not sit at the desk.

===Chinese===
"那么请容我说一句：为了那个目的走进这间屋子，对您必定意味着什么，我想我是体会得到的。还有，米莉森特小姐，当初我虽不知情，如今是知道了的，而且毫无悔意。
"毫无？"她低声道。
他摇了摇头。"毫无。我往下说？"
她又点了点头，抬起眼睛，久久直望着她父亲的画像。也许他此刻就在这里，都知道了，还在某种程度上为她到来而高兴。她还带着几分感激注意到，德里克没有坐在那张书桌前。

===Original===
“When I came first,” he continued, “I saw Perkins. She gave me a strange impression, but it was not altogether discomforting. I took the house without consulting my sister, being attracted to it in a way that I only began to understand by degrees. I actually felt what had happened here before being told about it. That isn’t the sort of thing one can explain, but﻿—”
“It doesn’t need explanation,” she put in.
He sent her a quick, searching glance. “It helps to have you say that. Well, after we moved in, the thing, or perhaps it was the influence, grew stronger﻿—I can’t express it in any other way﻿—till presently I was sure we were meant to come. I got some details from Perkins, but they were incomplete; I was convinced that I must wait for more﻿—which would certainly be furnished from some source.” He paused, reflected for a moment, and went on rapidly. “Does it seem impertinent for me, an utter stranger, to be so interested and allow myself to be drawn into something which is not my affair? If it does, I can only assure you that it is not curiosity, or,” he added thoughtfully, “the result of anything I have done or said.”

===Chinese===
"我头一回来的时候，"他接着说，"见到了珀金斯。她给了我一种奇怪的印象，但并不全然令人不安。我没有同妹妹商量就定下了这宅子；我被它吸引的那种方式，只是到后来才渐渐开始明白。人家还没告诉我，我就实实在在觉出了这里出过的事。这种事没法解释，可是——"
"这事不需要解释。"她插嘴道。
他飞快而锐利地看了她一眼。"听您这样说，我心里踏实了些。是这样，我们搬进来之后，那东西——也许该说那股影响力——越来越强，我没法用别的话表达——不久我就确信，我们是注定要来的。我从珀金斯那里得到一些细节，但不齐全；我深信必须等来更多的——而那更多的，必定会从某个来处送到。"他停了停，寻思片刻，又急急说了下去。"我这样一个十足的外人，这般上心，还由着自己被牵进一件与我无干的事里，您看是否有些唐突？若是唐突，我只能向您担保，这并非出于好奇，也不是"——他若有所思地补了一句——"我做过什么、说过什么的结果。

===Original===
“It is impossible to think that.”
“I’m glad you see it that way, because it brings me to Martin. Is it on account of Martin you were kind enough to come in?”
“Yes.”
“Then, some day, if you or Mrs. Millicent will tell me, I’d like to hear more about him; but meantime please be assured that Martin’s being here is all part of the rest of it. I knew what was said and thought about him when I took him on. He told me why he happened to come back at this particular time.”

===Chinese===
"谁也不会那样想。
"您能这样看我很高兴，因为这就说到了马丁。您肯赏光进来，正是为了马丁吧？
"是的。
"那么，日后您或者米莉森特太太若是愿意讲给我听，关于他我还想多知道一些；只是眼下请尽管放心，马丁在这里，同其余的一切是一体相连的。雇下他的时候，人家怎么说他、怎么想他，我都知道。他为什么偏偏在这个当口回来，也告诉过我。

===Original===
“Why was it?” asked Jean swiftly.
“He *had* to come. Telling you that seems to explain a good many other things one can’t very well put into words. I know now that Perkins had to stay, that I had to take this house, that you had to pass this way for the first time in many months; and I know, too, that the gathering is not yet quite complete. It is all utterly intangible; there is no one point on which one can put a finger and say the reason lies there; and one of the most remarkable things is that we can meet for the first time and talk like this. It is something more than fate; it is purpose.”

===Chinese===
"为什么？"琴急问。
"他非回来不可。这一句告诉了您，许多别的没法形诸言语的事，仿佛就都解释了。如今我知道，珀金斯非留下不可，我非租下这宅子不可，您非在许多个月里头一回走这条路不可；我还知道，这聚拢还没有全然完成。这一切全然无形无迹，没有哪一点能让人指住说：缘故就在那里；而最不可思议的一桩是，我们素昧平生，竟能这样交谈。这比命运更进一层；这是旨意。

===Original===
She looked at him wonderingly. The room, with its poignant memories, was speaking to her now, its ancient walls vibrant with mystical messages. Here was the sounding-box of the unknown, where in times past she had thrilled to mysterious whispers. Here her father had sat﻿—himself even, with all his love, something of a mystery﻿—and here at the end his life had been snatched from him. What reason was there to assume that evil and danger had passed away? And till it did pass the tale could not be complete.
“I am not going to try and thank you,” she said slowly, “for having made my coming here so much easier than it promised to be, but when I saw Martin I knew what I had to do. Mother was with me, but she could not face it and has gone on to the village. Martin looked at me as I came in and knows why I came. He must know that.”

===Chinese===
她惊叹地望着他。这间满贮锥心往事的屋子，此刻正在对她说话，古老的墙壁因神秘的讯息而震颤。这里是那未知的共鸣箱，从前她曾在这里为神秘的低语而颤栗。她父亲曾在这里坐过——他自己，纵有满腔的爱，也多少是个谜——而最后，他的性命就在这里被人夺走。凭什么假定邪恶与危险已经过去了呢？它们一日不过去，这故事便一日不得完全。
"我不打算道谢，"她缓缓地说，"谢您把我这趟进门变得远比预想的容易；只是我一见到马丁，就知道自己该做什么了。妈妈同我一起来的，可她受不住，先往村子去了。我进来时马丁望着我，知道我为何而来。这一点他必定知道。

===Original===
“Would you and your mother feel more comfortable if I sent Martin away?”
“No, you must not do that. We are in no danger from him. I mean you must not do it on our account. But there’s your sister and yourself to think of.”
He shook his head. “I am convinced that this need not trouble you. The police know of the new arrangement, and Martin knows that they know. No danger of the sort you mean lies there. I want to leave Martin to his roses and Perkins to her housework till something I cannot describe is reestablished. Beech Lodge seems to be waiting for that. Perkins and Martin are also waiting, though unconsciously. I am certainly waiting. And, Miss Millicent, I think that without knowing it you have been waiting, too.”
“Yes,” she whispered, “it’s the only thing.”

===Chinese===
"要是我把马丁打发走，您和您母亲会安心些吗？
"不，千万别那样做。他不会加害我们。我是说，不要为了我们打发他。倒是您妹妹和您自己，该想一想。
他摇了摇头。"我确信这一点不必让您操心。警察知道新的人事安排，马丁也知道他们知道。您说的那种危险，不在其中。我要让马丁守着他的蔷薇，珀金斯守着她的家务，直到一样我说不出的东西重新立起来。山毛榉山庄仿佛正在等它。珀金斯和马丁也在等，虽然他们自己不觉得。我自然也在等。还有，米莉森特小姐，我想您也在等，只是您自己不知道。
"是的，"她低声道，"只有这一条路。

===Original===
“Then, may I ask something that’s rather difficult to ask? I wouldn’t unless I believed that you too felt something that’s very difficult to express.”
“Please﻿—what is it?”
“You hold with me that we are all surrounded by influences we do not understand, and in so far as we are able to interpret them the difficult things become less threatening?”
“One cannot escape that,” she said slowly.

===Chinese===
"那么，我能问一件颇为难开口的事吗？若不是相信您也感到了某种难以言传的东西，我不会问。
"请问——是什么？
"您是否与我同见：我们人人都被种种不理解的影响力包围着，而我们越是能解读它们，艰难的事就越不那么逼人？
"这一点谁也逃不脱。"她缓缓地说。

===Original===
“I thought as much. But there are some who fight against such powers, and, believing them to be all for evil, are frightened, they know not why. If they are not frightened, they scout them. But since these powers are both for good and evil, and I believe those for good must be the strongest, it is only right to admit that the beneficent and invisible influences are always fighting for readjustments of some kind and will conquer in the end. If this were not the case, what advantage could there be in life? You believe all this?”
“I must believe it.”
“Well, my sister does not; she says she’s too practical, and I do not argue the point. Unless one can accept it, there’s no room for anything but restlessness and probably fear. So what I’d like to suggest, if I may, is that you do not say anything of all this to﻿—to anyone who does﻿—not see this as we do.”
“You mean my mother?” she said quickly.

===Chinese===
"我也这么想。可是有些人同这类力量相抗，认定它们全是恶的，于是害怕起来，自己也不知道为什么；若是不害怕，便对它们嗤之以鼻。但这些力量既兼着善恶两性，而我相信向善的那些必定最强，那么理当承认：那些冥冥行善的影响力，永远在为这样那样的重整而战，并且终将得胜。倘若不是这样，人生还有什么可取？这些您都信？
"我不能不信。
"唉，我妹妹就不信；她说自己太讲实际，这一点我不同她争。人若接受不了这个，心里便装不下别的，只有惶惶不安，多半还有恐惧。所以若蒙容许，我想提议：这一切您不要讲给——讲给任何不像——不像我们这样看的人听。
"您是指我妈妈？"她赶紧说。

===Original===
He nodded. “You told me she could not face coming in here, but you came, and that explained much.”
“Mother would not understand,” she admitted, “and I think you’re very wise. But is there nothing else I can do?”
“Yes, if you will, a little later on, tell me some of the things I would like to know. May I bring my sister to see you?”
“Please do; we should be very glad.”
She said goodbye. The ordeal she had dreaded was over and concluded in a fashion she never anticipated. It was all strange﻿—and yet not strange. She was persuaded that this interview had been dominated by something her father had left behind, in order that it might fight for what Derrick called readjustment. And in that she was ready to aid to the utmost. There was no room for fear now. She declined Derrick’s offer to walk home with her and went thoughtfully back with a new sense of being fortified in things that for years past had stirred secretly in her soul.

===Chinese===
他点了点头。"您告诉我她不敢进来；可您进来了，这就说明了许多。
"妈妈是不会明白的，"她承认道，"我想您非常明智。可还有别的我能做的吗？
"有。您若愿意，往后找个时候，把一些我想知道的事讲给我听。我可以带我妹妹来见您吗？
"请一定来；我们会非常高兴。
她道了别。她先前惧怕的那场煎熬过去了，收场的方式是她从未料到的。这一切都离奇——却又并不离奇。她相信，这场会面始终被父亲身后留下的一样什么主宰着，好让它为德里克所说的那种重整去作战。而在这件事上，她愿意竭尽全力相助。此刻再没有恐惧的余地了。她谢绝了德里克送她回家的好意，若有所思地往回走去，带着一种新的感觉：多年来在她灵魂深处悄悄鼓动着的那些东西，如今得了增固。

---
===Original===
Derrick sat in the study late that night, with no pretense at work. Beech Lodge had dipped into utter silence, and the fire was low. His mind was full of the visitor of the afternoon, whose coming had lent a new significance to his surroundings. Now he perceived more clearly what it must have cost her to come. He was conscious of her communicable courage, the charm of her youth, and above all of the fact that to her also something had whispered from the infinite. How vivid she was, how understanding!
He wondered, too, what impression she carried away. Had he said too much, or too little? In talking, as he had done, to the daughter of a murdered man while she sat in her father’s study beneath her father’s portrait, in taking on himself the office of avenger﻿—had he not already gone too far and too fast? Could Jean Millicent have done otherwise than approve while she must have been still struggling with profound and reawakened emotions? Had he been stilted and self-assured and pedantic? Had he assumed too much? These questions harassed him.
Against it he put the girl’s coming. She had not known what manner of person she would find but, braving the revival of her own loss, had determined to do what she could to save others from any tragic experience. This thought grew in his mind till, in turn, he recognized a new element in this strange affair. He had desired to answer if he could the voiceless petitions of the dead man, but now, in addition, he felt a wave of protection for those whom Millicent had left behind. It was this, he realized, that had animated him during his talk with Jean Millicent. And she had promised to help. He got up restlessly, lowered the lamp, and, moving to the French window, stared out at the moon-smitten lawn. How often must Millicent, who was so close tonight, have stared like this? Perhaps it was on such a night that the evil thing came, strong and merciless. But whence and how?

===Chinese===
那天深夜，德里克还坐在书房里，连用功的样子也不装。山毛榉山庄已沉入全然的寂静，炉火塌得低低的。他满脑子都是下午那位来客，她的到来给他四周的一切添了新的意义。此刻他更清楚地看出，走这一趟对她是多大的付出。他觉出她那股能感染人的勇气，觉出她青春的动人，而最要紧的是这个事实：对她，也同样有什么从无穷深处低语过。她多么鲜活，多么善解人意！
他还琢磨着她带走了怎样的印象。他说得太多了，还是太少了？同一个是被害者的女儿说话——她坐在父亲的书房里，坐在父亲的画像下——而他又把复仇者的职分揽上自身，他是不是走得太远、太快了？琴·米莉森特当时必定还在同深重而重被唤醒的情感挣扎，她除了认可，还能有别的做法吗？他是不是显得做作、自以为是、迂腐？是不是自作主张得过了头？这些问题折磨着他。
而在另一头，他摆上的是姑娘的来访。她并不知道等着她的会是怎样一个人，却甘冒着丧亲之痛的重新翻起，打定主意尽己所能，别叫旁人再遭悲剧。这个念头在他心里越长越大，直到反过来，他又认出这桩奇事里的一个新因素。他原本渴望的，是若能做到，便回应死者那无声的祈愿；可如今除此之外，又添了一阵护卫米莉森特身后之人的心意。他明白了，方才同琴·米莉森特交谈时，给他注入生气的正是这个。而她已答应相助。他心神不定地站起身，把灯捻低，走到落地窗前，凝望外面月光泼洒的草坪。今夜如此切近的米莉森特，从前该有多少次也这样凝望？那邪恶之物到来的那一夜，也许正是这样一个夜晚，强悍而无情。可它从何处来，又是怎样来的？

===Original===
It was in the midst of a space of profound silence that he heard the faintest click at the door. He started at that, for his sister had been long in bed, and Perkins’s room was in the far corner of the house. What moved in Beech Lodge now? The door was opening, so slowly that it was almost imperceptible. His hair began to prickle. Was this the evil thing, and what did it seek?

===Chinese===
正是在一段深不可测的寂静之中，他听见了门口最轻的一声咔嗒。他为之一惊：妹妹早上了床，珀金斯的屋子又在宅子最远的一角。此刻是什么在山毛榉山庄里走动？门正开着，慢得几乎察觉不出。他的头发根根发乍。这就是那个邪恶的东西吗？它在寻什么？

===Original===
He stood, breathless and motionless, his pulse hammering, till through the widening crack projected a hand, followed by a long arm and white-clad shoulder. The fingers were empty and extended as though feeling blindly. Then a face, pallid as of the dead. It was Perkins!
She glided forward without sound or speech, a wraith, a spirit of the night, so unreal, so remote as to be divested of human attributes, the thin hand still held out, exploring and testing the half-light that filtered through the silent chamber. It was the hand rather than the body that had life, with consciousness in its quivering fingertips. She was only partly dressed and wore a loose white wrapper that accentuated the tall straightness of her figure. Her black hair hung in two thick ropes over her shoulders; her feet were bare; and her face was that of one who sees unspeakable things. The eyes were wide open, and in their glassy stare was a strange hunger and a great question.
She came on like an uncaptured spirit, feeling delicately along the paneled wall, a creature of body and flesh, but directed by some mysterious influence beyond human ken. She did not look toward the window but paused for a moment to survey the portrait with an unearthly and profound recognition. From this she turned to the desk, leaning over it, her dangling ropes of hair rendered semi-luminous against the lamp, peering, peering, till at length the long, questing fingers found what they sought, and poised, quivering above the stain.
Now she swayed, leaning ever a little more forward, till at last her head drooped, her arms stretched out, and her lips touched that darkened patch where they rested in a mute and desperate caress.

===Chinese===
他屏着呼吸一动不动地站着，脉搏重重地敲打，直到渐渐撑宽的门缝里探进一只手，随后是一条长臂和一个着白衣的肩头。那手指空着，直直伸出，仿佛在盲然探路。然后是一张脸，苍白得像死人的脸。是珀金斯！
她无声无息、一言不发地向前滑来，一个幽灵，一个夜的精魂，虚幻、渺远得被剥去了人的一切属性，那只瘦手依旧伸着，探查、试探那渗过寂静内室的半明半暗。有生的与其说是身子，不如说是那只手，知觉就在它颤动的指尖里。她衣衫不全，穿一件宽松的白色晨袍，愈发显出身形的高挑笔直。黑发像两条粗绳垂在肩上；双脚赤着；那张脸，是一张看见了不可名状之物的脸。眼睛睁得极大，呆滞的凝视里有一种奇异的饥渴和一个巨大的疑问。
她像一个未受收服的魂灵继续前行，沿着嵌板的墙壁细细抚过——一具血肉之躯，却受着某种人类悟性之外的神秘力量的指引。她不看窗户那边，只停了一停，以一种超凡而深邃的相认端详那幅画像。随后她转向书桌，俯下身去，垂挂的绳状头发映着灯光，泛出半透的亮；她看啊，看啊，终于，那长长的、一路探寻的手指寻着了它们要找的东西，颤颤地悬停在那块污渍上方。
这时她晃了起来，身子越俯越低，终于头垂了下去，双臂摊开，嘴唇触到那一小片暗色的斑迹，停在那里，成了一个无声而绝望的爱抚。

===Original===
“Master,” she pleaded, “master, where are you now? Why did you go; why are you not here where you used to be? The evil waits still, and all is empty and cold and dead without you, all dead, all dead!”

===Chinese===
"主人，"她哀告着，"主人，你如今在哪里？你为什么走了；你为什么不在你从前的老地方？那邪恶还在等，没有你，一切全是空、是冷、是死，全死了，全死了！

===Original===
The voice ceased like a wail in the night, drowned in silence. Her lips pressed close to the stain till they seemed to infuse into it the message of her own blood, while the blind fingers groped and groped for that they could not find. Then with a sigh that hung tremulous in the throbbing air she moved to the portrait, made a slow, despairing gesture of farewell, and glided back to the door and out of sight.
Derrick, rooted where he stood, thrilled to a new light that began to flicker in his brain. The fabric of his imagination was becoming more substantial.
