He had seen the soul of a woman stripped of all disguise, and heard a voice that was robbed of all powers of concealment. The essential meaning of this danced before his mind’s eye.

===Chinese===
那嗓音像夜里一声哀号，止息了，沉没在寂静里。她的嘴唇紧贴在污渍上，仿佛要把自己血脉里的讯息灌注进去，而那盲然的手指摸了又摸，摸索着它们寻不见的那样东西。随后她叹了一声，那叹息颤颤地悬在悸动的空气里；她移步走到画像前，缓缓做了一个绝望的诀别姿势，又滑回门口，消失不见了。
德里克钉在原地，为一道开始在他脑中闪烁的新光而震颤。他想象的织物正变得越来越厚实。他看见了一个女人的灵魂剥尽了一切伪装，听见了一个被夺去全部遮掩之力的嗓音。这件事的根本含义，在他的心眼前翩然起舞。

## II Perkins / 第二章 珀金斯

===Original===
Miss Derrick left the room, and Perkins stood motionless as though she welcomed its silence. Her eyes took on a strange expression as she scanned this apartment, with every least detail of which she was utterly familiar. The paneling ran nearly to the ceiling, and was topped by a narrow shelf. The west wall was dominated by the fireplace, and in the corner, placed at a slight angle from the wall itself, was the big desk. Sitting there, one looked not out through the French window, but almost directly at the door from the main hall. The desk was already littered with Derrick’s manuscript, and toward it Perkins moved as in a dream.

She put one thin hand on the smooth leather surface, then bent over the massive frame, searching, it seemed, in the manner of one who hopes she may not find. Her attitude suggested that she had done this many times before, and always with the same result; but it did not affect the swift and silent touch with which she fingered the heavy mahogany corners and deep, carved molding of its intricate design. Presently she shook her head with a sort of patient resolution and turned on the portrait a look of extraordinary inquiry, as though Millicent’s eyes, peering from the pigment, could have directed her﻿—if they only would. The picture might have been alive, so keen was her regard, so expectant of an answer.

Evening had drawn on, and the study became peopled with soft mysterious shadows in which she stood like a priestess before some half-veiled shrine. She made no movement toward the lamp but in the gloom progressed without a sound from point to point, with here and there a lingering touch to furniture and woodwork. These intimate caresses blended her the more completely with all that surrounded her till she was merged and absorbed into the bodily human presentment of wood and stone. Finally she came directly under the portrait, bent her head in an attitude of profound thought, and remained quite motionless. She was standing thus when the front hall door opened and Derrick’s whistle sounded cheerily outside.

===Chinese===
德里克小姐（Miss Derrick）走出屋去，珀金斯（Perkins）一动不动地站着，仿佛正受用这屋子的寂静。她扫视这间屋子时，眼睛里浮出一种奇异的表情——这里最细微的一处一角，她都熟得不能再熟。护墙板几乎直抵天花板，顶上是一道窄窄的搁板。西墙以壁炉为主，墙角里，同墙面略略斜错着，摆着那张大书桌。坐在那里，望出去的不是落地窗，而几乎是正对着通门厅的那扇门。桌上已经摊满德里克（Derrick）的手稿，珀金斯朝它走去，恍如梦中。

她把一只瘦手放在光滑的皮面上，随即俯向那厚重的桌身，摸索着，那神情，像是一个巴不得什么也寻不着的人。她的姿态表明，这样的摸索她做过许多回，回回都是同一个结果；可这并不减损她那轻捷无声的抚触——她细细抚过沉重的红木包角，抚过繁复纹样上深深雕刻的线脚。片刻之后，她带着一种耐心的决断摇了摇头，把一束异乎寻常的探询投向那幅画像，仿佛米莉森特（Millicent）从油彩间窥望出来的眼睛，只要肯，原可以指点她似的。她的目光那么恳切，那么盼着一个回答，那张画简直像是活的。

夜色渐深，书房里住满了柔软而神秘的暗影，她立在其中，像立在半掩神龛前的女祭司。她不动那盏灯，只在幽暗里无声地从一个去处挪到另一个去处，时而对家具和木作流连地抚上一抚。这些亲昵的爱抚，把她同四周的一切交融得愈发彻底，直到她被吸纳进这木与石的人形显身里去。最后她径直走到画像底下，深深垂着头，一副沉思的姿态，便全然不动了。她正这样站着，门厅的大门开了，外面响起德里克欢快的口哨声。

===Original===
At that the maid smiled to herself with sudden pleasure, crossed the room swiftly, and became occupied with the tea-tray. Derrick entered. He did not see her at first and started at a slight rattle of china.

“Jove, Perkins, you made me jump! I thought you were part of the room.”

She did not answer. He sent her a quick searching glance, stood by the mantel, and, taking out his pipe, watched her silently. How amazingly she fitted into everything! No, he could not imagine Beech Lodge without this woman.

===Chinese===
一听见这声音，女仆便蓦地暗自欢喜一笑，快步穿过屋子，张罗起茶盘来。德里克走了进来。他起初没有看见她，瓷器轻轻一响，唬了他一跳。

"天哪，珀金斯，吓了我一跳！我还当你是这屋子的一部分呢。

她没有答话。他飞快而锐利地看了她一眼，站到壁炉旁，掏出烟斗，默默望着她。她同这里的一切契合得多么惊人！是啊，没有这个女人的山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge），他是没法想象的。

===Original===
“You will want to work now, sir?”

He nodded. “Yes, I think I will;” then, suddenly, “I say, how did you know I wanted to work?”

She gave a queer, twisted smile, the first he had seen on that ageless face﻿—a strange and almost grotesquely communicable look, with which she stepped at once from the role of servant and became a sort of administrator of something yet to be explained. But there was no lack of respect in her manner.

“I thought perhaps you might, sir.”

She took out the tray and, returning in a moment, adjusted the heavy curtains over the French window. He watched her light the desk-lamp and turn it low, feeling rested and soothed by every deft and noiseless movement. His senses were comforted by the indescribable certainty of her touch, which gave him an extraordinary feeling of confidence﻿—in something. And Perkins must know what this was. Presently he went to the desk and fingered his manuscript. It struck him that what he had already written was a little unreal and undirected. It didn’t go deep enough.

===Chinese===
"先生这就要用功了？

他点了点头。"是啊，我想是用功的时候了；"随即又忽然说，"我说，你怎么知道我要用功？

她咧出一个古怪而扭曲的微笑，那是他头一回在这张不见年纪的脸上看见的——一种古怪的、几乎怪诞得叫人心领神会的神情；凭着这一笑，她顿时走出了仆人的身份，变成了一件尚未说明之事的某种主事人。只是她的举止并不失恭敬。

"我琢磨着先生兴许要呢。

她端走茶盘，一会儿回来，把落地窗上厚重的帘子掩好。他看着她点亮书桌上的台灯，把光拧低，她每一个轻巧无声的动作都让他觉得安适熨帖。她那触手之间难以言传的笃定，抚慰着他的感官，给了他一种异乎寻常的信心——对某样东西的信心。而这东西是什么，珀金斯必定知道。随后他走到书桌前，翻了翻自己的手稿。他忽然觉得，已经写下的那些有点虚浮，有点无的放矢。写得不够深。

===Original===
“Shall I make up the fire, sir?”

“No, thank you. It’s not worth while till after dinner. But I’d like the lamp higher.”

She came slowly toward him. “Have you really seen this room by firelight, sir?”

===Chinese===
"我把火生旺些好吗，先生？

"不必了，谢谢。晚饭之前犯不上。不过，劳驾把灯拧高一点。

她缓缓朝他走来。"先生，您当真在炉火的光里看过这间屋子吗？

===Original===
He looked at her curiously and instantly pictured this ancient chamber with warm shadows flickering over its mellow casements. Depth and warmth; that’s what it would be, had always been. He knew this much.

“Perhaps you might make up the fire after all. Good suggestion!”

She obeyed, and he watched the effect﻿—more fascinating than he had imagined. The study took on a new and ghostly beauty. Its dancing shadows were populous with fantasy that died and was born while he stared. There were tenants of the past here that no change of ownership could ever displace; reminders of spoken things that had drifted from vanished lips; echoes of songs whose lilt had never become silent. It had ceased to be a room. It was a palace of dream and vision. And in the background stood Perkins.

===Chinese===
他好奇地望着她，眼前顿时浮现出这间古老的屋子：暖影在温润的窗棂上摇曳。深沉与温暖；那必是它的本相，一向就是。这一点他是知道的。

"那你还是把火生起来吧。好提议！

她照办了，他望着那效果——比他想象的还要迷人。书房焕发出一种新异而幽冥的美。跳动的暗影里栖满幻想，在他凝望之际生生灭灭。这里有往昔的住户，换多少任主人也撵它们不走；有从消逝的唇间飘散过的话语的余绪；有那轻快曲调至今未曾沉寂的歌的回声。它已不再是一间屋子。它是一座梦与幻影的宫殿。而在这一切的背景里，立着珀金斯。

===Original===
“By George!” he said under his breath.

“I thought you’d like it, sir.”

She was half invisible, and he started violently. “It’s wonderful, but I expected that.”

“Yes, it’s strange how one can tell.”

===Chinese===
"老天！"他压低嗓子说。

"我就知道先生会喜欢的。

她半隐在暗处，他猛地一惊。"真神了，不过我早料到了。

"是啊，人竟能这样料事，真是奇怪。

===Original===
He glanced at her, as though he had known her all his life. “There is something about this room, and I felt it the first time I came in. How old is it?”

“It has no age, sir.”

Derrick did not seem surprised. “I thought you’d say that.” He paused; then as though resuming some previous talk, “Who else has felt it?”

“Only Mr. Millicent since I came here, and his daughter. It was different with Mrs. Millicent, and she was frightened.”

===Chinese===
他望了她一眼，仿佛已同她相识了一辈子。"这屋子有些什么，我头一回进来就觉出来了。它有多少年纪了？

"它没有年纪，先生。

德里克似乎并不惊讶。"我就知道您会这么说。"他顿了顿，随后像接着一场早已开始的谈话似的，"还有谁觉出来过？

"打我到这儿来，只有米莉森特先生（Mr. Millicent），还有他的女儿。米莉森特太太（Mrs. Millicent）跟他们不一样，她是吓着了。

===Original===
“I think I understand that, too. Was this his favorite room?”

“Yes, that is his desk. I think that at the end he was frightened as well.”

“And you found him. How was that?”

She made an indefinite gesture. “They sent for me.”

===Chinese===
"这个我想我也明白。这间是他最喜欢的屋子吗？

"是，那张就是他的书桌。我想到了最后，他也害怕了。

"还有，人是你发现的。那是怎么一回事？

她做了个含混的手势。"是他们打发人来叫我的。

===Original===
Again he felt nothing of surprise. “Yes, because they had seen and knew. But why did you stay here after it happened?”

Perkins took one long, uncertain breath. “I did go away for a week, but I couldn’t stay. It was all silent in London where I went. Then I knew that it﻿—that they would not let me remain away, so I had to come back.” She gazed round this well-remembered room and seemed to signal that she acknowledged its potency.

Derrick looked at the littered desk and into the mask-like face. Her eyes were alight now, and not those of a lonely woman. She was, as it were, surrounded by friends. He wondered if they would ever be his friends.

===Chinese===
他又是一点也不觉得意外。"是啊，因为他们见过，也知道。可那件事之后，你为什么还留在这儿？

珀金斯长长地、犹疑不定地吸了一口气。"我是走开过一个礼拜，可我待不住。我去的伦敦，那儿一片死寂。后来我知道了，它——他们不肯让我留在远处，我只好回来。"她环视这间铭刻于心的屋子，仿佛示意：她认它的威力。

德里克看看摊满纸页的书桌，又看看那张面具似的脸。此刻她眼里有光，那不是孤独妇人的眼睛。她仿佛被一群朋友环绕着。他不知道，那些朋友会不会有朝一日也变成他的朋友。

===Original===
“Do you mind talking like this? I think I understand, but most people wouldn’t.”

“It makes me happier. For two years there have been no living words about it. I could never find anyone who understood at all since it happened, and Miss Millicent would not speak.” She hesitated, and sent him the faintest smile. “For the last two days the house has been amused.”

“How?” he demanded. Beech Lodge seemed to be stirring about him, and with slow palpitations of a monstrous life, throbbing in one vast pulse on which Perkins kept a cool, knowledgeable finger. It moved and breathed.

“It was at the men who came to take the inventory. They were such children; though one of them, and he was quite old, guessed at something in a general way. The other could never hear or see anything.”

===Chinese===
"这样说话，你不介意吧？我想我明白，可大多数人不会明白。

"这样倒叫我快活些。两年来，关于那件事，再没有一句活人的话。自打出事，我从来找不着一个稍微懂一点的人，米莉森特小姐（Miss Millicent）又不肯开口。"她迟疑了一下，朝他送来一丝极淡的微笑。"这两天，这宅子一直乐着呢。

"怎么讲？"他追问。山毛榉山庄仿佛正在他四周蠢蠢而动，以一种庞然生命的缓慢搏动，合在一下浩大的脉息里震颤，而珀金斯把一只冷静而内行的手指搭在这脉息上。它动着，呼吸着。

"是冲着那两位清点员来的。他们简直像小孩子；虽说其中一个——年纪挺大的那个——隐隐约约猜着了点什么。另一个么，是什么也听不见、看不见的。

===Original===
He nodded and, turning, caught a yellow flicker that touched the portrait into a strange similitude of life. Millicent’s eyes were speaking now, strange things to which he had no key. But only for a little while. The key was not far away. There came over Derrick the profound conviction that this was all arranged. It belonged to the cycle of appointed things. The stage was all set. If he could but keep his ears tuned to the elusive vibrations that permeated this solitary dwelling, he might decipher its mystery. And Perkins was part of it.

“Is that like Mr. Millicent?”

She nodded, with no surprise that he should know whose portrait it was. “Yes, and there was something about him very like you, sir. Not in appearance, but the other thing. He once told me that he began to hear and understand a little while he was a child. They commenced to talk before he left his first school. I’m glad, sir, that Miss Derrick does not understand.”

===Chinese===
他点了点头，转过身时，正撞见一抹黄色的微光拂过画像，把它点染得奇异地近似活人。米莉森特的眼睛此刻正在说话，说些他没有钥匙开启的怪事。不过这不会长久。钥匙就在不远的地方。德里克心头涌起一个深深的信念：这一切都是安排好的。它属于命定之事的轮回。戏台早已布好。只要他能把耳朵调准到那弥漫在这座孤宅里的难以捉摸的震颤，他或许就能解读它的秘密。而珀金斯正是其中的一部分。

"那张画像，像米莉森特先生本人吗？

她点了点头，对他认得画上是谁毫不诧异。"像，而且他身上有一样东西跟先生很相像。不是模样，是另一样。他有一回告诉我，他还是个孩子的时候，就开始听得见、懂得一点了。他离开第一所学校之前，他们就开始同他说话了。我很高兴，先生，德里克小姐是不懂这些的。

===Original===
“How do you know that?”

“Because she told me not to be lonely, as if one could. She thinks I’m a little mad, and that’s why I’m willing to stay here and not ask high wages.”

He did not answer, beginning now to perceive why he had been led to this isolated spot. Millicent stared down at him, and he was persuaded that from the picture proceeded a thin appeal for help﻿—or was it for revenge?﻿—Millicent whose life had been so suddenly snuffed out﻿—Millicent who had been afraid before he died. Afraid of what?

“You’re not afraid too, sir, are you? It’s no use if you are.”

===Chinese===
"这个你怎么知道的？

"因为她叮嘱我别孤单，说得人好像真能不孤单似的。她觉得我有点疯癫——所以才当真信了，我甘愿守在这儿，不讨高工钱。

他没有作声，此刻才开始明白，自己何以被引到这个僻远的地方来。米莉森特居高临下地凝视着他，他相信从那画框里正发出一缕细弱的求助之声——抑或是求复仇？——米莉森特，他的性命被那样猝然掐灭——米莉森特，死前曾满怀恐惧。怕的是什么呢？

"先生您不怕吧，嗯？您要是也怕，那就没有用了。

===Original===
He shook his head, scanning thoughtfully the books, the prints, the dull paneling, and heavy oaken floor.

“You believe,” he said slowly, “that all this has sucked in year after year something from mortality, something that is never quite lost, till, in time, wood and stone and paper become something much more than this, and radiate back to us, if we can only catch it, the wisdom and courage and love and evil they have so long absorbed. You believe all this, Perkins?”

Her eyes opened wide, filling with a strange light. She was no longer an impassive, middle-aged woman, the servant of the house, but a creature vibrant with feeling, as one who has unleashed her soul. Her lips moved inaudibly, as at some mystic shrine.

“Wisdom and courage and love and evil,” she repeated in an awed whisper. “Yes, yes, that’s it, all of it. Last time it was evil in Beech Lodge. The evil had been here for months and years, growing stronger and stronger. It began when Mr. Millicent got back from the East, and it never stopped. I tried to silence it but failed, and then it silenced him. The evil was too strong.”

===Chinese===
他摇了摇头，若有所思地扫视那些书、那些版画、暗沉的护墙板和厚重的橡木地板。

"你是不是相信，"他缓缓地说，"这一切年复一年地吮进了人身上的某样东西——一样永不全然消散的东西——直到有一天，木、石和纸页变成远不止这些的什么，再把它们久已吸收的智慧、勇气、爱与邪恶朝我们辐射回来，只要我们接得住。这些你都信吗，珀金斯？

她的眼睛睁大了，盛满一种奇异的光。她不再是那个无动于衷的中年妇人、这宅子的仆人，而是一个为情感所震颤的生灵，像刚刚放开自己灵魂的人。她的嘴唇无声地翕动着，如同置身某座神秘的神龛之前。

"智慧、勇气、爱与邪恶，"她敬畏地低声重复道。"是的，是的，正是这个，全都是。上一回，山毛榉山庄里是邪恶。那邪恶在这儿待了几个月、几年，越养越强。米莉森特先生从东方回来时它开始的，从此没有停过。我想叫它住口，可失败了，后来它叫他住了口。那邪恶太强了。

===Original===
“But it’s over now,” said Derrick steadily.

“No, it’s here yet, in this room,” she pointed to the portrait. “He knows. He’s been trying to tell me but cannot.”

“From whom does it come?”

“Wait, sir; you’re not ready yet. Nothing is quite ready, but it will be soon. That’s why you came. The others will come, too.”

===Chinese===
"可它如今过去了。"德里克沉着地说。

"不，它还在呢，就在这间屋里，"她指着那幅画像。"他知道。他一直想把话告诉我，可告诉不出。

"它从谁那儿来？

"等等，先生；您还没有备好。什么都还没有全然备好，可就快了。您就是为这个来的。其余的人也会来。

===Original===
He experienced a remarkable sensation of having lost all physical weight, and seemed to catch a low singing note as of a myriad of tiny voices, the far murmur of those who approached from the unknown. He could see Perkins, still motionless, and feel his own body, but this had no significance. As the wireless operator tunes his set till it abstracts from the invisible only that which is carried by its own individual wavelength and remains unaffected by all others, so Derrick began to pick up a series of vibrations that in a queer and remote fashion he recognized, but could not as yet interpret. Then he caught his own tones.

“So this air is full of that which can never die or disappear, and may save or destroy as it is written. It destroyed Millicent and may be the undoing of others unless it is brought to naught.”

“How else could it be?” Perkins covered her pale face, bent her head, and disappeared.

===Chinese===
他体验到一种奇异的感觉：仿佛失去了全部身体的重量，又仿佛捉到一个低低的吟唱之音，像千万个细小的嗓音齐作，是那些从未知处走近者的遥远的呢喃。他看得见珀金斯，她仍一动不动，也感觉得到自己的身体，可这些都无所谓了。正如无线电报务员调他的机器，直到从无形之中单单摘出由它自己的波长载着的那一个，对其余一切无动于衷，德里克也开始接收到一连串震颤，他以一种古怪而渺远的方式认得它们，却还不能解读。随后，他听见自己开了口。

"这么说，这空气里满是不会死、不会消散的东西，或救人，或毁人，全看是怎么写定的。它毁了米莉森特，若不把它消解掉，也许还要毁掉别人。

"不这样又能怎样呢？"珀金斯掩住苍白的脸，垂下头去，退出去不见了。

===Original===
Derrick stared at the portrait, his features transfigured with something that was not altogether wonder. It was all unreal yet enormously real. What surprised him most was that he should be admitted so readily to this “no man’s land” where mystery, like a cloaked figure, moved among the shadows of tragedy. How much was here? How much of it was his own fancy? Who was the real Millicent, the man within the man who had been afraid before he died? How and why did Millicent die? Did evil take on an embodiment and, emerging like an apparition from the unknown, butcher him where he sat? Derrick pictured him, shrinking back into his chair with starting eyes while something moved closer, closer. And then﻿—

A knock sounded at the door.

“If you please, sir, the inventory men would like to come in for a moment.” The impassive mask had fallen over her face again.

===Chinese===
德里克凝视着画像，面容因一种并非全是惊异的什么而变了样。这一切都不真切，却又真切无比。最教他吃惊的是，他竟这样轻易地被准许踏入这片「无人之境」——在那里，神秘像一个披斗篷的身影，在悲剧的暗影间穿行。这里有多少东西？其中多少又是他自己的幻想？真正的米莉森特是谁——那个人里头的人，那个死前满怀恐惧的人？米莉森特是怎样死的，又为什么死？邪恶可是取得了肉身，像幽灵从未知中现身，在他坐着的地方把他屠戮了？德里克想象着他缩回椅中、双目暴突的样子，而某样东西越逼越近，越逼越近。然后——

门上响起一记叩门声。

"对不起，先生，清点员们想进来待一小会儿。"那副无动于衷的面具又罩回了她脸上。

===Original===
“Eh! I thought they had finished.” He spoke jerkily, aware that the study had suddenly become void and silent. “All right, they may come.”

A shuffle of footsteps in the hall, and Mr. Jarrad entered deferentially, hat in hand. He was followed by Dawkins. The younger man looked amused, and a trifle superior.

“I beg pardon for disturbing you like this, sir, but on looking over our notes I find that my colleague has omitted to make an entry concerning this desk.”

“Anything the matter with the desk?” asked Derrick curiously.

===Chinese===
"咦！我以为他们已经完事了。"他说得断断续续，觉出书房已骤然空落而沉寂。"好吧，请他们进来。

门厅里一阵拖沓的脚步声，贾拉德先生（Mr. Jarrad）恭恭敬敬地走了进来，手里拿着帽子。后面跟着道金斯（Dawkins）。年轻的那位一脸觉得有趣的神气，还带着几分自矜。

"这样打搅您，实在过意不去，先生。只是复查记录的时候，我发现我的同事漏登了一笔关于这张书桌的条目。

"书桌有什么问题吗？"德里克好奇地问。

===Original===
“No, sir, it’s merely the point of its physical condition, which would naturally affect any possible question of dilapidations. When I examined it I noticed a large stain on the leather, quite faint and dull. It’s the sort of thing one generally finds on desks of this character, especially when there happen to be young people in the family. I did not detect it till for some reason I made a second inspection. Now it seems that either I did not mention this for record or, if I did, my colleague failed to make the entry. So with your permission I’ll show it to him.”

Derrick felt no surprise. “Certainly,” he said mechanically. “Do you need more light?”

Mr. Jarrad shook his head, advanced to the desk, reverentially moved a sheaf of manuscript, put on his glasses, and bent low over the glossy surface. Dawkins stood at his elbow looking openly incredulous.

“I can’t see anything, just the same,” said the latter, “and a stain is a stain.”

===Chinese===
"没有，先生，不过是它保存状况的一点问题，这自然牵涉到房屋损耗方面可能有的争议。我查验的时候，注意到皮面上有一大块污渍，很淡，发暗。这一路的桌子上常见这类东西，尤其是家里恰好有年轻人的时候。我也是不知怎么又查了第二遍，才看出来的。如今看来，要么是我当时没有报出来记录在案，要么我报了，同事漏登了这笔。所以想请您俯允，我指给他看看。

德里克并不觉得奇怪。"当然可以。"他机械地说。"需要多点灯吗？

贾拉德先生摇了摇头，走到书桌前，毕恭毕敬地挪开一叠手稿，戴上眼镜，俯身细看那光亮的皮面。道金斯站在他肘边，一脸公然的怀疑。

"我还是什么也看不见，"后者说，"污渍就是污渍呗。

===Original===
Mr. Jarrad shifted the lamp and peered hard. “Curious,” he murmured to himself. “How very curious! I could have sworn that﻿—ah﻿—there, my friend,” he nodded with satisfaction, “you can see it now. It seems a little more difficult to place than the last time, but there it is, and quite large.” He ran a thin finger over an irregular outline. “In a certain light it might be almost invisible. Very faint, I admit, but surely your young eyes are as sharp as my glasses?”

Dawkins scrutinized, nodded, mumbled an apology, and made an entry in the large book. Mr. Jarrad turned to Derrick.

“That’s what I referred to, sir, and it’s not my habit to overlook small things. The foundation of a sound inventory business is system plus what might be called perception.”

===Chinese===
贾拉德先生把灯挪了挪，使劲眯眼细看。"奇怪，"他喃喃自语，"真是奇怪极了！我本可以起誓说——啊——喏，我的朋友，"他满意地点点头，"你现在看得见了。似乎比上回难找准些，可它就在那儿，还挺大。"他伸出瘦手指，沿一处不规则的轮廓抚过。"在某种光线下，它几乎可以看不见。很淡，我承认，可你那双年轻的眼睛，总该同我的眼镜一样尖吧？

道金斯细细看了看，点点头，含糊地道了声歉，在那本厚大的清点册里记了一笔。贾拉德先生转向德里克。

"我说的就是这个，先生，小东西从我眼皮底下溜走，不是我的习惯。一家健全的清点行当，根基是条理，外加一样或许可以称为感知力的东西。

===Original===
“Perception?”

“Yes, sir. It involves a certain amount of sensitiveness, strange as that may sound, and the ability to perceive and record what is usually, in fact one might almost say always, missed by the casual observer. It’s not altogether a matter of training, either, but of instinct. Possibly there’s not one man in a hundred who would have spotted that; and if I were fanciful, sir, I would hazard the opinion that the desk was trying to hide it, which is of course absurd. In fact, though I see that you yourself have been sitting here, I am sure you did not observe it. Thank you, sir, and good night! We’ll be of no further trouble now.”

This oration being delivered in his very best manner, and the dignity of his profession thus established, Mr. Jarrad retired. When the steps died out, Derrick looked for himself. Close under the lamp he discerned a shadowy blotch of irregular shape, a rough pool with a tone a shade darker than the leather. It had apparently been subjected to hard rubbing. It was a discoloration of no particular hue, but as he gazed he knew without doubt that it had been made two years previously by the lifeblood of Henry Millicent.

===Chinese===
"感知力？

"是的，先生。它需要相当分量的敏感——听上去也许奇怪——还需要能察觉并记录下那些漫不经心的观察者通常、事实上几乎总是漏掉的东西。这也不全然是训练的事，而是本能的事。一百个人里未必有一个能看出那个来；倘若我富于想象，先生，我就要斗胆进一言：那张书桌正想把它藏起来——这当然荒唐。实际上，尽管我看得出您本人方才就一直坐在这儿，我敢肯定您并没有注意到它。谢谢您，先生，晚安！我们这就不再叨扰了。

这番演说以其最拿手的派头发表完毕，他这一行的体面也由此确立，贾拉德先生便告退了。脚步声消歇之后，德里克亲自去看。凑近灯下，他辨出一块形状不规则的暗影渍斑，一汪轮廓毛糙的渍迹，颜色比皮面深一层。它显然被狠狠擦搓过。那是一块说不出什么颜色的变色痕，可他凝望之下，已毫不怀疑地知道：两年之前，它是由亨利·米莉森特（Henry Millicent）的性命之血浸成的。

## IX The Escape / 第九章 脱逃

===Original===
It had been a cold night, and frost still sparkled on the dank grass when Derrick neared the Millicents’. He had spent sleepless hours picturing this meeting, recounting all there was to be said, and casting about as to how the story might be put so as to revive as little as possible the poignant memories of two years ago. It was a strange mission that carried him now to his girl, but she greeted him with a calm suggesting that she was not altogether unprepared. Mrs. Millicent, unmistakably agitated, pressed his hand with a nervous tremor.

“You have more news for us, Mr. Derrick? Jean has told me what you told her yesterday. It is all utterly puzzling, and I wish I could help, but I can’t.”

Derrick nodded sympathetically. There was no such fiber of courage here as had been transmitted to her daughter. She was gentle and patient, and her heart centered on Jean, but she was not the woman to grasp a situation like the present one. He wondered how much Millicent had taken her into his confidence, how much she actually comprehended of the real man who sometimes seemed to look out of those painted eyes, then concluded that this could only have been fractional. She might have soothed his secret fears, but she could never understand them. Her mind was too ordered, her horizon too defined. She loved as a mother, and mourned as a wife. That was her existence. There could be no object gained in probing this gentle breast.

But, with Jean, Derrick knew it was different. Hers were eyes that saw, and a brain that pierced beyond the obvious. She had her mother’s charm but her father’s imagination. Derrick knew, and it fortified him to know it, that she could follow, pace by pace, wherever he led, and that her vision might even be keener than his. She, like himself, responded to whispers from the unknown and was also undismayed. So when he told his story it was to her rather than her mother that the tale was recited.

Both listened in rapt attention, Mrs. Millicent in sheer wonder, Jean with a keen and fascinated absorption. When he came to the finding of the kris, the older woman shivered, but Jean, her eyes cloudy with thought, did not stir. When he concluded, he felt that while Mrs. Millicent’s heart was lacerated afresh, Jean was possessed of more profound and vital emotions. And it was she who spoke first.

===Chinese===
夜里天冷，德里克（Derrick）走近米莉森特家时，湿漉漉的草上还闪着霜光。他好几个钟头未曾合眼，一直在心里预演这次会面，把该说的话从头到尾默想了一遍，又反复斟酌这故事该怎样讲，才能尽量少搅起两年前那些锥心的旧忆。此刻把他送到心上人面前的，是一桩奇怪的使命；她迎着他，神色安详，看来并非全无准备。米莉森特太太（Mrs. Millicent）分明心绪激荡，握他的手时指尖带着紧张的颤抖。

"您又给我们带来消息了，德里克先生？琴（Jean）已经把您昨天对她说的都告诉我了。这一切实在叫人全然摸不着头脑，我但愿能帮上忙，可我帮不了。

德里克同情地点了点头。传给她女儿的那一股勇气，在她身上是找不到的。她温婉而隐忍，一颗心全系在琴身上，可她不是那种能把握住眼下这种局面的女人。他暗自纳罕，米莉森特生前到底向她吐露过几分心事；那个真正的男人——有时仿佛正从画像上那双眼睛里往外望着人的那个男人——她实际懂得多少。转而断定：所懂的必定只是些零星碎片。她也许抚慰过他暗藏的恐惧，却永远理解不了那些恐惧。她的头脑太井然，眼界太分明。她以母亲的身份去爱，以妻子的身份去哀悼，这便是她的全部生涯。在这样一颗温柔的心里探问，探不出什么结果来。

可是德里克知道，对琴就全然不同了。她那是一双看得见东西的眼睛，一副穿透得了表象的头脑。她有母亲的动人，更有父亲的想象力。德里克知道——知道了这一点，他心里也更有了底气——无论他领到哪一步，她都能一步步跟上，而她的眼力兴许比他的还要锐利。她同他一样，听得见来自未知的低语，也同样毫不畏怯。所以他讲起这段经历时，与其说是讲给她母亲，不如说是讲给她听的。

两人都听得入神：米莉森特太太是全然的惊愕，琴则是敏锐而痴迷的专注。讲到发现那把克里斯短剑（kris）时，年长的妇人打了个寒噤；琴却双眸蒙着沉思，纹丝未动。他讲完之后，觉出米莉森特太太的心又给撕开了一道新口子，琴心里占住的，却是更深沉、更紧要的情感。先开口的也正是她。

===Original===
“It is very strange that the peddler should tell you something I meant to tell you but forgot.”

“Yes?”

“It’s about the study. You remember, mother, how it always was?”

“Yes, dear.”

“The desk stood in the other corner, not where it is now, so that father looked out of the window. The sofa was between the fireplace and the window, and the screen between the door into the hall and the desk. Did the peddler seem to know that?”

“He did not say so but appeared to notice that things were changed. I asked Perkins about it then, and she told me what you have.”

===Chinese===
"真怪，货郎竟会对您说起一件我本想告诉您、却忘了说的事。

"哦？"

"是书房的事。妈妈，您还记得它原先一直是副什么样子吗？

"记得，亲爱的。

"书桌原先摆在另一个墙角，不在如今这个位置，好让父亲坐望窗外。沙发在壁炉和窗户之间，屏风立在通门厅的门和书桌之间。货郎像是知道这些吗？

"他没有明说，只是像察觉出了东西挪动过。我当时为这事问过珀金斯（Perkins），她告诉我的，正与您说的一样。

===Original===
“Don’t you think that in spite of what you found at the cottage he was really the guilty man?”

“But why?”

“For one thing, he might easily have had that﻿—that weapon in his pack without you seeing it, and﻿—”

She broke off, and stared at the bangle on her wrist, slowly drew it off, and handed it to Derrick.

“Please, I can’t wear it now.”

He nodded understandingly, pinched at the twisted metal which was shaped oddly like a serpent, and put it in his pocket. Jean breathed a little faster.

===Chinese===
"尽管您在那座小屋里有了那番发现，您不觉得他其实正是那有罪的人吗？

"可是为什么呢？"

"一来，那件——那件凶器，他尽可以搁在货担里不叫您瞧见，而且——

她顿住了话头，盯着腕上的手镯，缓缓褪了下来，递给德里克。

"求您收下，我如今戴不得它了。

他会意地点了点头，捏了捏那绞扭的金属——那形状古怪地像一条蛇——把它放进了口袋。琴的呼吸微微急促起来。

===Original===
“And, apart from that,” she went on, “doesn’t he seem to you to have been the superior intelligence? Your description of him is not that of an ordinary man, and he seems to have very nearly mesmerized those who were there, including the sergeant. Don’t you see that perhaps Martin and Perkins are, or were, only tools in his hands, and he represented to them some power they had to obey without question. One could then understand the look you say was on Martin’s face when the man died, and,” she added, “it would also explain Perkins acting as she did after dinner.”

“But Perkins was shaken beyond words.”

“Yes, because it meant that though the peddler was dead, the power behind him still operated.”

===Chinese===
"再说，撇开这一层不谈，"她接着说，"您不觉得，他正是那个更高一筹的智囊吗？您对他的描述，不是一个寻常人的描述；当时在场的人，连巡佐在内，似乎几乎都被他施了催眠术。您难道看不出，马丁（Martin）和珀金斯也许只是、或者当初只是他手里的工具，而在他们眼里，他代表着一种不得不无条件服从的力量？这样，您说那人死时马丁脸上的那种神色，便可以理解了；还有，"她补了一句，"这也能解释珀金斯晚饭后的那番举动。

"可是珀金斯当时惊骇得说不出话来。

"是的，因为这意味着：货郎虽死，他背后那股力量仍在运作。

===Original===
Mrs. Millicent got up unsteadily. “Jean, dear, I’ll have to leave you to talk the rest of this over alone. I’m sorry, darling, but﻿—but﻿—”

She went out hurriedly, and the girl was silent for a moment.

“Please don’t be upset about mother, and really it’s much better.” She put her hand impulsively on his. “Do you begin to see what it has meant to carry the mystery and the terror alone? She could not help me, and I’m glad for her sake.” She looked in his eyes with such utter confidence and appeal that he thought his heart would break.

“Oh, my dear, my dear,” he whispered, “you don’t know yet how well I understand. It will take all my life to show you.”

Jean turned pale, and from her parted lips came a little sigh of content that, faint as it was, penetrated his very soul. Then she breathed quickly, smiling at him as though she thanked him for a perfect understanding, and for knowing her spirit so well that he could afford not to say more.

===Chinese===
米莉森特太太身子发晃地站起身。"琴，亲爱的，余下的话你们俩单独去谈吧，我得走开。对不起，宝贝，可是——可是——"

她急匆匆走了出去，姑娘沉默了片刻。

"请别为妈妈难过，这样实在好得多。"她情不自禁把手搭在他手上。"您可开始明白了，独自一人担着这份神秘与恐怖，是什么滋味？她帮不了我，我倒替她高兴。"她望进他的眼睛，那里面是全然的信任和恳求，他只觉得心都要碎了。

"唉，我亲爱的，我亲爱的，"他低声道，"你还不知道我懂得有多深。要用我一生来向你表明。

琴的脸白了，微启的唇间逸出一声满足的轻叹；那叹息虽轻，却直透进他的灵魂深处。随后她急促地吸了口气，朝他微笑，仿佛在感谢他这份完满的懂得，感谢他把她的心灵体察得如此透彻，以至不必再多说一个字。

===Original===
“Is it not possible,” she continued quietly﻿—“and of course it is possible; we both realize it﻿—that Martin was unconsciously guilty? I mean that not till after it had happened did he realize what had taken place. If Blunt could dominate him yesterday, why not then?”

“Stranger things have happened,” he admitted.

“Well, if that’s the case it also explains Martin’s helplessness and Perkins’s silence. She knows that Martin did it while under this influence, while they both know that, now Blunt is dead, the influence cannot be proved. It would sound like a fairy-story in court.”

He nodded gravely. “All that may be. Does anything else occur to you in this connection?”

===Chinese===
"有没有这样的可能，"她平静地接着说——"当然可能，这一点我们俩都心里有数——马丁当时是在无知无觉中犯的罪？我是说，直到事情发生之后，他才明白出了什么事。倘若布伦特（Blunt）昨天都能支配他，当初为什么不能？

"比这更离奇的事也是有的。"他承认道。

"那么，倘若是这样，这也一并解释了马丁的束手无策与珀金斯的缄默。她知道马丁是在那股影响力之下动的手，而他们俩都知道，布伦特如今一死，这影响力便无从证明了。拿到法庭上去说，会像一篇童话。

他郑重地点头。"这些都有可能。在这件事上，你还想得起别的什么吗？

===Original===
“Nothing about the others at the moment, but Blunt sticks in my mind. You say he was partly Oriental?”

“He had native blood. I’m sure of that.”

“Then he was probably occult. Father was, but I have never told mother that. And death might not mean much to him, as after death he would expect his soul to live on in some other body. The poison he took must have been almost instantaneous, and﻿—”

She looked up suddenly. The big figure of Sergeant Burke was coming rapidly up the narrow brick walk that led to the porch. Hat off, he mopped at his red brow. A bicycle stood against the gate.

===Chinese===
"旁人么，眼下倒没有；布伦特却在我心里盘桓不去。您说过，他身上有一部分东方人的血统？

"他身上有土著的血。这一点我敢肯定。

"那么他多半通晓秘术。父亲就通，这话我从未对母亲说过。再说，死对他未必有多要紧，因为死后他还指望自己的灵魂活在另一副躯壳里。他吞下的那毒药，必定几乎是一霎致命的，而且——"

她猛地抬头。伯克巡佐（Sergeant Burke）那魁伟的身形，正沿着通往门廊的窄窄砖道快步而来。他摘了帽子，抹着通红的额头。门边靠着一辆自行车。

===Original===
“He seems very upset. Perhaps you’d better speak to him, Jack.”

She used the word before she knew it and bit her lip. Derrick hesitated a moment, sent her a brilliant smile, and went out. The sergeant’s bulk filled the doorway, and he breathed fast.

“I’m glad to find you, sir. Went to the Lodge first, and Miss Derrick told me you were here.” He gulped in more air. “A very extraordinary thing has happened.”

“What’s that?”

“Blunt, sir, has escaped!”

===Chinese===
"他像是心慌意乱得很。也许还是您去应一应的好，杰克（Jack）。

话出了口她才察觉用了这个称呼，不由咬住嘴唇。德里克迟疑了一瞬，回她一个灿烂的微笑，走了出去。巡佐庞大的身躯堵满了门洞，喘得急促。

"可算找着您了，先生。我先去了山庄，德里克小姐（Miss Derrick）说您在这儿。"他又猛吸了一大口气。"出了一件非常了不得的事。

"什么事？"

"布伦特，先生，他脱逃了！

===Original===
Derrick frowned a little. If this was a joke, it was a poor one; if not, the man was mad.

“I don’t follow you.”

“It’s just as I say, sir. He’s got away.”

“A dead man! Who took him?”

“Damn it, Mr. Derrick, don’t you understand English? He’s not dead﻿—he never was,” exploded Burke chaotically; “he’s come to life again, and escaped.”

===Chinese===
德里克微微皱眉。这若是个玩笑，未免拙劣；若不是，这人便是疯了。

"我没听懂你的话。"

"就是我说的这样，先生。他跑掉了。

"一个死人！谁把他弄走的？"

"见鬼，德里克先生，您听不懂英国话吗？他没有死——他压根儿就没死过，"伯克语无伦次地炸开了，"他又活转过来，跑了。

===Original===
Derrick blinked. It was ridiculous, absurd, and yet﻿—Burke’s face was so red, his eyes so strained, the whole great body of him labored under such excitement, that his earnestness could not be doubted.

“Will you please tell me exactly what has happened?” he said with slow and almost painful distinctness.

“I will. The body was taken to the jail at the same time as Martin, and I sent for Dr. Henry, but he was away at Eversleigh on some serious case. I put it in an empty room used as a morgue at the other end of the building from Martin’s cell. I examined it before I turned in. It was just the same, but colder, with the hands quite stiff, the face a sort of blue gray, and no pulse. A little after midnight I got to bed, knowing that Dr. Henry would come to me as soon as he arrived. He was out all night and didn’t get back till time for breakfast, after which he went straight to the station. I had been back for three hours then, saw Martin, who was all right, but didn’t go into the morgue. When I took Dr. Henry there it was empty﻿—and that’s all.”

Burke concluded this remarkable statement with an eloquent and helpless gesture, looking at Derrick with a sort of faint hopefulness that perhaps the thing was not quite as baffling as it sounded. He was grimly conscious that the Millicent case was reopened, but not in the manner and with the prospects that a few days ago were so comforting. His dreams of promotion had vanished. Why promote a man to escape from whom it was only necessary to feign death? But all the signs of death had been there. This and much more had jockeyed through his brain as he pumped savagely up the long hill from Bamberley village. His attitude now invited his amateur adviser to suggest the next move if he could. The story would be all over England in a day or two. And Burke hated to think of that.

===Chinese===
德里克眨了眨眼。这事荒唐、悖谬，然而——伯克的脸那么红，眼睛绷得那么紧，整副庞大的身躯都激动得直喘，他的诚恳无可怀疑。

"能不能请你把事情的经过原原本本告诉我？"他说得很慢，一字一板，几乎有些费力。

"我这就说。尸体是同马丁一道押回警局的，我派人去请亨利医生（Dr. Henry），他偏上埃弗斯利（Eversleigh）治一个重病人去了。我把尸体安放在一间充作停尸房的空屋里，在楼另一头，离马丁的囚室远远的。就寝前我验看了一遍。还是老样子，只是更凉，双手僵直，脸色青灰，脉息全无。半夜过后一点我才上床，心想亨利医生一到就会来找我。他外出一整夜，到早饭时分才回来，随后径直去了警局。那时我回局已有三个钟头，去看过马丁，他好好的，只是没进停尸房。等我领亨利医生过去，那里头空了——就是这么回事。

伯克说完这份非同小可的陈述，做了个既生动又无可奈何的手势，望着德里克，眼里带着一丝微弱的指望：这事也许并不像听上去那样无从下手。他冷峻地意识到，米莉森特一案重又开了局，却不是几天前那种宽人心怀的方式与前景。他升迁的美梦化作了泡影。一个只消装死便能从他手里脱逃的人，还提拔他做什么？可当时，死亡的种种征候原是样样俱在的。这一桩，还有别的许多，在他狠蹬着车、从班伯利（Bamberley）村爬那道长坡时，一路在他脑子里横冲直撞。他此刻的神态，分明是在请这位业余高参指一指下一步的棋——若指得出来的话。这桩故事不出一两天就会传遍英格兰，而这种事，伯克连想都不愿想。

===Original===
“You’ve heard of cases of suspended animation?” asked Derrick after a long pause.

“Yes, but I’ve never seen one before.”

“Nor I, but they’re not uncommon in the East. It’s evident that Blunt is master of most of those tricks, but so far as my knowledge goes the suspension is generally for much longer than a few hours. This, no doubt, is the effect of what he put in his mouth when Peters caught him.”

“That’s as I see it, but it doesn’t help matters.”

===Chinese===
半晌，德里克问："你听说过假死（suspended animation）的案例吗？"

"听说过，可从前一桩也没见过。

"我也没见过，可这类事在东方并不罕见。显然，布伦特是这一路把戏的大行家；不过据我所知，那种假死一般持续的时间，远比几个钟头要长。这一回，无疑是他被彼得斯（Peters）逮住那一刻塞进嘴里的东西的功效。

"我也是这么看的，可这无补于事。

===Original===
“What does Martin say?”

“Nothing; but I’m sure he knows.”

“Why?”

“There’s something in his face this morning, but I can’t read it. I’ve an idea that Blunt must have seen and spoken to him on his way out.”

Derrick whistled softly. “That’s more than possible.”

===Chinese===
"马丁怎么说？"

"什么也不说；可我敢肯定他知道。

"为什么？"

"今天早上他脸上有种什么，可我读不出来。我有个想法：布伦特出去的路上，必定见着了他，还同他说过话。

德里克轻轻打了个呼哨。"这不止是有可能。

===Original===
“The point is,” went on Burke, with a desperation he took no pains to conceal, “that if there’s anything to be done, it’s got to be done quickly. If by tonight we can fasten on something that will prove Martin’s guilt, the matter of Blunt’s escape won’t be quite so serious. If not, I doubt whether the discovery of that knife will actually convict him so long as Perkins sticks to the evidence she gave two years ago. That’s how the matter stands now.”

“I’d like to think a little before saying anything. Are you going back to the station?”

Burke nodded.

“Well, I’ll be there in, say, an hour and a half.”

===Chinese===
"要紧的是，"伯克接着说，那股破釜沉舟的劲儿他也懒得遮掩，"若还有什么可做，就得赶紧行动。倘若到今晚我们能坐实一样足以证明马丁有罪的东西，布伦特脱逃这一节就算不上太严重。不然的话，只要珀金斯咬定她两年前给出的证词，我很怀疑查出那把克里斯短剑，是否真能给他定罪。眼下的局面就是这样。

"我想先想一想再表态。你这就回警局去？"

伯克点了点头。

"好，我一个半钟头后到。

===Original===
The sergeant hesitated. “I might as well tell you, sir, that I’ve already gone a good deal beyond my official limits in the matter, but I’m ready to go further, which means risking my job, if you can see any light. I’ll wait for you at the station.”

He moved off with no spring in his walk, swung a thick leg over his wheel, and disappeared.

Derrick went back to Jean and by the tenseness of her face knew at once that she had heard everything. They looked at each other for a moment without speaking.

===Chinese===
巡佐迟疑了一下。"索性告诉您吧，先生，这案子我已经大大越出了职分的界限，可只要您能看出一线光，我还打算再往前走——那就等于拿我这份差事去冒险。我在警局等您。

他举步走开，脚步里没有一点弹力，一条粗腿划过车轮跨上去，便不见了。

德里克回到琴那里，一看她脸上绷紧的神情，立刻明白她全听见了。两人对望了片刻，谁也没有说话。

===Original===
“Well,” he said slowly, “isn’t it extraordinary?”

“No,” she answered under her breath, “not so extraordinary.”

“Why?”

“It’s all part of the rest of it. Do you remember what I said about some power operating behind?”

“Yes.”

“Well, it just means that you are dealing with things that can’t be explained by any reason or argument or logic, and Sergeant Burke hasn’t the right kind of experience for this. He’s fighting against things he can’t see. He’s hoping now that Martin or Perkins will break down and tell everything. They won’t.”

===Chinese===
"怎么样，"他缓缓地说，"够离奇了吧？"

"不，"她压低声音答道，"没有那么离奇。"

"为什么？"

"这同其余的一切本是一体相连。您还记得我说过的话吗——某种力量正在背后运作？"

"记得。"

"这就是说，您打交道的这些事情，任何道理、论辩、逻辑都解释不得，而伯克巡佐没有应付这一路事情的阅历。他是在同看不见的东西交手。他如今指望马丁或者珀金斯撑不住、把一切倒出来。他们不会的。

===Original===
“How do you know that?”

“I can’t explain, though I’m sure of it. Does anything suggest itself to you?”

“To be done now?”

“Yes.”

He shook his head. “Burke’s proposal seems to be all there is left.”

===Chinese===
"这你怎么知道？"

"我说不明白，可我确信不疑。你心里可有什么主意冒出来了？"

"眼下就着手做的？"

"是的。"

他摇了摇头。"看来伯克的提议，已是仅剩的一条路了。

===Original===
“I think perhaps there’s something else,” she said almost timidly. “Do you remember what you told me some weeks ago about the picture that must always be passing through a criminal’s mind?”

“Yes, distinctly.”

“And the strange impulse to return to the scene of his crime that he has to fight against? Well, let us assume that Martin is the criminal and has returned.”

“There’s no question of that,” he put in quickly.

“Perhaps not, but the picture he found was not the one he had been carrying with him.”

===Chinese===
"我想，也许还有一条别的路，"她说得几乎带着羞怯。"您还记得几个星期前对我说过的话吗——那幅必定总在罪人脑海里掠过的画面？"

"记得，记得真切。"

"还有那股他必须拼命压制、重回作案现场的奇怪冲动？好，咱们不妨假定：马丁就是那罪人，而且已经回来了。

"这一点没有疑问。"他赶紧插了一句。

"也许没有，可他见到的那幅画面，并不是他一路带在心里的那一幅。

===Original===
“Why?”

“The study had been changed﻿—I mean its arrangement; therefore the possible effect that might have been produced if he had seen the picture in actual existence did not take place.”

“Go on,” he said tensely.

“But if on the other hand, and without expecting it, Martin were brought suddenly face to face with that picture, if the study were reset just as it was before, and if”﻿—here she trembled, and went on bravely﻿—“if he thought he saw father lying there as he did see him two years ago, don’t you think that something real and truthful might be startled out of him?”

===Chinese===
"为什么？"

"书房变样了——我说的是它的陈设；因此，倘若他见到那幅画面活生生摆在眼前、本可能生出的效果，就没有发生。

"往下说。"他紧张地说。

"可反过来，倘若马丁在毫无防备之下，骤然被带到那幅画面跟前，倘若书房重新摆回原先的样子，倘若——"说到这里她颤了一颤，又勇敢地说下去——"倘若他以为自己看见父亲躺在那里，就像他两年前亲眼看见的那样，您不觉得，会从他身上惊出一些真实的、可信的东西来吗？

===Original===
“By Jove!” whispered Derrick. “Do you mean it?”

She nodded. “Yes, all of it. I don’t just know how I feel it, but I know, here.” She touched her breast. “It’s the right thing to do.”

“Would you help?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
"天哪！"德里克低声道。"你是当真的？"

她点了点头。"当真，全都当真。我说不清这感觉是怎么来的，可我知道，就在这儿。"她按了按自己的胸口。"这么做是对的。

"你肯帮忙吗？"

"肯。"

===Original===
“I hate to ask it. And if it’s attempted Perkins must know nothing about it.”

“No, she mustn’t; and, Jack, there’s something else.” It seemed natural now to call him Jack.

“Yes, Jean?” He lingered on the word. How near it brought him!

Her eyes told him that she, too, felt the nearness, but for the moment her brain was working too swiftly to yield to aught else.

“There’s the peddler. One can’t tell where he is, but not far away. I’m sure of that. He won’t finally go till he has that which he came for. Where is it now?”

===Chinese===
"我真不愿开这个口。还有，这事一旦做起来，珀金斯必须毫不知情。

"不能让她知道；还有，杰克，另有一件事。"到了这时，唤他杰克已显得自然而然。

"什么，琴？"他在这声唤上流连了片刻。这一声，把他带得多么近啊！

她的眼睛告诉他，她也感到了这份亲近；只是此刻她的头脑转得太快，容不下旁的事情。

"还有那个货郎。谁也说不清他在哪儿，可离得不远，这一点我有把握。不拿到他为此而来的那样东西，他不会当真走的。它如今在哪儿？

===Original===
“Behind the panel.”

“But if you do what I suggest, and tonight, it should be on the desk beside you.”

“Beside me?”

“Yes, if you﻿—if you take the part of my father.”

He caught his breath at this supreme courage. “Would you come and arrange the study?”

===Chinese===
"在暗板后头。

"可倘若您照我提议的做，就在今晚，它就该摆在书桌上，就在您手边。

"在我手边？"

"是的，假如您——假如您来扮演我父亲。

这份极至的勇气让他屏住了呼吸。"你肯来布置书房吗？"

===Original===
“Yes, when?”

“Let me settle that with Edith. I’ll see her at once and then go on to Burke. She’ll probably come this afternoon and ask you to dinner. Will that be all right?”

He longed to take her in his arms, but again it was only their eyes that met﻿—and spoke.

===Chinese===
"肯。什么时候？"

"时间让我同伊迪丝（Edith）商定。我这就去见她，然后接着去伯克那儿。她多半今天下午过来，请你们去吃晚饭。这样行吗？

他渴望将她揽进怀里，可又一次，相遇的只有他们的目光——目光诉说着一切。

---
===Original===
It was to Bamberley police station and not to Beech Lodge that Derrick went first. He found the sergeant in the little office, his face a map of uncertainty. He looked up inquiringly as the young man came in. The last few hours had been bad ones for Burke. Then Derrick put the matter without delay, told how the suggestion originated, added that he had agreed that it was the next and best move, and waited for the sergeant to speak. Presently the latter shook his head.

===Chinese===
德里克头一处去的，是班伯利警局，不是山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge）。他在那间小办公室里找到了巡佐，那张脸是一幅写满惶惑的地图。年轻人进来时，他探询地抬起了头。方才那几个钟头，对伯克委实难熬。德里克随即开门见山，把事情摊了开来，讲了这主意是怎么起的头，又说自己已经认定这是下一步最好的一着，然后等巡佐开口。过了一会儿，巡佐摇了摇头。

===Original===
“I dare not, Mr. Derrick.”

“Why not?”

“Stop and think, sir. Here’s a man under arrest, and I myself have charged him with complicity in murder. I’m responsible for him till the authorities proceed. One suspect has already escaped. Now you propose that I let the other man out of custody to try an experiment which is, well, Mr. Derrick, fantastic any way you put it.”

===Chinese===
"我不敢，德里克先生。

"为什么不敢？"

"您停下来想想，先生。这里有个人犯在押，还是我亲自指控他涉嫌谋杀的。在当局接手之前，我对他负着全责。一个嫌疑犯已经脱逃了。如今您却提议，让我把另一个人也提出羁押，去做一场试验——这场试验，唉，德里克先生，随您怎么说，都是异想天开。

===Original===
“Exactly; but if you stop to think, sergeant, the whole affair has been more or less fantastic ever since we started. We acted on possibilities, not probabilities, and you must admit we’ve dug up a good deal that didn’t come to light before.”

“Yes, I do admit it; also that ten to one we’ve got the man who killed Mr. Millicent. But I’m frank to say that I don’t like what’s bound to happen over Blunt’s escape. I’m only hoping that Martin’s evidence will let me down with a good general average.”

“And if you don’t convict Martin?”

“Then I lose my job,” said Burke grimly.

===Chinese===
"正是异想天开；可是巡佐，你停下来想想，这整桩事打从我们插手起，就多多少少带着异想天开的味儿。我们行事凭的是种种可能，不是十拿九稳，而你也得承认，我们挖出了不少从前不曾见天日的东西。

"对，这我承认；还承认十有八九，我们抓到的就是杀害米莉森特先生的凶手。可我坦白说，布伦特脱逃之后必然闹出的动静，我不喜欢。我只盼着马丁的证词，能让我总算账时不至于太难看。

"那要是你给马丁定不了罪呢？"

"那我就丢差事。"伯克冷冷地说。

===Original===
“Would you have to advertise the fact if you did personally bring Martin to Beech Lodge at, say nine thirty tonight?”

The big man stared at him. “No, but﻿—”

“Then look here. I’m willing to see this last attempt through if you are, but if you’re not, I step down and out. I can’t give you any reasons for saying that I think it will have surprising results, but I do feel that. Admitting that you risk your job, isn’t it worth while taking the chance of producing both the criminal and the evidence? If you decide otherwise, well and good. It’s going to be rather a thick night,” he added, glancing out of the window.

Burke weighed the chances, his eyes half closed, pushing out his broad, full lips and tapping on the bare table. Yes, the night promised to be thick. He saw himself, the guardian of Bamberley, sneaking out of the village in the fog, a criminal chained to his wrist, but himself the more agitated of the two. Against this he was aware that ever since the Millicent case had come to life things just as strange as this had been going on. A man of order and law and precedent, knowing the police code as a parson knows the Pentateuch, he shrank from outlawing himself by doing as Derrick proposed. But here again the consciousness of something beyond the ordinary that lay behind the Millicent case projected itself. He could see the grin that would run through police circles from John O’Groats to Land’s End when the Blunt story came out, and recoiled at the mere thought of it. Without something, as for instance a conviction, to counterbalance that escape, he was done. And he knew it. It was the vision of that official grin that decided him.

===Chinese===
"倘若你今晚九点半亲自把马丁带到山毛榉山庄，这件事难道还非得声张出去不成？"

大块头直瞪着他。"那倒不必，可是——"

"那就听我说。你若肯，我就陪这最后一搏干到底；你不肯，我就此抽身退出。我说这会有惊人的结果，道理一条也拿不出，可我实实在在这样觉得。就算你冒着丢差事的风险，有机会把人证和物证一并捧出来，难道不值得赌一赌？你另有决断，也好。今夜怕是个浓雾之夜，"他补了一句，朝窗外望了一眼。

伯克掂量着胜算，双眼半闭，噘着宽阔厚实的嘴唇，手指敲着光秃秃的桌面。是的，这夜注定雾浓。他看见自己——班伯利的守护人——在雾里偷偷溜出村子，手腕上锁着一名罪犯，而两人之中更为慌乱的却是他自己。可另一头他也明白，自打米莉森特一案还了阳，同这一样离奇的事就没断过。他是个讲秩序、法律与成例的人，对警界章程的烂熟，一如牧师之于摩西五经；要照德里克的提议行事、把自己放到法外去，他不免畏缩。可就在这里，那种意识又自己冒上头来：米莉森特一案背后，横着某种超乎寻常的东西。他看得见，布伦特一事传开之后，警界上下从约翰奥格罗茨（John O’Groats）到兰兹角（Land’s End）会怎样咧嘴而笑；光是一想，他就往后缩。没有一样东西——比如一纸定罪——来抵那场脱逃，他就完了。这一点他自己清楚。正是那幅官员咧嘴而笑的图景，让他拿定了主意。

===Original===
“Will you tell me exactly what you suggest I should do?” he asked heavily.

“First, say nothing to Martin. If you want to let Dr. Henry into this, do so, but that’s for you to decide. Fetch Martin to Beech Lodge at exactly nine thirty tonight. Perkins will bring you to the study door, which will be closed. She will knock, and there will be no answer. Then she will naturally open it, and you and she and Martin will see that room just as it looked after the murder two years ago. I will be at the desk in the position in which Millicent was found, and able to give assistance if you want it. You must not speak. I anticipate that Martin, or it may be Perkins, will break the silence, but it is sure to be Martin. His very first words should tell us what we want to know. That’s all.”

===Chinese===
"您能不能确切告诉我，您建议我怎么做？"他沉沉地问。

"第一，对马丁一字不提。你想让亨利医生知情，也可以让，但那由你决定。今晚九点半整，把马丁带到山毛榉山庄。珀金斯会领你们到书房门口，门关着。她敲门，无人应声。她自然会推开门，那时你、她和马丁看见的那间屋子，就同两年前凶案之后一模一样。我坐在书桌前，摆成发现米莉森特时的那个姿势，你们若要援手，我随时能够。你千万不可开口。我预计，马丁——也许是珀金斯——会打破沉默，但必定是马丁。他头一句话，就该把咱们想知道的说出来了。就这些。

===Original===
Burke listened with strained attention. “If I did bring Martin I couldn’t bring anyone else. I mean I couldn’t have anyone on duty outside. The two constables could not be allowed to know anything about this.”

Derrick, realizing that the point had been carried, sent him a grave smile. “I don’t think we need bother about the outside of the house tonight, but that’s your end of it. All I ask for is you and Martin at nine thirty. I’m not trying to persuade you into this, sergeant, so drop it if you don’t think it’s good enough. But it’s the only program I can suggest, and I’ve no alternative.”

Burke rose mountainously from his chair. “And I’ve tried to tell you what it involves me in, which is the risk of twenty years’ record and my present job.” He paused, then gave a determined grunt. “But I’ll do it.”

Derrick nodded. “I think you’re right, and sometimes a man moves further ahead in ten minutes than in twenty years. Nine thirty, sergeant.”

===Chinese===
伯克屏息凝神地听着。"我若真把马丁带来，就带不得旁人了。我是说，外头不能派人值守。那两名警员，绝不能让他们知道这件事的半点。

德里克明白这一关已经过了，朝他郑重一笑。"今晚宅子外头，我看不必多虑，不过那一头归你调度。我只求一件事：九点半，你同马丁到。我并不是要把你劝进这局里来，巡佐，你觉得不够好就作罢。可这是我提得出的唯一方案，我没有别的路。

伯克像一座山似的从椅子上起身。"我也尽力告诉了您，这会把我搭进什么里去——二十年考绩的风险，加上眼前这份差事。"他顿了顿，横下心来哼了一声。"可我干。

德里克点了点头。"我想你是对的。有时候，一个人十分钟能赶出的路，胜过二十年。九点半，巡佐。

## III The Man from the East / 第三章 东方来客

===Original===
A week passed at Beech Lodge, while Derrick endeavored to get down to work; but in spite of every effort, progress seemed impossible. Ideas, when they came, were illusory; his characters imbued themselves with strange aspirations and qualities, and plot after plot was displaced by the secret but constantly strengthening conviction that this novel was not, for the present at any rate, the most important thing in life. More than ever he was fascinated by Millicent’s study and the nameless advances seemingly made by the portrait of its late owner, and sat at the big desk for hours, fingering his pen, grasping at thoughts that continually eluded him. By the end of the second week he was assured there was something the dead man wanted him to do.

Of all this he said nothing to Edith, and it was a relief to know that she was of too practical a nature to harbor imaginings similar to his own. Her days were spent in settling down, and he agreed thankfully with all she proposed, stipulating only that the study itself should remain absolutely undisturbed. That room, he announced with an air of great contentment, had been designed and equipped to suit his particular fancy. When he said this it seemed that the portrait of Millicent signaled its silent approval.

===Chinese===
山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge）过了一个星期。这一个星期里，德里克（Derrick）竭力想安下心来做活；可无论怎样努力，进展似乎都无从谈起。灵感来是来，来了却是虚的；笔下的人物一个个自行沾染上古怪的抱负与品性；一个又一个情节，都被一种隐秘而日渐增强的确信挤到了一旁：至少就眼下而论，这部小说并不是人生里顶要紧的事。他比以往任何时候都着迷于米莉森特（Millicent）先生的书房，着迷于那位已故主人的画像似乎做出的种种无名的亲近表示，一坐到大书桌前便是几个钟头，手指捻着笔，去抓那些一回回从他手边溜走的念头。到第二个星期末了，他已经确信：那死人有一件事要他办。

这一切他对伊迪丝（Edith）只字不提；想到她天性太讲实际，断不会藏纳同他相似的想象，他也就放了心。她的日子全花在安顿家务上，她提的主意他无不欣然赞同，只提出一条：书房本身务必丝毫不加变动。他一副心满意足的气派宣布：那个房间，从设计到陈设，恰恰合他个人的癖好。他说这话的时候，米莉森特的画像仿佛默然示意，深表赞许。

===Original===
It was one evening when he was at the desk, trying as usual to classify his own thoughts, that Edith looked up from the book in her lap.

“Jack,” she said suddenly.

He put down his pen with relief. There were whispering shadows in the corner, and one could not work tonight.

“Yes, what is it?”

“Will you tell me something, quite honestly?”

He smiled and nodded. “It’s no particular effort to be honest with you. What am I suspected of now?”

===Chinese===
一天晚上，他正坐在书桌前，照例想把自己的思绪归归类，伊迪丝从膝头的书上抬起了眼睛。

"杰克（Jack）。"她忽然唤了一声。

他如释重负地搁下笔。屋角里有窃窃私语的暗影，今晚是做不成活的。

"嗯，什么事？"

"你肯老老实实告诉我一件事吗？"

他微笑着点头。"同你讲实话，毫不费事。这回又疑心我什么了？

===Original===
She glanced into the leaping fire, and turned with a quick, familiar motion. “How’s the book going? I do so want to know.”

“It isn’t making what one would call absolutely triumphant progress. It’s generally that way at first. Then later on you realize that you’ve done far more than you thought, and the happy issue is in sight.”

“Do you know yet whether Beech Lodge is as good a place to work in as you expected?”

“I think it is, quite,” he said slowly. “It’s a new atmosphere, and one doesn’t get it at once, but whatever I write here will be different and”—he hesitated an instant—“I think stronger than anything I’ve done yet. I can see that already.”

===Chinese===
她朝跳动的炉火瞥了一眼，随即用一个又快又熟稔的动作转过身来。"书写得怎么样了？我真想知道啊。

"它还没有取得人们所谓的凯旋式的进展。起初大抵都是这样。到后来你才发觉，自己已经做的远比想象的多，圆满的结局也望得见了。

"山毛榉山庄是不是同你指望的一样，是个做活的好地方——这你弄清楚了吗？

"我看是的，完全是。"他缓缓地说。"这是一种新的气氛，一时还捉摸不透它；可我在这里写出的无论什么，都会同以往不同，而且——"他顿了一瞬——"我想会比我从前的任何东西都更有力气。这一点我已经看出来了。

===Original===
“I’m glad you haven’t any second thoughts about the place.”

“But I have, quite a lot. They’re not sorted out yet. What about you? Too busy to think at all?”

She glanced at him oddly. “I’ve been trying to be too busy but haven’t quite succeeded.” She said this with a touch of reluctance, as though confessing to some feminine weakness.

“I hope they’re pleasant thoughts.”

“Not altogether, Jack. Sometimes they’re queer and sometimes a bit disconcerting. Foolish for a woman like me to talk like this, isn’t it?”

He laughed easily. “I know no person less foolish.”

===Chinese===
"你对这地方没有再起别的心思，我真高兴。

"起了，还不少呢。只是还没理出头绪。你呢？忙得连想的工夫都没有？

她古怪地瞥了他一眼。"我一直在想法子把自己忙得没工夫想，可没有完全办到。"她说这话带着几分勉强，像是在招认什么女人的短处。

"但愿你那些念头是愉快的。

"并不全愉快，杰克。它们有时古怪，有时又有点叫人发慌。像我这样的女人说这种话，未免犯傻，是不是？

他洒脱地笑了。"依我看，再没有谁比你更不傻了。

===Original===
She did not answer but continued to gaze into the fire, her eyes a little disturbed. Her brother wanted time to think, being convinced that it was most important that for the present at any rate Edith should remain unaware of certain things. Perkins, for instance. However competent Perkins might be, she could not in any sense be called a normal woman. Perhaps he was not at this time normal himself. Something assured him that no revelation would be made from the unknown to his sister. Her wireless set might be affected, but it was not tuned to the right wavelength. After all, there was no reason why matters should not proceed smoothly enough.

===Chinese===
她没有作答，只管望着炉火，眼里有点乱。她的哥哥需要时间想一想，因为他坚信，至少就眼下而论，顶顶要紧的是让伊迪丝对某些事情一无所知。譬如珀金斯（Perkins）。珀金斯无论怎样能干，无论如何也算不得一个寻常女人。也许他自己这时候也算不得寻常了。有什么向他保证：冥冥之中不会向他妹妹降下任何启示。她那台无线电机或许受得了感应，却没有调准波长。再说，事情本没有理由不顺顺当当地办下去。

===Original===
“Why are your reflections disconcerting?” he hazarded.

“I don’t know. It’s stupid of me, and I call myself an idiot for being affected at all. The funny thing, Jack, is that I’m gradually beginning to consider myself absolutely superficial to something or other—I don’t know what. The house is running well, and Perkins is a treasure; a little chilling at times, but the best servant I’ve ever had. Things seem to do themselves at her desire. Why should I feel superficial?”

He shook his head. “You’re anything but that. What else is the matter?”

“Nothing whatever, and yet—” She got up restlessly and balanced herself on the corner of the desk close to the dull stain. But it had no message for her. “If you say definitely that we made no mistake in taking Beech Lodge, I’ll feel a lot better. Isn’t it silly of me? There’s everything here one wants, and all a housekeeper could desire, but—”

===Chinese===
"你的心思为什么叫你发慌？"他试探着问。

"我不知道。这是我的愚蠢，为这点事就受影响，我真骂自己是傻瓜。可笑的是，杰克，我竟渐渐开始觉得，自己对某个什么东西——我说不清是什么——浅薄得很。家务运转得顺顺当当，珀金斯是件宝贝；有时有点凉飕飕的，却是我用过的顶好的仆人。事情仿佛凭她一念就自动办妥。我凭什么觉得浅薄呢？

他摇了摇头。"你同浅薄毫不沾边。还有别的事吗？

"什么也没有，可是——"她不安地站起身，一侧身坐上了书桌的一角，紧挨着那块黯淡的污渍。可那污迹没有音信给她。"要是你明明白白说一句，我们租下山毛榉山庄没有租错，我心里就会松快许多。我这是不是傻？这里样样俱全，当家主妇想要什么有什么，可是——

===Original===
He felt a touch of apprehension and laughed it off. “You’re only a bit lonely, and probably I’ve been selfish in planting you in such a lonely spot for the sake of that confounded novel. I admit to being a bit spoiled. But we have neighbors. What about the Millicents?”

“They’re about three miles from here in a cottage. Perkins tells me the daughter is twenty-two and very pretty but has never got over her father’s death. They were devoted to each other.”

“You’ll see them soon,” he said involuntarily.

“I hardly think so. They would not call under all the circumstances; at least it would be strange if they did.”

“Perhaps not, but—” He broke off. “Tell me more of what’s in your mind. You know what you are to me, and I can’t help feeling rather responsible.”

===Chinese===
他觉出一丝忧惧，一笑把它拂开了。"你不过是有点冷清；多半是我自私，为了那部该死的小说，把你安插在这么个冷清的地方。我认账，我是有点娇惯的。可我们有邻居啊。米莉森特家怎么样？

"她们住在离这儿约三英里的一座小屋里。珀金斯告诉我，那女儿二十二岁，人很标致，只是父亲的死，她一直没有缓过来。父女俩感情深得很。

"你很快就会见到她们的。"他不假思索地脱口说。

"我看未必。照眼下的种种情形，她们不会来拜望；至少要是来，倒奇怪了。

"也许不会，可是——"他顿住了。"多讲讲你心里的事吧。你知道你在我心里是什么分量，我不由得觉得自己对你颇负责任。

===Original===
“It’s hard to tell you without seeming an utter fool. It vexes and amuses me all at once,” she said simply. “It’s things I’ve never been conscious of before. I’m not actually conscious of them now, but it’s as if something had suggested their existence. At the same time I know I’ll never quite understand. I’m not built that way. Perhaps I get something through what I feel for you because you feel it, even though it’s past me. Does this all sound like gibberish? Then again it is as though both of us were being threatened. I wonder if you understand that all this is so different from anything I’ve felt before that I don’t quite know what to do.”

Derrick listened seriously. His first impulse was to laugh her mood away, but instantly there came to him from the surrounding shadows a warning that on no account must he be false to that which he himself believed. Pondering this, he knew that he could not deny these mysterious powers that now proclaimed themselves. He might desert their kingdom, but to disown it was impossible.

===Chinese===
"要讲给你听，又不像个十足的大傻瓜，实在难。"她坦率地说。"这叫我一面恼，一面又觉得好笑。那是些我从前从来没有意识到的东西。眼下我也并不真意识到它们，倒像有什么东西提示了它们的存在。可同时我又明白，我永远不会真懂。我不是那种构造。也许凭着我对你怀着的那份情，我也间接沾着一点什么，因为你是感到它的，尽管它越过了我的头顶。这些话听着像不像呓语？再有，又仿佛我们两个都在受着什么威吓。你可明白，这一切同我从前有过的任何感受都迥然不同，不同得我不知道如何是好。

德里克神情严肃地听着。他的头一个冲动，是一笑把她的心绪打发掉；可四面暗影里立刻传给他一声警告：万万不可背弃他自己所信的东西。他把这层想了想，明白对这些如今自陈其存在的神秘力量，他已无法否认。他可以逃离它们的国土，却不认这国土，是办不到的。

===Original===
“If the place does not agree with you, we’ll chuck it,” he said slowly.

She sent him a whimsical smile. “You know that’s out of the question, dear old boy. We simply can’t; we’re in too deep for the next year. And forgive me if I talk to you as though you were my sister, for that’s one of my selfish habits, and it’s really your own fault for standing it. Here we stick till that novel is finished and sold. I’m sorry it doesn’t go as fast as you would like.”

“It will when I get shaken down,” he answered doggedly. “Trouble is that one is apt to think of too many things at once. Then follows the discarding and selecting process, and I suppose I’m going through that now. The point is to be sure of retaining what is really worth while; and, when I begin to feel that, it means confidence and progress. In that last novel I didn’t quite know what to discard, and it jumps at me from every page. But now,” he concluded with a little lift in his voice, “I’ve an idea that I’m just on the edge of something big.”

“While your sister,” she murmured absently, “has a perfectly ridiculous sensation that she’s just on the edge of something deep, and hasn’t the slightest intention of falling over.”

She sent him a companionable smile and was soon lost in her book. Derrick struggled on with his opening chapters, thankful that she had made no searching inquiry into his own inward sensations. There was no sound save the methodical turning of a page and the scratch of a pen. The fire puttered its ruddy comfort, and Beech Lodge was dipped in an abyss of silence.

===Chinese===
"这地方要是同你不合，我们就把它退了。"他缓缓地说。

她朝他送去一个古灵精怪的笑。"你知道那是办不到的，亲爱的老伙计。我们简直退不起；往下这一年，我们陷得太深了。还得原谅我把你当姊妹似的同你说话——这是我自私的习惯之一，实在也要怪你自己，谁叫你受着呢。我们就钉在这儿，直到那部小说写完、卖掉。只可惜它不像你盼望的走得那么快。

"等我安顿定了，就快了。"他执拗地答。"毛病出在人容易一下子想的事太多。接下来便是取舍的工夫，我如今大约正走着这一步。要紧的是拿稳留下真正值得留的；我一觉出这个，便意味着信心和进展。上一部小说里，我简直不知道该丢掉什么，如今它从每一页上朝我扑过来。可是这回，"他收尾时嗓音微微一扬，"我有个感觉：我正站在一件大事的边上。

"而你的妹妹呢，"她心不在焉地低语，"却有一种荒唐透顶的感觉：自己正站在一处深渊的边上，却丝毫没有要跌下去的打算。

她朝他投来一个亲切的笑，很快便沉进书里去了。德里克继续同他的开头几章搏斗，暗自庆幸她没有刨根问底地探问他内心的感受。屋子里没有一点声音，只有一页一页规矩的翻动和笔尖的沙沙。炉火咕嘟着吐出红艳的暖意，山毛榉山庄浸在一片深渊似的寂静里。

===Original===
Presently the inner edge of one of the heavy curtains that hung over the French window stirred ever so slightly and at one point drew very slowly aside, leaving a narrow oval gap on the border of which a man’s fingers, short, broad, and strong, were visible. This gap widened inch by inch, till, framed in the dull fabric, there appeared a face. A mass of tumbled hair surmounted a low forehead, beneath which moved eyes that were dark, shining, and restless. The man might have been forty, with tanned skin, large and rather uncouth features, a broad mouth, heavy lips—blue-black and unshaven—and a strange, furtive expression. No part of his body was visible below the chin, and the face hung as though suspended like a threatening mask in midair. The roving eyes searched the room, darting from place to place with extraordinary quickness, and reflecting little pinpoints of light from the leaping flames. Finally they rested on Derrick and his sister with a look in which surprise mingled with a certain unconquerable composure. There was no fear in the look but rather the suggestion that this formidable stranger from the dark had been here before and was now making up his mind on some vital matter. Then the lips widened into a grin rendered repulsive by discolored teeth; the gap narrowed as silently as a leaf falls; face and fingers diminished and disappeared; the curtain trembled and hung straight; and there drifted into the room the faintest possible sound from without. It was over, like a baleful dream.

===Chinese===
忽然，罩着落地窗（French window）的一幅厚帘子，内侧的边上极轻极轻地一动，有一处缓缓地、缓缓地掀开了一条缝，留下一个窄窄的椭圆的口子，口子的边上，露出一双男人的手指，短、宽、有力。那口子一寸一寸地扩大，直到昏暗的帘布框出一张脸。一团蓬乱的头发覆在低低的额头上，头发下面转动着一双眼睛，黑的，亮的，不安分的。这人大约四十岁，皮肤晒成棕色，五官阔大而近于粗野，嘴阔，嘴唇厚厚的——青黑色，没剃过——带着一种古怪的、鬼祟的表情。下巴以下看不见他身体的任何部分，那张脸悬着，仿佛一枚含着威吓的面具吊在半空。那双游移的眼睛搜索着房间，飞快地从一个地方跳到另一个地方，映着跳动的火光，闪出一粒粒细小的亮点。最后它们落在了德里克和他妹妹身上，那一眼里有惊奇，也有一种压不垮的自若。那目光里没有惧怕，倒是透出这样一个意思：这个从黑暗里来的可怕生客，从前就到过这里，此刻正在一件要紧事上下着决心。随后那两片嘴唇咧开一笑，一口发黑的牙齿把笑意弄得叫人厌恶；帘缝又无声地合拢，像一片叶子落地；脸和手指缩下去、消失了；帘子颤了颤，垂得笔直；屋里飘进来一丝几乎不能再轻的、外面的声息。完了，像一场凶险的梦。

===Original===
Derrick looked up sharply. “Who was that?”

Edith, perceiving nothing, stared at him. His face was tense, his eyes very wide open. She struggled against a foolish sense of alarm.

“Where, Jack?”

“In this room. Did anyone come in just now?” He peered about, searching the dancing shadows, keyed suddenly to a strange pitch.

“No one,” she said. “Who could there be? I heard nothing.”

“That’s odd,” he murmured.

===Chinese===
德里克猛地抬头。"刚才是谁？

伊迪丝什么也没有察觉，怔怔地瞪着他。他的脸绷得紧紧的，眼睛睁得极大。她正同一个愚蠢的惊惶搏斗着。

"在哪儿，杰克？

"就在这屋里。刚才有人进来过吗？"他四下张望，搜索着跳舞的暗影，神经陡然绷上了一个古怪的调门。

"没有人啊。"她说。"能有什么人？我什么也没听见。

"怪了。"他喃喃道。

===Original===
She got up, stood beside him, and put a hand on his arm. “What’s odd, Jack? I wish you wouldn’t go on like this—and don’t be so mysterious, unless you want it to get on my nerves.”

“I had an extraordinary feeling that for a moment we were not alone.” He laughed, but it sounded a shade forced. “Dreaming as usual, I suppose. Sorry, Edith; I won’t do it again.”

But Miss Derrick, in spite of herself, had turned a little pale. For the past hour she had been trying to put out of her head a succession of strange thoughts about strange things, and she had nearly succeeded. Now she felt dizzy. Perhaps they had not been alone. But who could it have been? Mystery, breathless, confusing, and baffling, stole in on her like a secret assailant, attacking all senses save that of fear. Her pulse slowed—and beat tumultuously. She stepped to the bell and rang hard. Derrick looked at her with wonder.

“What’s the matter? There’s nothing to be frightened about!”

“How do you know?” she stammered. “I feel queer because I don’t know. I want to see someone who isn’t just ourselves,” she went on chaotically, “and I’m the more vexed because it has to—to be Perkins.” She covered her eyes unconsciously, like a child. “Jack, Jack, what is the matter with me? I’m acting like a fool.”

===Chinese===
她站起来，挨着他站定，把一只手搭上他的手臂。"什么怪了，杰克？我劝你别这样下去了——也别这么神神秘秘的，除非你要把我的神经磨出毛病来。

"我有一种异样的感觉：有那么一瞬，我们并不是独自在这里。"他笑了，可笑声听着有点勉强。"照例是做梦吧，我想。对不起，伊迪丝；我下不为例。

可德里克小姐（Miss Derrick）还是不由自主地白了一白脸。这一个钟头里，她一直想法子把一串关于怪事的怪念头赶出脑袋，也差不多赶干净了。这会儿她只觉得晕眩。也许方才他们当真不是独自在这里。可那能是谁呢？神秘——叫人屏息的、搅人的、无从捉摸的神秘——像个暗中的袭击者潜进她身里，凡百感官都遭了袭击，唯独恐惧除外。她的脉搏慢了下来——随即又狂乱地跳。她走到铃前，狠狠地按铃。德里克惊奇地望着她。

"怎么啦？没有什么可怕的呀！

"你怎么知道？"她结结巴巴地说。"我心里发毛，正因为我不知道。我想见一个不是我们自己的人，"她语无伦次地接着说，"偏又叫我不痛快的是，这个人还非得——非得是珀金斯。"她像孩子似的、不知不觉地捂住了眼睛。"杰克，杰克，我是怎么了？我这简直是在犯傻。

===Original===
He put his arm round her. “I’m awfully sorry, dear, but, really, it’s nothing. I hardly knew I spoke. Of course it is nothing. I’ll search the house if you like.”

“But would you find it?” she whispered. “Would you find it?”

Came a tap at the door, and Perkins entered, her face as blank as ever. Edith controlled herself with an effort and looked straight into the basilisk eyes.

“Perkins, has anyone come to the house just now?”

The maid glanced at her, impassive and inscrutable. “No, madam. Was anyone expected?”

===Chinese===
他伸手搂住她。"真对不起，亲爱的，可这实在没什么。我自己都几乎不觉得开了口。当然没有什么。你愿意的话，我把整座宅子搜一遍。

"可你找得到它吗？"她低声说。"找得到吗？

门上响起一记轻敲，珀金斯走了进来，脸还是往常那样一片空白。伊迪丝用力克制住自己，直直望进那双蛇怪似的眼睛。

"珀金斯，刚才有人到宅子里来过吗？

女仆瞥了她一眼，无动于衷，深不可测。"没有，太太。有谁是要来的吗？

===Original===
Edith could but answer with another question. “You—you have heard nothing within the last few minutes?”

“Nothing whatever, madam.” The voice carried no suggestion of surprise, but Perkins’s eyes met those of Derrick for a passing instant.

“Thank you. Please go to my room, and—and bring me a handkerchief. Are all the windows and doors fastened?”

“Yes, madam, except this one. Mr. Derrick told me to leave that to him.”

She disappeared. Derrick laughed and lit his pipe.

===Chinese===
伊迪丝只得再用一个问题作答。"你——这几分钟里，你什么也没有听见？

"什么也没有，太太。"那嗓音里没有一点惊讶的意思，可珀金斯的眼睛同德里克的眼睛相接了一瞬。

"谢谢。请到我房里去，还有——再给我拿一块手帕来。所有的门窗都闩好了吗？

"都闩好了，太太，只是这一扇除外。德里克先生吩咐过，这扇由他自己来管。

她退出去了。德里克笑了，点起烟斗。

===Original===
“You’re answered now, Edith! The house closed tight as a drum, and the only access from outside through this room.”

“Perhaps you’re right! Yes, of course you are; but, when she comes back, say something that will keep her for a minute; say anything at all. Please do that. I can’t explain, but I must hear some other voice, even Perkins’s comfortless accents. Jack, I am a fool.”

“You’re not very complimentary to my powers of entertainment,” he chuckled. “I won’t write any more tonight. We’ll get out the cards if you like.”

She shook her head and sent him a strange glance, as though wondering if he would understand. “It isn’t entertainment I want tonight.”

“Then what? I’m not in a position to offer much more.”

“I don’t know. It’s something like protection, but not quite that, either. I know it sounds absurd, but it’s the kind of thing that could only come from one who does not believe what you do about all this.” She made a gesture at the surrounding room. “I suppose it’s a sort of companion in my incredulity. You’re beginning to make things rather too much alive for my comfort, though I don’t believe in them at all.”

===Chinese===
"你这就有了答案了，伊迪丝！宅子关得像鼓面一样严实，从外面进来的唯一通路，就得经过这个房间。

"也许你说得对！是的，你当然对；只是等她回来，随便说点什么，留她一分钟；说什么都行。千万这样。我讲不清道理，可我必须听见另一个人的声音，哪怕是珀金斯那叫人宽不了心的腔调。杰克，我真是个傻瓜。

"你这话可不太捧我逗乐的本事。"他呵呵地笑。"今晚我不再写了。你乐意的话，我们把牌拿出来。

她摇了摇头，朝他投去古怪的一眼，像是不知道他懂不懂。"我今晚要的不是消遣。

"那要什么？再多的，我可拿不出了。

"我说不上。有点像保护，可又不全是。我知道这话听着荒唐，可这种东西，只能得自一个不像你这样信这一切的人。"她朝四下的房间做了个手势。"我想，这算是我这不信里头的一个同伴吧。你开始把事情弄得活过了头，叫我心里不安生，尽管我对它们一个字也不信。

===Original===
“There’s nothing here,” he protested quickly, “nothing but ourselves. Forget what I said. I was only dreaming aloud. It’s what the Scotch call havering.”

Even as he spoke there came to him the refutation of his own words. Millicent signaled his disapproval from the canvas overhead, and stinging whispers from the silence around proclaimed him false to his real belief. The protest died on his lips, and Edith looked at him keenly.

“I don’t want you to say what you don’t believe in the hope of stiffening me, but I’d be glad if you’d help to prevent my believing it, too. I don’t want to, and I don’t intend to. I’m tremendously in earnest about all this. The reason is that I know I haven’t got the right kind of mental machinery. It would break me all up, while on the contrary it is perfectly natural for you. All I want to do is to carry on here in the ordinary way and make it as easy as possible for you to work. That’s a woman’s job, Jack, and I’m satisfied with it and don’t want to go beyond it. If there’s anything that you’re forced to tell me, well, tell me, but don’t do any more. All this may sound rather hysterical, but it isn’t; and it’s because I know myself better than I begin to think I know you, even after all these years. So don’t try me more than you can avoid.”

===Chinese===
"这里什么也没有。"他急忙分辩，"只有我们自己。忘掉我说的话吧。我不过是把梦说出了声。这是苏格兰人所谓的「瞎念叨」（havering）。

话音未落，驳斥他这番话的东西就到了。头顶的画布上，米莉森特示意了他的不以为然；四下寂静里刺人的低语，宣告他背弃了自己真实的信念。抗议在他唇上消散了，伊迪丝锐利地看着他。

"我不要你为了给我打气，去说些你自己不信的话；可你要是能帮着我、叫我也不信，我就感激了。我不愿意信，也不打算信。这一切我是顶顶认真的。缘故是，我知道自己脑子里那套机件不对路。它会把我整个人击碎；反过来，这于你却自然得很。我一心想做的，就是照平常的样子在这里操持下去，让你做活尽可能地顺当。那是女人的本分，杰克，我对它心满意足，也不想越出它一步。倘有什么你不得不告诉我的，好，告诉我，可再多的就不必了。这些话听着也许颇像歇斯底里，其实不是；只因我认识我自己，比我开始认识你更深——哪怕相识了这么些年。所以，能不试我，就别试我。

===Original===
While she was speaking, Perkins entered as silently as before. Edith steadied herself, wondering how much the woman had heard. She took the handkerchief and made an indefinite gesture to her brother.

“I say, Perkins,” he put in, “this garden is running wild, and I’ve got to get someone at once or there’ll be nothing worth while in the summer. Do you know of any good man in the neighborhood?”

“I’m sorry; I don’t, sir.”

“What about the village? Any chance there?”

“I can’t say, sir. I haven’t been to the village for more than a year.”

===Chinese===
她说话的当儿，珀金斯同先前一样无声无息地进来了。伊迪丝定住神，心里掂量这话那女人听去了多少。她接过手帕，朝哥哥含混地做了个手势。

"我说，珀金斯，"他插进来说，"这园子快长疯了，我得马上寻一个人，不然到了夏天，就什么像样的东西都没有了。附近有好人手吗，你知道的？

"对不起，先生，我不知道。

"村子里呢？那儿有指望吗？

"说不准，先生。我有一年多没进过村子了。

===Original===
“Mr. Thursby’s man seems to have been very capable. Think you could find him?”

“I don’t know where he is, sir. He came once a week for the past year, but left the village about a month ago. There’s been no one since.”

“Did Mr. Thursby take over Mr. Millicent’s man?”

“No, sir.” Perkins’s expression changed ever so slightly. “He could not.”

“Why was that?”

===Chinese===
"瑟斯比（Thursby）先生那个人手像是很能干。你想能找到他吗？

"我不知道他在哪儿，先生。过去一年他每礼拜来一回，可大约一个月前离开村子了。此后就再没有人来。

"瑟斯比先生没有接手米莉森特先生的人手吗？

"没有，先生。"珀金斯的神色起了极细微的一点变化。"他不能。

"那是为什么？

===Original===
“Because Martin, Mr. Millicent’s man, had already left.”

“When?” said Derrick curiously.

“Three days after Mr. Millicent died.”

Edith gave an involuntary shiver. “Why should he do that so soon?”

Perkins glanced at the portrait with a kind of mute unconsciousness. “I cannot say, madam. Martin did not tell me.”

===Chinese===
"因为米莉森特先生的那个马丁（Martin），已经先走了。

"什么时候？"德里克好奇地问。

"米莉森特先生死后三天。

伊迪丝不由自主地打了个寒噤。"他为什么走得这么快？

珀金斯带着一种无声的、浑然不觉的神气，朝画像望了一眼。"我说不上，太太。马丁没有告诉我。

===Original===
“It’s more or less understandable,” hazarded Derrick; “probably Mrs. Millicent let him go. She wasn’t keeping on the place anyway. Do you happen to know where he went, Perkins?”

Edith looked up. “Does that matter, Jack?”

“Yes, I think so. The man’s reputation for roses spread all over the county, and I’d like to get him back if we could afford it. And it’s better to have someone who knows the ground, if possible. What about him, Perkins?”

“No one has heard of him from that day, as yet, sir.”

Edith got up with unmistakable decision. She was evidently feeling herself again.

“Good night, Jack. Perkins, please bring my hot water now.”

===Chinese===
"这多少讲得通。"德里克揣测道，"多半是米莉森特太太（Mrs. Millicent）辞了他。她横竖也不打算把这宅子维持下去了。他去了哪儿，你可碰巧知道，珀金斯？

伊迪丝抬起头来。"这要紧吗，杰克？

"要紧，我想。那人侍弄玫瑰的名声传遍了全郡；要是雇得起，我想把他弄回来。再说，倘若办得到，用一个熟悉这地界的人总好些。他有下落吗，珀金斯？

"从那天起，至今没有一个人听说过他，先生。

伊迪丝带着明白无误的决断站起身。她显然又恢复过来了。

"晚安，杰克。珀金斯，现在请给我打热水来。

===Original===
Derrick followed her with his eyes but said nothing. When he was alone, he seated himself again at his desk and looked musingly at his manuscript. How thin and unprofitable was all he had written, these doings of characters so obviously fictitious, so utterly divorced from the stinging realities of life. They saw little and felt less, being framed in paper and not flesh and blood. His long hand stole to the edge of the desk, avoiding that discolored patch, and clasped the solid frame as though to draw from it something like real inspiration. He now touched the shadow of Millicent’s lifeblood. His glance traveled then automatically to the portrait. Blood and paint! Between them they held the key of mystery. He scanned the composed features, feeling that the essence of what had once been Millicent was close by. Then it came to him that this essence of the murdered man had its own part to play and was no doubt playing it at this very moment, moving in mysterious channels and in league with mysterious powers. Recurrent and voiceless questions crowded upon him. What could Millicent mean to Perkins, that lank woman with the forbidding eyes? It seemed after a few moments that the painted lips quivered and tried to speak, and the quiet gaze took on something more than the mere flicker of firelight. What was it that Millicent was trying to convey?

===Chinese===
德里克的目光跟着她，却什么也没说。等他独自一人，他又在书桌前坐下，望着手稿出神。他写下的这一切多么单薄、多么无益啊——这些人物的行径，一看就是凭空编造的，同人生的刺人的真实全然脱节。它们见得少，感得更少，是纸框子糊的，不是血肉长的。他那只瘦长的手悄悄摸向书桌边沿，避开那块变色的疤，握住结实的桌框，仿佛要从它那里汲取一点真正的灵感。他此刻触着的，是米莉森特生命之血的暗影。他的目光随即不由自主地移向画像。血与油彩！这桩疑案的钥匙，就攥在它们两个手里。他细看那副安详的五官，觉得曾被唤作米莉森特的那点精髓就在近旁。于是他悟到：这被害者的精髓自有它要扮演的角色，而且此刻多半正在扮演着，在神秘的河道里行动，同神秘的力量结着盟。一串又一串无声的问题朝他涌来。米莉森特于珀金斯——那个眼光冷得吓人的瘦长女人——又能意味着什么呢？过了一会儿，仿佛那两片画出来的嘴唇颤动了，要想说话；那安静的凝视里，也添了一点不止是炉火闪烁的东西。米莉森特想传达的，究竟是什么？

===Original===
“What have you absorbed?” murmured Derrick, half aloud. “What is it you would tell me? You suffered here death and the fear that was perhaps worse than death, but why did you pay the price?” He began to write unconsciously, capturing the words as they came; strange words, unlinked with anything that had gone before, but pregnant with clouded suggestion. “You believed as I do that we are not the masters of things, but that each of us builds up around him invisible towers of influence, by which in time we are dominated. We store the air with records that the air cannot discard or obliterate, eloquent—yet having no voice; strong—yet casting no shadow. And behind it all are Things. We cry for them as children, and when the end comes it is hard to let them go.”

===Chinese===
"你吸进了什么？"德里克半出声地喃喃道。"你想告诉我的是什么？你在这里受了死，也受了那也许比死更可怕的惧怕；可你为什么付出这个代价？"他不知不觉写了起来，把涌到笔下的字句一一捉住；是些古怪的字句，同先前写下的一切都不相连，却孕育着朦胧的暗示。"你同我一样相信：我们并不是万物的主人；我们每一个人，都在自己四围筑起一座座看不见的影响之塔，到头来，我们反被这些塔主宰。我们把种种印记存进空气，空气甩它们不掉，也抹它们不掉；它们能言——却没有声音；有力量——却不投影子。而这一切的背后，是那些「物」。我们像孩子似的朝它们哭喊；到末日来临，要撒手放它们走，就难了。

===Original===
He was staring, puzzled, at what he had written, when Perkins came in, her face grave.

“If you please, sir, the gardener is here.” Her voice was a little breathless.

“What gardener? I thought you told me just a moment ago that you knew of no one.”

“It’s Mr. Millicent’s gardener,” she replied steadily.

“The man who has not been heard of for two years?”

“Yes, sir. He has just returned.”

===Chinese===
他正对着写下的东西发怔，珀金斯进来了，脸色凝重。

"对不起，先生，园丁来了。"她的嗓音有几分气急。

"什么园丁？我记得你刚才还说，一个人也不知道。

"是米莉森特先生的园丁。"她稳稳地答道。

"就是那个两年杳无音信的人？

"是的，先生。他刚回来。

===Original===
Derrick took a long breath. “What brings him back now?”

He regretted the question as soon as it was asked, for Perkins was regarding him as though wondering why he should be surprised. It was all part of something else, something bigger. Surely he must realize that.

“I do not know, sir. He only reached the village this evening and came straight here.”

“Does he expect me to engage him?”

“He would like to come back to his old place, sir.”

“How extraordinary!”

===Chinese===
德里克长长吸了一口气。"他这会儿回来，是什么把他带回来的？

话一出口他就后悔了，因为珀金斯正看着他，仿佛奇怪他何必惊讶。这一切原是另一桩事、一桩更大的事的一部分。这一点，他总该明白。

"我不知道，先生。他今晚才到村子，就径直来了这里。

"他指望我雇他吗？

"他想回他的旧职，先生。

"多离奇！

===Original===
Again Derrick spoke too hastily, and again he regretted it. Perkins did not answer. She stood passively, an austere expression on her sallow features; and, scrutinize as he might, there was no penetrating the veil that enshrouded her. She was an embodiment of something that defied his keenest analysis.

“Where has this man been for the past two years?”

“He did not say, sir.”

“You can tell me whether he was satisfactory in every way to Mr. Millicent?”

She nodded. “Mr. Millicent used to say that he was the best gardener in the county.”

===Chinese===
德里克又说得太急，又后悔了。珀金斯没有回答。她被动地立着，蜡黄的脸上是一副冷峻的表情；他怎么看，也看不透裹着她的那层帷幕。她是一种东西的化身，任凭他最犀利的剖析也无从攻破。

"这个人这两年在哪儿？

"他没有说，先生。

"他伺候米莉森特先生，各方面都还令人满意——这个你总可以告诉我吧？

她点了点头。"米莉森特先生从前常说，他是全郡最好的园丁。

===Original===
Derrick paused. “Perkins, I’m going to ask you another question, but you need not answer unless you like to.”

“I will tell you anything I know, sir.” She spoke steadily and without a trace of surprise.

“Then from all you know, and I refer to more than his ability as a gardener, do you think it would be a good thing to take him on?”

“Why do you put it that way, sir?”

“I leave that to you. The matter may be more important than one can realize—as yet.” He lingered a little over the last words.

“Then, yes, sir, if you want a garden like Mr. Millicent’s.”

The shrewdness of the answer took him aback. “Send him in,” he said shortly.

===Chinese===
德里克顿了顿。"珀金斯，我还有一问要问你，不过你不愿意的话，尽可以不答。

"凡我知道的，我都告诉您，先生。"她说得平稳，没有一丝惊讶。

"那么，凭你的全部所知——我指的不止是他做园丁的手艺——你以为雇下他，是件好事吗？

"您为什么这样问，先生？

"这个由你去想。这件事，兴许比人此刻所能意识的更要紧——眼下还看不出来。"末几个字，他说得慢了一点。

"那么，是，先生——只要您想要一座米莉森特先生那样的园子。

这答话的干练叫他一怔。"叫他进来。"他简短地说。

---
===Original===
The man entered, the man whose dark features had peered through the parted curtains a short hour before. He was powerfully built, very broad, and dressed in loose and much worn tweeds of a foreign cut. He came forward with the lurching walk of a seafaring trade, a colored handkerchief twisted round the column of his brown neck. His swinging hands were wide and knotted, and every motion spoke of great physical strength. No mere Sussex gardener this, who had spent his placid years among his roses and dahlias, but one who carried with him nameless suggestions of the jungle and the faint pounding of distant surf. Dangling his cap, he gave a sort of salute, making at the same time a swift survey of the room. From this furtive and searching glance it seemed to Derrick that the man missed something he knew of old in Millicent’s time, but no flicker of change of expression could be discerned on the weather-beaten face. The face itself was neither cruel nor merciless but conveyed a grim, implacable resolution. Here, reflected Derrick, was the man who disappeared three days after Millicent’s death. What brought him back now?

===Chinese===
进来的人，正是一个钟头前从掀开的帘缝里窥望的那张黑脸。他身板雄壮，极其宽阔，穿一身宽大破旧、外国式剪裁的粗花呢。他迈着跑海行当那种一颠一颠的步子走上前来，棕色的脖柱上缠着一条花手帕。他那双摆动的手又宽又多骨节，一举一动都透着极大的膂力。这决不是萨塞克斯（Sussex）的寻常园丁——在玫瑰和大丽花间消磨了太平岁月的那种；他随身带着丛林的无名暗示，带着远处涛声隐约的擂击。他把帽子悬在手里晃着，行了个近似敬礼的礼，同时把房间飞快扫视了一遍。从这一瞥的鬼祟与搜求里，德里克觉出，这人没寻到一样米莉森特在时他熟识的东西；可那张饱经风霜的脸上，辨不出一丝一毫的神色变化。那张脸本身既不残忍，也不冷酷，却传达着一种森然的、绝不通融的决意。这就是那个人，德里克想，米莉森特死后三天便消失的人。如今又是什么把他带回来的呢？

===Original===
“What is your name?”

“Martin, sir, John Martin.” The voice was deep and husky.

“Perkins tells me you were in Mr. Millicent’s service.”

“Yes, sir, for some years after his last trip to the East.”

“Did you come from the East with him?”

“No, sir, I—I was engaged here at Beech Lodge.”

===Chinese===
"你叫什么名字？

"马丁，先生，约翰·马丁（John Martin）。"嗓音低沉而沙哑。

"珀金斯告诉我，你伺候过米莉森特先生。

"是的，先生，伺候了几年，打他最后一趟去东方回来起。

"你是同他一道从东方来的吗？

"不是，先生，我——我是在这山毛榉山庄受的雇。

===Original===
“Several years service, yet you left three days after your employer died?”

Martin jerked up his head. “Yes, sir; that’s it.”

“How did you happen to go so quickly? Were you discharged by Mrs. Millicent?”

A dull flush rose in the tanned face. “You might as well ask how my master happened to die three days before I left, sir. Mrs. Millicent was giving up Beech Lodge and didn’t want a gardener. There was no other job in sight about here, and I couldn’t afford to hang on in the village.”

===Chinese===
"伺候了几年，主人一死，你三天就走？

马丁猛地扬起头。"是，先生；就是这样。

"你怎么走得这样快？是米莉森特太太辞的你吗？

晒黑的脸上涌起一股暗红。"您这样问，不如问问我的主人怎么恰好在我走的前三天死了，先生。米莉森特太太要放弃山毛榉山庄，用不着园丁了。这一带眼前也没有别的活路，我又耗不起，赖在村子里。

===Original===
Derrick nodded with seeming carelessness. “Perhaps that’s fair enough, and as it happens I do want a gardener, but you’ll have to satisfy me completely on all points before I consider you. The circumstances are a bit out of the ordinary.”

“I’m ready to tell you anything I can, sir.”

“Then where do you come from now?”

“Upper Burma, by way of Canada. I have a sister in Alberta.” He fumbled in his pocket. “Would you be wanting to see my passport?”

===Chinese===
德里克看似随意地点了点头。"这话或许说得过去。赶巧我也正要一个园丁，可在用你之前，你得样样都叫我完全满意。眼下的情形，有点不比寻常。

"凡我能说的，我都愿意告诉您，先生。

"那么，你这回是从哪儿来的？

"上缅甸（Upper Burma），打加拿大转过来。我有个妹妹在艾伯塔（Alberta）。"他在口袋里摸索。"您要不要看我的护照？

===Original===
“Not now, at any rate. I don’t understand why you should clear out of Sussex for Burma just because there was no job close at hand.”

“Well, sir, to tell the truth, I was that upset I wanted to get away as far as possible. I couldn’t put the master out of my head. He’d always been good to me from the first day I came, and we liked the same things, sir.”

“What was that?”

“Roses.”

He shot this out with rumbling assurance, and, strange as it sounded, Derrick believed him. It was difficult to picture this great hulk among the roses, these thick fingers training the delicate buds, but Martin’s reputation had already been established far beyond Beech Lodge.
