"好啦，这桩事办完了！"他搓着手说。"园子的事，我再没有不知道的了。瑟斯比在这地方一定花过一大笔钱。怪的是他竟舍得不住，因为某种意义上，他像是仍旧恋着它。怪人，那一位。他对自己的那些改良，口气几乎是抱歉的。不管怎样，我们到底整个儿入主了。

===Original===
“That’s just what I feel, and, Jack, I do hope it will be just what you want.”

“It is absolutely. I know that already, if you don’t find it too slow and remote. I’m a bit guilty on that score. I suppose there are some of the right sort in the neighborhood, and the Millicents are not far off. Did you learn anything satisfactory about that maid?”

“Yes,” she said slowly. “Mrs. Thursby’s report is that she’s very competent and trustworthy and possibly willing to do the whole thing herself. So I think I’ll keep her if she’ll stay.”

“Good. I thought you would. A bit out of the common, that woman.”

The door opened as he spoke, and Perkins came in with the tray. The two glanced at each other, and watched her silently. The long, deft fingers moved with a sort of definite precision, lingering over the silver as though the touch of it conveyed an actual pleasure. This deliberate procedure was marked by a noiseless precision. One could not imagine a woman like this making a mistake. Her face, absolutely impassive, betrayed nothing. While she was in the room she seemed part of it, and from her there spread something that almost suggested ownership. Then she went out, as silent as themselves. Derrick sat up.

===Chinese===
"我正是这个感觉。还有，杰克，我一心盼它正合你的心意。

"完全合。这我早就知道——只要你嫌它不够热闹、太偏僻。这一层我有点心虚。左近总有些对路的人家吧，米莉森特家又住得不远。那个女仆，你打听到什么称心的结果没有？

"打听到了，"她缓缓地说，"瑟斯比太太的说法是：她极能干、靠得住，兴许还愿意一个人担起全部活计。所以只要她肯留，我就想留下她。

"好。我料到你会。那个女人，有点不同寻常。

他正说着，门开了，珀金斯端着托盘进来。兄妹俩对视一眼，默然看她。那双修长灵巧的手带着一种确凿的精准动作着，在银器上流连，仿佛那触感本身就递过来一阵实际的愉快。这道从容的程序，处处是无声的精确。这样的女人会出岔子，是想也想不出的。她的脸绝对纹丝不动，不露一丝内情。她在屋里的当儿，就像是屋子的一部分，从她身上散出一种近乎主人家的气派。随后她出去了，同他们自己一样无声。德里克坐直了身子。

===Original===
“By George!” he said softly.

“What is it, Jack?”

He laughed. “Hanged if I know yet; something in the air. Probably it’s only the new and rather ideal surroundings that set one’s fancy going. You don’t feel anything, do you?”

“Only that I want my tea dreadfully. I had quite a talk with Mrs. Thursby.”

“What sort is she? Like her husband?”

“I should think so. Limited, you know, but doesn’t put on airs and is very honest apparently. She actually said that Perkins made her feel like an intruder but that it would be different with us. She says we suit Beech Lodge better than they did. It was rather pathetic.”

===Chinese===
"老天！"他轻声说。

"怎么，杰克？

他笑了。"鬼知道呢——眼下还说不上来；空气里有点什么。多半不过是这崭新的、过分理想的周遭，把人的想头撩拨起来了。你没觉出什么来吧？

"只觉得我可想茶想得要命。我同瑟斯比太太谈了不小的一阵。

"她是个什么样的人？像她丈夫？

"我看像。眼界有限，你知道，可不摆架子，而且看得出非常诚实。她亲口说，珀金斯弄得她觉得自己像个闯进来的外人，对你们就会两样。她说，我们比他们更配山毛榉山庄。这话听着叫人有点心酸。

===Original===
He put down his cup. “I can easily imagine that. The people who modernized Beech Lodge are our own sort and have a good deal in common with us. For instance, when the Thursbys picked up the place I don’t believe they were meant to, or expected. It’s different now. We were. I knew that as soon as I stepped into the hall.”

“Don’t be absurd, Jack! Expected by whom?”

“Perkins, for one, and no doubt by other people, or things; it doesn’t matter which, but I’m sure of it.”

“Jack,” she protested. “You’re rambling!”

“Well,” he answered slowly, “you just remember this talk, and see. We blend with the place, we’re suitable and acceptable, while the Thursbys were not. That’s obvious at a glance, and they certainly felt it themselves.”

“But how could we be expected by anyone who didn’t know us? You can’t explain that.”

===Chinese===
他放下茶杯。"这我很容易想象。把山毛榉山庄翻修一新的人家，原是同我们一样的人家，同我们共通的地方也多。可比如说，瑟斯比家当初买下这地方，我不信那是命定的，也不信有谁盼着他们来。如今就不同了：我们是。我一踏进门厅，就知道了。

"别犯傻，杰克！被谁盼着？

"珀金斯算一个，多半还有别的人，或者别的东西；是人是物不打紧，可我拿得稳。

"杰克，"她抗议道，"你越说越没边儿了！

"喏，"他缓缓地答道，"你只管记住今天这番话，往后看。我们同这地方相融，我们合宜、被接纳；瑟斯比家不然。这是一眼就看得出的，他们自己也分明觉出来了。

"可是，一个不认识我们的人，怎么会盼着我们来？这个你可解释不了。

===Original===
He looked at her with sudden gravity. “Did you never have a curious sensation that you were doing things for the second time?”

“Now you’re joking. Have some more tea?”

“No,” he said, “I’m not, and there’s no explanation for it. In fact I’ve an idea that they’re not meant to be explained; at least not yet. But I felt it the minute I got here.”

“But, Jack,” she protested, “you saw the house; you liked it, especially as you couldn’t quite afford it; and of course you were impulsive and took it. What has that to do with a servant, or anyone else?”

“Perhaps nothing whatever. It’s a wonderful place to work in.”

“I think that’s the best way to look at it. What did Mr. Thursby talk about?”

===Chinese===
他忽然郑重地望着她。"你从来不曾有过那种古怪的感觉——觉得自己眼下做的事，是第二遍做？

"你这是开玩笑。再喝一点茶吗？

"不，"他说，"我没有开玩笑，这也没有解释可讲。说实在的，我有种想法：这一类事，本就不打算叫人解释；至少眼下不。可我一到这儿，就觉出来了。

"可是杰克，"她抗议道，"你看见了这宅子；你喜欢它，尤其因为你还不大租得起它；你自然是一时冲动，把它租了下来。这同一个女仆、同一个别的什么人，有什么相干？

"兴许毫不相干。这是个干活的好地方。

"我看，这是看待它最好的法子了。瑟斯比先生都谈了些什么？

===Original===
“Mostly roses and mulch.” He broke off suddenly, regarding his sister with an intense and puzzled expression. “I’ve an extraordinary impression that someone died in this room not long ago; someone who didn’t want to die and wasn’t ready for it.”

“What do you mean?” she stammered. “Please, Jack, don’t go off on that tack the very day we reach here. You’ll never get anything done.”

“I mean just that; I’m perfectly sure someone did. Perkins will know, and, I say, perhaps that’s what﻿—”

“Jack,” she interrupted hastily, “please leave Perkins to me. When Mrs. Thursby was here she said that there was a sudden death in this room about two years ago, and﻿—”

“Millicent?” he shot out.

===Chinese===
"多半是玫瑰和护根。"他蓦地打住，带着强烈而困惑的神情端详妹妹。"我有个非同寻常的印象：不久以前，有人死在了这间屋里；一个不想死、也没有预备好死的人。

"你这是什么意思？"她结结巴巴地说。"求你，杰克，别在我们到的头一天，就往这条岔道上跑。你往后可什么都做不成了。

"我就是这个意思；我确信有人死了。珀金斯会知道的，我说，兴许那就是——

"杰克，"她急忙打断，"珀金斯的事交给我。瑟斯比太太在这儿的时候说了，大约两年前，这间屋里出过一桩猝死，还有——

"米莉森特？"他脱口而出。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said helplessly.

“Murdered?”

“I assumed that. He was found at his desk. Mrs. Thursby seemed to want to say more, and yet not want to.” Miss Derrick paused, aware of her brother’s penetrating gaze. He would soon know it all in any case, and perhaps it was wisest to clear the air as much as possible at the outset.

“Now I understand why the rental asked was so low,” she continued. “The Thursbys simply got frightened. But I’m astonished you asked no questions on your account.”

He shook his head and stared at the portrait. “The questions will come later on. I haven’t got them ready yet. By the way, Edith, that’s Millicent over the fireplace. He’s been trying to tell me something ever since we came into the house; what you call a speaking likeness. Now I’ve got it, and he’s trying to smile.”

===Chinese===
"是。"她无助地说。

"是被人谋杀的？

"我是这样推想的。他伏在书桌上被人发现。瑟斯比太太像还有话要说，又像不愿说。"德里克小姐顿了顿，察觉哥哥正目光逼人。横竖他不久全会知道；或许起头就把空气涤荡得越干净越好。

"如今我明白，租金为什么那样低了。"她接着说，"瑟斯比家简直是吓跑的。可你竟一句也没替自己问过，这叫我吃惊。

他摇了摇头，盯住那幅画像。"问题往后再来。我还没有把它们备齐呢。对了，伊迪丝，壁炉上头那一位，就是米莉森特。打我们进宅起，他一直试着要告诉我什么；就是你说的那种会说话的画像。这会儿我得着了：他在试着笑。

===Original===
“I wish you wouldn’t go on like that, Jack. Please don’t.”

“It’s nothing in the world to be nervous about. This sort of thing is going on all the time around all of us. Some see it, and others don’t.”

“But how did you know?” she asked nervously.

“Can’t tell you that; it’s not a matter of reason or information. Some people call it the influence of the inanimate, which is rather a bald way of putting it. I’ve got the idea that it’s the permanence of things that are universally put down as lost, or at any rate as only transient. Just imagine, for instance, that nothing is really lost, but that everything, every act and motion, and even word, is registered in some kind of extraordinarily delicate vibration, so delicate that it is quite imperceptible to the average person. But the record is there nevertheless; in fact the entire universe is throbbing and quivering with such records that he who can may read, or at least perceive. Go a little further and admit that the more tense the act or word the more keen the pitch of the ethereal record, and one begins to appreciate what is really implied by what we call coincidence, and how it is that often, after many years, mysteries are solved that long baffled any approach to solution. It really means that someone was sensitive enough to decipher the record that was always there. I’ve an idea it may turn out like that in the case of Millicent. And when you ask me how I knew someone died suddenly in this room, I can’t answer in any other way than this. I just knew; that’s all.”

===Chinese===
"你别这样讲下去了，杰克。求你别讲了。

"天底下没有什么好紧张的。这种事，时时刻刻在我们大家身边进行着。有人看得见，有人看不见，如此而已。

"可你是怎么知道的？"她不安地问。

"说不上来；这不是讲道理、讲见闻的事。有人管它叫无生命之物的影响——这说法未免太秃。我得到的想法是：那些被世人一概认作丢失了的东西，或者至少认作转瞬即逝的东西，其实长存不灭。只要这样想象一下：没有一样东西真会丢失；每一件事、每一个动作、乃至每一句话，都以某种精微至极的震动记录在案，精微到寻常人全然无从察觉。可记录总在那里；事实上，整个宇宙都带着这样的记录在震颤悸动，谁有本事，谁就可以读，至少可以感知。再进一步，承认那一桩事、那一句话越是紧张，这道空灵记录的音调就越尖，人便开始领会，我们所谓巧合真正意味着什么；多少年来悬而不决、无从下手的谜案，为何常常在多年之后得解。这实际等于说，有人敏感到足以破译那份始终都在的记录。我有种想法，米莉森特这案子，或许就会这样见分晓。所以你问我，怎么知道有人在这间屋里暴死，我只能这样回答：我就是知道；仅此而已。

===Original===
Edith felt utterly confused. She was a practical girl, with a healthy dislike of anything that might upset the normal progress of everyday affairs, and for years had stood between her brother and the drab realities of life, in order that his fancy might have untrammeled swing. Imagination, either on her own part or that of others, had never heretofore caused her any discomfort. She admitted its value, but the process by which it worked was beyond her. Now, however, she experienced a sudden distaste for her new surroundings. Derrick’s eyes had taken on an intense, far-pitched stare as though he were probing things beyond her own ken. He seemed to be moving away from her.

“I wonder if I’m going to like this house,” she hazarded.

He pulled himself together and laughed. “Buck up, old thing, and you mustn’t mind if I wander a bit. It’s too late to take exceptions after signing a year’s lease.”

She glanced at him seriously and a little anxiously. “It’s only that you’ve been in a sort of half-world ever since we got here. Now I must settle this matter of Perkins.”

“Right! And I’ve got to find a gardener. And look here, Edith; speaking of half-worlds, isn’t it possible that that’s about all we get in any case﻿—the obvious half?”

“Don’t be so introspective, and see if you can’t find something cheerful outside. And, Jack, will you ask Perkins to see me here?”

===Chinese===
伊迪丝觉得全然糊涂了。她是个讲实际的姑娘，本着一种健康的厌恶，讨厌一切可能搅乱日常事务正常进程的东西；多年来她挡在哥哥同生活里灰扑扑的现实之间，好让他的幻想得以无拘无束地摆荡。想象力，不论是她自己的还是旁人的，此前从不曾叫她为难过。她承认它的价值，可它运作的门道，她参不透。如今，她却对新近的周遭生出一种突兀的嫌恶。德里克的眼睛带上了一种灼热而渺远的凝望，仿佛正在探测她自己眼界之外的东西。他像是在离她而去。

"不知我还喜不喜欢这座宅子。"她试着说。

他定住神，笑了。"打起精神，老伙计；我信马由缰的时候，你可别见怪。一年的租约都签了，再挑三拣四，也迟了。

她郑重而略带忧虑地望了他一眼。"只是打我们到这儿，你一直像待在半个世界里。眼下，我得把珀金斯的事定下来。

"对！我还得寻个园丁。还有，听着，伊迪丝；说起半个世界——我们到头来拿到手的，会不会本来就只有那一半：显见的那一半？

"别这么一味内省了，去看看外头能不能寻着点叫人高兴的东西。还有，杰克，麻烦你去叫珀金斯到这儿来见我。

===Original===
He kissed her and strolled to the door. “If I may make a foolish manlike suggestion it would be that when you’re talking to Perkins you try to imagine this place without her. I’ve tried and failed. I’ll send her in.”

She sat for a moment, deep in thought, till very soon it seemed there was nothing to be anxious about after all. Her brother’s fanciful mind had merely unearthed something which he must inevitably have discovered before long. The mystery might hold him for a few days, till his restless imagination moved on elsewhere. It had always been like that in the past. The fact that Millicent died here two years ago could mean nothing to new tenants. All houses were built to live and die in. Beech Lodge was charming and well arranged, and they had leased it on nominal terms. It was true that the terms were, perhaps, suspiciously nominal, but she pushed this thought aside to make room for others more helpful and constructive. She confessed to being piqued with herself for giving any evidence of discomfort, and would in future take less notice of her brother’s whimsical ideas. Then she looked up and saw Perkins.

“You sent for me, madam?”

Miss Derrick regarded her with absorbed interest. How old was this woman? At first appearance she seemed never to have been young, but her smooth skin and straight figure suggested that she could not be much past forty. It was the grave, inscrutable face that baffled. It carried no trace of expression and revealed no play of the mind. In the dark eyes moved a kind of secret light, quickening at times into a fleeting gleam that was instantly extinguished. In these moments Perkins appeared to receive communications from a source privy to herself, messages that illumined a nature of which the outer world knew but little; and, save for these occasional and passing glimpses, her face was like a mask. Miss Derrick, held for an instant voiceless by something she could not understand, wondered what sort of private life had been led by a woman who looked like this. The pause lengthened, but Perkins stood, passive and undisturbed.

===Chinese===
他吻了吻她，踱到门口。"倘若容我提一个愚蠢的、男人气的建议，那就是：你同珀金斯说话的时候，试试想象这地方没有她。我试过，不成。我叫她进来。

她坐了一会儿，沉在思索里，很快便觉得，到底没有什么可焦心的。哥哥那颗多幻的心，不过是掘出了一件他早晚注定要发现的东西。这桩谜也许会抓他几天，等他那不安分的想象挪往别处去。从前一向如此。米莉森特两年前死在这里，这个事实对新租客毫无意义。天下的宅子造出来，本就是为了在里面生老病死。山毛榉山庄可爱而妥帖，他们租下的条件低得近乎象征。不错，那条件也许低得可疑地近乎象征；可她把这念头推到一旁，好给那些更有益、更有建树的念头腾地方。她承认，方才露出不安的形迹，自己有点着恼，往后要少去理会哥哥那些古怪念头。随后她抬起头，看见了珀金斯。

"太太打发人叫我了？

德里克小姐怀着入神的兴味打量她。这女人多大年纪？乍一看，她仿佛从来不曾年轻过；可她光滑的皮肤、笔直的身段，又表明她至多四十出头。叫人无从捉摸的，是那张沉静莫测的脸。它不带一丝表情的痕迹，也不显露一点心思的动静。那双黑眼睛里游动着一星隐秘的光，时而一旺，化作一道转瞬即灭的闪。在这样的时刻，珀金斯像是正从一个只有她自己知道的源头领受音信，那些音信照亮了一种外界知之甚少的天性；除却这些偶尔一现、一现即逝的窥见，她的脸便是一副面具。德里克小姐让一种自己无从索解的东西霎时夺去了言语，纳闷一个长着这样一张脸的女人，过的会是怎样一种私生活。停顿拖长了，珀金斯却只管立着，被动而安然。

===Original===
“I’ve had a talk with Mrs. Thursby,” said Edith rather stiffly, “and she mentioned you. It was quite satisfactory.”

“Yes, madam.”

The flatness of her tone announced that it was immaterial what Mrs. Thursby might have said. Obviously the latter meant nothing to Perkins. There was no superiority in her manner; just a total lack of interest.

“So if you would like to stay now, I would be very glad to have you.”

Perkins’s thin lips moved ever so slightly, and the faintest trace of a smile flitted over the blank features. She made a little gesture that put her late employer definitely out of the reckoning.

===Chinese===
"我同瑟斯比太太谈了一场，"伊迪丝相当生硬地说，"她说起了您。谈得十分圆满。

"是，太太。

她那平板的语气等于宣布：瑟斯比太太爱说什么，全不相干。那位太太在珀金斯心里，明摆着等于零。她的做派里没有一丝居高临下；只有全然的兴味索然。

"那么，您要是现在愿意留下，我有您就太好了。

珀金斯薄薄的嘴唇极轻微地动了动，一痕几乎看不见的微笑掠过那张空白似的脸。她做了个小手势，把她先前那位雇主，干脆逐出了盘算之外。

===Original===
“I always stay, madam,” she said quietly.

Edith stared at her. “Why always? I don’t quite understand.”

“I came here to Mrs. Millicent, and”﻿—here there was again the ghost of a smile﻿—“I even stayed with Mrs. Thursby, and I’m quite willing to stay with you. People come and go, but nothing has really changed.”

This announcement was made with such calmness that Miss Derrick found herself for a moment robbed of speech. Whoever came or went, this woman would always be at Beech Lodge, no more detachable than the roof which covered it. Jack had suggested that his sister try to imagine the place without Perkins, and now she saw what he meant. She began to recognize herself as part of a procession which passed before the sphinx-like eyes of this house-parlor-maid, a procession to which the woman ministered in order that she might live, but to which she revealed no fraction of her inner self. It was strange to be thus classified. But what was the alternative?

“I am glad you are so fond of the house,” she said uncertainly; “and now it comes to a matter of wages.”

===Chinese===
"我一向留着的，太太。"她平静地说。

伊迪丝瞪着她。"为什么「一向」？我不大明白。

"我是来伺候米莉森特太太的，还有"——说到这里，又浮起一丝笑的影子——"我连瑟斯比太太那儿都留了下来，如今也十分愿意陪你们。人来人往，可什么都不曾真的变过。

这话说得这样平静，德里克小姐一时竟被夺去了言语。无论谁来、谁去，这女人总会在山毛榉山庄，同覆盖宅子的屋顶一样拆不走。杰克曾劝妹妹试试想象这地方没有珀金斯；如今她懂了他的意思。她开始认出，她自己不过是一支队伍里的一员，打这位宅子的客厅女仆那双斯芬克司般的眼睛跟前鱼贯而过；这女人伺候这支队伍，为的是活下去，却不向它显露一星半点内心。被人这样归类，滋味古怪。可不然，又有什么路呢？

"您这样爱这座宅子，我很高兴，"她拿不稳地说，"那么，接下来就说到工钱吧。

===Original===
Perkins’s eyes wandered to the portrait over the mantel. Wages, it seemed, were the last thing in her mind. “There will be no difficulty about that, madam.”

Miss Derrick leaned forward involuntarily. “I don’t quite understand. They are very important, to me.”

“I mean, madam, that I don’t ask for high wages.”

Miss Derrick, though greatly puzzled, breathed a sigh of relief. “The most I can pay is forty pounds a year. And of course there’s a cook to be found. Can you help me there?”

Perkins’s face softened a shade. “Forty pounds will be quite sufficient, and you will not need a cook.”

===Chinese===
珀金斯的眼睛游到炉台上方的画像上。工钱，看样子，是她心里最末一样东西。"这一层不会有困难的，太太。

德里克小姐不由自主地朝前欠了欠身。"我不大明白。工钱很要紧——对我。

"我是说，太太，我不讨高工钱。

德里克小姐虽然大惑不解，却松了口气。"我至多付得起一年四十镑。当然，还得寻个厨子。这一层您能帮我吗？

珀金斯的脸柔和了一分。"四十镑尽够了，您也用不着厨子。

===Original===
“But are you sure you can do it all?” Miss Derrick felt distinctly bewildered.

“Yes, madam.” The woman said this with so complete a finality that the subject closed forthwith. It was something more than mere competency. There was no spark of animation in her expression. Her attitude suggested that while household duties were unavoidable they were also of a secondary character, and the conversation was becoming pointless. Edith wondered whether some personal tragedy were not hidden behind this immutable barrier and experienced a throb of sympathy at the narrowness of such a life.

“You see, Perkins, I realize that you are taking on a good deal of work. You must not overtax yourself.”

“It is only work of the hands, madam.”

===Chinese===
"可您拿得准，一个人担得完全？"德里克小姐觉得实实在在地糊涂了。

"拿得准，太太。"这女人把话说得斩钉截铁，再无余地，话题当即终结。这不单是胜任与否的事。她的表情里没有一点活气。她的态度表明：家务虽则免不了，却也只属第二等；这场谈话正变得不着边际。伊迪丝纳闷，这道恒常不移的屏障后面，是不是藏着一桩私人的惨剧；又为这样窄仄的一条命，感到一阵怜悯的悸动。

"您要知道，珀金斯，我明白，您这是把一副很重的担子担上了。千万别把自己累坏。

"这不过是手上的活，太太。

===Original===
The new mistress of Beech Lodge shifted her ground hastily. “Is this house very old?”

“This room is the oldest part; about two hundred years, I think.”

“You must have got very fond of the place.”

The woman looked slowly about. Her lips were slightly parted, and her eyes were full of shadows, like the eyes of those who know exactly what they are about to see. Something might have been passing from her to those mellow panels in exchange for some other communication she was drawing from them.

“I have been here for eight years, madam; and it may be that the place has got fond of me.” She said this with a subtle change in her tone, as though for an instant she had lifted a corner of a curtain in order to test the other woman’s perception of what lay beyond.

===Chinese===
山毛榉山庄的新主妇赶忙换了阵地。"这宅子很老吗？

"这间屋是最老的一部分；我想，约莫两百年了。

"您必定十分爱这地方了。

那女人缓缓地四下里望。她的双唇微微启着，眼里满是暗影，像那些确切知道自己将要看见什么的人的眼睛。仿佛有什么正从她这里传递给那些温润的护壁板，以换取她正从它们那里抽取的另一宗音信。

"我在这儿八年了，太太；兴许，是这地方喜欢上我了。"她说这话时，声调里带着一丝微妙的变化，仿佛有一瞬，她掀起了一幅帷幕的一角，来掂一掂对面这个女人，对帷幕后头的东西识得多少。

===Original===
“I can’t quite follow you there, Perkins.”

“No, madam? It doesn’t matter.”

Miss Derrick remembered what Mrs. Thursby had said about wanting to tell the woman to shout out whatever was in the back of her head and have done with it. It was understandable now, and she felt the same desire. The difficulty was going to be to regard Perkins simply as a maid and not a creature of mystery. Again she tried to think of Beech Lodge without her, and again she failed.

“I’m afraid you must have been very lonely here, especially after Mr. Thursby left.”

“I was never alone, madam. That is﻿—” She broke off in strange confusion. “I never feel lonely.”

Miss Derrick shivered in spite of herself. She perceived something now; but it was only a curtain, with no suggestion of what was behind. A thought darted through her brain. She recalled the strange manner of Mrs. Thursby, her restlessness, her obvious desire to get away, especially from this room. Mrs. Thursby had felt like an intruder, that round-faced, good-natured, unimaginative woman. Perhaps all were intruders here except Perkins, even Millicent himself. At the recollection of Millicent her pulse faltered. Perhaps that was why Millicent had been﻿—She forced herself to speak evenly.

===Chinese===
"这一层我跟不大上，珀金斯。

"是吗，太太？不要紧。

德里克小姐想起瑟斯比太太说过的那句话：恨不得叫这女人把后脑里憋着的东西喊出个一了百了。如今这话可懂了；她也起了同样的渴望。难就难在，要把珀金斯单单当作一个女仆看，别当作一个谜一样的生灵。她又试着去想没有珀金斯的山毛榉山庄，又一次失败了。

"只怕您在这里一定非常孤单，尤其是瑟斯比先生走了以后。

"我从来不曾独自一个，太太。这就是——"她古怪地乱了一乱，说不下去。"我从不觉得孤单。

德里克小姐不由自主地打了个寒噤。她如今觉出点什么了；可那只是一道帷幕，对幕后的事，没有一点暗示。一个念头从她脑子里射过。她想起瑟斯比太太那古怪的做派、那坐立不安、那分明想走的心思，尤其是想离开这间屋子的心思。瑟斯比太太——那个圆脸、好心肠、毫无想象力的女人——尚且觉得自己像个闯入的人。或许在这儿，人人都是闯入的人，唯独珀金斯不是，连米莉森特本人也是。一想起米莉森特，她的脉搏乱了一拍。或许，那正是米莉森特所以——她强使自己，匀匀地说下去。

===Original===
“Perkins, will you please be quite candid with me and say whether you have ever seen anything in this house which﻿—which should not be here?”

The black eyes rounded. “I do not understand.”

“Have you ever seen what you thought was a ghost? I know there are no such things, but some people think they see them.”

“There are no ghosts here, madam.” She shook her head slowly. “I would know if there were.”

===Chinese===
"珀金斯，可否请你对我完全坦白，说说你可曾在这宅子里见过什么——什么不该在这儿的东西？

黑眼睛睁圆了。"我不明白。

"你可曾见过什么你以为是鬼的东西？我知道没有鬼这种东西，可有些人自以为看见了。

"这儿没有鬼，太太。"她缓缓摇头。"要有，我会知道。

===Original===
“Then will you explain what you meant when you said you were never alone?”

Perkins made the same slight indefinite gesture. “I’m sorry I said that, madam, and it was foolish of me. It’s only my fancy and doesn’t mean anything. Perhaps it’s my way of filling up empty hours, and sometimes I say things without thinking. You surprised me, because Mrs. Thursby never asked me any questions like that.”

Miss Derrick pulled herself together. “Well, Perkins, perhaps you’re quite right, and it really doesn’t matter. I suppose it’s the strange house and the feeling of not being settled that makes one curious about all kinds of things. When you’ve had your tea please come upstairs, and I’ll show you about the linen. Also I hope you’ll help me all you can to make matters go smoothly in the house, on account of Mr. Derrick’s writing. It’s important he should be disturbed as little as possible. And,” she added genially, “please don’t fill your head with fancies about never having been alone.”

===Chinese===
"那么你可否解释一下：你说你从来不曾独自一个，是什么意思？

珀金斯又做了那个同样轻微而含糊的手势。"说了那话，我抱歉，太太；那是我糊涂。那不过是我的想头，不作数的。兴许是我填补空空时辰的法子；有时候，我说话不加思索。您吓了我一跳，因为瑟斯比太太从没问过我这样的话。

德里克小姐定住神。"好吧，珀金斯，兴许你全然是对的，这实在无关紧要。我想，是这座古怪的宅子，加上还没安顿下来的那点感觉，叫人对种种事都起了好奇。你用完茶，请上楼来，我带你看看被褥家什。还有，望你凡事帮衬着点儿，让宅子里的事顺顺当当，看在德里克先生写作的份上。顶要紧的，是尽量别去搅扰他。还有，"她和气地添了一句，"可别再拿什么「从来不曾独自一个」的想头，去填你的脑子了。

## V The Paper-Knife / 第五章 裁纸刀

===Original===

The village of Bamberley lay about two miles from Beech Lodge, a homelike nest of buildings gathered in a wrinkle of the Sussex hills. It was well removed from any main road, and its thatched roofs and crooked cobbled streets had fortunately escaped the demoralizing finger of progress. It was, in fact, just as it had always been in the memory of its oldest inhabitant. A village green, with the pens of the cattle market just across the road, a rambling public house, whose swinging sign creaked cheerily when the wind was high, a few diminutive shops, the contents of which were huddled in the meadows, perhaps a hundred cottages, a dozen more pretentious buildings dominated by the village institute﻿—and then the encircling hills, velvet and brown and wide, patched with irregular coverts and dotted as far as the eye could reach with farmhouse and barn.

Bamberley happened to be the most important of four adjoining villages; so here were the police headquarters of that utterly rural district. It was a neat brick building with the local jail immediately behind, standing where the crossroads provided the main interest in life.

The road from Beech Lodge climbs the crown of a low hill ere it dips into the village; and Derrick, as he strolled toward the station and looked down on all this, thought he had never seen anything so peaceful.

The sergeant, a large, ruddy-faced, cylindrical man, greeted him with undisguised interest, and Derrick lost no time in getting to the point. They talked in the tiny office, which seemed filled by the other man’s bulk. Derrick knew what he wanted, for this visit had occasioned him much thought. He was aware, too, that minor officials in isolated places were apt to regard with a jealous eye anything that might infringe on their position and privilege. It was at once obvious that the sergeant felt an added sense of responsibility when the visitor asked if he might read the official documents in the Millicent case.

===Chinese===

班伯利村（Bamberley）坐落在离山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge）约两英里的地方，是一窝家园似的屋舍，拢在萨塞克斯（Sussex）丘陵的一道褶皱里。它离哪条大路都远，茅草的屋顶和歪歪扭扭的鹅卵石街巷，侥幸逃过了「进步」那根败兴的手指。事实上，在村中年纪最老的住户的记忆里，它一向就是这副模样。一片村中草坪，一路之隔便是牲口市场的围栏，栏里的牲口挤挤挨挨簇在草场上；一家东歪西斜的酒馆，风紧的时候，悬着的招牌伊呀作响，响得欢畅；几间小铺子；大约百来座村舍；还有十来栋更气派的屋子，以村民会馆为首——再往外，便是环抱的群山了：天鹅绒似的，棕黄而开阔，缀着一块块不规整的树丛，目力所及，星罗棋布全是农舍与谷仓。

班伯利恰是毗邻四村之中最要紧的一个；于是这一片十足乡野之地的警察总部便设在了这里。那是一栋齐齐整整的砖楼，本地拘留所紧贴其后，立在十字路口上——那路口，便是此地生活里头一等的热闹。

从山毛榉山庄来的路，先爬上一座小丘的丘顶，再俯身探进村里；德里克（Derrick）缓步走向警局，一路俯瞰着这一切，心想从没见过这样太平的景象。

巡佐生得高大，红脸膛，圆桶似的一个人，迎候他时毫不掩饰心头的兴致；德里克也不耽搁，开门见山便入了正题。两人在那间小办公室里谈起来，那屋子像是全叫对方的块头填满了。德里克知道自己要的是什么，因为这一趟来访，害他前思后想了许久。他也明白，偏居一隅的小吏，凡遇可能触犯其地位与特权的事，惯于拿一只戒备的眼睛看人。来客一问能否调阅米莉森特（Millicent）案的官方文书，巡佐顿觉肩上多了一重责任，这是一望即知的。

===Original===

Burke had been prepared by the constable for Derrick’s coming, and during the past few weeks had chafed at his delay. He wanted to talk about the Millicent case more than anything else in the world. It was the biggest and most baffling puzzle in his career, and for a day or so the eyes of England had turned curiously toward Bamberley. After the inquest they turned away to the next sensation, leaving the police force of that tiny community with the stinging sensation of having fallen grievously short. Since then, Burke, feeling his position more than he would ever admit, had explored every avenue that presented itself to his methodical mind. And always with the same result. Now, after nearly two years of silence, the thing was up again, brought up by a complete stranger who had actually taken into his employ the man suspected of the crime, against whom no definite charge could be laid. Burke secretly wondered whether by any chance Derrick and his gardener knew each other a good deal better than appeared on the surface of things. This was undoubtedly a matter for caution.

“The point is, sir,” he said slowly, “that I have no authority to tell you anything whatever, unless it is clear that the law may be aided thereby, and you have supplementary evidence with a direct bearing on the case.”

Derrick nodded. “I quite understand, sergeant, and that’s entirely reasonable. Would you sooner I looked up the counsel acting for the crown at the inquest? I’m quite willing, if you’d rather not talk about it.”

Burke reflected. He did not want to lose anything that might help himself, nor did he want to go beyond his boundaries. There was probably nothing here, but he could not get the reappearance of Martin out of his head. He had walked past the cottage at Beech Lodge only the week before and had a look at the man. Martin had nodded coolly and gone on with his work. A hard man, any way one took him.

“Please yourself, sir, about that; but if you’ll tell me what’s in your mind perhaps it won’t be necessary to go any further.”

===Chinese===

德里克要来，警员早给伯克巡佐（Sergeant Burke）递过信，过去几个星期里，他等他等得心焦。天底下他最想谈的，莫过于米莉森特一案。那是他职业生涯里最大、最无从下手的一桩谜题，曾有一两天的工夫，全英格兰的眼睛都好奇地转向了班伯利。验尸审讯（inquest）一过，那些眼睛便转向下一桩耸动的事件去了，只给这个小地方的警队留下一阵火辣辣的刺痛：痛感失职之重。打那以后，伯克——他把这份位置看得比嘴上肯承认的重得多——把他那颗条理分明的头脑里想得出的每一条路都探了个遍，而结果回回一样。如今，沉寂了将近两年，这桩事又冒了头，把它重新翻起来的，是个十足的外来人，此人竟把案件的嫌疑犯收进了自己手下当差——虽说对那嫌犯，谁也提不出确凿的指控。伯克暗自纳闷，德里克同他那位花匠的相知，会不会远比表面看去深得多。这无疑是要谨慎从事的。

"要紧的是，先生，"他慢吞吞地说，"我没有任何职权告诉您半点事情，除非显然于执法有助，而且您手头握有与本案直接相关的补充证据。"

德里克点了点头。"这我完全明白，巡佐，而且这样再合理不过。要不要我改去请教验尸审讯时代表王室一方的律师？您若不愿谈，我乐意走这一趟。"

伯克思量起来。他不愿漏掉任何于自己有益的东西，也不愿越出自己的界限。这里头多半什么也没有，可马丁（Martin）重新露面这件事，他怎么也放不下。就在上个星期，他还走过山毛榉山庄那座小屋，瞧了那人一眼。马丁冷淡地点了点头，径自干活去了。无论怎么看，都是个硬扎的人。

"那件事随您的便，先生；不过您若肯把心里的想法说给我听，也许就不必再往下惊动谁了。"

===Original===

“It may take a little time, sergeant.”

Burke glanced out of the window and along the crossroads. “We’re not likely to be disturbed this morning.”

“Then I’ll begin with a question. Do you believe in the theory that when a serious crime has been committed, I mean one of passion or revenge, that the criminal, wherever he may be, is constantly reminded of it by the process of his own brain﻿—that in spite of all he can do he builds up picture after picture, and lives it all over and over again?”

“There are too many proved instances of that to doubt it.”

“And do you also believe that something constantly suggests to such a man that he should go back and revisit the scene of the crime?”

===Chinese===

"那要费一点时间，巡佐。"

伯克朝窗外、朝十字路口掠了一眼。"今早不大会有人来打扰。"

"那我就从一个问题说起。您信不信这样一套理论：一个人犯下重罪之后——我是指出于激情或仇怨的那种罪——不论身在何处，他自己头脑的运转都会不断把这桩事提醒给他——任他百般设法，画面还是一幅接一幅地堆叠起来，逼他把一切一遍又一遍地重新活过？"

"查实的先例太多了，由不得人怀疑。"

"那么您是否也相信，有什么东西会不断怂恿这样的人回去，重访作案的地方？"

===Original===

“There was the Hardwick case, like that,” said Burke reminiscently. “You remember the Gloucester Square doctor who was killed by the man who afterwards took rooms immediately opposite the doctor’s house; and the murderer never could tell why, except that it seemed the only thing to do.”

“Then I take it that in your profession the likelihood is really weighed and considered.”

“Yes, sir, it is. Some of the London men who came down here two years ago were talking about it.”

“Another point is the matter of coincidence. How do you feel about that?”

“It’s something that has played a big part in our work. One can’t put it aside. Coincidence and the other things you’ve mentioned often seem to run together.”

“And you know, of course, that Mr. Millicent’s gardener turned up very soon after I took Beech Lodge?”

===Chinese===

"那样的案子有过，哈德威克案（the Hardwick case）就是。"伯克的话音里带着追忆。"您记得格洛斯特广场（Gloucester Square）那位医生吧：杀他的凶手，事后偏偏在医生家正对面租下了住处；那凶手始终说不出缘由，只觉得似乎就该这么做。"

"那么我是否可以认为，在你们这一行里，这种可能性是当真被掂量、被考虑的。"

"是的，先生，当真掂量。两年前从伦敦下来的几位同行就谈论过这个。"

"另一点是巧合的问题。这个您怎么看？"

"在我们这一行里，它起过很大的作用。谁也撇不开它。巧合同您方才提到的那些，常常像是连在一道跑的。"

"当然，您也知道，我接下山毛榉山庄之后没多少日子，米莉森特先生的花匠就冒出来了？"

===Original===

“Yes, Mr. Derrick; Constable Peters reported that you had authorized him to occupy the cottage.”

“Then can you guess what brought him here all the way from Burma?”

“Did he go that far?”

Derrick nodded. “And came back by way of Canada﻿—”

“There might be several reasons,” said the big man thoughtfully.

===Chinese===

"知道，德里克先生；彼得斯警员（Constable Peters）报过，说是您准他住进了那座小屋。"

"那您猜得出，他大老远从缅甸（Burma）跑到这儿来，图的是什么吗？"

"他跑到了那么远的地方？"

德里克点了点头。"回来时还绕道加拿大（Canada）——"

"缘故嘛，可以有不止一种。"大块头若有所思地说。

===Original===

“Well, as a matter of fact there is but one.”

“How do you know, sir?”

“Martin told me himself.”

“What was it?” Burke’s tone had changed a little.

“He *had* to come. He had no bones about saying so.” Derrick paused a moment. “Sergeant, could an innocent man have felt like that?”

The sergeant stared at his own massive boots, glittering mountains of leather that shone with official luster.

===Chinese===

"可是事实上，缘故只有一个。"

"您怎么知道的，先生？"

"马丁亲口对我说的。"

"是什么？"伯克的口气微微变了。

"他非来不可。这一点他毫不讳言。"德里克顿了顿。"巡佐，一个清白无辜的人，可能有那样的感受吗？"

巡佐低头盯住自己那双大靴子——两座亮闪闪的皮革之山，泛着官家的光泽。

===Original===

“Anything else, Mr. Derrick?”

“Of course you remember Perkins?”

“Perfectly; the sort of woman one can’t forget.”

“Yes; a strange character, showing nothing on the surface, and so much a part of Beech Lodge that we took her on with the house.”

Burke grinned. “I can see that she hasn’t changed much.”

“No, she can’t change. But did you know that she walked in her sleep?”

===Chinese===

"还有别的吗，德里克先生？"

"珀金斯（Perkins）您当然记得？"

"记得真切；那种女人，想忘也忘不掉。"

"是的；一个古怪人物，面上什么也不显露，又同山毛榉山庄长成了一体，我们索性连宅带人一并接了下来。"

伯克咧嘴一笑。"看得出，她没怎么变。"

"是的，她变不了。可您知道她梦游吗？"

===Original===

The sergeant looked at him sharply. “For a newcomer, sir, you’ve unearthed a good deal. I never heard that before.”

“And would you think it of interest if I told you that the desk at which Mr. Millicent was found is of particular attraction to both Martin and Perkins?”

“Why do you say that?”

“Because I’ve seen them both examining it closely when they thought they were unobserved. They were looking for something, sergeant.”

Burke got up, stood at the diminutive window, and with his hands folded behind his back stared at the verdant expanse of Bamberley Green. Obviously he was thinking very hard. Derrick lit his pipe and contemplated the big frame, the thick neck, and round, neatly clipped skull. There was no promise of great ability here, no quick perception, no imaginative brain. Burke found his inspiration in his official regulations. Law, order, and discipline, was it not all in a book? He was the type for whom it was hard to let go, and impossible to forget. And the biggest thing in his life was still the Millicent murder. It hurt, just as Constable Peters had intimated. Presently he turned.

===Chinese===

巡佐锐利地看了他一眼。"您这新来乍到的，先生，掘出的东西可真不少。这一层我从前从没听说过。"

"倘若我告诉您，米莉森特先生伏尸的那张书桌，对马丁和珀金斯都有一种特殊的吸力，您会觉得此事值得留意吗？"

"您为什么这样说？"

"因为我亲眼见过他们两个，在自以为无人看见的时候，把那张桌子凑近了细细察看。他们在找一样什么东西，巡佐。"

伯克站起身，立在那扇小小的窗前，双手背在身后，凝望着班伯利草坪一望的葱绿。他分明在苦苦地想。德里克点起烟斗，端详着这副高大的骨架、那截粗脖子，和一颗剪得齐整的圆脑袋。这里头看不出大才干的指望，没有敏锐的洞察，也没有想象力旺盛的头脑。伯克的灵感，都到官家章程里去找。法律、秩序、纪律——这不全写在一册书里么？他是那一种人：撒手难，忘掉更不可能。而他此生头一桩大事，始终是米莉森特凶案。那案子硌痛着他，正如彼得斯警员暗示过的那样。过了一会儿，他转过身来。

===Original===

“Mr. Derrick, in most cases of crime, and especially that of murder, the force is pestered with amateur detectives who believe they have the one and only clue. It’s very often a reporter for some paper. They make all kinds of trouble, and always mix things up if you give them any rope. But you’ve said enough to justify me in talking about what took place at the inquest on Mr. Millicent; though, mind you, it’s entirely unofficial.”

“That’s all I ask, sergeant; and if this thing can be solved I have no desire to appear in it at all. I’d much sooner not. If we get any results, they’re yours, not mine. I don’t pose as an amateur detective; but, from what I have already seen and know, I believe this thing can be run to earth.”

Burke reached to a shelf above his head and took down a large leather-bound volume. On the well-thumbed pages of this were pasted envelopes, from one of which he extracted a docket bearing the name of Millicent, with a date. The manner in which he turned to it suggested that this procedure had often taken place before. He cleared his throat and began rather stiffly.

“At ten thirty on the night of October fourteenth﻿—that’s two years ago less three days﻿—I was just leaving this office when Paling, the groom of Dr. Henry, drove up in great haste and said that I was wanted at once at Beech Lodge by the doctor, who was himself at that time at the Lodge. He had been summoned there by Martin, Mr. Millicent’s gardener, who told him that a murder had been committed. We galloped all the way to the Lodge, arriving there at ten thirty-seven by my watch. I left instructions here that Constable Franklin should follow me without delay. I was admitted by the maid Perkins, who took me to the study, where I found Mrs. Millicent, her daughter, and the doctor. A lamp was burning on the desk, and beside it was Mr. Millicent, lying forward so that his head rested on the desk. He was quite dead. There was a large wound in his neck that had bled profusely and formed a puddle among his papers. The doctor very wisely had left things undisturbed, because his first examination proved that life was extinct.”

===Chinese===

"德里克先生，办刑事案件，尤其人命案，警局叫一班业余侦探缠得不可开交，个个都自信握着唯一的那条线索。十有八九，是哪家报馆的记者。他们惹出种种乱子，只要给他们半截绳子，必定把事情搅成一团。可您方才说的话，已足够让我有理由谈谈米莉森特先生验尸审讯的经过了；不过您记着，这纯属非官方。"

"我求的仅此而已，巡佐；这桩事若能有个了结，我丝毫不想在里头露面，最好全不露。倘若有了结果，功劳是您的，不是我的。我不充业余侦探；只是就我已见的、已知的而言，我相信这件事追得到根底。"

伯克抬手从头顶的架子上取下一大册皮面簿子。簿子内页翻得起了毛边，上面贴着一排纸袋，他从其中一只里抽出一页案签，写着米莉森特的名字，还有日期。他翻到那一处的熟稳姿态说明，这套程序先前他已做过不知多少遍。他清了清嗓子，一板一眼地开了腔。

"十月十四日晚上十点三十分——就是两年前差三天——我正要离开这间办公室，亨利医生（Dr. Henry）的马夫帕林（Paling）赶着车急急来到，说医生要我即刻去山毛榉山庄，他本人那时已经在山庄。是马丁去请的医生——米莉森特先生的花匠——他告诉医生说出了凶案。我们一路疾驰赶到山庄，按我的表，十点三十七分到达。我在这儿留下指示，命富兰克林警员（Constable Franklin）即刻随后跟来。女仆珀金斯开门放我进去，领我到书房，米莉森特太太、她女儿和医生都在那里。书桌上一盏灯还亮着，米莉森特先生伏在灯旁，头枕在书桌上。人已全然断了气。颈间一道大创，血流得凶，在他的文件中间积成一洼。医生很明智，一应东西都原样未动，因为他初验之下已断定性命绝了。"

===Original===

“Were Perkins and Martin in the room at this time?” asked Derrick evenly.

“No, only Mrs. and Miss Millicent and the doctor. Perkins and Martin waited in the hall with the doctor’s groom.”

“And then?”

Burke turned a page. “It was, of course, most important not to destroy the slightest clue that might have been left, so a very careful examination of the room was made, with exact measurement of the position in which the body was found. I searched the room, examined the door leading to the lawn, and found that it was fastened. By this time Constable Franklin had arrived, and he helped. We went over the entire ground floor, made sure that all windows were closed, then locked the study door, and took Mr. Millicent upstairs to his own room. I left the constable on guard outside with instructions that no person should be allowed to enter or leave the grounds.”

“What sort of a night was it?”

“Dull, mild, and rather cloudy, with no rain.”

===Chinese===

"那个时候，珀金斯和马丁在房里吗？"德里克平声静气地问。

"不在，只有米莉森特太太、米莉森特小姐和医生。珀金斯和马丁同医生的马夫一道在门厅里候着。"

"然后呢？"

伯克翻过一页。"当然，最要紧的是不可毁掉可能留下的哪怕最细微的线索，于是我们把房间极仔细地查验了一遍，尸身被发现时的位置也做了精确测量。我搜查了房间，检查了通往草坪的那扇门，发现门是闩好的。这时富兰克林警员到了，从旁帮手。我们把整个底层查了一遍，确认所有窗户都关着，随后锁上书房的门，把米莉森特先生抬上楼，送回他自己的房间。我留警员在外面看守，吩咐任何人不得进园子，也不准出园子。"

"那是个什么样的夜晚？"

"阴沉、和暖，云层颇厚，没有下雨。"

===Original===

“And the outside of the house?”

“Nothing could be done till next morning except make sure that any tracks should remain undisturbed; but after a most careful examination we found nothing of the kind. My own conclusion, and it has not been changed since, was that the blow must have been struck by some member of the household﻿—or”﻿—here Burke paused significantly﻿—“at any rate someone in the family service. Mind you, Mr. Derrick, this is absolutely unofficial.”

“I quite understand that. Now what can you tell me about the inquest?”

“I was just coming to that. The witnesses were narrowed to five: Mrs. Millicent and her daughter, Dr. Henry, Perkins, and Martin. I’ll take them in their order, so Mrs. Millicent comes first. She told a very simple story. Her husband was forty-five, and the latter part of their married life had been spent at Beech Lodge. He had at one time a very comfortable income, which latterly had been reduced by speculations. They were not, however, in difficult circumstances, although she seemed to know very little of his financial affairs. He was always much interested in anything that had to do with the Orient. So far as she was aware he had no enemies. He spent a good deal of his time in the garden and often went for long walks, always alone. Since his last trip to the East, from which he returned five years before his death, he seemed to have some kind of worry, of which he would never speak, or explain. Letters had arrived for him from Singapore, at which his worry seemed to increase; but he always destroyed these and never referred to their contents. From what I make of it, he was up to his eyes in something he found it necessary to conceal from those he cared for most. There had been no hard words with any of the staff, and no stranger had been at the house that day so far as we could learn.”

===Chinese===

"宅子外面呢？"

"第二天早上之前无计可施，只有保全痕迹一途，不许扰动；可待到极其仔细的查验之后，那样的痕迹半点也没寻见。我个人的结论——至今不曾更改——是：那一击必是宅中某人下的——或者，"说到这里，伯克意味深长地顿了顿，"无论如何，是家中佣仆中的某一个。您记着，德里克先生，这话纯属非官方。"

"这个我完全明白。那么验尸审讯的事，您能讲些什么给我听？"

"我正要讲到它。证人筛到最后剩了五位：米莉森特太太和她的女儿、亨利医生、珀金斯，还有马丁。我按顺序讲，那么先说米莉森特太太。她讲的事很简单。她丈夫四十五岁，婚后后半段的日子是在山毛榉山庄度过的。他一度进项相当宽裕，近来折在了投机上。不过他们的境况并不窘迫，虽然家里的钱财事务，她似乎知道得极少。凡与东方沾边的事物，他素来兴致极浓。据她所知，他没有仇家。他许多时光消磨在园子里，还常常长途散步，总是独自一人。自上一次东方之行归来——那是在他死前五年——他像是有了一桩心事，从不说起，也从不解释。有信从新加坡（Singapore）寄来给他，信一到，他的忧烦似乎便重一层；可他总把信随看随毁，从不提信中的内容。依我看，他深陷在一桩什么事里，深到觉得必须瞒住他最疼爱的人。他与仆佣之间不曾恶言相向，就我们所能查知的，案发那天也没有生人到过宅里。"

===Original===

“I understand that Mrs. Millicent engaged Perkins, while later on her husband employed Martin. How much later?”

“About a year.”

“So that any collusion between them before this is improbable?”

“I should say so; and it seems that they took very little notice of each other at any time.”

“Then, as far as we have gone, the period between the actual moment of the murder and the time when Perkins notified Mrs. Millicent is unaccounted for.”

Burke nodded. “Exactly!”

===Chinese===

"据我所知，珀金斯是米莉森特太太雇下的，后来她丈夫又用了马丁。中间隔了多久？"

"一年上下。"

"这么说，两人此前串通的可能就很小了？"

"我是这么看的；而且这两个人平日似乎彼此极少理会。"

"那么，就目前谈到的而言，从凶案实际发生的那一刻，到珀金斯禀报米莉森特太太，中间那段时间还没有着落。"

伯克点头。"正是！"

===Original===

“Before we go on to the other evidence, can you tell me whether anything was missed after the murder?”

The sergeant opened another envelope, extracting a sheet of brown paper some eighteen inches long.

“This is a drawing made by Mrs. Millicent of a thing that her husband used as a paper-knife. It’s not been found since that night.”

Derrick took it eagerly and scrutinized the outline of a murderous-looking weapon. Its curving blade must have measured a foot, being chopped off at the point in a curious and characteristic fashion. The handle was heavy and carried a short guard. Its deadly curve was unmistakable.

===Chinese===

"在往下谈旁的证词之前——凶案之后可曾短少了什么东西，您能告诉我吗？"

巡佐打开另一只纸袋，抽出一张牛皮纸，约莫十八英寸长。

"这是米莉森特太太画的图，画的是她丈夫拿它当裁纸刀（paper-knife）用的那样东西。自那晚之后，它就再没找到过。"

德里克急切地接过来，细看那件凶相毕露的兵器的轮廓。弯弯的刃身足有一英尺长，尖端以一种古怪而独特的手法斜削而去。柄沉甸甸的，带一道短短的护手。那道夺命的弧线，一望而知。

===Original===

“By George!” he said. “That’s a Malay kris!”

“Yes, Mr. Millicent got it in the East and seemed to attach some kind of sentimental value to it. He always kept it on his desk. Of course, it may be that it was there for protection, though the average man would have preferred a revolver. On the other hand, you can see what chance anyone would have against a thing like that.”

“Then there are two assumptions,” answered Derrick thoughtfully, “one that the person who committed the crime knew that this thing was on the desk available for his purpose; the other, that he came without any evil intent, but a dispute developed and in a burst of anger he picked up the kris, and struck.”

“And there’s just one person to whom both of those cases might apply, at ten o’clock at night,” said Burke grimly, “the person against whom we have no evidence.”

“I agree with that. Did anything else disappear at the same time?”

===Chinese===

"好家伙！"他说。"这是把马来克里斯短剑（kris）！"

"是的，米莉森特先生从东方带回它的，看去像是把它当个念想似的珍重着。他总把它搁在书桌上。当然，它摆在那儿或许是为了防身，虽说寻常人多半宁可选一支左轮枪。可话说回来，对着那样一件东西，谁有多少胜算，您也看得出来。"

"这样便有两种设想，"德里克沉吟着回答，"一种是：作案的人早知道这样东西就摆在桌上，正好为他所用；另一种是：他来时本无歹意，不料起了争执，一时怒起，抄起那把克里斯短剑，便下了手。"

"而晚上十点钟，只有一个人，两种情形都套得上，"伯克冷冷地说，"偏偏对这个人，我们一星证据也没有。"

"这一点我同意。同一时候，还有别的东西不见了吗？"

===Original===

“So far as we know only one thing, and that apparently not of any importance. It was a sort of little toy image, about three or four inches high, that Mr. Millicent used as a paperweight. It was carved out of a block of jade. He used to joke about it in a queer sort of way and say it was more valuable than they knew. Sometimes it was on his desk, but only when he was in the room himself. At other times he used to hide it away; but no one ever knew where. He never talked about it, except in that joking manner. It seems to have been an ugly-looking thing, too, but Mrs. Millicent could not make a drawing of it.”

A sudden light danced in Derrick’s eyes. “Then there was no concealment about this?”

“No more than that it used to be stowed away, and he’d never allow it to be touched. You know how men sometimes get queer ideas about things?”

“Yes, I know.”

“And it’s generally something quite unimportant. Well, it was like that with this image. Matter of fact, it was so ugly that no one in the house seemed to want to touch it, except Perkins.”

“Ah!” said Derrick slowly. His eyes were very keen. “Now, there are a few other questions I’d like to ask, but first you might tell me what other evidence was given.”

===Chinese===

"据我们所知，只少了一样东西，而且看去无关紧要。是一种小小的、玩偶似的神像，三四英寸高，米莉森特先生拿它作镇纸。它是由一整块玉雕成的。他惯常以一种古怪的方式拿它打趣，说它的贵重远超他们所知。有时它也摆在他书桌上，但只在他本人身在房中的时候。其余时候，他总把它藏起来；藏在哪里，却从来无人知晓。除了那种玩笑话，他对它绝口不谈。听说那还是个丑东西，只是米莉森特太太画不出它的样子。"

德里克眼里倏地跳起一道光。"那么，关于这东西，并没有什么隐秘？"

"无非是惯常把它收起来、从不许人碰它罢了。您知道，人有时会对某些东西生出怪念头？"

"知道，我知道。"

"而那东西十有八九全不要紧。这尊神像就是这样。实话说，它丑得宅里似乎谁都不想碰它——除了珀金斯。"

"啊！"德里克缓缓地说。他的眼睛极是锐利。"此外还有几个问题我想问，不过您不妨先讲讲，其余的证词都说了些什么。"

===Original===

The sergeant glanced out of the window. “That’s queer! I was going to say that Miss Millicent couldn’t tell us anything important, and there she is now.”

Derrick looked up. The girl was just abreast of the tiny office, walking slowly. Involuntarily she turned her head, and their eyes met. Color mounted to her cheeks, and she bowed. Derrick went out to her quickly. There were no preliminaries.

“May we come over in a few days? I think perhaps you could help then.” He spoke as though their last conversation had only been interrupted.

“Do!” she nodded.

“And till then I hope you’re not worrying, or anxious?”

She shook her head, smiled, and sent him a look of complete confidence. “Would it seem odd if I said that I worry less now than in the past two years?”

===Chinese===

巡佐朝窗外望了一眼。"真怪！我正要说，米莉森特小姐讲不出什么要紧的东西，她这不就在眼前了。"

德里克抬起头。姑娘正走到小办公室的窗前，步子迈得很慢。她不由自主地回过头来，两人的目光相遇了。红晕浮上她的双颊，她欠身行了一礼。德里克快步走了出去。没有半句寒暄。

"过几天我们可以过去吗？我想到那时，您或许帮得上忙。"他说话的口气，仿佛他们上一次的谈话只是被打断了片刻。

"来吧！"她点头道。

"在那之前，但愿您不再忧心，不再焦灼？"

她摇了摇头，嫣然一笑，递来一瞥全然的信赖。"倘若我说，如今我比过去两年里的任何时候都少忧心了，这话听着可会古怪？"

===Original===

“I’m so glad of that!”

“It’s quite true. I’m happier, and so is mother. I”﻿—she hesitated a little﻿—“I think we don’t feel so horribly alone.”

“You’re not.” His voice was queerly strained. “Indeed, you’re not.”

She glanced at him again, then turned quickly away.

Derrick looked after her, following the slight figure till it came to the corner of the green. Something of him went with her, and he reentered the sergeant’s office wondering at himself.

Whatever doubts the latter might have had about this unofficial conference had been laid at rest. The new master of Beech Lodge was animated by more than mere curiosity. That was now established; and, surveying the past two years, the big man realized how heavily the unfathomed crime had rested on his own spirit. The memory of it could never leave him till the mysterious scroll was unrolled. This visit of Derrick’s might result in nothing; but, in a way not entirely clear, the chance of solution seemed at last a little more probable. He looked at the young man almost with respect.

===Chinese===

"听了这个我真高兴！"

"千真万确。我快活些了，母亲也是。我——"她微微踌躇了一下——"我想，我们不再觉得那么孤苦伶仃的了。"

"你们并不孤单。"他的嗓音异样地发紧。"真的，并不孤单。"

她又望了他一眼，随即倏然转身去了。

德里克目送着她，追着那轻盈的身影，直到她走到草坪的拐角处。他身上有什么随她去了，而当他重新走进巡佐的办公室时，还在暗自惊异。

这场非官方的晤谈无论曾叫巡佐起过什么疑心，此刻都已安放妥帖。山毛榉山庄的新主人，心头驱动着他的，绝不止是一点好奇。这一点如今已坐实；而这大块头回望过去的两年，才明白那桩未解的凶案在自己心头上压得有多沉。不把那卷神秘的卷轴展开，这段记忆便永远离不开他。德里克此来也许毫无结果；可是，以一种说不十分清楚的方式，破案的指望似乎终于大了一分。他望着这年轻人，目光里几乎带着敬意。

===Original===

“As I said, Miss Millicent could really tell us little more than her mother. She seemed just as frightened of something that might still take place as of what had happened. She knew about the image, but nothing of its history; and my impression was that she linked it up with the crime in a way that none of the rest of us did. She had no explanation of this. I got the impression that she understood her father, if one can put it that way, better almost than her mother﻿—although I have no real reason for saying this.”

Derrick glanced at him shrewdly. “Nevertheless, I’m glad you mentioned it. Anything else?”

“No, sir. Perkins was the next witness. She had been in Mrs. Millicent’s employ for nearly five years. An Englishwoman, aged thirty-eight, she had traveled a good deal before she went into service. She stated that on the night in question she was on her way upstairs from the servants’ hall﻿—there was no other servant there at the time﻿—and passed the study. The door was closed, and there was no sound; but she could see the lamplight under the door. A little later, when she was ready for bed, she went back to the servants’ hall for a book and noticed that the door was ajar and the lamp still burning.

“She went in, thinking that Mr. Millicent had gone to bed and forgotten to put it out. There she found him, bent forward over the desk, his head on one side and a deep wound in his neck from which the blood had poured in a pool. She said that for a moment she could not move, then ran upstairs, hammered at Mrs. Millicent’s door, and told the latter that there had been an accident in the study. Mrs. Millicent called to her to send Martin at once for the doctor, so she raced down to the cottage at once without going again into the study. She found Martin, who ran for Dr. Henry, coming back a little later with the doctor and groom in the cart. Then the groom came for me. As you probably know, Beech Lodge is about halfway between Bamberley and the doctor’s house.”

===Chinese===

"正如我方才所说，米莉森特小姐其实讲不出比她母亲多多少的东西。她像是对于已然发生的事和可能还会发生的事，怀有同等的恐惧。她知道那尊神像，却不知它的来历；而我的印象是，她把它同凶案联系在了一起，用的是我们其余谁都不曾用的一种联系法。对此她说不出个所以然。我还得到一种印象：她懂她的父亲——如果容许这样说——几乎比她母亲懂得还深，虽说这么讲，我并没有真凭实据。"

德里克精明地瞟了他一眼。"尽管如此，您提起这一层，我很高兴。还有吗？"

"没有了，先生。下一位证人是珀金斯。她在米莉森特太太手下做事将近五年。英国人，三十八岁，入佣之前游历过不少地方。她陈述说，案发那晚，她正从仆役厅（servants' hall）上楼——当时宅里没有别的仆人——路过书房。门关着，一点声息也无；可她看得见门缝下透出的灯光。过了一阵，她解衣预备安寝，又回仆役厅去取一本书，发觉书房门半开着，灯仍亮着。"

"她走了进去，只当米莉森特先生已经上床，忘了熄灯。就在那里，她发现了他：伏在书桌上，头歪向一边，颈间一道深创，血从创口涌出，积成一泊。她说有一霎她动弹不得，随后跑上楼，擂响米莉森特太太的房门，告诉她说书房里出了意外。米莉森特太太喊她即刻打发马丁去请医生，于是她一口气奔下小屋，没有再进书房。她找到了马丁，马丁跑去请亨利医生，不多时便同医生和马夫一道赶着车回来了。随后马夫来请我。您多半知道，山毛榉山庄正处在班伯利和医生家的中间。"

===Original===

“Did Perkins admit having missed anything from the desk?”

“She mentioned the paper-knife but said nothing about the image till she was questioned.”

“And then?”

The sergeant reflected a moment. “I didn’t make much of what she said then. She was very upset, and rambled a good deal, till I think the coroner was glad to have done with her. I almost thought she attached as much importance to that as to the paper-knife, but of course she was hysterical.”

“Possibly,” murmured Derrick. “So I take it that Martin could not actually have seen the body till he returned with the doctor?”

“That is his evidence, which I will come to in a minute, and also Perkins statement. It would be a matter of perhaps twenty or twenty-five minutes after Perkins waked Mrs. Millicent.”

===Chinese===

"珀金斯可曾承认书桌上短了东西？"

"她提到了裁纸刀，可那尊神像，直到受了诘问才说起。"

"然后呢？"

巡佐寻思了片刻。"她当时说的话，我没大放在心上。她心绪大乱，语无伦次地说了许久，我看得验尸官（coroner）都巴不得快些问完她。我几乎觉得，她把那神像看得同裁纸刀一般要紧，可她自然正歇斯底里。"

"也许吧。"德里克喃喃道。"那么据我理解，马丁其实要等随医生回来，才可能见到尸体？"

"他的证词如此，这个我马上就要讲到，珀金斯的陈述也一样。那大约是珀金斯唤醒米莉森特太太之后，二十到二十五分钟的事。"

===Original===

“And Mrs. Millicent, and I suppose her daughter, stayed with the body till the doctor came?”

“Yes.”

“Where was Perkins then?”

“Also in the study, trying to help Mrs. Millicent, who she thought was going off her head.”

“Let me go back a minute. The first time Perkins passed the study on her way upstairs the door was shut, and the next time ajar. How long intervened?”

“Perkins says perhaps half an hour, while she undressed.”

===Chinese===

"那么米莉森特太太，还有我想她女儿也在一起，一直守着尸体直到医生来？"

"是的。"

"那时珀金斯在哪里？"

"也在书房里，她只当太太快要急疯了，正从旁扶持着她。"

"让我退回去问一句。珀金斯头一回上楼经过书房，门是关着的；第二回却半开了。中间隔了多久？"

"珀金斯说，大约半个钟头，是她解衣就寝的工夫。"

===Original===

“So during that half-hour the crime was committed, and the door was probably left ajar by the murderer?”

“I could never see it any other way, Mr. Derrick.”

“And that is the time left unaccounted for?”

“Exactly. Now you’ve reached the point where I’ve had to leave the thing for two years, and you’ve reached it by the same road of reasoning.”

Derrick smiled. “Tell me what the doctor said, sergeant.”

===Chinese===

"那么凶案就发生在这半个钟头里，而那扇门，多半是凶手出去时留的半开？"

"除此之外，我看不出别的解释，德里克先生。"

"而这，也正是没有着落的那段时间？"

"正是。您如今到达的那一点，正是我不得不把这事搁下两年的地方，而且您是循着同一条推理的路走到的。"

德里克微微一笑。"给我讲讲医生的证词吧，巡佐。"

===Original===

“Very little. He testified that from the condition of the body life could not have been extinct for more than one hour.”

“That again narrows it down to about one half-hour in which the thing happened. The question is what did happen, so perhaps we’d better hear what Martin said.”

“There again it didn’t amount to much. He stated that he was smoking in the garden of the cottage when Perkins came running in, half dressed, crying out like a mad woman that Mr. Millicent had been murdered, and﻿—”

“She used the word ‘accident’ to Mrs. Millicent,” interrupted Derrick.

“Yes, but not this time. She told Martin to get Dr. Henry as soon as possible. There was no horse at Beech Lodge then, so he ran all the way to the doctor’s place. The rest of it coincided with Perkins’s evidence. He also said that he had been outside the cottage all the evening and could swear that no one had entered the grounds from the road.”

===Chinese===

"寥寥数语。他作证说，就尸身的状况判断，断气不会超过一个钟头。"

"这就又把事情收窄到大约半个钟头之内了。问题是那半个钟头里究竟发生了什么，所以咱们最好还是听听马丁怎么说的。"

"他的证词也没什么分量。他陈述说，他正在小屋的园子里抽烟，珀金斯跑了进来，衣衫不整，疯婆娘似的喊着说米莉森特先生被人谋害了，而且——"

"她对米莉森特太太用的词，可是「意外」。"德里克插话道。

"是的，可这一回不是。她叫马丁尽快去请亨利医生。那时山毛榉山庄没有马，他便一路跑到医生家。其余的部分与珀金斯的证词相合。他还说，那一晚他始终在小屋外面，可以起誓，没有一个人从大路进过园子。"

===Original===

“Had there been any difference between him and Mr. Millicent?”

“Apparently not. Mr. Millicent had been in the garden with him that afternoon, discussing the pruning of the roses and general preparations for the winter. Mrs. Millicent confirmed this, subsequently, and said that her husband trusted the man implicitly.”

“Did Martin mention the paper-knife?”

“He was questioned but said he knew nothing about it. From what the others testified, it seems that he very seldom came into the house, so it’s reasonable he should not have known.”

“Or the image?” asked Derrick thoughtfully.

“No, sir, nothing of that, either.”

===Chinese===

"他同米莉森特先生之间，可曾有过什么龃龉？"

"看去没有。那天下午，米莉森特先生还同他一起在园子里，商量月季的修剪和过冬的各项准备。米莉森特太太后来证实了这一点，还说她丈夫对这人信之不疑。"

"马丁提到那把裁纸刀了吗？"

"问过他，可他说对此毫不知情。照旁人的证词看，他似乎极难得进宅子，所以不知道也在情理之中。"

"神像呢？"德里克若有所思地问。

"也没有，先生，那一样也没有。"

===Original===

“And how long had he been in Mr. Millicent’s employ?”

“A matter of something less than five years.”

“And before that?”

“According to his statement, knocking about in the Orient.”

===Chinese===

"他在米莉森特先生手下做了多久？"

"将近五年的样子。"

"在那之前呢？"

"照他的陈述，是在东方一带东漂西荡。"

===Original===

“Do you think it is possible that he may have met his master somewhere in the East, and the fact never came out?”

“I hadn’t thought of that, but now it begins to seem possible.”

“And that there had for some time existed between them something that ultimately culminated in murder?”

“We could not get as far as that at the inquest, sir.”

“Let it stand for the present. What was Martin’s manner or attitude while he gave evidence?”

===Chinese===

"您看有没有这样的可能：他早先在东方什么地方遇见过他的主人，而这层关系从未显露出来？"

"这个我从前没想过，可如今听着，倒渐渐像是可能了。"

"而且两人之间早已存在着一桩什么，到头来酿成了谋杀？"

"在验尸审讯上，我们问不到那么远，先生。"

"这一点暂且存疑。马丁作证的时候，态度举止如何？"

===Original===

“A bit surly, as he always is, though I think without meaning it. It’s a bit against him that he’s apt not to look one in the face.”

Derrick nodded. “Now I’ll only put one or two more questions. From what you know, do you imagine there can be any link or understanding between him and Perkins?”

The sergeant shook his head with decision. “What makes me feel there is not is that, from all I can gather, Perkins dislikes the man.”

“That seems to be so. When I took him on she preferred to do the boots and coals herself, though he was available. She’s doing them now. On the other hand, Martin has come back around the world, and Perkins seems riveted to the house. Neither of them displayed any particular interest in their wages. Martin jumped at thirty shillings a week, which is not much as things go now. The point is, why are they both so keen on Beech Lodge?”

Burke stroked his chin. “I suppose that’s one of those coincidences you spoke of. I’ll admit that they almost certainly know a good deal more than we’ve been able to get out of them, but we haven’t got enough evidence to hang your hat on. One can’t make an accusation on anything else, much less an arrest. It’s up to me to prove that so-and-so is guilty, and not for him to prove that he isn’t.”

===Chinese===

"有些乖戾，他一向如此，不过我想并非有意。他不惯于正眼看人，这一点对他略微不利。"

德里克点了点头。"最后我只再问一两个问题。依您所知，您认为他同珀金斯之间，会不会有什么勾连或默契？"

巡佐斩钉截铁地摇了摇头。"叫我觉得没有的缘故是：就我所能打听到的，珀金斯看不上这个人。"

"看去是这样。我用他的时候，明明有他可使，她却宁愿自己包下擦靴搬煤的活计。如今还是她在做。另一面，马丁绕了地球一圈回来，珀金斯却像是铆死在这宅子上。两个人对自己的工钱都不见得上心。马丁一听每周三十先令便一口应承，照如今的行市，这算不得多。要紧的是：他们两个为什么都这样恋着山毛榉山庄？"

伯克抚着下巴。"我料想，这就是您说的那种巧合。我承认，他们两个知道的，几乎必定比我们从他们嘴里掏出来的多得多；可我们手里的证据，还挂不起一顶帽子。凭这点东西，别的控告提不出，遑论逮捕。该由我来证明某某人有罪，而不是由他来证明自己无罪。"

===Original===

“What then would you call a step toward real evidence?” asked Derrick, with a little lift in his voice.

“Proof that either Perkins or Martin had been lying at the inquest, or”﻿—he added with an incredulous smile﻿—“the discovery of that paper-knife, or even the image.”

Derrick put his hand in his pocket and laid a small dark green object on the table.

“Was it at all like this, sergeant?”

The blood rushed suddenly to the big man’s temples. “My God, sir! where did you find that?”

===Chinese===

"那么，怎样才算朝着真凭实据迈了一步呢？"德里克问，声音里透起一点昂扬。

"证明珀金斯或者马丁在验尸审讯上撒了谎，或者，"他添了一句，脸上挂着一丝不敢置信的笑，"找到那把裁纸刀，或者哪怕那尊神像。"

德里克把手伸进口袋，把一件小小的、深绿色的东西搁在桌上。

"它像不像这个，巡佐？"

大块头的血猛地涌上两鬓。"我的天，先生！您在哪儿找到它的？"

## VIII The Power of the Unknown / 第八章 未知之力

===Original===

Derrick walked quickly back, slackening speed as he approached the Lodge, and reentering the grounds from the direction in which he had started. There was a light in the cottage kitchen, but neither man came to the window as he passed. In the study he found Edith beside the tea-tray. She handed him his cup, and with it sent an inquiring glance.

“How’s your friend the peddler behaving himself, and what did Jean say?”

He flushed a little. “She didn’t say very much, but”—he smiled reminiscently—“she took the bangle.”

“I’m glad of that, my dear,” she said softly. “Had she ever heard anything of the peddler?”

“Not a word, nor has Sergeant Burke.”

===Chinese===

德里克（Derrick）快步往回走，临近山庄时放慢了脚步，从出发时的那个方向重新进了园子。小屋的厨房里有灯，可他经过时，两个人都没有到窗前来。在书房里，他看见伊迪丝（Edith）守在茶盘旁边。她把茶杯递给他，随着杯子一同送来询问的一瞥。

"你那位货郎朋友规矩不规矩？琴（Jean）又说了什么？"

他微微一红。"她没说多少话，不过"——他回味地一笑——"她把那只金镯收下了。"

"这就好，亲爱的。"她轻声说。"那货郎的事，她从前可听见过什么风声？"

"一个字也没听过，伯克巡佐（Sergeant Burke）那儿也一样。"

===Original===

“You’ve been there, too?”

He nodded. “I thought it best to have a chat with him. He’ll be here in a few minutes.”

“Why, has anything happened?”

“No, but something may, and I want to be ready, in case.”

“I don’t understand, Jack. What do you anticipate?”

“Well, our friend has an odd idea that he may be able to suggest something that would help in the Millicent matter in the way of a clue if he could see the place where it happened. So I’m having him in here shortly with Martin, who doesn’t seem to fancy the visit at all. The sergeant won’t be in evidence, and they know nothing about him.”

===Chinese===

"你也上了他那儿去？"

他点点头。"我想还是同他谈一谈的好。他过几分钟就到。"

"怎么，出了什么事？"

"没有，可也许会出；我想先有个备，以防万一。"

"我不明白，杰克。你料到什么了？"

"是这样：咱们那位朋友有个怪念头，说只要看一看出事的地点，他兴许能提示出点什么，在米莉森特的案子里帮上一把，算一条线索。所以我这就把他同马丁（Martin）一起请进来——马丁对这一趟似乎全不领情。巡佐不露面，他们对他一无所知。"

===Original===

“Oh!” she said slowly, “can I do anything except keep out of the way? I’ve an idea that’s what you want me to do.”

Derrick laughed. “It is, exactly. There’s one other thing. I’d like to see Perkins for a minute before the others come.”

Edith got up. “Then finish your tea, and I’ll send her in for the tray. She’s been even more queer than usual today, so I fancy she knows that man is here. Good luck to you, brother, and I’m so glad I know what you’re working for.” She bent over, kissed him impulsively, and went out.

He sat motionless for a moment, vibrant with the knowledge that he was playing for great stakes. Martin—the peddler—Perkins—the jade god—all intervened between him and the goal of his desire. At that his nerves seemed slowly to be turned to steel.

===Chinese===

"哦！"她缓缓地说，"除了躲开，我还能做点什么？我有个感觉，你要我做的就是躲开。"

德里克笑了。"正是，一点不差。还有一件事：趁旁人没来，我想先见珀金斯（Perkins）一分钟。"

伊迪丝站起身。"那你就把茶喝完，我打发她进来收盘子。她今天比平时还要古怪，我猜她已经知道那个人在这儿了。祝你好运，哥哥；我如今知道你奔走的是什么了，真叫人高兴。"她俯下身，情不自禁地吻了他一下，出去了。

他一动不动地坐了片刻，心里震颤着，知道自己正在下一场输赢巨大的赌注。马丁——货郎——珀金斯——玉神——全都横在他同他渴望的目标中间。想到这里，他的神经仿佛慢慢炼成了钢。

===Original===

The door opened. Perkins came in and busied herself with the tray, and for the first time he noted that her fingers were trembling. Something of the transitory pity he had felt for Martin came over him, and he made a gesture toward a chair.

“Please sit down a minute, Perkins. I want to ask you something.”

She seated herself silently and sent him a blank glance.

“What I want to inquire is something more about Martin. Can you tell me nothing of his history before he came to Mr. Millicent?”

“Why should you ask me, sir?”

===Chinese===

门开了。珀金斯进来收拾茶盘，他头一回注意到她的手指在抖。一阵他先前对马丁起过的、转瞬即逝的怜悯，又向他袭来，他朝一张椅子做了个手势。

"请坐一会儿，珀金斯。我有话要问你。"

她默默坐下，朝他投来茫然的一瞥。

"我要打听的，还是马丁的事。他来米莉森特先生手下之前的历史，你当真一点也说不上来？"

"您干吗问我，先生？"

===Original===

“Who else is there to inquire from? You occupy just the same trusted position that you have for years past. You’ve let me into your feelings enough to know that you perceive things that are not usually seen, and you’re aware that I’m doing what I can to clear up the mystery of your master’s death. Shall I say to you that I’m convinced you are trying to shield someone in this affair?”

“Don’t say that, sir,” she whispered shakily.

“What other conclusion can I come to?”

She stared at him as though he was an intruder on some strictly private domain and had come to rifle her very soul.

===Chinese===

"不问你问谁？你占的还是这么多年来那个受信任的位置。你让我窥见了你够多的心事，足见你能察觉寻常人看不见的东西；你也明白，我正尽力弄清你主人之死的谜。我可不可以说：我确信你在这件事里想包庇什么人？"

"别这样说，先生。"她颤声低语。

"不这样结论，又叫我能怎么结论？"

她直瞪着他，仿佛他闯进了一块纯属私禁的领地，是来劫掠她灵魂的。

===Original===

“Do you think there’s any connection between the murder and the arrival of this peddler?”

Perkins shook her head. She made no attempt to disguise her knowledge of the stranger’s advent and now seemed touched with the same helplessness that had so lately swept over Martin. Her hands were slack in her lap, and he noted their smoothness and strength.

“I’m afraid I cannot help,” she muttered.

He looked straight into the passionless eyes. “And yet you must know so much more than I do. Here, in this room, the voice of a dead man is sounding now, asking for vengeance. There are other voices, we have both heard them, but this is the clearest. Here your master died, and the evil thing triumphed, and you told me that fear came before he died, the fear that is worse than death. Can’t you hear that voice?”

The blank-faced woman shivered as he spoke, and Derrick knew that the truth had crept a little, a very little nearer than ever before. There was mystery in the study, but the greatest mystery of all was locked within this unresponsive breast. There was some chord which, if he could only touch it, would vibrate in unison with her guarded secret and unloose its bonds. Perkins trembled again and waited.

===Chinese===

"你看这桩凶杀，同这货郎的到来，有什么牵连没有？"

珀金斯摇了摇头。她毫不掩饰自己知道那生客的来临，这会儿似乎也染上了方才席卷过马丁的那同一种无助。她的双手松松地搁在膝上，他注意到那双手的光滑与有力。

"我怕帮不上您。"她咕哝道。

他直望进那双无情的眼睛。"可你知道的，必定比我多得多。就在这里，就在这个房间里，一个死人的声音此刻正响着，声声索仇。还有别的声音，我们都听见过，可这一个最清晰。你的主人死在这里，邪恶的东西得了胜；你告诉过我，他死之前先来了惧怕，那比死更可怕的惧怕。那声音你听不见吗？"

他说话的时候，那个木然着脸的女人直打颤；德里克知道，真相又爬近了一步——比以往任何时候都近了那么一小步。书房里自有神秘，可顶大的神秘，锁在这副无动于衷的胸怀里。定有某一根心弦，只要他能触到，就会同她守着的秘密应声共振，解开它的束缚。珀金斯又抖了一下，等着。

===Original===

“He was good to you, as everyone has told me,” went on the steady voice, “and it seems that you were devoted to him. For six years you had his confidence and lived under this roof. I do not know what may have taken place before that, if anything, but is six years forgotten so soon?”

“Don’t!” she said brokenly. “Don’t!”

“Two men are coming here in a few moments,” he persisted. “Of one of them I know little, and nothing of the other. But I am assured that in the peddler’s heart are things at which I have not guessed. He, too, has his secret, or he would not be here. He poses as a stranger, but something tells me that he is no stranger to Martin, and perhaps not to you.”

“Why do you say that?” she flashed.

“It matters not why, but I have my reasons. It may be that there are now assembled all those who were here two years ago, and the Millicents are not far away. One of these men was in the grounds of Beech Lodge when its master met his death, Perkins; was the other here, too?”

He shot out these last words in a tone so sharp and commanding that the woman quailed visibly. Her fascinated eyes were fixed on him in a stare that began to be strangely hypnotic, till it seemed that she was receding visibly from his reach, dwindling to a distance, and leaving behind her only a baffling intelligence that mocked and dared him to follow if he could. She had recoiled, but with her secret locked tighter than ever. He became aware that fear, though fear was in her every motion, could not conquer her. She relied apparently on powers that from long use had become stronger than fear. When at last she spoke, it was as though a safe distance had been established and her spirit had caught its breath again. She seemed now safe from further probing.

===Chinese===

"他待你好，这是人人都对我说的，"那平稳的嗓音接着说，"而你也似乎一心向着他。六年了，你受着他的信任，住在这片屋顶底下。在那之前发生过什么，倘若有过，我不知道；可六年，就这样快忘了吗？"

"别说了！"她哽咽着说。"别说了！"

"两个人转眼就要到这里来，"他不依不饶，"其中一个我知道得少，另一个，我全然不知。可我确信，货郎心里藏着我没有猜到的东西。他也有他的秘密，不然他不会在这里。他装作生客，可有什么告诉我：他于马丁并非生客，于你，恐怕也不是。"

"您为什么这样说？"她抢白道。

"为什么不要紧，我有我的道理。如今聚在这里的，也许正是两年前在这里的那些人；那几位米莉森特，也都不远。主人丧命的时候，这两个人里有一个就在山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge）的园子里，珀金斯；另一个，当时也在这里吗？"

末几句话他抛得又尖又厉，俨然是命令，那女人明显地怯了。她那双勾住了似的眼睛死盯着他，那凝视渐渐带上一种古怪的催眠意味，直到她仿佛眼睁睁地从他够得着的地方退远、缩小，缩成远远的一点，只留下一段费解的灵智，嘲弄他、激他，看他敢不敢追。她退却了，可她的秘密锁得比先前更紧。他明白过来：惧怕虽渗在她的一举一动里，却降服不了她。她显然倚仗着什么力量，那力量因久用而强过了惧怕。末了她开口时，仿佛一段安全的距离已经立起，她的元气又缓了过来。此刻她似乎不怕再受盘问了。

===Original===

“I have told you already what happened that night, how I found the master”—here she hesitated a little—“and then went for Martin. There was no one at the cottage but him. There is nothing else to be said.”

“And Blunt,” he said again. “The man who will be here in a few minutes, the man who is so anxious to enter this room, has he never been here before?”

“I am not Blunt’s keeper,” she parried. “I do not know, but”—and at this point an extraordinary light flickered through her dull eyes—“he may have been. I cannot see in the dark.”

“He made an offer for something this afternoon,” said Derrick quietly, “something that seemed of little worth to me.”

She looked at him silently, as though in contempt of his childishness.

===Chinese===

"那晚的事我已经告诉过您了：我怎样发现主人"——说到这里她迟疑了一下——"随后又怎样去找马丁。小屋里除了他没有别人。再没有什么可说的了。"

"还有布伦特（Blunt），"他又说。"几分钟内就要到这里来的那个人，那个急着进这房间的人——他从前当真没有来过？"

"我不是布伦特的看守。"她避而不答。"我不知道，不过"——说到这里，一点异样的光从她呆滞的眼里闪过——"他也可能来过。黑地里的事，我看不见。"

"今天下午他要出价买一样东西，"德里克不动声色地说，"一样在我看来不值几个钱的东西。"

她默默看他，仿佛嗤笑他孩子气。

===Original===

He felt in his pocket and leaned forward. “The offer was for the original of this,” he replied, and put the wax image on the desk immediately in front of him.

In the next moment he snatched it away. Perkins, springing with convulsive strength, had laid her nervous grip on the model, her eyes suddenly ablaze with mad cupidity. In a fraction of time she was transfigured into a wild thing dominated by one uncontrollable desire, and her movement had the swiftness of light. Her hands closed like claws, but even as she touched the thing her grip relaxed, for in that instant she knew it was not real. She sent Derrick the same strange look of baffled incredulity he had received from the peddler, then sank back in her chair, trembling and unnerved. Her gaze rested on what lay safe in his grasp, wandered to the picture of her master, and round the paneled walls, searching for what she knew must be somewhere close at hand. The hunger in her eyes slackened, becoming reborn again as though fanned into life by this knowledge, till again she was almost a demon, urged by some driving force, terrible in its power.

Once more the light faded, the tense figure slowly relaxed, the face resumed the sphinx-like character to which he was so well accustomed, and there was before him the former Perkins, silent, mysterious, and remote. She quivered as though from the storm that had passed over her and, with her body limp, waited for what might come.

===Chinese===

他往口袋里一摸，俯身向前。"他要买的，是这件东西的真身。"他答道，一面把那尊蜡模（wax image）放到书桌上，正对着自己面前。

话音未落，他把它夺了回去。珀金斯爆出一股痉挛性的气力，猛扑上来，一只紧张的手已经攫住那蜡模，双眼骤然烧起疯狂的贪欲。一眨眼间她变了形，成了一只野物，被一股压不住的欲念主宰着，那动作快得像光。她的双手像爪子一样合拢，可指尖刚触到那东西，攥劲便松了——就在那一瞬，她知道它不是真的。她朝德里克送来一瞥，同他从货郎那里得到的、那种受挫而不肯信的怪眼色一模一样；随即瘫回椅子里，抖着，泄了劲。她的目光落在他手里安然无恙的那样东西上，游到主人的画像上，又扫过四壁的细板墙，搜寻着她明知必在近旁的什么。她眼里的饥渴暗了下去，随即又死灰复燃，仿佛叫这一点认知扇旺了，直到她重新几乎成了一个恶魔，被某种驱迫的力量推着，那力量大得可怕。

光又暗了下去，绷紧的身影慢慢松弛，那张脸恢复了司芬克斯似的、他熟到不能再熟的神情；他面前又是原先那个珀金斯，沉默，神秘，隔膜。她微微发颤，像一场暴风雨刚从她身上刮过；身子软着，等着接踵而来的一切。

===Original===

“Does Martin want the real image, too, like yourself and Blunt?” he asked deliberately.

She remained silent, her lips pressed tight.

“Then what is this thing?”

Even while he spoke there came to him the certain knowledge that in the emerald depths of the hidden figure lay that which passed man’s understanding. Nor could any man tell how this should be. The fact was potent enough, and, as to the rest, it mattered not when or why. The tiny god exemplified something for which there was no explanation. It was absurd to expect Perkins to make one. It rested in the abyss that yawns at the feet of all, whether they see it or not. Sometimes one might touch it in the darkness, only to lose it. The thought of it imposed sudden silence in careless hours and made the lips dry and the blood tingle as it does when we feel on our brows the touch of vanished fingers, and out of nothingness comes the echo of a remembered voice. No, there was no explanation. Perkins spoke after a stinging pause.

===Chinese===

"马丁是不是也想要那真身，同你、同布伦特一样？"他不慌不忙地问。

她不作声，嘴唇抿得紧紧的。

"那么这东西究竟是什么？"

话犹未了，他心里已经生了确凿的知识：那尊藏着的小小神像，它祖母绿的深处卧着一样东西，非人的理解所能及。它何以如此，也无任何人能说清。事实本身已经够有力了；至于其余，是何时、是为何，都无关紧要。这小小的神明昭示着一样无从解释的东西。要指望珀金斯给出解释，未免荒唐。它卧在那道深渊里——深渊张开在所有人脚边，不论他们看不看得见。人有时会在黑暗里触到它，却又失掉它。想到它，漫不经心的时刻会骤然噤声，嘴唇发干，血液刺麻，就像我们觉出消逝的手指抚上眉心、虚无之中传来一个记得的声音的回响时那样。不，没有解释。珀金斯在一阵刺人的沉默之后开了口。

===Original===

“Where did you find it? I mean the other?”

“It found me. Can you understand that?”

She nodded, her eyes still wide. “All the time I knew it was here. I could hear it talking, talking in the dark.”

“It has been there for two years, and I do not know how much longer. Did it send the fear that was worse than death?”

“What else could have sent it? But it was not on his desk when I found him.”

===Chinese===

"您在哪儿找到它的？我说的是那另一尊。"

"是它找到了我。这个你能懂吗？"

她点了点头，眼睛还睁得大大的。"我时时刻刻都知道它在这儿。我听得见它说话，在黑地里说个不停。"

"它在里头待了两年了，还要久多少，我不知道。那比死更可怕的惧怕，是它放出来的吗？"

"不是它还能是什么？可我发现他的时候，它不在他书桌上。"

===Original===

“Then if the man who killed your master had captured this as he hoped, there would have been no death here that night?”

“No,” she whispered, “no death, and perhaps no fear.”

“So that the man who wanted it then may after all be the same as the one who wants it now, and, having washed his hands, he returns for what he then sought?”

Again the sudden light in the baffling eyes, as of torches lit in the gloom. Derrick saw it and racked his brain. It was not an old thought that moved behind the mask now, but some conception new to that mysterious mentality. Were Blunt indeed the criminal, and assuming his return to recapture his prize, why should the suggestion of this produce so vivid a reaction? If this were the truth, why conceal it? What could this woman lose by coming into the open? She would write herself down a liar, and an innocent man be avenged. No, there was something else, and it beckoned a mystical finger to Derrick’s imagination and invited him on. The grim reality of the moment fell on him like a cloak. In a few more clock-ticks there would be others with whom to deal.

===Chinese===

"那么，倘若杀你主人的那个人当真如愿攫到了它，那天晚上这里就不会有死了？"

"不会，"她低声说，"不会死，也许也不会有惧怕。"

"这么说，当年要它的人，到头来也许就是如今要它的同一个人；他洗净了手，又回来讨当年没讨到手的东西了？"

那双费解的眼睛里又倏地亮起光来，像幽暗中点起的火把。德里克看见了，搜索枯肠。此刻在那面具后面走动的，已不是一个旧念头，而是那副神秘心智里全新的一道念头。倘若布伦特真是凶手，又假定他回来是为了重攫他的猎物，那么这话为什么引出这样剧烈的反应？倘若这是实情，又何必遮遮掩掩？这女人开诚布公，又能损失什么？她无非自认先前说了谎，一个无辜的人便得昭雪。不，还有别的；它朝德里克的想象勾着一根神秘的指头，引他上前。此刻森冷的现实像一件斗篷落到他身上。再过几个钟摆的嗒嗒，就要有旁人要应付了。

===Original===

“Perkins,” he said evenly, “for better or worse this matter must soon take another form. Two men will shortly be in this room, and one of them in all probability is guilty of murder. You know this, and I know it. The hand of fate may descend suddenly and point clearly, or it may be that the innocent may suffer for the guilty. God forbid that this should happen, but it has happened before, and sometimes because those who knew the truth were not there to tell it or, knowing it, kept an infamous silence. I ask you again, has Blunt to your knowledge ever been at Beech Lodge before, and, if so, was he here at the time your master died?”

“I am not Blunt’s keeper,” repeated the woman.

Derrick slid the wax image into a drawer. “Thank you, Perkins. You’ve told me what I wanted to know.”

===Chinese===

"珀金斯，"他平平地说，"是好是歹，这件事很快就要换一个形态。两个人转眼就到这屋里，其中一个十有八九犯着杀人的罪。这你知道，我也知道。命运之手也许骤然降下，指得分明；也许无辜的替有罪的受苦。上天不容有这种事，可这种事从前有过，有时就因为知道真相的人不在场、说不出，或者明知真相，却守着可耻的缄默。我再问你一遍：据你所知，布伦特从前到过山毛榉山庄没有？倘若到过，你主人死的时候，他在不在这里？"

"我不是布伦特的看守。"那女人重了一句。

德里克把蜡模滑进抽屉。"谢谢你，珀金斯。你想说的，我都听明白了。"

===Original===

The door closed behind her. Derrick did not stir but waited till the last sound died away. The hour of decision had come, and there was but one thing to do. He took a glance at Millicent’s calm face, read in it a mute approval, and, opening the invisible panel, took the jade god from its dark recess.

Setting it a little on one side of the lamp, he stared hard into its pygmy countenance. There still sounded in his ears Blunt’s voice telling of strange gods in strange countries, and there came now the unforgettable whisper of the East, with its mystery, its scarlet passions, its swift terrors, its throbbing invitations, and the jungle call of its fevered life. There was more than that. On these miniature lips was set the smile of sardonic knowledge and the curve of utter evil. The lids that lay over the slant and lazy eyes were heavy with slumber, but it was a repose that carried with it no oblivion. Unnameable knowledge rested on the face, a knowledge that sneered at good and gathered to itself the wickedness of misty centuries. Here was the touch of supreme art, the superb assurance of a master hand, but the issue was to charge the mind with a blinding comprehension of all that decent men most strive to forget.

Still staring, he yielded unconsciously to the spell. Beech Lodge grew oddly indefinite. The landmarks of his mind seemed unsubstantial. He was free as the wind, with neither kith nor kin. He found himself wondering why for months he should have been possessed by the desire to avenge a man he never saw. The tiny green eyes suggested that Millicent, and even Millicent’s daughter, did not matter so much after all. “Come East,” they signaled, “where man can taste all the wild joys of life, and women know how to love as do no others. Books, what are books? Dead things and dusty against the curve of a breast and the languorous hours of tropic nights. Good is ever the same, and it is only evil that changes, assuming a thousand lovely shapes, inviting, alluring, the wine that, having tasted, no man may forget. Come and drink deep while your blood is hot. There are those who wait to show you the way, and soon it will be too late.”

===Chinese===

门在她身后合上。德里克不动，直等到最后一丝声息消散。决断的时辰到了，可做的事只有一件。他望了一眼米莉森特平静的脸，读出无言的赞许，随即开开那看不见的暗板（panel），把玉神从幽暗的龛里取了出来。

他把它摆在灯侧稍偏的地方，死死盯进那张侏儒似的脸。耳中还响着布伦特讲异国邪神的声音；此刻又来了那不可忘怀的东方的低语，带着它的神秘、它猩红的情热、它倏忽的恐怖、它悸动的召唤，还有它热病般的生命发出的丛林之唤。不止这些。那两片小小的唇上，凝着讥诮全知的笑，凝着彻头彻尾的邪恶的弧线。覆在斜睨慵懒的双眼上的眼皮沉沉欲睡，可那安息里不带半点遗忘。那脸上栖着无可名状的知识，一种嗤笑善良、把雾蒙蒙的多少世纪的罪孽收归己有的知识。这里是至高艺术的笔触，是一只大师之手的超凡自信；可它的宗旨，却要叫头脑昏眩地彻悟正派人拼命想忘掉的一切。

他还在盯着，不知不觉降服在那符咒之下。山毛榉山庄变得奇异地模糊。他心里的界标仿佛都不实在了。他自由得像风，无亲无故。他发觉自己纳闷：为什么几个月来，他竟被一股念头缠住，要为一个素未谋面的人报仇。那双绿色的小眼睛提示说，米莉森特，甚至米莉森特的女儿，到头来其实都算不得什么。"到东方来，"它们打着信号，"在这里，男人尝得着人生一切狂野的欢乐，女人懂得爱，爱得别处的女人都比不上。书，书算什么？死东西，灰扑扑的东西，怎比得胸乳的弧线、热带之夜慵懒的时辰。善永远是那一个善，只有恶千变万化，幻出千种妩媚的形状，邀人，诱人，像那一杯酒，尝过一口的人一辈子忘不掉。趁血还热，来痛饮吧。有人等着给你引路；再迟，就来不及了。"

===Original===

Thus spoke the jade lips; thus cajoled the jade eyes. Even the milky fingers with their narrow, transparent nails seemed to lose their stiffness and beckon, while the blood deserted Derrick’s heart and the hair prickled on his head. He was listening to the soul of the man who had carved this thing, and what manner of man or devil could he be? But, whoever he was, he knew, Derrick felt that, and knew it utterly. Yes, life was short, too short. Perhaps the jade god was right!

His brain began to swim, and the image now to recede, now to approach, dwindling to a pinpoint, and swiftly enlarging till it towered over him, when something drifted in from the outer world. He blinked like one wakened from sleep. It was a tapping at the French window. He got up and crossed the room unsteadily. There was visible through the glass a peaked hat, a broad, red face, and a pair of bright, inquiring eyes. He breathed deeply and with a sudden sense of relief. Here was something sane and strong and wholesome. It seemed to clear away the miasma that surrounded him.

He stepped out and found the sergeant flattened against the wall in a vain endeavor to minimize his own bulk.

===Chinese===

玉的唇这样说着；玉的眼这样哄着。连那几根乳白的手指，连同窄而透明的指甲，仿佛也卸了僵硬，朝人招手；德里克心头的血退尽了，头皮上汗毛倒竖。他是在听雕成这东西的那个人的灵魂；那是怎样一个人，或是怎样一个魔鬼呢？可不管他是谁，他懂——德里克感觉得到——彻底地懂。是啊，人生短，太短了。也许玉神说的不错！

他的头脑晕眩起来，那神像忽而退远、忽而逼近，缩成一粒星点，又霍然放大，大到高塔般压上他的头顶——这时，有什么从外面的世界飘了进来。他眨了眨眼，像从睡梦里惊醒的人。是落地窗（French window）上的叩击。他站起身，脚步不稳地穿过房间。玻璃后头看得见一顶尖顶帽、一张宽阔的红脸、一双发亮的探问的眼睛。他深深呼吸，陡然觉出宽慰。这是一样清明、结实、健全的东西。它仿佛把他四围的瘴气一扫而空。

他跨出去，只见巡佐把自己贴在墙上，枉费心机地想把那副大身板缩小些。

===Original===

“Got here as soon as I could, sir, and had a squint at the cottage; they’re both there. Peters is behind the hedge at the back. Anything new since I saw you?”

“There may be a good deal. I think it’s likely that the peddler is the man we want after all, and not Martin. The woman Perkins declines to say whether she has seen him before or not, also whether he was in this neighborhood the night of the murder.”

“Good enough, sir. That ought to help. Anything else?”

Derrick glanced at his watch. “Yes, the sight of the image produced on her the same effect precisely as it did on the others. She, too, tried to get it. That’s all there’s time to say now, sergeant. The men ought to be here in five minutes.”

“Are you armed, sir?”

===Chinese===

"我尽快赶到的，先生，还朝小屋瞄了一眼；他们俩都在。彼得斯（Peters）埋伏在小屋后头的树篱后面。自打我见了您，有什么新的动静吗？"

"也许有一大堆。我想，我们要找的人到头来多半是那货郎，不是马丁。珀金斯那个女人不肯说，她从前见过他没有；也不肯说凶案那天夜里，他来没来过这一带。"

"好极了，先生。这该用得上。还有别的吗？"

德里克看了看表。"有。那神像在她身上引起的效果，同在旁人身上一模一样，分毫不差。她也扑上去要抢。眼下来得及说的就这些了，巡佐。那两个人五分钟后就该到。"

"您带家伙了吗，先生？"

===Original===

“Yes, but I hardly think it’s necessary. You’ll be able to attend to that end of it. Mind you, I’m not at all sure that anything is going to happen. This is only a shot in the dark. Can you see the image on the desk quite clearly from where you are?”

“Yes. Is that the real one? It looks somehow more alive than the other.”

Derrick smiled. “Just what Blunt told me. The dummy wouldn’t serve the purpose with him, so we must take this chance. Don’t stir unless one of them tries to get away with it. If no such attempt is made, it’s for us to make the next move. I take it, sergeant, you’re willing to work with an amateur a little while longer?”

Burke nodded grimly. “I’ll follow anyone who can lead me to the man who killed Mr. Millicent.”

He moved back and out of sight. It was nearly dark now, and Beech Lodge was encircled with ghostly shadows. Edith had obliterated herself in her bedroom, and was pretending to read. All she asked was that this too serious playacting be concluded as soon as possible. It deranged the house and made her restless and uncomfortable. Derrick manipulated the curtains so that they hung partly open, revealing the French window, then seated himself at the desk and shot an oblique glance at the jade god. He was not afraid of it but experienced no desire to stare straight into those emerald eyes. He glanced at Millicent’s portrait, asking mutely whether so far all was well done, but Millicent seemed uninterested. What could he mean by that? Then steps in the hall, and low voices, and a tap at the door.

===Chinese===

"带了，可我想多半用不上。那一头自有你去照应。您记住，出不出事，我一点没把握。这不过是黑地里放一枪。从您待的地方，书桌上那神像看得清楚吗？"

"清楚。那是真的？不知怎么，它比那尊蜡模看着活。"
