德里克笑了。"布伦特同我说的一字不差。蜡模糊弄不了他，所以我们只好冒这一回险。除非他们哪个想把它抢走，否则你别动。要是没人动手，下一步就由我们来走。我猜，巡佐，你愿意再同一个外行合作一小会儿吧？"

伯克严峻地点点头。"谁领我找到杀米莉森特先生的凶手，我就跟谁走。"

他退回去，不见了。天差不多黑透了，山毛榉山庄叫鬼影幢幢围了一圈。伊迪丝把自己消隐在卧室里，装着读书。她只求这场过分认真的做戏赶紧收场。它搅乱了整个家宅，叫她心神不宁、坐立不安。德里克拨弄帘子，让它们半开着，露出落地窗，然后在书桌前坐下，朝玉神斜投了一眼。他不怕它，却也毫不想直直盯进那双翡翠的眼睛。他看了看米莉森特的画像，无言地问：到如今为止，一切办得妥当吗？可米莉森特仿佛意兴索然。这是什么意思？随后，门厅（hall）里响起脚步、低语，门上响起一记轻敲。

===Original===

Came Perkins’s flat tones saying that Martin and Blunt were outside. She looked not at all at the image but seemed to know it was the original. Whatever emotion it may have aroused, she gave no sign, and he marveled at her self-repression.

“All right, they may come in, and I think you’d better stay in the room while they are here.”

A flicker of surprise flitted across the blank face. Then she nodded with only the ghost of a smile. It seemed that she was not unwilling to stay, and the smile was a little satirical and rather cruel, he thought. But he remembered that she was not Blunt’s keeper. In the next moment the men entered, their caps in their hands. Derrick leaned back in the big chair. The curtain was up now.

===Chinese===

珀金斯平板的嗓音来说，马丁和布伦特在外面。她一眼也不看那神像，却仿佛知道它是真身。它也许激起过怎样的情绪，她一概不露，他惊叹她的自制。

"好，让他们进来吧；我想他们在这儿的时候，你顶好也留在屋里。"

一片惊异掠过那张木然的脸。随后她点了点头，唇边只浮一丝若有若无的笑。她似乎并不反对留下，而且那笑带点讥诮，还挺狠，他想。可他记起来了：她不是布伦特的看守。说话间两个男人进来了，帽子捏在手里。德里克朝大椅背上一靠。此刻，幕启了。

===Original===

“Blunt,” he said with slow distinctness, “it may be that we are both wide of the mark in this attempt, and, frankly, I don’t see how you can be of any real assistance. It is only because you told me that sometimes you had been able to get under the skin of things that I’m making it. You understand that?”

The peddler nodded, and for an instant their eyes met. The man’s gaze swung back to the thing he had been staring at since he crossed the doorstep. Irrepressible hunger and desire was in the stare. Derrick seemed oblivious to this.

“The murder took place in this room two years ago. Martin has told you that, I assume?”

“Yes, sir, he has.”

“It occurred between nine and ten at night. Over the mantel you will see a picture of Mr. Millicent, who was found dead in this chair where I am sitting. Apparently he had not time to make any defense. This jade thing used sometimes to stand in front of him, but it seems that it cannot have been there that night. It is not known, as yet”—here Derrick paused for a second—“how the murderer entered the house.”

===Chinese===

"布伦特，"他说得缓慢而清楚，"这一场尝试，也许我们两个都不沾边；老实讲，我看不出你能帮上什么真正的忙。只是因为你对我说过，你有时能钻到事情的皮底下，我才安排了它。这个你明白？"

货郎点了点头，有一瞬，两人的目光相接。那人的视线又荡回他自打跨进门坎就盯住不放的那样东西上。那一盯里压不住地淌着饥渴与贪欲。德里克仿佛浑然不觉。

"凶案两年前就发生在这个房间里。这个马丁已经告诉你了，我料想？"

"是，先生，告诉了。"

"案发在晚上九点到十点之间。壁炉上方你们看见的，是米莉森特先生的画像；他就是在现在我坐的这把椅子上被发现的，人已经死了。看样子，他连招架的工夫都没有。这件玉器从前时常摆在他面前，可那天夜里，它似乎不可能在那里。凶手是怎么进宅子的——"说到这里德里克顿了一秒——"至今无人知晓。"

===Original===

He hesitated an instant, then looked suddenly at Perkins. “That’s right, isn’t it? It’s not known?”

“Not as yet, sir,” she answered slowly.

Martin made an involuntary gesture, but the peddler wheeled and sent the woman a swift and penetrating glance that had in it something of contempt, as though he had caught the drift of her words and they actually amused him.

“Can you tell me anything more, sir?”

“Yes, though it may be you know it already from Martin. The weapon that is believed to have been used has disappeared, a Malay kris that was always on this desk. No motive was then ascribed to the crime, but it now seems that this might have been robbery, which was unsuccessful. No strangers are shown to have been at the house that day, and not as far as Perkins is aware have any been here till very recently. No clues—and I take it that it is possible clues in which you are interested—were left. Now you can tell me if anything suggests itself to you. If you want to ask any questions, ask them.”

===Chinese===

他迟疑了一瞬，忽然看向珀金斯。"是这样吧？无人知晓？"

"至今不晓，先生。"她缓缓地答。

马丁不由自主地做了个手势，货郎却霍地转身，朝那女人送去迅疾而透骨的一瞥，一瞥里带着几分轻蔑，仿佛他捉住了她话里的去向，而且还当真觉得有趣。

"您还能告诉我一些别的吗，先生？"

"能，虽然这些你也许早已从马丁那里听来了。人们相信行凶所用的那件武器失踪了，是一柄马来克里斯短剑（kris），原先总摆在这张书桌上。当初这罪案没有归结出任何动机，可如今看来，这也许是一桩没有得手的劫案。那天没有生人到宅子里来过——至少查不出来；照珀金斯的说法，直到近来，也没有生人来过。线索——我想你感兴趣的正是可能的线索——一条也没留下。现在，你有什么想到的，可以告诉我。想问什么，尽管问。"

===Original===

The bright eyes were fixed on the speaker’s face. Martin was rooted to the ground but cast furtive looks at the peddler, swerving from these to stare with a dumbfounded expression at the image. He had nearly mastered his feelings, but there was a twitch in his fingers he could not manage to control. Perkins, her lean hands folded, regarded Blunt with a fixed and provocative gaze, as though inviting him to escape if he could from the net she was weaving. But Blunt seemed unmoved. His keen eyes slowly examined every angle of the room, scrutinized Millicent’s portrait with temporary interest, then traveled to desk and chair, mentally photographing their minutest detail. Finally he looked at the French window, and Derrick wondered if by chance he knew what waited outside.

“Was that door locked at the time?” he asked after a long pause.

Derrick turned to Perkins. “Was it?”

“Yes,” she said curtly.

===Chinese===

那双亮眼睛钉在说话人的脸上。马丁在原地生了根，却朝货郎偷觑，觑着觑着又滑开，去瞪那神像，一脸的目瞪口呆。他的情绪差不多拿捏住了，可手指上还有一下一下的抽动，管不住。珀金斯交叠着瘦手，用一道定定的、挑衅的目光打量布伦特，仿佛在邀请他：有本事就从她织的网里逃出去看看。可布伦特无动于衷。他敏锐的眼睛缓缓查验房间的每一个角落，带着一时的兴味细看米莉森特的画像，然后移到书桌和椅子上，在心里把最细小的细节一一拍了照。末了他望向落地窗，德里克暗想：他会不会碰巧知道，外头有什么在等着？

"出事那会儿，那扇门锁了吗？"隔了半晌，他问。

德里克转向珀金斯。"锁了吗？"

"锁了。"她简短地说。

===Original===

“And the front door?”

“I am not sure of that. Mr. Millicent usually saw to it before he came upstairs.”

Martin started. “What are you trying to get at?” His voice was rough and threatening, his eyes vicious.

For answer the peddler fixed on him a glittering stare, whereat the gardener blinked and was silent. Derrick caught his breath. The very air was now ominous.

“Anything changed here since the murder happened?” asked Blunt with a curious lift in his voice.

===Chinese===

"前门呢？"

"这我说不准。米莉森特先生通常在上楼前亲自查看。"

马丁一惊。"你到底想干什么？"他嗓音粗厉，带着威吓，眼里凶光直冒。

货郎的回话，是钉住他一记雪亮的凝视；园丁眨了眨眼，哑了。德里克屏住气。此刻连空气都带了凶兆。

"凶案发生以后，这里有什么挪过地方吗？"布伦特问，嗓音里带着一种古怪的扬起。

===Original===

“Just what do you mean?”

“Things are talking to me now. They’re a bit confused, and all I can get is that this room may not be the same as it was then.”

Perkins put her hand to her throat. “How do you know?” she whispered.

Derrick leaned tensely forward. This was evidence, new evidence.

“Go on, Blunt. Tell me just what you’re after.”

“I mean, are things in the same place as when that man was killed?”

===Chinese===

"你这话究竟什么意思？"

"东西这会儿正同我说话呢。它们有点乱，我只听出一点：这屋子也许同那时候不一样了。"

珀金斯一只手按上喉咙。"你怎么知道的？"她低声说。

德里克绷紧了身子朝前俯。这是证词，新的证词。

"说下去，布伦特。你追的究竟是什么，明说。"

"我是说：那些东西，还在那人被杀时的老地方吗？"

===Original===

A slight sound escaped from Perkins, and her nostrils dilated, while Derrick caught a swift but meaning glance that passed between herself and the gardener.

“I don’t know; I never thought of that. Are they, Perkins?”

“No.” She spoke with a sort of satisfaction, not unmingled with surprise. “And,” she added meaningly, “no one else has asked that question for two years.”

“Why do you ask, Blunt?”

The peddler seemed untroubled. “In a way, I was told to,” he broke off, and regarded Perkins with absolute composure. “What change is there now?”

“The desk was in the other corner,” she said faintly, “and facing the window, and this screen was on the other side of the fireplace opposite the sofa.” She got this out with a quick look at Martin in which she seemed to expect his approval and almost thanks.

===Chinese===

珀金斯喉咙里逸出一丝轻响，鼻翼张了张；德里克同时捉住一道飞快而有含意的眼色，在她同园丁之间一来一往。

"我不知道；这我从来没想过。挪过吗，珀金斯？"

"挪过。"她说得带着几分快意，又掺着惊奇。"还有，"她意味深长地补了一句，"两年来，没有第二个人问过这个问题。"

"你为什么问这个，布伦特？"

货郎似乎毫不慌张。"也算是有谁叮嘱过我。"他顿住，带着全然的镇定打量珀金斯。"如今有什么不一样？"

"书桌原先在那边的墙角，"她声音发虚地说，"脸朝着窗；这座屏风原先在壁炉另一侧，正对着长沙发。"她说出这几句时，飞快地看了马丁一眼，那一眼里像等着他的认可，几乎还等着他的道谢。

===Original===

“Then anyone sitting at the desk would naturally see out of the window but would not notice the door without turning?” put in Derrick sharply.

“Yes, sir, it was like that.”

“Well, Blunt, does all this take you anywhere?”

The peddler came a shade nearer the desk. His eyes were now half closed, and his dark features had smoothed out till they were strangely inexpressive. He might have been under the influence of a dream. The silence began to throb, and over Beech Lodge crept the touch of the mysterious East. None moved, for in that moment the jade god asserted his domination. The air seemed to palpitate, tremulous with unseen vibrations, and a whisper of wind drifted from the puttering fire. Then Blunt began to speak in a sort of half-chant without color or inflection, his voice sounding thin and clear and distant and carrying with it a nameless note of authority.

===Chinese===

"那么，坐在书桌前的人，自然而然看得见窗外，不回头就注意不到房门？"德里克尖锐地插话。

"是，先生，正是那样。"

"怎么样，布伦特，这些都把你带到哪儿了吗？"

货郎朝书桌又挪近一点。他的眼睛已经半阖，黝黑的五官熨平了，平得异样地没有表情。他仿佛正受着一场梦的支配。寂静开始搏动，神秘的东方的手，悄悄爬上了山毛榉山庄。没有一个人动，因为那一刻，玉神亮出了他的主宰。空气仿佛在颤，叫看不见的震波动得簌簌发抖，一缕风声从咕嘟着的炉火边飘散出来。随后布伦特开口了，用的是一种半诵半唱的调子，无色，无抑扬，嗓音听着又细、又清、又远，带着一种无可名状的威权的音色。

===Original===

“I see far away a picture of a place, large and poorly lighted. Strange people are there, moving without sound, and strange smells are in the air. Around it there are many trees, and when one comes that way a whisper runs ahead through the forest, telling of his coming. I see a man not unlike this one”—here the peddler made a gesture at the portrait—“but dressed otherwise and with his skin dark like that of the quiet people. He has journeyed from across the sea, drawn there he knows not why, and saying nothing of the purpose of his journey, because he himself did not know it. Traveling slowly, and taking at times many false trails, he comes at last to this place, and, staying not long, goes away by night, but not empty-handed. Behind him he leaves sorrow and a great anger and fear.”

The voice trailed out uncertainly, and a shudder ran through the peddler’s body. His whole figure was now swaying, and his head moved with a slow rhythmic motion.

“Go on,” said Derrick tensely.

“Not far from this place there is another man, and to him many call as with one voice, and a burden is laid upon him, and after a little while he is not seen there any more. Meantime the first man has returned to his own land and the faces he knew best, and tried to shake off the memories of what he had done and that distant place. But he could not do this. Time went on, and always in his dreams he returned there and could not forget. The thing he had taken was his master. At first when he wanted it, he thought he loved it, and then learned it was not love but fear. It was a thing of power, and stronger than himself. Mystery was in it, and thereby it was able to give tongues to that which could not otherwise speak. It was a tongue for the dumb.”

===Chinese===

"我远远看见一幅地方的图景：很大，光线昏暗。那里有奇怪的人，走动没有声音，空气里有奇怪的气味。四围是许多树木，谁打那边来，一声低语就先穿过林子跑在前头，报着他的来到。我看见一个人，同这一位不无相像"——说到这里，货郎朝画像做了个手势——"只是装束不同，皮肤晒得同那些安静的人一样黑。他从海外远道而来，被什么牵引到那里，他自己也不知道为什么；他对这趟跋涉的目的只字不提，因为他自己原也不知道。他走得很慢，不时走错许多岔道，末了终于到了这个地方，停留不久，趁夜走了——却不是空着手。他身后留下悲恸，留下大怒，留下惧怕。"

那嗓音游移着淡了下去，一阵寒颤滚过货郎的全身。他整个人此刻摇晃起来，脑袋带着缓慢的节奏摆动。

"说下去。"德里克绷紧了声音说。

"离那地方不远，还有另一个人；许多人像用同一个嗓音朝他呼喊，一副担子落在他身上，过不多久，那里就再看不见他了。这时，第一个人已经回了故土，回到他最熟的脸孔中间，想抖掉他做过的事、连同那遥远地方的种种记忆。可他抖不掉。时间往前走，他在梦里总回到那里，忘不掉。他带走的那样东西，是他的主宰。起初他想要它时，以为自己爱它，后来才明白那不是爱，是惧怕。它是一件有权能的东西，比他本人还强。神秘就在它身里；凭这神秘，它能叫原本不能开口的开口。它是哑巴的一条舌头。"

===Original===

Derrick nodded without knowing it. The world was full of clearing mists through which he began to perceive that which heretofore was hidden. His eyes wandered to Perkins. She stood rigid, as under a spell, her soul carried away by some invisible stream. Martin’s furtive gaze had changed, and his face was graven with despair, behind which moved desperate possibilities. Derrick saw these and thankfully remembered the man crouching against the wall outside.

“Go on,” he repeated.

“Others had heard that voice, thousands and thousands of them, and they too loved and hated and desired and feared this thing. It was always like this from the very first, because its hate had conquered love, and the fear in it was at war with desire. It had sucked in all that the hearts of men can feel, and because of its wisdom, and because it was at war with the spirit of Buddha, it had been kept close till that day. But only those on whom the spirit of Buddha rested might know the greatness and danger of this thing. And it was written that should it go from that place death would follow wherever it went.”

===Chinese===

德里克不知不觉点了头。世界正满是散开的雾，透过雾，他开始看见先前藏着的东西。他的目光游到珀金斯身上。她僵立着，像中了咒，魂灵叫一道看不见的水流卷走了。马丁那偷偷觑人的眼神变了，脸上刻着绝望，绝望后面动着孤注一掷的种种可能。德里克看在眼里，感激地想起外头贴墙蹲着的那个人。

"说下去。"他又说了一遍。

"别人也听见过那个声音，成千上万的人；他们也一样，爱这东西，恨这东西，渴求它，惧怕它。打一开始它就是这样，因为它的恨征服了爱，它身里的惧怕同欲念交战。人心感得到的一切，都被它吸了进去；由于它的智慧，由于它同佛陀（Buddha）的灵交战，它被紧紧藏到了那一天。可只有佛陀之灵栖身其上的人，才识得这东西的伟大与凶险。而且早有明文写着：它一旦离开那个地方，死亡将随它所到之处。"

===Original===

Something in the unbroken monotone captured the brain of Derrick, and the room swam. A mesmeric influence was at work. Everything around him began to slide, smoothly, imperceptibly. Was Millicent’s death so important after all? Soon it would be forgotten—with all else. What did he owe Millicent in any case? Why trouble to waste his time on another man’s affairs? Perkins, Martin, and even Blunt himself became blurred in this general indistinction, merging peacefully with other unrealities.

“So death came into this room, brother to fear, following the steps of the doomed. It was in no hurry but waited till fear had established itself firmly. There was not any escape, and there could be none, and the man who was to die walked between them for years, seeing their faces whichever way he turned.” The peddler waited an instant and leaned slightly toward Martin. “So it will be with the next appointed to die.”

Perkins was as though turned to stone, and Derrick’s breath came faster. There fell a stinging silence, while the atmosphere seemed to hum and quiver. Then from Martin proceeded a strange choking sound, and in that second Blunt leaped forward. With the swiftness of light he traversed the ten feet between him and the desk and grasped the image. At the mere touch of this, an amazing virility shot through his body, and he darted like a stone from a catapult across the room toward the French window. Derrick tried to shout, but his tongue had lost its power. Following a violent splintering of glass and wood, a bull-like roar from Burke, and the lithe figure was halfway over the lawn. Behind it lumbered the big frame of the sergeant, losing ground at every stride.

===Chinese===

那不停顿的单调声调里，有什么攫住了德里克的头脑，房间旋转起来。一股催眠的力量正在运转。他四围的一切开始滑动，平滑地，不知不觉地。米莉森特的死，到头来真那么要紧吗？转眼它就会被忘掉——同其余一切一起。再说他欠米莉森特什么？何必多事，把光阴浪费在别人的事上？珀金斯、马丁，连布伦特本人，都在这一片模糊里晕开了，安安静静地融进其余的不真实里。

"于是死亡进了这个房间，同惧怕是兄弟，跟着那注定者的脚步。它不慌不忙，只等惧怕扎稳了根。逃脱是没有的，也不可能有；那个该死的人夹在他们中间走了多年，转向哪边都看得见他们的脸。"货郎顿了一瞬，朝马丁微微倾过身去。"下一个注定要死的，也将如此。"

珀金斯像化成了石头，德里克的呼吸急了起来。一阵刺人的寂静落下，空气仿佛嗡嗡发颤。随后，从马丁喉咙里发出一声古怪的哽咽，就在那一秒，布伦特腾身而起。他以光一般的迅疾掠过他同书桌之间的十英尺，一把攥住神像。单是触到它，一股惊人的元气便射遍他全身；他像弹弓里飞出的石子，射过房间，直奔落地窗。德里克想喊，舌头却失了力。接着是玻璃和木头炸裂的巨响、伯克公牛般的怒吼，那矫健的身影已经掠过草坪的一半。他身后，巡佐的大身架咚咚地追赶，一步落后过一步。

===Original===

Oblivious of the others, Derrick dashed out and took up the chase. The jade god was in flight now. He had drawn level with Burke when there sounded directly ahead the noise of a struggle, a sharp whistle, the curse of a man who is strained to the utmost, and finally a strange, shrill cry. At that the sergeant slackened his pace.

“That’s Peters,” he panted, laboring for breath. “I gave him orders to station himself there behind the hedge, and a good job, too. He’s got our friend.”

Derrick sped on. “Come along,” he shouted over his shoulder. “He may need help.”

Burke grunted. “Not him, with a chap that size, but the little devil pushed his finger into my throat, and I saw stars. Make your own pace, sir, but it’s all right now.”

===Chinese===

德里克顾不上旁人，冲出去加入了追赶。玉神这会儿是在逃亡。他刚同伯克追平，正前方就响起了搏斗声、一声尖利的哨音、一个把气力使到极限的男人的咒骂，末了是一声古怪的尖嘶。听到那声，巡佐放慢了脚步。

"是彼得斯。"他喘着说，挣命似的喘气。"我命令他埋伏在那儿树篱后头，干得好。他把咱们的朋友拿下了。"

德里克继续飞奔。"跟上来！"他回头喊，"他兴许要人帮手。"

伯克哼了一声。"帮他？对付那么个身量的家伙，用不着。倒是那小魔鬼拿手指捅进过我嗓子，我眼前直冒金星。您按您的步子来，先生，这下没事了。"

===Original===

On the other side of the hedge the peddler lay flat, the constable bending over him. The face of the latter was flushed and the collar of his tunic torn. He saluted mechanically when Derrick ran up but said nothing till Burke arrived, breathing like a leaky bellows.

“I don’t know what to make of this, sergeant. The fellow ran practically into my arms before he knew where he was and put up no end of a fight. He got his finger into my throat and would have done me in if I hadn’t thrown him. Then he got up and went for me again like a wild animal. I got this thing away from him, and he spun round on his toes, put something in his mouth, and crumpled up. Now he looks as though he were dead, but I haven’t used any unnecessary force.”

“All right, Peters; he’s not dead. It’s only bluff. You can make your charge now, Mr. Derrick, and we’ll run him in.”

“Charge? I’ve nothing to charge him with.”

===Chinese===

树篱那一边，货郎直挺挺躺着，警员（constable）俯身在他上方。警员满脸通红，制服领子撕破了。德里克跑过来时，他机械地行了礼，却一言不发，直到伯克赶到，喘得像一只漏风的风箱。

"我说不上这算怎么回事，巡佐。这家伙还没弄清自己在哪儿，就简直撞进了我怀里，接着没完没了地顽抗。他把手指捅进我嗓子，要不是我把他摔出去，我就交代了。后来他爬起来，又像野兽似的朝我扑。我把这东西从他手里夺下来，他就脚尖一旋，往嘴里塞了点什么，瘫了下去。这会儿他看着像死了，可我没有用多余的力气。"

"好了，彼得斯；他没死。那是唬人。您现在可以提出控告了，德里克先生，我们把他抓进去。"

"控告？我没什么可控告他的。"

===Original===

Burke grinned. “Do I take it that he attempted burglary and smashed that door by your request?”

Derrick laughed outright. “I’d clean forgotten that already.” What he did not tell the sergeant was that somehow he felt immeasurably younger and happier.

“Well, it’s plenty to hold him for a while till we get at the real thing. This will be theft and damage to property. Pick him up, Peters!”

“One minute,” interrupted Derrick. “Did he say anything to you?”

“Not a word, sir.”

===Chinese===

伯克咧嘴一笑。"那我这样理解对不对：他入室行窃未遂、砸坏那扇门，是奉了您的请求？"

德里克放声大笑。"这个我早忘得一干二净了。"他没告诉巡佐的是：不知怎么，他觉得自己年轻了无数岁，也快活了无数分。

"嗬，凭这个够押他一阵子，等我们够着正经的。罪名是偷窃加毁坏财物。把他抬起来，彼得斯！"

"慢着。"德里克插进来，"他同你说过什么吗？"

"一个字也没有，先生。"

===Original===

The young man did not answer but knelt quickly beside the prone figure. A sickly color, half gray, half blue, was stealing slowly over the peddler’s features. His eyes, partly open, were glazed and sightless. His body, so lately animated by amazing vigor, had crumpled like a wet leaf. Derrick, feeling himself queerly numb, slid a hand under the torn shirt. No pulse of life was discernible. Close by lay the jade god, its tiny malignant face sneering up from the wet grass. The master of Beech Lodge saw it and shuddered. Was this the next man appointed to die, and had he been the prophet of his own passing? Then Burke knelt beside him, stared hard in his turn, and gave the white beard a strong and sudden jerk.

It came away in his hand, revealing a thin, oval face, a firm mouth and chin, the face of a man not over forty. The jerk had parted the lips, and these sent out a mocking grin, suggesting that it was nothing to Blunt what they did now. Derrick’s breath nearly stopped. A new shadow fell across the body. He looked up and saw Martin. There was a grim satisfaction in the gardener’s dark eyes. It shot through Derrick’s mind that this would free Martin from further suspicion. Burke stared at him, too, then at Derrick. He did not speak, but the same thought was in his mind. He turned again to the limp figure in the grass.

“It looks as though your friend were done for this time, Martin. I’ll not ask you anything now. Your opportunity will come later. Better give Peters a hand and take this chap to the cottage.”

===Chinese===

年轻人没有回答，径直在那俯卧的身影旁跪下。一种病恹恹的颜色，半灰半蓝，正慢慢漫上货郎的五官。他的眼睛半睁着，玻璃一样，没有视物。他的身体——方才还灌注着惊人的活力——这时瘫了，像一片湿透的叶子。德里克觉得自己古怪地麻木着，把一只手滑进那撕破的衬衫底下。生命的脉搏摸不到了。近旁躺着玉神，那张小小的凶脸从湿草里朝上冷笑。山毛榉山庄的主人看见它，打了个寒噤。这就是下一个注定要死的人吗？他可做了自己死亡的先知？这时伯克也跪到他身边，狠狠盯了一阵，伸手把那把白胡子猛地一拽。

胡子应手落在他掌中，露出一张瘦长的鹅蛋脸，一张坚定的嘴和下巴——一个不出四十岁的男人的脸。那一拽掰开了双唇，唇间送出一个讥嘲的咧笑，仿佛在说：如今随他们怎么办，布伦特全不在乎。德里克的呼吸几乎停了。一道新的影子落上那具躯体。他抬头，看见了马丁。园丁的黑眼睛里盛着一种冷峻的快意。这念头从德里克心里闪过：这一来，马丁算是不再受嫌疑了。伯克也盯着他看，又看德里克。他没有说话，心里想的却是同一件事。他重新转向草地上那瘫软的身影。

"看你这位朋友这回是完了，马丁。现在我什么都不问你。你的机会往后有。帮彼得斯一把，把这小子抬到小屋去吧。"

===Original===

The peddler was carried away, his slight frame sagging limply between gardener and constable. Derrick, watching this, yielded to a vivid realization of the immutability of fate. Ten minutes ago this man was charged with life, throbbing with a desire that he hugged to his soul, and for which he had journeyed from a mysterious country, forgetting all else in one supreme ambition. Now the thing that had driven him thus far had struck its own ambassador, the next appointed to die, and the thing itself leered up from the ground at his feet, malevolent, devilish, and seemingly yet unsatiated. Derrick picked up a stone and was about to splinter the sneering jade when something flickered in the green eyes, mocking and immune, warning him that the time was not yet. Presently he felt that Burke was regarding him with broad amusement.

“If I may say so, sir, I wouldn’t smash it yet. We’ll need it for evidence, and if possible I’d like to hear what Perkins and your gardener have to say about the thing. Shall I take it to the station?”

Derrick stiffened. “No, thanks,” he said abruptly. “I’ll look after it till it’s needed. I think perhaps it feels more at home at the Lodge.”

He picked up the jade god from the ground, dropped it in his pocket as though the touch burned him, and went slowly across the lawn beside Burke. Passing the house, he saw Edith at a bedroom window and waved her a cheery greeting. She signaled back, and he noticed that she smiled with relief. What a standby she had been, he reflected. In a flash his thoughts sped to Jean. He had not seen Perkins, but the woman was at the study window, her hands at her thin breast, a sort of ecstatic joy in her sallow face. So on to the cottage, where the peddler’s body had been deposited on the kitchen floor. Derrick regarded it silently, and again that recurrent sense of unreality came over him.

===Chinese===

货郎被抬走了，轻瘦的身架在园丁和警员中间软软地垂着。德里克望着这一幕，深深觉出命运的不可移易。十分钟前，这人还满身充着生命，为一个他搂在灵魂里的欲念而搏动；为了它，他从一片神秘的国土远道而来，在一桩至高的野心面前忘掉了其余一切。如今，把他一路驱到这里的那个东西，击倒了它自己的使者——下一个注定要死的人；而那东西本身，正在他脚边从地上睨着笑，恶毒，邪恶，而且看样子还没有餍足。德里克拾起一块石头，正要把那冷笑的玉砸个粉碎，翡翠的眼睛里忽然有什么一闪——嘲弄的，不受伤的——警告他时候未到。随即他觉出伯克正满脸津津有味地瞧着他。

"要我说的话，先生，先别砸。我们还要它做证物；再就是，能成的话，我想听听珀金斯和您家园丁对这东西怎么说。要不要我把它带回警局？"

德里克绷住了。"不，谢了。"他突兀地说，"要用之前，我来保管。我想，它或许还是待在山庄自在些。"

他从地上拾起玉神，往口袋里一丢，仿佛那一触烧手，随后同伯克并肩慢慢走过草坪。经过宅子时，他看见伊迪丝在一扇卧室窗前，朝她快活地挥了挥手。她回了信号，他注意到她宽慰地笑了。这些日子她是怎样一根顶梁柱啊，他想。他的念头倏地飞到琴身上。他没有碰见珀金斯，可那女人就立在书房窗前，双手抵着瘦削的胸口，蜡黄的脸上是一种出神的喜乐。就这样走到了小屋，货郎的尸身已经安放在厨房地板上。德里克默默看着它，那阵阵来袭的不真实感，又一次罩住了他。

===Original===

“What next?” He turned to the sergeant.

“Nothing at the moment, sir, till we get hold of Dr. Henry. It will be queer to have him here once more in the same matter. Had this man any possessions, Martin?”

The gardener gave an odd smile and picked out of the corner a tightly knotted pack.

“This was all I saw. It’s trinkets and suchlike, but he didn’t show them to me.”

“Has this not been opened since Blunt gave Mr. Derrick that sight of his wares?”

===Chinese===

"接下来怎么办？"他转向巡佐。

"眼下没事可办，先生，等我们把亨利医生（Dr. Henry）请来。为同一桩案子，又要请他到这儿来一回，可真叫古怪。这人有什么随身物件吗，马丁？"

园丁咧出一个古怪的笑，从墙角拎出一个捆得死紧的货包。

"我就见过这个。是些小摆设之类，他没拿来给我看。"

"打布伦特给德里克先生看过他的货色以后，这包就没开过？"

===Original===

“Not so far as I know. He slept in his clothes.”

Burke fingered the bundle but did not slacken its knots. He sent Derrick a thoughtful glance.

“It’s not likely there’s anything else of importance, and from what we’ve seen today we’re pretty near the end of the Millicent case. Would you step outside a minute, sir?”

Derrick followed him, wondering a little. Burke halted out of earshot.

“I don’t want to say anything unnecessary in front of Martin,” he explained, “but all we’ll need now is what I’m convinced they are ready to tell us about Blunt’s last visit. We’re in a position to use pressure to bring out that evidence, and with it will come the reason, which so far beats me, for their ever trying to conceal the fact that he was the murderer. One thing I can imagine is that he had them hypnotized in some way, and as a matter of fact I began to feel hypnotized myself when I was listening to that chant of his outside the French window. Did you get any of it? If it had not been for that I would have nabbed the chap when he came out. As it was I felt half asleep.”

===Chinese===

"据我所知没有。他睡觉都不脱衣裳。"

伯克拨了拨那包袱，却没有松它的结。他若有所思地朝德里克望了一眼。

"里头再有什么要紧东西，多半指望不上；照今天这一幕看，米莉森特的案子快到头了。您肯到外头站一分钟吗，先生？"

德里克跟了出去，心里有点纳闷。伯克在听不见的地方停住脚。

"我是不想在马丁跟前多说废话，"他解释道，"可如今我们缺的，只剩布伦特上一回来访的事——我笃定他们憋着想告诉我们了。我们如今的位置，尽可以加点压力，把那证词挤出来；连带也就出来了那个缘由——为什么他们一直想瞒下他是凶手这个事实——这缘由到如今还把我难住。我能想到的一种是：他用什么法子把他们催眠了；说实话，方才我在落地窗外头听他念那咒似的调子，我自己都开始觉着被催眠了。您听出什么没有？要不是那调子，他一出来我就把他拿了。结果呢，我半睡半醒的。"

===Original===

Derrick nodded reminiscently. “Yes, I did get it. Anything else with regard to either Martin or Perkins?”

“Nothing tonight, except that I would not say another word. Let this thing soak in, and it will do the work for us. In a day or so they will both be anxious to tell all they know. Now, just as a matter of precaution, I’m going to search the cottage, with your permission.”

“All right. It’s practically empty. Martin only brought a bundle, and I sent him a few odds and ends from the house to make the place livable. Shall I tell him?”

“Yes, sir, if you please.”

They went in together. Peters had lit his pipe and was smoking placidly with no concern for the thing on the floor, but Martin stood, still staring down. There was a kind of wonder in his face, and with it a strange thankfulness. He was like a man who straightens his shoulders after they have been crushed by some killing load.

===Chinese===

德里克回味地点点头。"听了，听出了一点。关于马丁或者珀金斯，还有别的吗？"

"今晚没有了——只有一条：一个字也别再提。让这事在他们心里洇透，它自会替我们做工。一两天内，他们俩都会抢着把知道的全倒出来。还有，防患于未然，我想搜一搜小屋——您许可的话。"

"行。那屋里几乎是空的。马丁只带了一个包袱，我又从宅子里送了他些零七八碎，好叫那地方住得下去。要我去同他说吗？"

"好，先生，劳驾您。"

两人一同进去。彼得斯已经点上了烟斗，心安理得地抽着，全不理会地板上那样东西；马丁却站着，还朝下望着。他脸上有一种惊奇，伴着惊奇的是一种古怪的感恩。他像一个卸下压碎肩头的要命重负、终于直起腰来的人。

===Original===

“Martin,” said Derrick crisply, “Sergeant Burke is going to make a search of the cottage.”

“That’s all right so far as I’m concerned, sir, but there isn’t anything here except what anyone can see.”

There was that in his apparent readiness which gave his master a feeling of solid relief. The latter found himself glad to admit that for months he had been on the wrong trail. There were matters still to be explained, deliberate lies to be accounted for, that secret search of the study to be acknowledged and justified; but all this, thought Derrick, was mysteriously involved with the potent thing that now dragged at his pocket, and when the light did come no corner would be left obscure. He remembered, too, that at times Martin had looked like an honest man. And did villains ever love roses like this gardener of his?

“Martin,” he said, “you’d better leave the sergeant alone while he’s making this search; he won’t need you.”

The man nodded with the air of one who has nothing to fear, cast another contented glance at the peddler’s body, and went out. They watched him cross the drive, hesitate a moment as though deliberating which way to turn, then stand, his hands deep in his pockets, staring down the road. Again Derrick felt reassured.

===Chinese===

"马丁，"德里克干脆地说，"伯克巡佐要搜一搜小屋。"

"我没什么说的，先生，只是这儿除了人人看得见的，再没有别的了。"

他那副显然的爽快里，有一点叫他的主人觉出踏实的宽慰。主人发现自己乐于承认：几个月来，他走的是一条岔道。还有事情待解释，有处心积虑的谎话要交代，还有那趟对书房的秘密搜查要认账、要说清；可这一切——德里克想——都同此刻坠着他口袋的那件有权能的东西神秘地绞在一起；等光真到来时，哪个角落也不会留着昏暗。他还记得，马丁有时候看着像个老实人。再说，恶棍可有像他这园丁这样爱玫瑰的？

"马丁，"他说，"巡佐搜的时候，你顶好别在他跟前转；他用不着你。"

那人点了点头，一副心里没鬼的气派，又朝货郎的尸身投去餍足的一眼，出去了。他们看着他穿过车道，踌躇了一下，像在盘算往哪边走，随后站定，双手深插在口袋里，朝大路那头望去。德里克又一次放了心。

===Original===

“Sergeant, I’m greatly relieved about that chap, even though I did bark up the wrong tree.”

Burke rubbed his big palms together. “Well, sir, it was a fortunate kind of bark just the same.”

“So it’s turned out. Now while you’re making this search could the constable go up and stay in front of the house? Also, he might just assure Miss Derrick that everything is quite all right. She’ll be more convinced if it doesn’t come from me.”

Peters got his orders, and the two were alone. Burke gave a broad grin. The idea of promotion had flashed into his mind. Then he, too, indulged in a long stare at what had been Blunt.

“Well, sir, I expect we’ve both got the same conclusion in our heads now. Curious, too, how it’s come about.”

===Chinese===

"巡佐，那人一了结，我心里轻松透了——虽说先前是我扑错了树。"

伯克搓着他两只大手掌。"嗬，先生，这一扑倒是扑了个吉利。"

"结果也正是。对了，趁你搜的这工夫，能让那位警员上去守在宅子前头吗？再让他去同德里克小姐（Miss Derrick）说一声，一切都妥了。这话不出自我口，她反倒更信。"

彼得斯领了命走了，屋里剩下两个人。伯克咧开大嘴一笑。升官的念头已经闪过他心里。随后他也对着布伦特的原身，久久端详了一阵。

"嗬，先生，我估摸着这会儿咱们俩脑袋里是同一个结论。怪也就怪在，事情竟是这样成的。"

===Original===

“What’s that, sergeant?”

“That we needn’t dig any deeper to find the man who killed Mr. Millicent. That theory of a criminal returning to the scene of his crime certainly worked in this case.”

“Yes,” said Derrick thoughtfully, “but what brought Martin back?”

“I’ve an idea we’ll get that out of him in a day or two. Have you studied this chap’s face, sir?”

===Chinese===

"什么结论，巡佐？"

"就是：要找杀米莉森特先生的人，不必再往深里挖了。罪犯重返作案现场那条道理，在这案子上算是应验了。"

"是啊，"德里克若有所思地说，"可又是什么把马丁带回来的呢？"

"我有个想法：一两天里就能从他嘴里掏出来。您端详过这家伙的脸没有，先生？"

===Original===

Derrick scrutinized the rigid features. They were gray now, the lips still set in a strange cynical smile. It was not the face of a peddler but had unmistakable signs of birth and breeding. The head was well shaped, the ears small and set close to a finely molded skull, the forehead high and rather broad, the eyes far apart. Nothing of the murderer was suggested here, but much of the dreamer, the visionary, the adventurer of sudden purpose. Over him was the touch of the East, visible in the olive tinge of his skin, the slenderness of hands and wrists, and the faint blueness at the base of his narrow fingernails. Derrick pondered over the possible history of this man with the build of an aristocrat and the insignia of the Orient. What strange tales those fixed lips might have told. But they were all his secret now.

“He’s not a peddler,” he said, turning to Burke, “and probably never was. We’ll have to depend on Martin and perhaps Perkins for the rest of it. Are you going to have a look at that pack of his?”

It was unrolled on the floor beside its late owner but revealed nothing more than the trinkets Derrick had already seen. The man’s pockets were empty save for a knife and a few coins, and the clothing itself bore no marks that yielded the slightest clue to his identity. Burke made a grimace.

===Chinese===

德里克细察那副僵住的五官。这会儿它们灰了，双唇还凝着那个古怪的、愤世的笑。这不是一张货郎的脸，分明带着出身与教养的印记。头型端正，耳朵小巧，贴着一颗塑造精微的头颅，额头高而开阔，两眼分得很开。这里看不出一点凶手的样子，倒看得出许多梦想家、幻视家、突发奇想的冒险家的东西。他身上覆着东方的一触，看得见处在他皮肤的橄榄色、手与腕的纤细、窄指甲根上淡淡的那圈青。德里克琢磨着这个人可能的历史：一副贵族的骨架，一身东方的徽记。那两片闭紧的唇里，本可以吐出多少离奇的旧事。可如今，那些全是他一个人的秘密了。

"他不是货郎，"他转身对伯克说，"多半从来也不是。剩下的，我们只得指望马丁，也许还有珀金斯。他那包货，你要不要看一看？"

包袱在原主身旁的地板上摊开了，露出的无非是德里克已经见过的那些小玩意。那人的口袋里只有一把小刀和几枚硬币，衣裳本身也拿不出半点能指认他身份的印记。伯克做了个苦脸。

===Original===

“We’ve drawn a blank this time; now I’ll have a look through the cottage. How long did you say Martin had been with you?”

“Something more than three months now, and he brought all he had on his back. I don’t fancy you’ll find much of interest here.”

The sergeant rooted about with a certain methodical deliberation, finally coming to a small bureau, the drawers of which he pulled open with the manner of one who expects nothing. Martin’s personal property was in truth scanty. He paused at the bottom drawer and looked up.

“Matter of fact, Mr. Derrick, while we know our dead friend is the fellow who held the knife, we’ve got to admit that we can’t prove it unless we drag the truth out of the others. Martin must know perfectly well that he’s up against a sort of third degree examination, and what convinces me that he’s ready to give us the inside of this thing is that already he’s looking almost cheerful. And if he weakens, that woman Perkins will weaken, too. I’m about finished here now.”

===Chinese===

"这回我们抽了个空签；现在我搜一遍小屋。您方才说，马丁跟了您多久了？"

"三个多月了，他把全部家当都穿在身上带来的。这里头我不指望你找出什么有意思的。"

巡佐按着章法不紧不慢地翻寻，末了来到一张小写字台前，用一种不指望什么的神气拉开抽屉。马丁的私物实在少得可怜。他在最底下的抽屉前停住，抬起头来。

"实话说，德里克先生，咱们虽然知道咱们这位死掉的朋友就是持刀的那位，也得承认：不从旁人嘴里把真相拖出来，咱们就证明不了。马丁心里透亮，他躲不过一场熬审（third degree）；叫我信他预备把这案子的内里全交给我们的，是他这会儿看着几乎有几分轻快了。他要是一松口，珀金斯那个女人也会松。我这儿也搜得差不多了。"

===Original===

He jerked open the last drawer as he spoke, jerked so strongly that it came out on the floor. Like the others it was empty. But between the bottom of it and the floor itself lay a small bundle of dirty shirts.

“Your man isn’t what you’d call exactly a careful housekeeper. He needs a wife.” He picked up the bundle between thumb and forefinger. “Look at this.”

Came a dull knock, a clatter on the floor, and a knife with a broad, curved blade a foot long and a strangely carved handle slid across the floor and rested almost touching the lifeless palm of the peddler. The big man drew in his breath with a great gust and stood glowering. His eyes met Derrick’s.

===Chinese===

他说话间猛地拽开最后一个抽屉，拽得那么猛，抽屉整个落到了地板上。同其余的一样，它是空的。可是在抽屉底和地板之间，躺着一小包脏衬衫。

"您这人可算不上什么讲究的当家汉。他缺个老婆。"他拇指食指拈起那包东西。"瞧这个。"

闷闷一声磕响，一样东西当啷落在地板上——一柄刀，阔而弯的刀身足有一英尺长，柄上雕着古怪的花样——它滑过地板，停下来时，几乎碰着货郎那只没有生气的手掌。

===Original===

“Call in your gardener!” he said huskily.

Derrick’s brain was in a whirl. He stared back and, not trusting himself to speak, tapped at the window. He could see Peters pacing slowly up and down in front of the Lodge, and Martin, who was still standing in the same place, apparently plunged in thought. The latter turned at the sound, mechanically touched his cap, and came slowly back. The sergeant nodded, put his hand in his pocket, and stepped a little on one side of the door. A shadow darkened the threshold, and as the gardener crossed it a grasp of iron fastened on his shoulder.

“John Martin, I arrest you for complicity in the murder of Henry Millicent. Anything you now say may be used against you.”

===Chinese===

大个子一大口倒吸了凉气，铁青着脸站住。他的眼睛碰上了德里克的眼睛。

德里克的脑子轰的一声乱了。他瞪回去，不敢信自己的嗓子，便敲了敲窗。他看见彼得斯在宅子前头慢慢踱步，马丁还站在老地方，像沉在心事里。听见敲窗，马丁转过身，机械地碰了碰帽檐，慢慢往回走。巡佐点点头，一只手插进口袋，侧身闪到门边。一道影子暗了门坎；园丁跨过去时，一只铁爪扣上了他的肩膀。

"约翰·马丁（John Martin），你因同谋杀害亨利·米莉森特（Henry Millicent）被捕。你现在说的任何话，都可能用作对你不利的证词。"

===Original===

A few minutes later Derrick walked slowly and rather wearily toward the house, and Edith met him at the door. For her the past hour had been full of a drama almost too tense for her practical soul, and she realized what it must have meant to her brother. One look at his face was enough. She hooked her arm into his and led him into the dining-room, where dinner was ready. At the door she pressed his hand for an understanding instant.

“I’m not going to say a thing about it, nor are you, till afterward. Perkins saw the whole thing, and the poor woman is happier than I’ve ever seen her. Congratulations, brother; and now forget it for an hour.”

He sat down with a vast relief. It seemed strange that in the midst of this deadly game such matters as food and cooking should proceed uninterrupted. It was Perkins’s work, Perkins, who, outwardly undisturbed by that which must have shaken her very spirit, was still the perfect servant, the ageless domestic automaton. He knew that Edith did not want him to look at the woman, but could not refrain from quick cursory glances at moments when she could not detect them. There was really no difference, except that the sallow cheeks had a faint color, and the lips were a shade less grim than usual. For the rest of it her face was still a mask, her figure just as unbending, her movements just as measured and deliberate. But what secret thoughts must be traversing that unlocked mind, what emotions stirring in her breast! And through it all she seemed not to know that he was there.

===Chinese===

几分钟以后，德里克缓缓地、颇有些疲惫地朝宅子走去，伊迪丝在门口迎住他。过去这一个钟头，对她这颗讲实际的灵魂来说，那场戏紧张得几乎过了头；她也明白，这于她哥哥意味着什么。看一眼他的脸就足够了。她挽起他的手臂，把他领进饭厅，晚饭已经备好。在门口，她紧了紧他的手，会心地一瞬。

"这件事我一个字不提，你也别说，过后再说。珀金斯把整件事都看在眼里了，那可怜的女人，比我见过的任何时候都快活。恭喜你，哥哥；现在，把它丢开一个钟头。"

他坐下来，一身说不出的轻松。说来奇怪：这场要命的游戏正中间，饭菜和烹调这样的营生，竟照旧一环不乱。这是珀金斯的活计——珀金斯，那件必定摇撼过她灵魂的事，在她外表上竟不见一丝波纹，仍是那完满的仆人，那不知岁月的家务机器。他知道伊迪丝不愿意他去看那女人，可他忍不住，趁她察觉不到的当口，飞快地扫上一眼。实在看不出什么分别，只是蜡黄的脸颊上有一点淡淡的血色，嘴唇比平时松了那么一丝。其余的，她的脸还是一副面具，身板还是那样挺直不弯，动作还是那样有板有眼、从容不迫。可是，多少隐秘的念头该正穿过那颗解了锁的心，怎样的情绪该正在她胸中翻搅！而自始至终，她仿佛并不知道他在场。

===Original===

Later, in the study, he filled his pipe, shot a contented glance at Millicent’s portrait, took the jade god from his pocket, and set it on the desk where so often it had glimmered before. Edith scanned it with an interest she had never displayed till this evening, and sank comfortably into a big chair.

“Well,” she said curiously, “aren’t you going to tell me anything about it?”

“Yes, dear, everything.” He paused for a moment. “First of all, the thing is practically over, except another inquest and what will naturally follow that.”

“The last thing I saw was that poor man being carried to the cottage. Then that nice constable came up and talked to me as though I were six years old. I did like that. But there was no real information in it.”

Derrick laughed. “I’m afraid I did that.”

===Chinese===

后来，在书房里，他装满烟斗，朝米莉森特的画像投去餍足的一瞥，从口袋里取出玉神，摆上书桌——它从前在那上头幽幽放光，已有多少回了。伊迪丝打量它，那份兴致直到今晚才头一回露出来，随后舒舒服服陷进一把大椅子。

"怎么样，"她好奇地说，"你打算一点口风都不向我露吗？"

"讲，亲爱的，全讲。"他顿了片刻。"首先，这件事差不多完了，只剩又一场验尸审讯（inquest），和随之自然而然要来的那些。"

"我最后看见的，是那个可怜人被抬进小屋。随后那位和气的警员上来同我说话，倒像我只有六岁。那样子我真爱。可惜里头没有一点正经的消息。"

德里克笑了。"那恐怕是我吩咐的。"

===Original===

“I thought you had. Did you notice any difference in Perkins at dinner? Isn’t her control amazing?”

“Not much, except that she seemed in a way less grim.”

“Of course she is. She must have suspected the peddler all along, and when she saw him carried off like that one can imagine what she felt—at least one could if it weren’t Perkins.” She hesitated. “Is he dead?” she asked gravely.

He nodded. “The life seemed to go out of him when he was struggling with the constable. Peters said he put something in his mouth—which was no doubt poison.”

Edith shuddered. “How dreadful! It was the fear of the other kind of death, wasn’t it? What did Martin say or do then?”

===Chinese===

"我就猜是你。晚饭时你留意到珀金斯有什么两样吗？她的克制是不是叫人吃惊？"

"没大两样，只是她仿佛松了些，不那么紧绷着了。"

"她当然松了。她必定从头就疑着那货郎；看见他就那样被抬走，她心里什么滋味，想也想得出——至少，换个人想得出；偏偏她是珀金斯。"她迟疑了一下。"他死了吗？"她正色问。

他点点头。"他同警员搏斗的时候，那口气就像散了。彼得斯说他往嘴里放了什么——不用说，是毒药。"

伊迪丝打了个寒噤。"多可怕！那是怕另一种死法，对不对？马丁那时说了什么，做了什么？"

===Original===

“Nothing, but stare and stare and look satisfied in a grim sort of way.”

“He must have been something more than satisfied; so is Perkins. This is probably the first evening for two years when they have known peace. You remember, Jack, I told you I didn’t think Martin was really guilty.”

“Martin,” said Derrick slowly, “is now in jail, charged with complicity in Millicent’s murder.”

At the door came a sudden and violent crash. It had opened without sound, and there stood Perkins with the ruins of coffee-cups at her feet. Her hands were gripped together, her lips parted, and the suffering of the damned was written on her colorless cheeks. Her eyes, now large and staring, seemed to be fixed immovably on space. Then, imperceptibly, she regained a sort of shuddering consciousness.

“I’m extremely sorry, madam, but I tripped over the doormat.”

===Chinese===

"什么也没说，只是瞪着，瞪着，一副冷森森的餍足。"

"他岂止是餍足；珀金斯也是。这多半是两年来他们头一个得着安宁的夜晚。你还记得吧，杰克，我说过，我从不认为马丁真有罪。"

"马丁，"德里克缓缓地说，"这会儿在押了，罪名是同谋杀害米莉森特。"

门口猛地响起一声剧烈的碎裂。门原是无声开着的，珀金斯立在当处，脚边是一摊咖啡杯的残骸。她双手死死绞在一起，嘴唇张着，惨白的脸颊上写满了堕入地狱者的苦楚。她那双此刻睁大直瞪的眼睛，仿佛钉死在虚空上。随后，不显山不水地，她挣回了某种战栗着的知觉。

"实在对不住，太太，我叫门口的蹭鞋垫绊了一下。"

===Original===

The voice was lifeless, devoid of inflection, so flat as to be almost unhuman. She stooped, gathered up the shattered china, and disappeared. Edith, too shaken for a moment to speak, regarded her brother with frightened astonishment.

“What do you mean?” she stammered presently.

“Exactly that. Neither you nor Perkins could see what happened after Blunt was taken to the cottage.”

He went on with a sort of labored carefulness and told her all, shooting meanwhile quick glances at the door, where shortly Perkins would reappear. Neither of them doubted that she would be master enough of herself for this. In the middle of it she came in, looking straight ahead. The tremor had left her body, her hands were again steady, her face impassive as ever. She put the tray beside her mistress and went out. At the click of the latch Edith gave a gasp.

“I didn’t know such a woman existed,” she whispered. “Till a minute ago she thought that Martin was a free man and innocent.”

===Chinese===

那嗓音没有生气，没有抑扬，平板得几乎不似人声。她俯身拾起碎瓷，消失了。伊迪丝一时震得说不出话，带着惊骇望着哥哥。

"你这是什么意思？"她半晌才结巴出来。

"就是那个意思。布伦特被抬进小屋以后的事，你同珀金斯都看不见。"

他带着一种吃力的仔细，接着把一切讲给她听，一面朝门口飞快地瞟——珀金斯转眼就要再进来。两人都不怀疑，这一关她照样拿得下来。讲到一半，她进来了，目视前方。颤栗已经离开了她的身体，双手重新平稳，脸同往常一样不动声色。她把托盘放到女主人身旁，出去了。门闩咔哒一响，伊迪丝倒抽一口气。

"我竟不知道世上有这样的女人。"她低声说。"到一分钟以前，她还想的是：马丁是个自由身，而且清白。"

===Original===

He shook his head. “Free, perhaps, but not innocent. It was obvious from what little I got out of her this afternoon that she was doing all she could to divert suspicion to Blunt, without actually accusing him. She was afraid of Blunt and wanted to get rid of him.”

“But why save Martin at the expense of Blunt?”

“That I can’t say.”

“But the only evidence you have against Martin is that the kris was found hidden in his cottage wrapped up in his clothing?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===

他摇了摇头。"自由，也许；清白，未必。今天下午我从她嘴里挤出的那一点，已经再明白不过：她在竭尽全力把嫌疑引到布伦特身上，只是不当真指控他。她怕布伦特，想除掉他。"

"可为什么牺牲布伦特，保马丁呢？"

"这个我说不上。"

"可你们手里的证据，无非是那柄克里斯短剑，藏在他小屋里、裹在他衣裳里被搜出来？"

"是。"

===Original===

“Could that be called final and sufficient? Could he be convicted on that?”

“It’s enough to start with and puts it up to him to disprove his guilt, and he can’t do that without telling the whole story.”

Edith was unconverted. “He actually left that thing, which may be enough to condemn him, hidden in an old shirt where anyone could have found it. That doesn’t seem likely, does it?”

“Perhaps not, but there it was.”

“Jack,” she said suddenly, “that’s not the action of a guilty man. How long had the peddler been there?”

===Chinese===

"那能算铁证、算十足吗？凭那个能给他定罪？"

"开头足够了，逼着他自己去洗清嫌疑；而不把整个故事和盘托出，他就洗不清。"

伊迪丝没有被说服。"他居然把那样一件、也许足够定他罪的东西，塞在一件旧衬衫里，藏在谁都找得到的地方。这看着像话吗？"

"也许不像话，可它就在那儿。"

"杰克，"她忽然说，"有罪的人不干这样的事。那货郎到这儿多久了？"

===Original===

“Only a few hours, as you know.”

“And why did he ask if this room was the same as it was the night of the murder?”

“I’ve been puzzling over that. It could not have been a shot in the dark, and it laid him open to the suspicion that he had seen the place before.”

“Then, listen, Jack,” she said excitedly. “I’m sure he did see the place before. Everything points to that, and you’ve got the wrong man, and it was Blunt who killed Mr. Millicent on account of that thing.” She pointed to the jade god. “Can’t you see how clear it is? He had some sort of hold over Martin and Perkins, probably through that same horrid influence, and they were afraid to incriminate him. Two years afterward he turns up again, and Martin was amazed and terrified to see him, thinking the matter was done with. While he is with Martin, and that was very cleverly arranged, they have arguments which you overheard, and somehow he manages to conceal in Martin’s clothes the knife, or one just like it, before making another attempt at the image. You’ll have to be frightfully careful now what is done, or an innocent man may be punished.”

Derrick looked at her, genuinely puzzled.

===Chinese===

"不过几个钟头，你是知道的。"

"还有，他为什么要问，这屋子同凶案那晚是不是一个样？"

"这一直叫我纳闷。那不会是瞎蒙的一枪；那一问，反倒把他自己亮了出来，教人疑心他从前见过这个地方。"

"那你听着，杰克，"她兴奋地说，"我确信他从前就是见过这个地方。一切都指向这一条：你抓错了人；为着那样东西，杀米莉森特先生的是布伦特。"她指着玉神。"你难道看不见这里头的清楚？他手里攥着马丁和珀金斯什么把柄，多半就是凭那同一股邪劲，他们才不敢咬他。两年之后他重新露面，马丁看见他，又惊又怕，只当那件事早了结了。趁他同马丁在一处——这一手安排得真巧——两人拌了嘴，叫你听了去；他不知怎么把那把刀，或者一把一模一样的刀，藏进了马丁的衣裳，然后才去再夺神像。往下你们得千万小心行事，不然一个无辜的人可能遭殃。"

德里克看着她，当真迷惑了。

===Original===

“There may be something in that. Anything else to suggest?”

“No, I’m not a detective, but it’s the way any sensible person would look at it, if I may say so. And, yes, there is one thing.”

“What is that?”

“I’d go straight to Jean tomorrow morning and tell her the whole story. She might be able to help, as it will probably suggest other things to her you haven’t discussed yet.”

Derrick took a long breath. “I will,” he said.

===Chinese===

"这话兴许有点道理。还有什么要提的？"

"没了。我不是侦探；可要我说句话，凡是个明白人，都会这么看。对了，倒还有一件。"

"什么？"

"依我说，明儿一早就该直接去见琴，把整个故事原原本本讲给她听。她说不定帮得上忙；这故事多半会让她想起些你们还没议到的旁的事。"

德里克长长吸了一口气。"我会去的。"他说。

## XIII The Sacrifice / 第十三章 祭献

===Original===

Three days later Mrs. Millicent and Jean turned in at the gate of Beech Lodge. It was the first time in more than two years they had been there together. Halfway up the drive they were met by Edith, who came out anxious to do what she could to help in what she knew was a trying moment. She kissed Jean affectionately.

“I’m so glad to see you both. You’ll find the house at loose ends, for it isn’t actually running, but just moving, so please forgive that. Our temporary servants are very temporary, I’m afraid.”

Mrs. Millicent nodded. She had dreaded the visit and somehow felt more at peace than she had expected. But her heart sank a little when she entered the house. In the hall she looked mutely about and hesitated as Edith led the way to the study.

“Jack doesn’t know you’re here yet,” said the latter cheerfully. “I’m rather pleased with him today.”

===Chinese===

三天之后，米莉森特太太（Mrs. Millicent）和琴（Jean）在山毛榉山庄（Beech Lodge）的大门口拐了进去。两年多来，这是她们头一回一同回到这里。车道走到一半，伊迪丝（Edith）迎了上来——她明知这是个煎熬的时刻，特意出宅来迎，只想尽自己所能帮衬一把。她亲热地吻了琴。

"见到你们两位，我真是高兴。宅子里眼下乱着呢，因为它其实还没运转起来，只是刚搬动，还请多多包涵。我们那几个临时仆人，只怕临时得很彻底。"

米莉森特太太点了点头。她本来怕极了这一趟，不知怎么，心里竟比预想的平静。可一走进宅子，她的心还是微微一沉。在门厅里，她默默地环顾四周，待伊迪丝引路往书房去时，脚下踌躇起来。

"杰克（Jack）还不知道你们来了。"伊迪丝欢欢喜喜地说，"他今天倒叫我相当满意。"

===Original===

“Why?” smiled Jean.

“He’s actually got to work again, more like the old Jack than for months. I hope you’ll keep him at it when your turn comes.”

She opened the door as she spoke. Derrick, who was behind a litter of manuscript, jumped up, thrilled at the sight of his visitor. Mrs. Millicent’s eyes swept the familiar room, fighting lest she see what she feared to see. She noted that the big desk was now covered with baize, the rugs differently arranged, the prints rehung, and a flower-box in the window. Photographs were on the table, another lamp on the desk, new ornaments on the narrow shelf above the dark wainscoting. She recognized the thought that lay behind all this, and it touched her deeply. Then her glance was drawn to the portrait, and she sat down, overcome for the moment.

“Please don’t mind me,” she said valiantly. “I’ll be all right in a second, and it’s quite right I should come here first.” She looked gratefully at Edith. “I’ll be able to say thank you presently. Somehow you’ve made the room seem ever so much bigger.”

===Chinese===

"为什么？"琴含笑问。

"他当真又埋头干活了，几个月来头一回像是旧日的杰克。但愿往后轮到你管他的时候，你也能叫他这么勤快。"

她一面说，一面推开了门。德里克（Derrick）正埋首在一堆凌乱的字稿后面，一见来人，跳起身来，满面喜色。米莉森特太太的目光扫过这间熟悉的屋子，一面同自己交战着，唯恐看见自己怕看见的东西。她注意到那张大书桌如今蒙上了台面呢，地毯换了铺法，版画重新挂过，窗台上添了一只花箱。桌上摆了相片，书桌上多了一盏灯，深色护壁板上方的窄架上也换了新的摆设。她认出了这一切背后的一番心思，心里深深感动。随后她的目光被那幅画像牵了过去，一时支撑不住，坐了下来。

"请别理会我。"她强自镇定地说，"我一会儿就好，况且先到这间屋来，本也是应当的。"她感激地望了伊迪丝一眼。"待会儿我才有气力道谢。不知怎么，你们把这间屋子弄得敞亮了不知多少。"

===Original===

Edith filled the gap of her brother’s silence. His eyes were dwelling on Jean’s lovely face, with its smooth oval and the delicate lips. Her throat was very white and perfectly molded, while neck and shoulder joined in a lissom curve he found amazingly attractive. There was strength in the slim straightness of her body, and grace in every gesture; but her chief allure lay in her eyes. These, full of changing light, seemed like calm, deep pools in the shadows of her dark brows, reflecting mood and thought with a sweet and rare fidelity. They held a soft luster all their own. For an instant Derrick stood quite motionless, a little blinded by it all. Then he heard Edith’s voice and responded to a note in it that was meant for him, though she spoke to Mrs. Millicent.

“I thought perhaps you’d sooner come in here at once, and it won’t be so hard the next time.”

Mrs. Millicent nodded, but her lips were trembling.

“Have you been very much bothered by strangers?” asked Jean quickly. “I’ve seen so many in the village, and most of them seemed on their way out here.”

“It was appalling till yesterday; then Sergeant Burke put a man on the gate, and that stopped it.”

===Chinese===

伊迪丝填补了兄长的沉默。他的目光正流连在琴那张可爱的脸上：光洁的鹅蛋脸，娇柔的唇。她的颈子极白，塑造得完美无瑕，脖颈与肩头连成一道柔韧的曲线，他觉得那曲线迷人得惊人。她身姿瘦削挺拔，透着一股力量，一举一动皆是风致；而她最大的魅力在那双眼睛。那双眼睛盈盈流转着变幻的光，像是浓黑眉影掩映下两泓沉静的深潭，把情绪与心思映照出来，忠实得又甜美、又罕见。它们自有一种柔和的光泽。有一霎，德里克站在那里全然不动，被这一切照得微微目眩。随后他听见了伊迪丝的声音，应和了其中一缕专为他而发的音调——虽然她说话的对象是米莉森特太太。

"我想着，你们也许情愿一下就先进这间屋来，这样下回再来，就不至于这么难受了。"

米莉森特太太点了点头，嘴唇却在发颤。

"生人把你们纠缠得厉害么？"琴赶忙问。"我在村子里见了好多，多半瞧着都是奔这儿来的。"

"到昨天为止简直骇人；后来伯克巡佐（Sergeant Burke）在大门上派了一个人，这才止住了。"

===Original===

“Where is Martin?” asked Mrs. Millicent. She had looked for him among the rose-trees and been relieved not to see him.

“He left yesterday,” said Derrick.

“Where did he go?”

“He didn’t say. In fact, I didn’t even see him, or know he was going. I noticed that he wasn’t in the garden at noon, and the tool-shed was closed; so I went to the cottage and found a note addressed to myself. It was rather pathetic. He just wrote that since there was nothing to keep him here now, he was going back. He didn’t say where, but it was probably to the Orient. There was a month’s wages due to him today, and he didn’t want them. Then he thanked me for treating him decently, said he was glad I was going to do what I told Blunt I proposed to do, and that was all, except a postscript about the Lady Hillingdons.”

===Chinese===

"马丁在哪儿？"米莉森特太太问。她方才在月季树间寻过他，没寻见，倒松了一口气。

"他昨天走了。"德里克说。

"去了哪儿？"

"他没说。其实我连他的面也没见着，连他要走都不知道。我注意到中午园子里没了他人影，工具棚也上了锁；我便到小屋去，发现一张写给我本人的条子。写得相当凄然。他只写道，如今既没有什么再留得住他，他要回去了。没说回哪里，多半是回东方。今天本该付他一个月的工钱，他也不要了。随后他谢我待他不薄，说他很高兴我打算去做我对布伦特（Blunt）说过的那件事；再就是关于「希灵登夫人」月季的一条附笔，如此而已。"

===Original===

“Poor Martin!” said Jean under her breath.

“And that other man?” added her mother.

“He will be free tomorrow, and he also will go.”

“To Burma?”

“I think so. He’s being detained till then on a technical charge only. He looks different now, with none of his former spring and activity. That’s because he knows what is going to be done. He seems dazed, and in a queer way almost horrified, as though it were sacrilege. It was the same way with him at the inquest, which was very short, considering everything. Burke, on the other hand, is like another man and bursting with importance. He expects to be regarded as an authority on unusual cases, and probably will be. There’s a great demand for his photograph already.”

===Chinese===

"可怜的马丁！"琴低声说。

"另外那个人呢？"她母亲添了一句。

"他明天就自由了，也要走。"

"回缅甸去？"

"我想是。到那时为止，只是拿一条技术性的罪名暂时押着他。他如今模样变了，从前那股敏捷矫健一丝也无。那是因为他知道将要做的是什么事。他像是懵了，还古怪地近乎惊骇，仿佛那是亵渎神明。在验尸审讯上他也是这副样子——权衡种种，那场审讯简短得很。伯克倒像换了一个人，浑身上下涨满了一股要紧劲儿。他指望人家把他看作办理奇案的权威，多半也真能如其所愿。他的照片如今已经有很多人来讨了。"

===Original===

“And what did the inquest result in?” she asked timidly.

“Only that the poor woman died at her own hands while under temporary insanity. There could be no other conclusion. Martin was not charged with anything before, so there was really nothing he needed to be cleared of. His evidence, as well as that of Blunt, was taken and accepted, and a statement will most likely be issued about what took place here two years ago. Martin was afraid he would be prosecuted for perjury, but the fact that it was his own wife gets him free of that. So really the matter is closed now, and it’s just a case of living down what is always bound to continue for a little while after a thing of this sort. If I were you I wouldn’t read the papers for a few days, and then it will be replaced by something else.”

He broke off, pitched his mind as far as possible from the subject, then remembered that there was one duty still to perform to close the affair for all time.

===Chinese===

"验尸审讯的结果是什么呢？"她怯怯地问。

"不过是裁定：那可怜的女人在一时精神错乱之下，死于自己之手。旁的结论下不出来。马丁先前不曾受过任何指控，所以实在没有什么需要洗刷的。他的证词同布伦特的一并录下并被采信，大约还会就两年前此地的旧案发布一份公告。马丁原怕自己因伪证（perjury）获罪，可死的是他自己的妻子，这一条便开脱了他。所以说，这件事如今当真了结了，剩下的无非是熬日子——这样一桩事过后，风言风语照例总要持续一小阵——熬过去就是了。我要是你们，这几天就别看报纸，过不了多久，它自会被别的事顶下去。"

他顿住了，把心思极力从这话题上荡开，随后想起还有最后一桩义务要履行，好把这件事千秋万代地了结。

===Original===

“I had a note from Mrs. Thursby this morning,” said Edith musingly. “She wrote that they would be passing this afternoon, and might they come in.”

Jean looked up. “She must be tremendously curious.”

“I expect so. She’s rather that sort of woman. I haven’t seen them for about three months.”

Mrs. Millicent smiled a little. “She’s a great believer in the power of money and even thought I’d sell my husband’s portrait, to which she took a great fancy. I couldn’t have it with me, as there’s no room for a big picture in our cottage. There are some more things upstairs, too, that are ours; but I sold everything else in this room.”

===Chinese===

"今天早上我收到瑟斯比太太（Mrs. Thursby）一张条子。"伊迪丝若有所思地说。"她写道，他们今天下午路过这里，可否进来坐坐。"

琴抬起头来。"她一定好奇得不得了。"

"我料想是。她就是那么一种女人。我大约有三个月没见他们了。"

米莉森特太太微微一笑。"她顶信银钱的威力，甚至想着我会肯卖我丈夫的画像——她对那幅画喜欢得了不得。我不能把它带在身边，我们如今住的小屋里，搁不下一幅大画。楼上还有几件东西也是我们的；但这间屋里其余的东西，我都卖掉了。"

===Original===

Derrick shot a swift inquiring glance at Jean and made a slight gesture toward the mantel. She looked puzzled for a minute, then nodded.

“You didn’t sell this, Mrs. Millicent?” He touched the panel, and the jade god gleamed from its wooden prison.

She put her hand to her breast. “So that is where it was kept! I never knew till Jean told me. No, I didn’t sell it. I never thought of that.”

===Chinese===

德里克朝琴飞快地递去一个询问的眼色，又朝壁炉台那边微微做了个手势。她怔了片刻，随即点了点头。

"这一件您没有卖掉吧，米莉森特太太？"他碰了碰那块暗板，玉神（the jade god）便从木头的囚笼里透出光来。

她把手按在胸前。"原来它一直收在那里！直到琴告诉我，我才知道。没有，我没有卖它。我从未想到过它。"

===Original===

“It’s hard to say just what it suggests to me now,” he began slowly, “and still more what it may really mean to a man like Blunt. It’s one of those things to which there’s no straight answer. But if there had been no jade god here”﻿—he paused, then added with a brilliant smile﻿—“I wouldn’t have found Jean. Edith doesn’t believe in all this, but﻿—”

“I didn’t say that,” interrupted his sister, “but just that I didn’t understand, and”﻿—she shook her head decisively﻿—“I didn’t want to.”

“Perhaps you were the most right,” he chuckled, “when you suggested that the thing wasn’t somehow healthy.”

“If I did, I stick to it. It’s beastly.”

===Chinese===

"它如今究竟在我心里唤起什么，很难说清，"他缓缓开口道，"而对于布伦特那样的人，它真正的分量就更难说了。这种事情，没有一个直截了当的答案。可倘若当初这里没有玉神，"他顿了顿，随即绽出一脸明亮的笑，"我便寻不着琴了。伊迪丝不信这一切，不过——"

"我没有说不信，"他妹妹插嘴道，"我只是说我不懂，而且——"她斩钉截铁地摇了摇头，"我也不想懂。"

"也许你最是对的，"他轻声笑道，"当初是你说，这东西在那层古怪的意思上，算不得有益身心。"

"我要真说过，如今也还认账。它邪门得很。"

===Original===

Mrs. Millicent put out a hand as though to touch it, but withdrew at the stare of the tiny basilisk eyes. It seemed to her that this fragment of carved stone, glimmering opaquely as the rays of the level sun filtered through it, still threatened her, and she felt grateful for the steadiness of the hand that held it. Youth was about to dissipate the nightmare of the past. But somehow she did not want to see the thing done.

“I think,” she said, with a glance at Edith, “that you and I might let these two perform the ceremony by themselves.”

Edith laughed and nodded. “Jack will certainly smash the end of a finger before it’s over, and I can see by his face that he’s in tune for a regular oblation. It’s that sacrificial look.”

Derrick grinned cheerfully but did not speak. When they were alone he put the image on the mantel and took his girl in his arms.

===Chinese===

米莉森特太太伸出一只手，像要触它，却在那对小蛇怪眼睛的瞪视下缩了回来。在她看来，这片雕琢过的石头，在平射的日光渗过它时幽幽地泛着滞光，仍在威胁着她；她感激那只托着它的手这样稳。青春年华就要把过去的梦魇驱散了。可不知怎么，她并不想亲眼看着这件事做出来。

"我想，"她望了伊迪丝一眼，说，"你我两个不妨回避一下，让这一对自己去行那仪式吧。"

伊迪丝笑着点了点头。"杰克准会在完事之前砸了自己一截指头，况且瞧他那副脸色，他正来劲，要行一场像模像样的祭献呢。就是那副祭献的神情。"

德里克快活地咧嘴一笑，却没有作声。等只剩他们两个人了，他把神像搁上壁炉台，将他的姑娘拥进怀里。

===Original===

“It’s years since I saw you.”

She smiled back, her face very close to his. “Dearest, it’s only three days.”

“Which is three too many. What an inspiration of your mother’s! Do you know what smashing that thing will be like with you here?”

“What, Jack?”

===Chinese===

"我像有几百年没见着你了。"

她回以一笑，脸凑得极近。"最亲爱的，不过才三天。"

"而这已是多出来的三天了。你母亲这主意多妙！你可知道，有你在场，把那东西砸掉会是怎样一种滋味？"

"怎样，杰克？"

===Original===

“Like gathering up all that is dark and ominous and deadly in the world, and obliterating it in front of everything that is sweet and lovely and desirable. You never knew that the first one to go was the one who made it, and then fear of it began to spread. I’ll tell you about it some day﻿—the whole story. But now it’s all ended and done with.”

“Where will you break it, Jack?”

He stole a glance at Millicent’s portrait. “Here, on the hearth, under that. I think he’ll know about it and be glad. It won’t burn, but I’ve got a wax duplicate that ought to make a pillar of flame.”

Opening a drawer in the desk, he took out a hammer and the model, then laid the image on the tile hearth.

===Chinese===

"就像把世间一切黑暗的、凶险的、致命的东西收拢到一处，当着一切甜美的、可爱的、叫人心向往之的东西的面，把它抹除净尽。你从不知道，头一个遭殃的正是造它的那个人，随后对它的恐惧便蔓延开来。哪天我把全部始末讲给你听。不过如今，这一切都已经收场了结了。"

"你要在哪里砸它，杰克？"

他朝米莉森特先生的画像偷觑了一眼。"就在这里，壁炉跟前，在那幅画底下。我想他会知晓这件事，而且会高兴的。它烧不着，可我备了一件蜡模，该能烧起一根火焰的柱子。"

他拉开书桌的一只抽屉，取出一柄铁锤和那件蜡模，然后把神像搁在瓷砖铺的壁炉底上。

===Original===

“There is proof, at any rate for you and me,” he said thoughtfully, “that this exercised a strange influence over the minds of many persons. It is the object of fear among thousands we shall never see, and the story of it has run through valleys and hills on the other side of the earth where the brown people talk of it in whispers. It has brought men round the world, and there are others who are waiting for the word that will bring them, too. Just so long as it exists there will be pain and theft and crime and fear. And this is the finish of all that, darling.”

He raised the hammer. Driven with all the strength of his wrist, it fell fair on the malignant head. There was a shivering sound as of tinkling glass, and the jade god dissolved into mottled green fragments. He felt a sharp pang in his thumb. An emerald splinter quivered there, like a miniature javelin beaded with blood.

“Evil to the very end,” he grunted, then struck again.

===Chinese===

"至少对你我而言，这是明证，"他若有所思地说，"它对许多人的心智施过一种奇特的影响。在成千上万我们永远见不着的人中间，它是恐惧的对象；在地球那一面的山谷丘壑间，它的事迹四处流传，棕色皮肤的人们谈起它时都压着嗓音。它曾把人从天涯海角引到这里来，还有另一些人正等着一声召唤，好把他们也引了来。只要它存在一天，就还会有苦痛、偷窃、罪孽与恐惧。而这一切，如今都在这里收场了，亲爱的。"

他举起了铁锤。腕上全副的力气抡将下去，端端正正落在那颗凶恶的头颅上。一声脆响，如同玻璃的碎鸣，玉神崩解成斑斑驳驳的绿色碎片。他感到拇指上一阵锐痛。一枚翡翠似的碎片钉在那里颤动，像一支小小的标枪，凝着血珠。

"作恶作到最后一刻。"他哼了一声，又是一锤。

===Original===

The god’s head dwindled to powder. He swept back the wreckage and dropped the wax model into the smoldering embers. Flame shot up, leaping, sputtering, and hissing. They stood staring at it, their cheeks touching. It was in Derrick’s mind that in this flame the dross of life was being burned away. Jean did not move till the fiery pyramid subsided. And as it died there came the sound of a horn from the drive.

“The Thursbys,” he said disgustedly. “Do you want to see them?”

“Please, no. What had I better do?”

“I’d go to your mother, and please ask Edith to join me here.” He gave a sudden little smile. “I’ve a sort of foolish idea that﻿—” He stopped, glanced at the hearth, and shook his head. “No, it’s too foolish.”

===Chinese===

神像的头颅碎成了齑粉。他把残屑扫到一旁，将蜡模投进将熄未熄的余烬里。火苗腾地蹿起，跳动着，噼啪作响，嘶嘶有声。两人站在那里望着它，脸颊相偎。德里克心里想的是，生命的渣滓正在这火焰里烧尽。直到那座火焰的金字塔矮了下去，琴才挪动身子。而就在火将熄时，车道上传来一声喇叭。

"瑟斯比一家。"他腻烦地说。"你想见他们吗？"

"请不要。我最好做什么去？"

"我劝你上你母亲那儿去，还请叫伊迪丝来这儿陪我。"他忽然露出一丝笑意。"我有个傻乎乎的念头——"他停住了，瞥了一眼壁炉，摇了摇头。"不，太傻了。"

===Original===

“Tell me quickly.”

“Wait till Thursby has gone. Kiss me quickly instead.”

She vanished, her cheeks glowing. A moment later Edith came in.

“Well, our friends are here, but why couldn’t they be content with what’s in the papers?”

===Chinese===

"快告诉我。"

"等瑟斯比走了再说。先赶快亲我一下。"

她倏然去了，双颊烧得通红。片刻之后，伊迪丝走了进来。

"好了，咱们的朋友们到了。可他们怎么就不能满足于报纸上登的那些呢？"

===Original===

He had no time to answer, for the Thursbys were already in the hall. Mrs. Thursby swept in like a fresh breeze, followed by her husband. Derrick thought the latter looked a little sheepish.

“My dear,” said the stout woman explosively to Edith, “what a perfectly awful time you must have had! We were over in France when we read of it, and even now when I think of that woman Perkins it gives me the shivers. I’ve blamed myself so much for not telling your brother everything the first time he came here.”

“Matter of fact,” chimed in Thursby, with a sidelong glance at the portrait, “I didn’t say anything because it didn’t seem necessary. I reckoned that ignorance was bliss so far as you were concerned, and we’d had rather a dose of it ourselves. The agents thought so, too.”

===Chinese===

他来不及回答，瑟斯比夫妇已经到了门厅。瑟斯比太太像一阵新风似的卷了进来，丈夫跟在后头。德里克觉得后一位的神色略显讪讪。

"亲爱的，"胖太太冲着伊迪丝炸开了话匣，"你们这段日子一定可怕极了！事情见报时我们正在法国，直到如今，一想起珀金斯那个女人，我还浑身发抖。你哥哥头一回来这儿时，我没把一切都告诉他，为此我不知道责备了自己多少回。"

"其实呢，"瑟斯比插话道，斜眼瞥了那幅画像一眼，"我什么也没说，是因为觉得没有必要。我盘算着，就你们而言，无知便是福，况且我们自己也领教得够受了。经纪人也这么想。"

===Original===

“Perhaps it was,” said Derrick dryly, “and there’s no real harm done. The thing is finally cleared up.”

“As I said before, I could never understand that woman,” went on Mrs. Thursby, “but of course I do now. She must have been disappointed in love early in life, and married Martin to get even with someone else. Women often do that and pay for it afterward. But fancy living with her as we both did! Fancy a mad housemaid at your bedside saying the tea is ready, and thinking, perhaps, about killing one all the time. I wonder what sent her mad, Mr. Derrick. Didn’t you hear that?”

“There was insanity in her family.”

“Had she been like that for long?”

“A good many years, it seems.”

===Chinese===

"也许是这样，"德里克淡淡地说，"好在并没有真正伤着谁。事情总算水落石出了。"

"正如我从前说的，那个女人我从来捉摸不透，"瑟斯比太太接着说，"可如今自然明白了。她一定早年失恋过，嫁给马丁是为了同另一个人扯平。女人们常干这种事，事后又要付出代价。可想想我们俩竟同她住在一处！想想一个疯女仆站在你床边说茶备好了，心里没准儿时时刻刻盘算着宰掉你。我纳闷是什么把她逼疯的，德里克先生。这个你没有听说么？"

"她家族里有精神错乱的病史。"

"她那样儿已经很久了么？"

"看去有许多年了。"

===Original===

Mrs. Thursby took a deep breath. “Well, that was the only thing the matter with Beech Lodge.”

“What?” asked Edith curiously.

“A crazy housemaid. I felt that as soon as we left the place. Of course,” she continued reflectively, “you’ll think I must have been a bit crazy myself for not discharging her. I did make up my mind to that a good many times, but when it came to looking her in the face and saying she wouldn’t be wanted any more, I﻿—well, I just couldn’t. Silly, wasn’t it?”

“I can almost understand that.”

“Glad you can. I couldn’t. Was she nice to you?”

“She was a wonderful servant.”

===Chinese===

瑟斯比太太深深吸了一口气。"唉，山毛榉山庄的病根，也就这一个。"

"什么？"伊迪丝好奇地问。

"一个疯女仆。我们一搬离那地方，我就有这个感觉了。当然，"她若有所思地接着说，"你们准会想，我不把她辞退，自己也准有点儿疯。我的确许多回拿定了主意，可一到要看着她的脸、对她说以后用不着她了的时候，我——唉，我就是开不了口。傻，是不是？"

"这个我几乎能体会。"

"你能体会，我很高兴。我可不能。她待你们好么？"

"她是个不可多得的好仆人。"

===Original===

“Well, you see she liked you, but gave me the creeps. And the funny thing was that I couldn’t imagine the house without her, though it seems perfectly natural now, and this room is ever so much brighter.”

Thursby nodded. “It’s rather a pity you couldn’t imagine it.”

The stout woman laughed. “James has never quite forgiven me.”

“For what?” asked Derrick. His eyes were keen.

===Chinese===

"这个么，你们瞧，她喜欢你们，却叫我起鸡皮疙瘩。可笑的是，那时候我竟没法想象宅子里没有她，虽说如今看来再自然不过，这间屋子也亮堂了不知多少。"

瑟斯比点了点头。"可惜你当时想象不出来。"

胖太太笑了起来。"詹姆斯（James）到现在也没有完全饶恕我。"

"饶恕什么？"德里克问。他的眼睛很敏锐。

===Original===

“For letting the place at all. We took another, stayed in it a month, then gave that up, and have been living in hotels ever since. I hate living in my trunks.”

“You don’t happen to be in the market for Beech Lodge, do you?”

She sent him a swift look of intelligence. “Whatever made you think of that? Are we, James? If I do the letting, you generally do the renting.”

Light began to dawn on the Derricks, and Edith made a cautious little signal.

===Chinese===

"怪我当初不该把这宅子租出去。我们另租了一处，住了一个月，又放弃了，打那以后一直住旅馆。我恨透了从箱笼里掏日子过。"

"你们眼下不至于正打山毛榉山庄的主意吧？"

她敏捷地递来一个心照不宣的眼色。"你怎么会想到这个？咱们是想么，詹姆斯？但凡我张罗着往外租的，到头来多半由你租回来。"

德里克兄妹心里渐渐亮堂起来，伊迪丝小心翼翼地打了个小暗号。

===Original===

“My brother is only joking, of course. The idea is too funny. We’ve just had all the expense and trouble of moving in, and it’s foolish to dream of anything but staying here. Don’t mind what he says.”

Thursby pushed out his lips. “Oh, I don’t know that it’s so foolish. If circumstances, I mean business ones, are satisfactory, nothing is foolish. I learned long ago that when my wife gets a premonition that we’re going to do something, we most always do. For instance,” he blurted, “if she were to say she had a feeling we were going to move back to Beech Lodge I’d bet on it. It’s safe money.”

Derrick laughed. “Aren’t you reckoning a little without your host?”

“I know it sounds like that. I say, I wonder what Mrs. Millicent thought of all this.”

===Chinese===

"我哥哥不过是说笑，当然。这念头太可笑了。我们才刚刚费钱费力搬进来，除了在这里住下去，再妄想别的就是犯傻。别理会他的话。"

瑟斯比噘起嘴唇。"噢，我说不上这念头有多可笑。倘若情况——我是说生意上的情况——叫人满意，那就没有什么可笑的。我早就明白，我太太但凡起了预感，说我们要做什么事，我们十有八九真会做。比方说，"他脱口而出，"她若是说她有种感觉，觉得我们要搬回山毛榉山庄去，我就押这个宝。稳赚的钱。"

德里克笑了。"你们这不是有点儿打如意算盘，没把正主儿算在里面么？"

"我知道听着是这么回事。我说，不知米莉森特太太对这一切作何感想。"

===Original===

“She probably thinks it’s a sort of release for that woman and everyone else,” put in his wife hastily; “and that’s the only way to look at it. A sort of a general cleanup, I call it. Fancy that gardener coming back, too. He must have been the only person in the world who wasn’t frightened of his wife.”

“Where do you think you’ll be this summer?” interposed Edith.

Mrs. Thursby folded her plump hands. “I shouldn’t be surprised if that depended on you,” she said calmly.

“Oh!”

===Chinese===

"她多半觉得，这对那个女人和其余所有人都是一种解脱，"他的妻子赶忙插话，"而且也只能这么看。依我说，这是一场大扫除。再想想那个花匠居然回来了。他准是世上唯一不怕自己老婆的人。"

"你们今年夏天打算在哪里过？"伊迪丝插进来问。

瑟斯比太太交叠起她那双肥胖的手。"这件事要是取决于你们，我可一点不奇怪。"她平静地说。

"哎呀！"

===Original===

The other woman nodded and went on with a kind of placid deliberation. “My dear, it’s no earthly use beating about the bush any longer, and I’m going to come straight out with it. Very soon after we let this place to you, we took another, didn’t like it, and then I knew we’d been too impulsive about letting Beech Lodge, and I wanted to come back to it, Perkins or no Perkins. I never gave the dreadful woman a thought, because she didn’t seem to matter nearly so much when one had not to look at her. I told my husband about it, but he only laughed, said I had changed my mind too late in the day and the idea was absurd. Later we went over to France for a while.”

“Were you there long?” asked Derrick curiously.

“No, only a few weeks. I couldn’t settle down somehow. Then we read about what happened here, and I knew what was the matter with me. It was just as though that woman had telegraphed me that she was out of the way now, and I might come back.” She paused, with an odd expression on her round face, and glanced approvingly round the room. “So now, if it is possible to arrange it, I want to come. If you’re agreeable, then it’s up to your brother and my husband. So far as I’m concerned, it’s not a matter of money, and James knows that.”

===Chinese===

那一位点了点头，带着一种安详的从容接着说下去。"亲爱的，再拐弯抹角就半点用处也没有了，我索性打开天窗说亮话。我们把这宅子租给你们之后没多久，就另租了一处，不喜欢，那时我就明白，当初出租山毛榉山庄，我们太冲动了，我想搬回来，管它珀金斯不珀金斯。那个可怕的女人我压根没放在心上，因为用不着见着她的时候，她似乎就无足轻重多了。我把这话告诉了我丈夫，可他只是笑，说我改主意改得太晚，这念头荒唐。后来我们去法国待了一阵。"

"在那边待得久吗？"德里克好奇地问。

"不久，只住了几个星期。我不知怎么总安顿不下来。后来我们读到了这里发生的事，我明白自己的毛病出在哪里了。那就好比那个女人给我来了封电报，说她已经不碍事了，我可以回来了。"她顿了顿，圆脸上带着一种古怪的表情，赞许地把这间屋子环视了一圈。"所以现在，只要安排得成，我就想回来。你们若是首肯，接下来就看令兄和我丈夫的了。依我这边，这不是钱的事，詹姆斯是知道的。"

===Original===

She leaned back with a nod which announced that on this subject she had now emptied her mind, and there was no chance of misunderstanding it on the part of her husband. He was the means to the end. Thursby’s hands were deep in his pockets, and he stared out over the lawn, his brows puckered, as though he were adding up figures, which indeed he was. Edith’s eyes caught those of her brother, and she signaled a message that left no possibility of doubt in his mind. At that he turned to Thursby:

“Shall we have a stroll? I’ve put in quite a lot of new roses, and there’ll be something of a show here next summer.”

The little man nodded jerkily, and they went out. Mrs. Thursby sat up straight and heaved a contented sigh.

===Chinese===

她向后一靠，点了点头，这一点头等于宣布：在这个题目上，她心里的话已经倒空，她丈夫方面绝无误解的可能。他不过是达到目的的手段。瑟斯比双手深插在口袋里，凝望着外面的草坪，眉头蹙起，像是在加一串数字——他也确实是在加数字。伊迪丝的目光接住了兄长的目光，递过去的信号让他心里再无半点疑惑。于是他转向瑟斯比：

"咱们去走走怎么样？我新种了好些月季，明年夏天这里会很有一番可看的。"

小个子男人一磕一磕地点了点头，两人出去了。瑟斯比太太直起身来，满足地舒了一口气。

===Original===

“Then, that’ll be all right, if it suits you. Isn’t it all queer?”

“I think everyone feels that.”

“Well, of course I don’t know the ins and outs of it, only what’s in the papers, and I suppose there’s a lot more, but I felt that neither you nor I had much to do with that woman staying on here. However, I’ve my eye on a jewel of a girl now who will go anywhere. Do you suppose if those men agree there’ll have to be another inventory?”

“I’m afraid so, though we haven’t had time yet to do much damage. That French window was broken, but it’s been repaired.” She paused, while something drew her eyes to the hearth. “And there’s that jade image,” she added uncertainly; “but that’s Mrs. Millicent’s.”

===Chinese===

"那么，只要合你们的意，这事就算妥了。这一切不都古怪得很么？"

"我想人人都有这个感觉。"

"这个么，内情底细我自然不知道，只知道报纸上登的那些，想必还有好多没登的；可我总觉得，那个女人留在这里，你我都没有多少干系。不过，我已经相中了一个宝贝似的姑娘，哪里都肯去。你看，要是那两位先生谈妥了，还得再办一回清点、立一册清点册吧？"

"恐怕是的，好在我们还没来得及造成多少损坏。那扇落地窗破过，已经修好了。"她顿了顿，有什么东西把她的目光引向了壁炉。"还有那尊玉像，"她迟疑地添了一句，"可那是米莉森特太太的。"

===Original===

“What jade image? I never saw one here. Where is it?”

“What’s left of it is in the fireplace.”

The stout little woman stooped and picked out an emerald splinter.

“My dear, what perfectly lovely stuff! Were you going to throw it away?”

“It’s Mrs. Millicent’s, and she asked to have the image destroyed.”

“And jade, too! How queer some people are! It’s very fashionable now, and there’s enough here to make some gorgeous earrings.”

===Chinese===

"什么玉像？我在这儿从来没见过一件。在哪儿？"

"剩下的都在炉膛里。"

矮胖的小个子妇人俯下身去，拣出一枚翡翠似的碎片。

"哎呀亲爱的，这料子多美啊！你们本来打算扔掉么？"

"那是米莉森特太太的，她要求把神像毁掉。"

"还是玉的！有些人多古怪！如今玉正时兴呢，这里剩的就够打一副漂亮耳环了。"

===Original===

The thought of the remodeled god with his cold fingers at her throat gave Edith an involuntary chill.

“I really don’t want it, and am sure Mrs. Millicent doesn’t, so please take it if you wish.”

Mrs. Thursby dropped the splinter into her bag, got on her knees, and poked about among the ashes.

“I’m afraid the rest is all dust. What a pity! I’ve been trying to mesmerize James for years into buying me something of jade, but he simply won’t. Now I’m going to give him a surprise, so please don’t say a thing about it. Here they come now, and I think it’s all arranged. James is pretty quick in business matters.”

===Chinese===

想到那尊改造过的神像用它冰凉的手指扣在自己的喉咙上，伊迪丝不由得打了个寒噤。

"我真的不想要它，而且米莉森特太太肯定也不要，所以你们若愿意，就请拿去吧。"

瑟斯比太太把碎片丢进手袋，跪到地上，在灰烬里拨拉起来。

"只怕其余的都成了灰。多可惜！多年来我一直想把詹姆斯催眠过来，叫他买件玉器给我，他偏偏不肯。如今我要给他一个意外，所以请你们一个字也别提。他们来了——我看都谈妥了。詹姆斯办起生意上的事，手脚利落得很。"

===Original===

The Thursbys’ car rolled away a few minutes later, and Derrick darted upstairs. He found Jean and her mother in Edith’s room and, linking arms, marched them cheerily back to the study, where Edith waited with a patience in which there was no virtue whatever. Then he put his arm round Jean.

“Thursby,” he said contentedly, “was like clay in the hands of the potter. I began by reminding him that not only had we the lease till next winter, but also the right of extension for another three years on the same terms. He pretended to have forgotten that, but of course he hadn’t. Then I hinted that I’d get into frightful trouble with Edith if I upset all her plans, and that helped a good deal. It was quite clear from his manner that he had his orders. I dwelt as much as I dared on the discomfort of moving and all that, and the more I said the more anxious he got. He must have the highest regard for his wife’s wishes. Anyway, it’s arranged. He makes good the cost of our moving here, gives five hundred for the cancellation of the lease, and also meets the cost of our moving out. And I think that’s about all.”

===Chinese===

几分钟后，瑟斯比家的汽车驶走了，德里克一阵风似的奔上楼去。他在伊迪丝房里找到了琴和她母亲，挎起两人的胳膊，欢欢喜喜地把她们押送回书房——伊迪丝早已等在那里，那份耐心里头可没有半点美德可言。随后他搂住了琴。

"瑟斯比啊，"他心满意足地说，"简直是陶匠手里的一团泥。我开头先提醒他，我们不仅握着到明年冬天为止的租约，还有按原条件续租三年的权利。他装作忘了这一条，当然他没忘。随后我暗示，我若把伊迪丝的全盘安排搅乱，跟她可没法交代，这一条帮了大忙。瞧他那副神气，他分明是奉了太太之命行事的。我又在搬家如何劳民伤财上头，拣敢说的尽量多说，我说得越多，他越急。他对他太太的心愿必定敬若圣旨。总之，事情谈妥了。他补偿我们搬进来的全部费用，付五百镑作退租费，还承担我们搬出去的开销。我想大概就是这些了。"

===Original===

“How perfectly wonderful!” said Jean. “Aren’t you glad?”

“Glad is no word for it.”

“Jack,” put in Edith, “I never knew before you were such a business man.” She paused and glanced at him suspiciously. “Just when have you committed us to that move?”

“A month from today. I thought it over carefully and decided that ought to suit everyone.”

“What!”

===Chinese===

"这可太好了！"琴说。"你不高兴吗？"

"高兴二字根本不够形容。"

"杰克，"伊迪丝插话，"我从前竟不知道你还是这么一个生意人。"她顿了顿，狐疑地瞥了他一眼。"你到底是什么时候把咱们这次搬家敲定的？"

"从今天起一个月。我仔细盘算过，觉得这个日子该当合所有人的意。"

"什么！"

===Original===

Derrick’s eyes grew soft. He leaned over to Mrs. Millicent and took both her hands in his.

“May I have Jean a month from today?” he said very gently.

===Chinese===

德里克的目光变得温柔了。他向米莉森特太太俯下身去，双手握住了她的双手。

"一个月后的今天，肯把琴许给我吗？"他极为轻柔地说。
